# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 20 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 1

Om. Śrī. Homage to Śrī Gaṇādhipati.

वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे । यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमानि गजाननं ॥ (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame | yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāni gajānanaṃ ‖*)

यस्य निश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं ॥ (*yasya niśvasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyo 'khilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ ‖*)

वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्यायी सदाशिवः । तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyī sadāśivaḥ | tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*)

With the Bhāṣya of Sāyaṇa. Ṛgveda-Saṃhitā. Part 20.

The fourth chapter in the fourth Aṣṭaka.

The fifth Maṇḍala.

Sūkta 63

**Introduction**

A brief account of Mitra and Varuṇa.

In the eleven sūktas of this Maṇḍala, from the 62nd to the 72nd, Mitra and Varuṇa are praised. A brief account of these deities is given here.

The companionship of Mitra and Varuṇa is well known. Very often both are praised together. Often there is praise addressed to Varuṇa individually. But it is rare for Mitra to be praised without Varuṇa. As the commentator explains, Mitra is the presiding deity of the day and Varuṇa

### Printed p. 2

of the night; if this is indeed the fact, then the companionship of the two is natural. Commenting on the mantra Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-89-3, Sāyaṇa writes आहरभिमानिनं देवं । मित्रं वा अहरिति श्रुतेः (*āharabhimāninaṃ devaṃ | mitraṃ vā ahariti śruteḥ*); since there is a Vedic sentence showing the identity of Mitra with the day (ahas), he states that Mitra is the deity presiding over the day, and that Varuṇa is known as the one presiding over the night. The word "Varuṇa" is formed from the root "vṛ", which has the sense of covering. This deity binds the wicked with his noose, and the night belongs to Varuṇa. वृणोति पापकृतः स्वकीयैः पाशैरावृणोतीति रात्र्यभिमानिदेवो वरुणः । श्रूयते च-वारुणी रात्रिः । (तै. ब्रा. १-७-१०-१ इति) (*vṛṇoti pāpakṛtaḥ svakīyaiḥ pāśairāvṛṇotīti rātryabhimānidevo varuṇaḥ | śrūyate ca-vāruṇī rātriḥ | (tai. brā. 1-7-10-1 iti)*)

Likewise, in stating the meaning of the mantra 1-141-9, he gives the same etymologies and adds that Aryaman is the one who stands between this pair of deities (आर्यमा उभयोर्मध्यवर्ति देवः (*āryamā ubhayormadhyavarti devaḥ*)). In the explanation of the mantra 1-90-1 (आर्यमा अहोरात्रविभागस्य कर्ता सूर्यः (*āryamā ahorātravibhāgasya kartā sūryaḥ*)) he indicates that the Sun, who makes the division of day and night, is called Aryaman. Again, in the Bhāṣya on the mantra 2-38-8 the nature of Varuṇa is described thus:

योनिं स्थानं निमिषि निमेषे सवितुरस्तमये सति विश्रमार्थं प्राणिभ्यः प्रयच्छति । वरुणस्य रात्रेर्निर्वाहकत्वात् । (*yoniṃ sthānaṃ nimiṣi nimeṣe saviturastamaye sati viśramārthaṃ prāṇibhyaḥ prayacchati | varuṇasya rātrernirvāhakatvāt |*)

Varuṇa is the one who, after sunset, provides living beings with a place of rest. He is the manager of all the affairs of the night.

In 1-35-1, Mitra and Varuṇa are invoked together with the deities Agni, Savitṛ and Night: "For the accomplishment of our welfare I invoke Agni with prayer. For our help I call Mitra and Varuṇa. I call Night, who gives rest to the world." Thus they are praised in company with the other deities. In some places it is also stated that the Sun himself is what the word Mitra denotes (मित्रशब्दस्य सूर‍्यवाचित्वात् (*mitraśabdasya sūryavācitvāt*) Tai. Saṃ. Bhā. 1-8-16-1).**NOTE** In the same context, giving the etymology of the word Varuṇa as well, he says वरुणशब्दस्य अंधकारवदावर्तकवाचित्वात् । (*varuṇaśabdasya aṃdhakāravadāvartakavācitvāt |*); that is, Varuṇa is known by this name because by nature he spreads everywhere like darkness. It is also said: मैत्रं वा अहः । वारुणी रात्रिः ॥ (*maitraṃ vā ahaḥ | vāruṇī rātriḥ ‖*) (Tai. Brā. 1-7-10-1)

न वा इदं दिवा न नक्तमासीदव्याकृतं ते देवा मित्रावरुणावब्रुवन्निदं नो विवासयतमिति । (*na vā idaṃ divā na naktamāsīdavyākṛtaṃ te devā mitrāvaruṇāvabruvannidaṃ no vivāsayatamiti |*) (Tai. Saṃ. 6-4-8-3)

मित्रो अहरजनयद्वरुणो रात्रिं ॥ (*mitro aharajanayadvaruṇo rātriṃ ‖*) (Kṛ. Saṃ. 6-4-8-3)**NOTE**

### Printed p. 3

In the beginning there was no division of the world into day and night; everything was unmanifest. Then the gods said to Mitra and Varuṇa, "Make for us the division of day and night"; whereupon Mitra made the day and Varuṇa the night. So the passage of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā declares.

In the Ṛk-Saṃhitā, in the praise of Varuṇa:

स क्षपः परिषस्वजे न्यु १ स्रो मायया दधे स विश्वं परि दर्शतः । (*sa kṣapaḥ pariṣasvaje nyu 1 sro māyayā dadhe sa viśvaṃ pari darśataḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-41-3)

This same Varuṇa, of beautiful form, embraces the night. By his power of māyā he creates the day and establishes everything in its own appointed place and time; so he is extolled.

In the Bhāṣya on Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-87-1:

अस्तं गच्छन् सूर्य एव वरुण इत्युच्यते । स हि स्वगमनेन रात्रीर्ज नयति (*astaṃ gacchan sūrya eva varuṇa ityucyate | sa hi svagamanena rātrīrja nayati*)

Sāyaṇa explains that the Sun, when he goes to his setting, is himself called Varuṇa, and that by his own going he creates the night.

अयं वै लोको मित्रः । असौ वरुणः । (*ayaṃ vai loko mitraḥ | asau varuṇaḥ |*) (Śa. Brā. 12-9-2-12)

The Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa declares that this earth is Mitra and the heavenly world is Varuṇa. Thus the description of their qualities takes many forms.

**The excellent qualities of Mitra and Varuṇa**

The special qualities of Mitra and Varuṇa, as they are described in the Ṛk-Saṃhitā, are briefly set out here. Of these two deities, Varuṇa is praised as one who can at times come within the sight of the sacrificer.

"I have seen Varuṇa, who is visible to all, with my own eyes. I have also seen with my own eyes his chariot standing upon the earth." (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-25-18)

"When I obtained the sight of Varuṇa, his splendour had a radiance equal to the splendour of Agni." (7-88-2)

"Varuṇa, of firm resolve and of great nature, exercising lordship, sits at ease in his dwelling-place." (1-25-10)

"While he stands clad in a golden mantle and exercises lordship, his attendants, or else other deities, stand around him on every side." (1-25-13)

### Printed p. 4

"The sacrificers, the performers of the sacrifice, who enter the house of Varuṇa and worship him face to face: that house has a thousand doors." (7-85-5)

"In the firm house with a thousand supporting pillars, the radiant Varuṇa dwells together with Mitra. Both are of unobstructed splendour." (राजाना (*rājānā*); अहृणीयमाना (*ahṛṇīyamānā*)) (2-41-5; 5-62-6)

"These two, Mitra and Varuṇa, lords of the world, mount an excellent chariot, drawn by horses, which at sunrise takes a golden colour and at sunset changes into the form of iron, and they set out on their journey." (5-62-8)

"Mounting this chariot and travelling in the highest atmosphere, both of them survey all the heaven and earth completely." (5-62-4, 8; 5-63-1)

"Varuṇa has wide-spreading eyes; therefore he is far-sighted." (1-25-5, 16; 8-90-2)**NOTE**

"This same Varuṇa is thousand-eyed (सहस्रचक्षस् (*sahasracakṣas*))." (7-34-18)**NOTE**

In some places Varuṇa is praised as one whose messenger is golden-hued (हिरण्यपक्षं वरुणस्य दूतं (*hiraṇyapakṣaṃ varuṇasya dūtaṃ*)) (10-123-6).

In still other places the Sun is described as the eye of Mitra and Varuṇa (चक्षुर्मित्रस्य वरुणस्य (*cakṣurmitrasya varuṇasya*)) (1-1-5-1; 7-63-1; 7-61-1; 10-37-1).**NOTE**

Aryaman, who belongs to the very group of the Ādityas, together with Mitra and Varuṇa, is described in many places as "सूरचक्षसः (*sūracakṣasaḥ*)" (for example, 7-66-10).

"Mitra and Varuṇa have hands fit for the most skilful action."

"Varuṇa is designated in many places by the epithet 'Rājā' (king). He is the lord of gods, men and the rest. He is the sovereign of the whole world." (1-24-7, 8; 4-1-2; 5-40-7; 7-64-1; 10-103-9; 10-173-5; 10-132-4)

"त्वं विश्वेषां वरुणासि राजा ये च देवा आसुर ये च मर्ताः (*tvaṃ viśveṣāṃ varuṇāsi rājā ye ca devā āsura ye ca martāḥ*)" (2-27-10)

"Varuṇa is the king of all the worlds (विश्वस्य भुवनस्य राजा (*viśvasya bhuvanasya rājā*))." (5-85-3)

"Moreover, he is the emperor (सम्राट् (*samrāṭ*)) of all beings, moving and unmoving." (7-87-6; 1-25-10; 2-28-6; 5-85-1; 6-68-9; 8-42-1)

### Printed p. 5

"Varuṇa's lordship does not depend on the help of others; he has independent lordship (स्वराट् (*svarāṭ*))." (2-28-1)

"Just as the epithets 'Rājā', 'Samrāṭ' and the like are applied to Varuṇa individually, so there are also many occasions on which they are applied to Mitra and Varuṇa together." (1-71-9; 1-76-1-4; 1-137-1; 5-62-6; 5-63-2, 3, 5; 5-65-2; 5-68-2; 7-64-2; 8-23-30; 8-25-4, 7, 8; 8-101-2; 10-65-5)

Power, kṣatra-vigour, lordship and similar extraordinary qualities are praised as the special qualities of Mitra and Varuṇa. Together with the Ādityas, Mitra and Varuṇa are known as Kṣatriyas. (1-24-6; 1-25-5; 1-136-1; 5-66-2; 5-67-1; 5-68-1, 7; 6-49-1; 6-51-10; 6-67-5, 6; 7-34-11; 7-64-2; 8-25-8; 8-67-1; 8-100-2)

Because of these qualities they are also designated by the epithets "Rudras" and "Asuras" (असुरा तानार्या (*asurā tānāryā*)). Though they have divine qualities, they are terrible. (5-70-2, 3; 7-36-2; 8-25-4; 8-27-20; 7-65-2)

The epithet "Asura", which denotes divine power, is applied more often to Varuṇa. (1-24-14; 2-27-10; 5-85-5, 6; 8-42-1) It is also used of other deities in some places.

More than all the other epithets, the word "Māyā" indicates the most extraordinary quality of Varuṇa. In some places Varuṇa is denoted by the word "Māyin" itself. (6-48-14; 7-28-4; 10-90-10; 11-147-5)**NOTE** In one or two contexts both Mitra and Varuṇa are addressed as "Māyā". (3-61-7; 5-63-4)

By this Asura power and by this power of māyā both of them send down rain, and both are of firm ordinances (dhṛtavrata).

**The individual qualities and acts of Varuṇa**

All the acts of Varuṇa relate to the working of the universe. Endowed with boundless intelligence, Varuṇa made heaven and earth and is their upholder; and as lord of all the worlds he has taken possession of them all. These ordinances of Varuṇa are altogether extraordinary. (8-42-1)

The three worlds, the heavenly world, the earth and the atmosphere, all depend on this Varuṇa, and all of them are contained in him. (7-87-5)

### Printed p. 6

This same Varuṇa has caused the Āditya, who is of a revolving nature and of golden form, to shine in the atmosphere. (5-85-2; 6-70 1; 7-86-1; 7-87-6; 8-41-4, 5, 10)

The wind, which pervades the whole atmosphere and makes a deep sound, is the breath of Varuṇa. (7-87-2)

This Varuṇa has made an endless path for the Sun's journey. For the rivers, which flow obedient to his command, he has dug channels. (1-24-8; 2-28-4; 7-87-1)

By the skill of his intelligence the rivers, though they ceaselessly and without break pour their waters into the oceans, cannot fill them and make them overflow. As all the commands of that Varuṇa are firm, so they are unobstructed and not to be transgressed. (5-85-6; 3-54-98)**NOTE**

As mountains stand firm, all these things stand resting on Varuṇa alone. (2-25-8; 1-25-6, 10; 1-44-14; 1-141-9; 2-1-4; 8-25-2)

By the ordinances of this Varuṇa, whose ordinances are firm, the stars shine at night. The radiant moon also shines by the greatness of his ordinances. And this moon and the stars lose their light by day. (1-24-10)

Neither the birds that fly in the atmosphere nor the rivers that flow without break can know the power of this Varuṇa or the force of his wrath. (1-24-6)

His messengers alone, however, can know fully the conditions of the two worlds. (7-87-3; 1-24-13; 6-67-5)

He knows the flights of the birds that travel in the atmosphere, the courses of the ships that move on the oceans, the paths of the winds that reach the most distant regions, and all the affairs of past, future and present time. (1-25-7)

Without the help of Varuṇa no living being can even wink an eye. (2-28-6)

The true dealings of men and their untrue dealings he sees directly, understands, and gives the fruits that are due. (7-49-7)

Varuṇa teaches the secrets of truth to the sage Vasiṣṭha. (7-87-4)

But he does not disclose his secret truth, nor that of Mitra, to one who is dull of mind and without discernment.

### Printed p. 7

The power and the omniscience of Varuṇa are described in the Atharvaveda in this way.

बृहन्नेषामधिष्ठाता अंतिकादिव पश्यति । यस्त्यायन्मन्यते चरन्सर्वां देवा इदं विदुः ॥ (*bṛhanneṣāmadhiṣṭhātā aṃtikādiva paśyati | yastyāyanmanyate caransarvāṃ devā idaṃ viduḥ ‖*)
(Ā. Ve. 4-16-1) **NOTE**

Varuṇa, who rules as lord over all these worlds and is of vast form, sees directly the entire doings of each world as if he were very near to all the worlds, and he knows all of it thoroughly. If any man does a wrong deed in secret and imagines that he has done it without anyone knowing, he is mistaken. The gods know all secrets.

यस्तिष्ठति चरति यश्च वंचति यो निलायञ्चरति यः प्रतंकं । द्वौ सन्निषद्य यन्मंत्रयेते राजा तद्वेद वरुणस्तृतीयः ॥ (*yastiṣṭhati carati yaśca vaṃcati yo nilāyañcarati yaḥ prataṃkaṃ | dvau sanniṣadya yanmaṃtrayete rājā tadveda varuṇastṛtīyaḥ ‖*)
(4-16-2)

Whoever stands, whoever moves about, whoever slips away and wanders in secret, whoever enters a house for shelter or hides in a concealed place, all this Varuṇa finds out. If two persons sit together and carry on a secret plot, Varuṇa, without their knowing it, sits between them as the third and understands that inner plot.

उतेयं भूमिर्वरुणस्य राज्य उतासौ द्यौर्भहती दूरे अंता । उतो समुद्रौ वरुणस्य कुक्षी उतास्मिन्नल्पे उदके निलीनः ॥ (*uteyaṃ bhūmirvaruṇasya rājya utāsau dyaurbhahatī dūre aṃtā | uto samudrau varuṇasya kukṣī utāsminnalpe udake nilīnaḥ ‖*)
(4-16-3)

The rule of this earth belongs to Varuṇa, the lord. The heaven too, which is boundless and of vast form, belongs to him alone. The oceans, which belong to both heaven and earth, are the bellies of Varuṇa. In this little pool of small extent too Varuṇa is present.

उत यो द्यामतिसर्पात्परस्तान्न स मुच्यातै वरुणस्य राज्ञः । दिवस्पशः प्रचरंतीदमस्य सहस्राक्षा अति पश्यंति भूमिं ॥ (*uta yo dyāmatisarpātparastānna sa mucyātai varuṇasya rājñaḥ | divaspaśaḥ pracaraṃtīdamasya sahasrākṣā ati paśyaṃti bhūmiṃ ‖*)
(4-16-4)

Even if there is one who could pass beyond the atmosphere, he too cannot escape from the grip of Varuṇa, the lord. His messengers descend from the atmosphere and take possession of this whole earth. And, having a thousand eyes, they scrutinise the whole earth completely.

### Printed p. 8

सर्वं तद्राजा वरुणो विचष्टे यदंतरा रोदसी यत्परस्तात् । संख्याता अस्य निमिषो जनानां अक्षानिव श्वघ्नी नि मिनोति तानि ॥ (*sarvaṃ tadrājā varuṇo vicaṣṭe yadaṃtarā rodasī yatparastāt | saṃkhyātā asya nimiṣo janānāṃ akṣāniva śvaghnī ni minoti tāni ‖*)
(4-16-5)

Varuṇa knows all the things that lie between the two worlds, heaven and earth. He knows also the things that lie in the regions beyond these two worlds. He notices even the subtle moments in which men wink their eyes and counts them all. As a gambler throws the dice, so Varuṇa examines all these minutely and keeps all these dealings under his own control.

ये ते पाशा वरुण सप्त सप्त त्रेधा तिष्ठंति विषिता रुशंतः । सिनंतु सर्वॆ अनृतं वदंतं यस्सत्यवादी अति तं सृजंतु ॥ (*ye te pāśā varuṇa sapta sapta tredhā tiṣṭhaṃti viṣitā ruśaṃtaḥ | sinaṃtu sarvè anṛtaṃ vadaṃtaṃ yassatyavādī ati taṃ sṛjaṃtu ‖*)
(4-16-6)

O Varuṇa, may all those snares of yours, which are divided sevenfold and stand in three shining forms, bind tightly the man who speaks untruth. May they not bind the man who speaks the truth, even though they pass close to him.

From what is described in this way it is understood that Varuṇa holds the ordering of all beings under his own control. Because he is able to put an end to the troubles of his people by thousands of kinds of remedies, he is prayed to show kindness out of his measureless and unfathomable bounty, to destroy the sin of his people and to favour them. (1-24-9)

He is prayed to remove sin as one undoes snares. (2-5-5; 5-85-7, 8)

He is entreated not to take away the lives of men but to increase their span of life (मा न आयुः प्रमोषीः । प्र ण आयूंषि तारिषत (*mā na āyuḥ pramoṣīḥ | pra ṇa āyūṃṣi tāriṣata*)). (1-24-11; 1-25-12)

It is asked that he forgive the sacrificer even if, through ignorance, the sacrificer should accidentally transgress the ordinances of Varuṇa. (1-25-1)

That he binds with his snare those who transgress his ordinance and commit sin, and punishes them fully, is stated clearly in many places. (1-24-15; 1 25-21; 6-74-4; 10-85-24; A. Ve. 4-16-6) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 9

In some places Mitra and Varuṇa, both furnished with snares, are praised together. For example, in the mantra 7-65-3 their praise is given, and it is warned that no man who sets himself against them can be able to subdue them, and it is also stated that they are opponents of untruth. In some other places Indra and Varuṇa also are said to be furnished with snares and to bind and punish those whose dealings are untrue. (7-84-2)

Although he inflicts cruel punishment, Varuṇa also has the generosity to forgive sinners, and he forgives those who have sinned through ignorance. (7-87-7)

He is the one who protects immortality by his wisdom.

There is the hope that the virtuous will have the good fortune to see Varuṇa ruling in the highest state together with Yama in the other world. (10-14-7)

Thus both the deeds that show the greatness of Varuṇa and the epithets that describe his extraordinary powers indicate Varuṇa's lofty position, spiritual and moral. Such a high station cannot belong to any other god. Besides the passages quoted above, the greatness of Varuṇa is praised in many other places. Of these, only the purport of some mantras is given.

"O Varuṇa, to find out the sin committed through ignorance I trust the wise and question them." All those sages, with one voice, declare, "Varuṇa is angry with you. Therefore, O Varuṇa, forgive this sin and release us from the snare."

"O Varuṇa, what is that great sin because of which you, taking offence, wish to punish severely me, your friend and your worshipper? O radiant Varuṇa, whose power cannot be checked, tell me the nature of that sin of mine. Freed from it, I shall afterwards undertake your worship."

"If the sin committed by our forefathers in former times has followed us, free us from it. If we ourselves have committed sin, protect us from that also. As, when a thief is stealing cattle, one frees them from their bonds and makes them free, and as one frees from the rope's bond a calf eager to suck at its mother, so free me from the bond that is sin."

"O Varuṇa, we did not commit this sin deliberately. It is the result of our delusion. We committed this sin through ignorance caused by liquor, by being overcome with anger, by the vice of gambling and the like, and through the vices of desire and the rest.

### Printed p. 10

Delusion, which has power over us and is stronger than our resolve, has made us do this sin. Even our sleep is a cause of sin for us." (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-86-3 to 6)

"O Varuṇa, do not make me go to the house made of earth. O Varuṇa of excellent valour, favour me. Have mercy on me."

"O Varuṇa, who hold a sharp weapon, I tremble when I look at you, like a leather bag filled with wind. Have mercy on me."

"O powerful and pure Varuṇa **NOTE**, I am unable to be one who follows your pure command, and only through my weakness have I transgressed your command. But since weakness alone is the cause in this matter, forgive this sin and show mercy to me."

"Though I stand in a place full of water, I am tormented by thirst. Since I am your praiser, do not prolong my trouble; have mercy on me and remove my trouble."

"O Varuṇa, whatever offence we may have committed against the gods who dwell in the shining world, or whatever offence against you in the form of transgressing your command, knowing that all of it is due to our ignorance, make us free from that sin. Do not punish us for those sinful deeds. Have mercy on us." (7-89-1 to 5)

Having prayed in this way in some places, the same sage in another place recalls the friendship between himself and Varuṇa, reminds Varuṇa of all the kindnesses that the gods had done to him formerly, asks what the reason is that Varuṇa should be unkind to him now alone, and entreats his mercy.

"Varuṇa had seated Vasiṣṭha in his own boat. By the favour of that Varuṇa, Vasiṣṭha too attained the highest sagehood of his life, and he worshipped him also with the aim that long life should be gained by him."

"O Varuṇa, where is that friendship which was formerly between the two of us? Where has that mutual feeling that was between us gone? O Varuṇa, who shine by your own radiance, I have come as a suppliant to your house of a thousand doors."

"O Varuṇa, the sage who was your friend, your closest intimate, who was your own, who was worthy of your love, may have committed sin through ignorance; he may have committed an offence against you. But do not make us (myself and those who belong to me) undergo the fruit of that sin. O wise god Varuṇa, give protection to the sage who praises you." (7-88-4)

### Printed p. 11

The nature of the companionship of Mitra and Varuṇa, and their actions.

The same epithets that have been used to show the greatness of Varuṇa are used to praise Mitra and Varuṇa together.

These two, Mitra and Varuṇa, uphold heaven and earth and are lords over them. As owners of the radiant heaven and earth, they have established the sun in the atmosphere. (5-62-3, 5-69-1, 4, 5-63-7, 6-67-5, 7-61-4, 18-13-2-2)

These two, Mitra and Varuṇa, are the protectors of all the worlds (विश्वस्य भुवनस्य गोपा (*viśvasya bhuvanasya gopā*)). (2-27-4, 5-62-9, 7-51-2, 8-25-1, 10-126-4)

It is in obedience to the command of these two, Mitra and Varuṇa, that the great atmosphere also shines. (10-65-5)

They themselves are the creators and do good to the whole world. (5-62-3, 5-63-1 to 3)

Their divinity surpasses the extent of the heavenly world and the length of the rivers. (1-151-9)

These two gods (together with Aryaman **NOTE**), just as they are attractive to the virtuous, so are they opponents of evil deeds and of untruthful living, and are of most terrible form to men who are of evil mind and speak untruth. (1-152-1, 7-66-13)

They carry out their works in accordance with their firm resolves, which cannot be obstructed even by the immortal gods. (5-63-7, 5-69-4)

These Mitra and Varuṇa bring even a man who is without discernment and foolish to be endowed with discernment. They are omniscient. (7-60-6, 7)

As a herdsman watches his cattle, Mitra and Varuṇa look upon all men from the atmosphere itself and know their dealings. (7-60-3, 9, 16)

### Printed p. 12

In many places they are described as truthful in nature, pure in soul, and helpers of holy sacrificial acts (ऋतावृधा (*ṛtāvṛdhā*); ऋतजाता (*ṛtajātā*), ऋतस्म गोपौ (*ṛtasma gopau*), ऋतावाना (*ṛtāvānā*)). (1-2-8; 1-23-5; 1-136-4; 2-27-4; 5-63-1; 5-65-2; 5-67-4, 7-64-2; 7-66-3; 8-23-30; 8-25-8)

These two alone are the lords of truth and the masters of lights (यतस्य ज्योतिषस्पती (*yatasya jyotiṣaspatī*)). (1-23-5)

Mitra and Varuṇa, together with the other Ādityas, destroy sinful deeds and untruth **NOTE**. (2-27-4; 7-60-5)

The man who neglects to recite the praise of these two groans in distress. (1-12)

They are entreated, together with Aditi, for the forgiveness of sin committed through ignorance by the praisers.

They are prayed to, together with Aryaman, to make one free from sin. (2-27-14)

The description of the personal nature of Mitra. (10-12-6)

In the 59th sūkta of the third maṇḍala of the Ṛk-saṃhitā Mitra alone is praised. The purport of only some of the mantras that describe his qualities is given here.

"Mitra, uttering his own dear speech, impels men to their respective works. He makes heaven and earth stand firm and is their support. Without even winking his eye he watches all beings. Offer the oblation of ghee to this Mitra."

"O Mitra, son of Aditi, may food be gained by the man who worships you with the most excellent sacrificial acts and offers you praise. For the man who has come under your protection there is neither death nor defeat. From very near or from a distant place, there is no calamity at all for such a man."

"This venerable Mitra is the best. He alone is the lord, he alone is powerful and wise. May we be under his protection."

Approaching with reverence this Mitra, who makes men know their respective duties and impels them to those duties, and who favours the worshippers, one should offer to him a worship fit to win his favour.

### Printed p. 13

"Mitra, of great form, by his greatness surpasses even the atmosphere; by his power he has transcended the earth."

"Even the five peoples beginning with the Niṣādas (or the Gandharvas and others) offer worship to Mitra, who is the support and the helper of the states and conditions of all the gods, the powerful benefactor." Thus there is one sūkta, in the form of praise, addressed to Mitra alone. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-59-1 to 8)

The opinion of the German scholar Roth on the greatness of Mitra and Varuṇa.

In the journal named Journal of the German Oriental Society this scholar has given his opinion about Mitra and Varuṇa in this way.

"In the group of deities reckoned as the Ādityas, the relation of Mitra and Varuṇa to each other is a close one. The passages in which Mitra and Varuṇa are praised together are more numerous than those in which Varuṇa alone is praised. The sūkta that praises Mitra is only one (3-59). Whichever of the two is being described, the same epithets are applied to both; but in some passages of the Saṃhitā and other texts there are also occasions where the subtle difference between the two is explained. Mitra is the luminous one who shines in the atmosphere as the source of the day. Varuṇa, though he shines in the form of radiance as lord over all the world and all time, is praised chiefly as the presiding deity of night. For example, in a mantra composed by the sage Vasiṣṭha he is praised thus: 'O Varuṇa, of you two, one (you) are of unobstructed power and the most excellent guide of the path for all; Mitra gives vigour to all creatures and impels them to their duties.'"
(7-36-2)

That is to say, the capacity and the lordship of bringing delight and activity to the world, as the radiance of the day, giving vigour to all living beings, belong to Mitra. This is a limited authority. Even though Varuṇa is described as the presiding deity of night, his lordship is all-pervading and belongs to all times.

"Although there is a limited description of his nature such as 'Varuṇa is the form of the setting sun' (7-87-1, Sā. Bhā.) **NOTE**, there is truth in that too. As the very name of Varuṇa indicates, he is a special radiance pervading the atmosphere. All existence in the world is contained within him, the all-pervading. His nature is infinite. Neither the human eye nor the imagination of the mind can comprehend such an infinite nature. This radiance, spread out most widely, cannot be reached by the eye in the time of the day

### Printed p. 14

time. At night the veil that covered Varuṇa disappears and his nature becomes known a little. Moreover, the other radiances that pervaded the atmosphere, the cloud and the space between heaven and earth also disappear. Therefore at this time Varuṇa alone, shining singly, becomes visible even to the eye."

The relation of Varuṇa to the element of water.

In the Ṛk-saṃhitā Varuṇa is not regarded as the presiding deity of the ocean. Nevertheless, in some passages a close relation is shown between Varuṇa and the water that pervades heaven and earth. These explanations indicate the propriety of the occasions in the Purāṇas and other works that describe the relation of Varuṇa to water and make known his extraordinary actions.

"O Ṛbhus, sons of Śakti, the Maruts come from the heavenly world desiring you, Agni from the earth, Vāyu from the atmosphere, and Varuṇa from the waters of the atmosphere." (1-161-14)

"O waters that are in the atmosphere and flow, for whose flowing channels have been dug and made ready, those that are self-produced, that run towards the ocean, that are shining and pure, protect us." (7-49-2)

"May those waters protect us in whose midst the lord Varuṇa moves, watching the true and untrue dealings of men."

May those waters, in whose midst the gods beginning with Varuṇa rejoice, protect us fully. (7-49-3, 4)

"O lords, protectors of the sacrifice, masters of the ocean and the rivers, O Mitra and Varuṇa, graciously provide us food here and pour down rain from the atmosphere." (7-64-2)

In the above mantra (7 49-3), in which he is described as "Varuṇa within the waters", the ocean that is mentioned must be the ocean of the atmosphere. In the Purāṇas and other works Varuṇa alone is described as connected with the ocean. But in the quoted mantra 7-64-2 it is understood that the epithet सिंधुपती (*siṃdhupatī*) is applied to Mitra and Varuṇa together. The prayer "pour down food and rain from the atmosphere" supports their lordship of the ocean. In the mantra 7-49-2 the waters indicated by या आपो दिव्या उत वा स्रवंति (*yā āpo divyā uta vā sravaṃti*) must be waters of the earth, in the opinion of the scholar Max Muller. Sāyaṇa

### Printed p. 15

though he does not generally designate Varuṇa as lord of the ocean (samudrapati) or lord of the rivers (sindhupati), explains, when interpreting mantras such as 1-161-14 and 8-69-12, that he is जलाभिमानी देवः (*jalābhimānī devaḥ*). In 8-41-8 Varuṇa is described as the ocean contained within (अपीच्यः समुद्रः (*apīcyaḥ samudraḥ*)). In some other places also it is described in the same way. For example—

"O Varuṇa, as rivers flow and reach the fathomless depth below, so the seven rivers also flow into your jaw." (8-69-12)

As it is said that Soma dwells in the plants, so Varuṇa also is praised as dwelling in the oceans and the like (वना वसानो वरुणः (*vanā vasāno varuṇaḥ*)). (9-90-2)

The sentence of the Vājasaneyi-saṃhitā praises thus: "Varuṇa, the child of the waters, made his dwelling in the waters that are in the form of mothers" (अपां शिशुः (*apāṃ śiśuḥ*)). (Vā. Saṃ. 1-7)

In the Atharvaveda also, of the mantras that make known the relation of Varuṇa to water, only the purport of some is given here.

"May Varuṇa from the midst of the water, and Soma from the mountain region, call you." (Ā. Ve. 3-3-3) **NOTE**

"O Varuṇa, as soon as you, my divine father, pour down rain, the streams breathe." (Ā. Ve. 4-15-12)

"Varuṇa is the lord of the waters. Mitra and Varuṇa are the lords of rain." (Ā. Ve. 5-24-4)

"O Varuṇa, your golden house is situated in the waters." (Ā. Ve. 7-83-1)

In the Taittirīya-saṃhitā the relation of the waters to Varuṇa is stated in this way.

आपो वरुणस्य पत्नय आसन्ता अग्निरभ्यध्यायत्तास्समभवत्तस्य रेतः परापतत्तदियमभवद्यद्वितीयं परापतत्तदसावभवदियं वै विराडसौ स्वराट् । ( (*āpo varuṇasya patnaya āsantā agnirabhyadhyāyattāssamabhavattasya retaḥ parāpatattadiyamabhavadyadvitīyaṃ parāpatattadasāvabhavadiyaṃ vai virāḍasau svarāṭ | (*)
(Tai. Saṃ. 5-5-4-1)

The waters are the wives of Varuṇa. Agni desired them and also obtained union with them. His seed was discharged. As the result of the first discharge, this earth came into being. As the result of the second discharge, the atmosphere came into being. It is said that this earth is the virāṭ and the atmosphere is the svarāṭ. In the Purāṇas and other works Varuṇa is praised as the lord of the oceans.

### Printed p. 16

Roth and Westergaard have set out the historical development of this idea, showing in what circumstances in history, and how, the relation of Varuṇa to the waters has been described. (Since Western method is included in it, it does not appear necessary to explain it here. **NOTE**

The introduction ends.

## Sūkta 63 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋतस्य गोपाविति सप्तर्चं सप्तमं सूक्तं । आत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका । ऋतस
> सप्तार्चनाना जागतमिति । आत्रेयोऽर्चनाना नाम ऋषिः । जगती छंदः । तुह्यादि
> परिभाषया मित्रावरुणौ देवता ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta, beginning ऋतस्य गोपौ (*ṛtasya gopau*), is the seventh sūkta of the fifth anuvāka and has seven Ṛks. The Anukramaṇikā says ऋतस्य सप्तार्चनाना जागतं (*ṛtasya saptārcanānā jāgataṃ*): the seer is Arcanānā Ātreya and the metre is Jagatī. By the convention तुह्यादि परिभाषया (*tuhyādi paribhāṣayā*) the deities are Mitra and Varuṇa. The ritual use is laiṅgika, that is, by indication of the text itself.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning ऋतस्य गोपा (*ṛtasya gopā*), is the seventh sūkta in the fifth anuvāka of the fifth maṇḍala. It contains seven Ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: ऋतस्य सप्तार्चनाना जागतं (*ṛtasya saptārcanānā jāgataṃ*). The seer of this sūkta is a man named Arcanānā Ātreya; the deities are Mitra and Varuṇa; the metre is Jagatī. The use is laiṅgika.

##### Particulars of the sūkta

- Sūkta 63
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 63
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 1
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 7
- Seer: Arcanānā Ātreya
- Deity: मिश्रावरुणौ (*miśrāvaruṇau*) **NOTE**
- Metre: Jagatī

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ऋत॑स्य गोपा॒वधि॑ तिष्ठथो॒ रथं॒ सत्य॑धर्माणा पर॒मे  ॑
> व्योमनि ।  
> यमत्र॑ मित्रावरु॒णाव॑धो यु॒वं तस्मै॑ वृ॒ष्टिर्मधु॑मत्पिन्वते दि॒वः ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 17

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यतस्य । गोपौ । अधि । तिष्ठधः । रथं । सत्यधर्माणा । परमे । विओमनि ।  
> यं । अत्र । मित्रावरुणा । अवथः । युवं । तस्मै । वृष्टिः । मधुमत् । पिन्नते ।  
> दिवः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे ऋतस्योदकस्य गोपौ रक्षितारौ सत्यधर्माणा सत्यधर्माणौ युवां
> रथमधि तिष्ठथः । आरोहथोऽस्मद्यज्ञमागमनार्थं । कुत्र । परमे व्योमनि
> निरतिशय आकाशे । हे मित्रावरुणौ युवं युवामत्रास्मिन्यज्ञेयं यजमानमुवथो रक्षथः तस्मै यजमानाय वृष्टिः पर्जन्यो मधुमदुदकं दिवो
> द्युलोकात्पिन्वते । सिंचति । वर्धयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, protectors of ṛta, that is, of water, whose dharma is truth: you two mount the chariot in order to come to our sacrifice. Where? In the highest sky, that is, the sky that has nothing beyond it. O Mitra and Varuṇa, the sacrificer whom you two protect here in this sacrifice, for that sacrificer the rain, that is, Parjanya, sprinkles and makes grow the sweet water from the world of heaven; पिन्वते (*pinvate*) means "sprinkles" and "makes grow".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—to water
- गोपौ (*gopau*)—the protectors; you who are protectors and whose dharma is truth, O Mitra and Varuṇa (सत्यधर्माणा (*satyadharmāṇā*))
- परमे व्योमनि (*parame vyomani*)—in the most excellent atmosphere
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the chariot
- अधि तिष्ठथः (*adhi tiṣṭhathaḥ*)—you mount and sit
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- अत्र (*atra*)—in this sacrifice
- यं (*yaṃ*)—whichever sacrificer
- अवथः (*avathaḥ*)—you protect
- तस्मै (*tasmai*)—for that sacrificer
- वृष्टिः (*vṛṣṭiḥ*)—Parjanya
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from the world of heaven
- मधुमत् (*madhumat*)—the sweet water
- पिन्नते (*pinnate*)—pours down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, protectors of water, whose dharma is truth, you mount the chariot in the most excellent atmosphere. For the sacrificer whom you protect in this sacrifice, Parjanya pours down sweet water from the world of heaven.

##### English translation
Guardians of water, observers of truth, you ascend your car in the highest heaven : to him whom you, Mitra and Varuna, protect, the rain sends down the sweet (shower) from the sky.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this sūkta the immense power of Mitra and Varuṇa in sending down rain is described.

ऋतस्य गोपौ (*ṛtasya gopau*)—For the words ऋतस्य गोपौ (*ṛtasya gopau*) in this Ṛk the commentator has given the explanation उदकस्य रक्षितारौ (*udakasya rakṣitārau*), that is, protectors of the waters. Since the word ṛta means sacrifice or truth,

### Printed p. 18

it can also be explained as protectors of the sacrifice or of truth. Here ṛta means वरुणस्य व्रतं (*varuṇasya vrataṃ*), the acts that relate to the regulation of the universe, universal laws. It is said in many places in the Ṛgveda that the vrata (laws) of Varuṇa is the chief cause for keeping the affairs of the universe or world under a fixed rule. Since in the present sūkta the praise of Mitra and Varuṇa concerns rain, it is proper to explain the word ṛta as water.

सत्यधर्माणा (*satyadharmāṇā*)—This indicates that the acts of these Mitra and Varuṇa are of the nature of truth, and also that they have the power to impel the acts of others and to bring them under the control of truth, that is, of the regulation of the universe.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒म्राजा॑व॒स्य भुव॑नस्य राजथो॒ मित्रा॑वरुणा वि॒दथे॑ स्व॒र्दृशा॑।  
> वृ॒ष्टिं वां॒ राधो॑ अमृत॒त्वमी॑महे॒ द्यावा॑पृथि॒वी वि च॑रंति  ॒॑
> तन्यवः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> संराज् । अस्य । भुवनस्य । राजथः । मित्रावरुणा । विदथे । स्वःवृशा ।  
> वृष्टिं । वां । राधः । अमृतत्वं ईमहे । द्वावापृथिवी इति । वि । चरंति । तन्वयः ॥२॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणा मित्रावरुणे सम्राजौ सम्यग्राजमान् युवामस्य भुवनस्य
> राजथः । ईशाथे । स्वर्दृशा स्वर्गस्य द्रष्टारौ युवां विदथेऽस्मदृज्ञे सम्राजाविति
> संबंधः । वां युवां वृष्टिं राधो धनं वृष्टाख्यं धनममृतत्वं स्वर्गं
> चेमहे । प्रार्थयामहे । युवयोस्तन्मवो विस्तता रश्मयो द्यावापृथिवी वि
> चरंति । विविधं प्राप्नुवंति । अथवा वृष्टिं धनममृतत्वं चेमहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, you two, who shine well (सम्राजौ (*samrājau*)), rule over this world; the verb राजथः (*rājathaḥ*) means "you two have lordship". You are seers of heaven (स्वर्दृशा (*svardṛśā*), "those who look upon heaven"), in our sacrifice (विदथे (*vidathe*)); this is to be construed with "you who shine well". We beg of you two rain, wealth (राधः (*rādhaḥ*) is wealth, namely the wealth called rain), and immortality, that is, heaven. Your extended rays range variously over heaven and earth, that is, reach them in various ways. Or else: we beg rain, wealth and immortality. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य भुवनस्य (*asya bhuvanasya*)—of this whole world
- सम्राज् (*samrāj*)—you who are lords
- स्वर्दृशा (*svardṛśā*)—you who look upon heaven
- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- विदथे (*vidathe*)—in this sacrifice

### Printed p. 19

- राजथः (*rājathaḥ*)—you shine
- नां (*nāṃ*)—from you **NOTE**
- वृष्टिं (*vṛṣṭiṃ*)—in the form of rain
- राधः (*rādhaḥ*)—wealth
- अमृतत्वं (*amṛtatvaṃ*)—freedom from death
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we pray for
- तन्यवः (*tanyavaḥ*)—your extended rays
- द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—all of heaven and earth
- वि चरंति (*vi caraṃti*)—move about in various ways

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, lords of this whole world and seers of heaven, you shine in this sacrifice. From you we pray for wealth in the form of rain and for freedom from death. Your extended rays move about in various ways over all of heaven and earth.

##### English translation
Imperial rulers of this world, you shine, Mitra and Varuna, at this sacrifice, the beholders of heaven : we ask of you the wealth (that is) rain, and immortality, for your forms traverse earth and heaven.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

सम्राजौ (*samrājau*)—The meaning stated here is सम्यक् राजमान् (*samyak rājamān*), "shining well". Since the word सम्राट् (*samrāṭ*) also has the meaning "king of kings, emperor", that meaning may be given here.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒म्राजा॑ उ॒ग्रा वृ॑ष॒भा दि॒वस्पती॑ पृथि॒व्या मि॒त्रावरु॑णा॒ विच॑र्षणे ।  
> चि॒त्रेभि॑र॒भैरुप॑ तिष्ठथो॒ रवं॒ द्यां व॑र्षयथो॒ असु॑रस्य  ॒॑
> मायया ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> संराजा । उग्रा । वृषभा । दिवः । पती इति । पृथिव्याः । मित्रावरुणा । विचर्षणे इति  
> विचर्षणे ।  
> चित्रेभिः । अभ्रैः । उप । तिष्ठथः । रवं । द्यां । वर्षयथः । असुरस्य । मायया ॥

### Printed p. 20

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> सम्राजौ सम्यग्राजमानावुग्रीद्योर्णबलौ वृषभा वर्षितार् दिवो
> द्युलोकस्य पती स्वामिनौ पृथिव्याश्च पती विचर्षन्णे सर्वस्य द्रष्टारौ मित्रावरुणौ ।
> एवं महानुभावौ चित्रेभिश्चायनीयैरभ्रैर्मेघैः सह रवं स्तोत्रमुप
> तिष्ठथः । पश्चात् द्यां च वर्षयथोऽसुरस्योदकनिरसितुः पर्जन्यस्य मायया
> प्रज्ञया सामर्थ्येन ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

सम्राजौ (*samrājau*) means those who shine well; they are fierce and mighty **NOTE**. वृषभा (*vṛṣabhā*) means those who shower rain. They are the lords of the world of heaven and also the lords of the earth, and the seers of all, Mitra and Varuṇa. Being of such great power, they approach the praise (रवं (*ravaṃ*), that is, stotra) together with variegated, attractive clouds. Afterwards they cause heaven to rain by the māyā, that is, the wisdom or power, of Parjanya, the असुर (*asura*), the one who sends down water.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सम्राजौ (*samrājau*)—you who shine fully
- उग्रा (*ugrā*)—you of terrible power
- वृषभा (*vṛṣabhā*)—you who shower rain
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—of the world of heaven
- पती (*patī*)—lords
- पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)—protectors of the earth
- विचर्षणी (*vicarṣaṇī*)—you who have the power to see everything
- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- चित्रेभिः (*citrebhiḥ*)—attractive
- अभ्रैः (*abhraiḥ*)—with clouds
- रवं (*ravaṃ*)—to hear the sound of our praise
- उप तिष्ठथः (*upa tiṣṭhathaḥ*)—come near to us (and afterwards)
- असुरस्य (*asurasya*)—of the mighty Parjanya
- मायया (*māyayā*)—with the help of wisdom
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the atmosphere
- वर्षयथः (*varṣayathaḥ*)—cause to pour down rain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, who shine fully, who have terrible power, who shower rain, who are lords of the world of heaven, protectors of the earth, and have the power to see everything: come near us with attractive clouds to hear the sound of our praise. Afterwards, with the help of the wisdom of the mighty Parjanya, cause the atmosphere to pour down rain.

##### English translation
Imperial and mighty showerers, lords of heaven and earth, beholders of the universe, you approach, Mitra and Varuna, with variegated clouds to hear the sound (of your praises), and cause the sky to (send down) rain by the power of the emitter of showers.

#### Ṛk 4

### Printed p. 21

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मा॒या नां॑ मित्रावरुणा दि॒वि श्रि॒ता सूर्यो॒ ज्योति॑श्चरति  ॒॑
> चित्रमायुधं ।  
> तम॒भ्रेण॑ वृ॒ष्ट्या गू॑हथो दि॒वि पर्ज॑न्य द्र॒प्सा मधु॑मंत  
> ईरते ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> माया । वां । मित्रावरुणा । दिवि । श्रिता । सूर्यः । ज्योतिः । चरति । चित्रं । आयुधं।  
> तं । अभ्रेण । वृष्टा । गूहथः । दिवि । पर्जन्य । द्रप्साः । मधुमंतः । ईरते ॥४॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणा नां युवयोर्माया दिवि श्रिता । आश्रिता । कैषा
> युवयोर्मायोच्यते । सूर्यः सर्वस्य प्रेरको ज्योतिर्दिप्यमानश्चित्रं
> चायनीयमायुधमुक्तलक्षणायुधरूपश्चरति । परिभ्रमत्यंतरिक्षे शत्रुमारकत्वादायुधमुच्यते । तं सूर्यमभ्रेण मेघेन वृष्ट्या दिवि गूहथः । गोपायथः।
> हे पर्जन्य देव त्वया मित्रावरुणाभ्यां वृष्ट्यर्थं प्रेरितेन मधुमंतो द्रप्सा
> ईरते । ईर्यंते । त्वत्सृष्टा वा गच्छंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, your māyā is lodged in the sky (the printed block has नां (*nāṃ*) for "your" **NOTE**). What is this māyā of yours said to be? The sun, the impeller of all, a light, shining, a wonderful and attractive weapon of the kind described, moves about; he travels round in the atmosphere, and is called a weapon because he kills enemies. That sun you conceal (गूहथः (*gūhathaḥ*), that is, गोपायथः (*gopāyathaḥ*), "you guard") in the sky with cloud and with rain. O god Parjanya, impelled by you for the sake of rain by Mitra and Varuṇa, the honeyed drops go forth (ईरते (*īrate*), they are sent), or else they go forth sent by you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- माया (*māyā*)—extraordinary power
- दिवि (*divi*)—in the atmosphere
- श्रिता (*śritā*)—is situated (is manifest)
- सूर्यः (*sūryaḥ*)—the sun, the impeller of all
- ज्योतिः (*jyotiḥ*)—as radiance
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—as attractive
- आयुधं (*āyudhaṃ*)—as the weapon of your māyā
- चरति (*carati*)—moves in the atmosphere
- तं (*taṃ*)—that sun
- अभ्रेण (*abhreṇa*)—with cloud
- वृष्टा (*vṛṣṭā*)—with rain
- दिवि (*divi*)—in the atmosphere
- गूहथः (*gūhathaḥ*)—you cover
- पर्जन्य (*parjanya*)—O god Parjanya (by you alone, impelled by Mitra and Varuṇa)
- मधुमंतः (*madhumaṃtaḥ*)—full of sweet juice
- द्रप्साः (*drapsāḥ*)—waters
- ईरते (*īrate*)—flow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, your extraordinary power is situated in the atmosphere. The sun, the impeller of all, moves in the atmosphere as radiance, as something attractive, and as the weapon of your māyā

### Printed p. 22

that sun you cover in the atmosphere with cloud and with rain. O god Parjanya, from you alone, impelled by Mitra and Varuṇa, waters full of sweet juice flow.

##### English translation
Your device, Mitra and Varuna, is manifested in heaven, when the light (that is) the sun, your wonderful weapon, moves (in the firmament) : him you invest in the sky with cloud (and) with rain ; and (your) sweet drops, Parjanya, fall (at their desire).

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> रथं॑ युंजते म॒रुतः॑ शु॒भे सु॒खं शूरो॒ न मि॑त्रावरुणा॒  ॑
> गवि॑ष्टिषु ।  
> रजां॑सि चि॒त्रा वि च॑रंति त॒न्यवो॑ दि॒वः स॑म्राजा॒ पय॑सा न  
> उक्षतं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> रथं । यंजते । मरुतः । शुभे । सुखं । शूरः । न । मित्रावरुणा । गोऽइष्टिषु!  
> रजांसि । चित्रा । वि । चरंति । तन्यवः । दिवः । संराजा । पयसा । नः । उक्षतं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणौ युवयोरनुग्रहात् मरुतः शुभ उदकार्थं सुखं
> शोभनाक्षद्वारं रथं युंजते । अश्वैर्योजयंति । शूरो न शूर इव । स यथा
> युद्धार्थं रथं युनक्ति तद्वत् । गविष्टिषु गवामुदकानामेषेषु निमित्तेषु
> चित्रा चायनीयानि रजांसि लोकान् तन्नवस्ततास्ते मरुतो वि चरंति । विविधं
> चरंति । तस्मात्तैः सहितौ हे सम्राजा युवां दिवो द्युलोकात्पयसोदकेन
> नोऽस्मानुक्षतं । सिंचतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, by your favour the Maruts yoke, for splendour, that is, for the sake of water, a chariot with a good axle-hole; they yoke it with horses, like a hero who yokes his chariot for battle. गविष्टिषु (*gaviṣṭiṣu*) means on these occasions, namely the quests for cows, that is, for waters. Those Maruts, whose bodies are spread out, move about variously, through the wonderful, attractive worlds. Therefore, O you two who shine well, together with them, sprinkle us with water, that is, with rain, from the world of heaven.

### Printed p. 23

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे मित्रावरुणौ (*he mitrāvaruṇau*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts
- शूरो न (*śūro na*)—as a hero prepares his chariot for battle
- शुभे (*śubhe*)—for the sake of water
- सुखं (*sukhaṃ*)—that moves easily
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—their chariot
- युंजते (*yuṃjate*)—they join with horses and make ready
- तन्यवः (*tanyavaḥ*)—those clouds whose bodies are spread out widely
- गविष्टिषु (*gaviṣṭiṣu*)—for the sake of sprinkling water
- चित्रा (*citrā*)—attractive
- रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*)—all the worlds
- वि चरंति (*vi caraṃti*)—move about
- सम्राजा (*samrājā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa, who are lords
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from the world of heaven
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- पयसा (*payasā*)—rain
- उक्षतं (*ukṣataṃ*)—pour down

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, as a hero prepares his chariot for battle, the Maruts join with horses and make ready, for the sake of water, their chariot that moves easily. Those clouds, whose bodies are spread out widely, move about through all the attractive worlds for the sake of sprinkling water. O Mitra and Varuṇa, who are lords, pour down rain on us from the world of heaven.

##### English translation
The Maruts harness their easy-going chariot, Mitra and Varuna, for (the emission of ) water, as a hero (harnesscs his war-car): their forms traverse the different spheres to distribute the rain : do you, therefore, supreme rulers, shed upon us water from heaven.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वाचं॒ सु मि॑त्रावरुणा॒विरा॑वतीं प॒र्जन्य॑श्चि॒त्रां व॑दति॒ त्विषी॑मतीं ।  
> अ॒भ्रा व॑सत म॒रुतः॒ सु मा॒यया॒ द्यां व॑र्षयतमरु॒णाम॑रे॒पसं॑ ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 24

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वाचं । सु । मित्रावरुणौ । इरावतीं । पर्जन्यः । चित्रां । वदति । त्विषिमतीं ।  
> अभ्रा । वसत । मरुतः । सु । मायया । द्यां । वर्षयतं । अरुणां । आरेपसं॥६॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणौ युवयोरनुग्रहात् पर्जन्यो मेघः सु सुष्टिरावतीमन्नवतीमन्नसाधिकां चित्रां चायनीयां त्वषीमतीं दीप्तिमतीं वाचं
> गर्जनशब्दं वदति । शब्दयति वृष्ट्यर्थं । मरुतश्चाभ्राभ्राणि मेघान् सु सुष्टु
> वसत आच्छादयंति मायया स्वप्रज्ञया । युवां च पर्जन्येन मरुद्धिश्च
> सहारुणामरुणवर्णामरेपसमपापां वृष्टविघातिनीं द्यां वर्षयतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, by your favour Parjanya, the cloud, utters well a voice that is food-bearing, that is, productive of food, wonderful and attractive, radiant: a thundering sound, he sounds it for the sake of rain. The Maruts, too, cover the clouds well with their own wisdom (मायया (*māyayā*)). And you two, together with Parjanya and the Maruts, cause to rain the sky that is reddish, free from fault (without sin), and वृष्टविघातिनीं (*vṛṣṭavighātinīṃ*) **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्रावरुणौ (*mitrāvaruṇau*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa (by your influence alone)
- पर्जन्यः (*parjanyaḥ*)—Parjanya
- सु (*su*)—exceedingly
- इरावतीं (*irāvatīṃ*)—that which brings about food
- चित्रां (*citrāṃ*)—attractive
- त्विषीमतीं (*tviṣīmatīṃ*)—radiant
- वाचं (*vācaṃ*)—the thundering sound
- वदति (*vadati*)—he makes manifest
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—the Maruts also
- मायया (*māyayā*)—by their power in the form of wisdom
- अभ्रा (*abhrā*)—the clouds
- सु (*su*)—exceedingly
- वसत (*vasata*)—they cover (you two also)
- अरुणां (*aruṇāṃ*)—golden-coloured
- अरेपसं (*arepasaṃ*)—free from stain
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the world of heaven
- वर्षयतं (*varṣayataṃ*)—you cause to pour down rain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, by your influence alone Parjanya makes manifest a thundering sound that exceedingly brings about food, is attractive and radiant. The Maruts also, by their power in the form of wisdom, cover the clouds exceedingly. You two also cause the world of heaven, which is golden-coloured and free from stain, to pour down rain.

##### English translation
The cloud, (through your will), Mitra and Varuna utters a wonderful sound, indicative of radiance, and announcing (abundant) food: the Maruts thoroughly invest the clouds with (their) devices, and (along with them), you two cause the purple and faultless sky to send down rain.

### Printed p. 25
#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> धर्म॑णा मित्रावरुणा विपश्चिता व्र॒ता र॑क्षेथे॒ असु॑रस्य  ॒॑
> मायया ।  
> ऋ॒तेन॒ विश्वं॒ भुव॑नं॒ वि रा॑जथः॒ सूर्य॒मा ध॑त्तो दि॒वि चित्र्यं॒  ॑
> रथं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> धर्मणा । मित्रावरुणा । विपःचिता । व्रता । रक्षेथे इति । असुरस्य । मायया ।  
> ऋतेन । विश्वं । भुवनं । वि । राजथः । सूर्यं । आ । धत्थः । दिवि । चित्र्यं । रथं॥७॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणा विपश्चिता प्राज्ञा युवां धर्मणा जगद्वारकेण वृष्ट्यादिलक्षणेन कर्मणा व्रता यज्ञादिकर्माणि रक्षेथे । पालयथः । आसुरस्य मेघानां
> निरसितुः पर्जन्यस्य मायया प्रज्ञया च ऋतेनोदकेन यज्ञेन वा निमित्तेन
> विश्वं भुवनं सर्वं भूतजातं वि राजथः । विदीपयथ इत्यर्थः । सूर्यमा
> सूर्यं च चित्रं पूज्यं रथं रंहणस्वभावं दिवि द्युलोके धत्थः । धारयथो
> जगदुपकारार्थं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa addresses Mitra and Varuṇa as wise ones. By your world-supporting action (dharman), which consists of rain and the like, you protect the rites (vrata), that is, the sacrifices and the other ceremonies. He takes asura as Parjanya, the one who casts down the clouds, and māyā as his wisdom. By ṛta, which he explains as water or else as the sacrifice, taken as the cause, you make the whole world, every kind of being, shine. He explains वि राजथः (*vi rājathaḥ*) as "you cause to shine". In heaven you hold the sun, and also a wondrous, worshipful chariot whose nature is swift motion; you sustain them for the good of the world. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- विपश्चिता (*vipaścitā*)—you, the wise
- असुरस्य (*asurasya*)—of the powerful Parjanya
- मायया (*māyayā*)—with the help of his extraordinary power
- धर्मणा (*dharmaṇā*)—by your actions such as causing rain to fall
- व्रता (*vratā*)—the sacrifices and other rites
- रक्षेथे (*rakṣethe*)—you protect
- ऋतेन (*ṛtena*)—by water
- विश्वं भुवनं (*viśvaṃ bhuvanaṃ*)—the whole world
- वि राजथः (*vi rājathaḥ*)—you cause to shine
- दिवि (*divi*)—in the world of heaven
- चित्र्यं (*citryaṃ*)—worshipful
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—in the form of a chariot
- सूर्यं (*sūryaṃ*)—the sun
- आ धत्थः (*ā dhatthaḥ*)—you have upheld (for the good of the world)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, you, the wise, protect the sacrifices and other rites with the help of the extraordinary power of the powerful Parjanya and by your actions such as causing rain to fall.

### Printed p. 26
By water you cause the whole world to shine. You have upheld (established) in the world of heaven the sun, who is worshipful and in the form of a chariot, for the good of the world.

##### English translation
Sapient Mitra and Varuna. by your office you protect pious rites, through the power of the emitter of showers: you illumine the whole world with water: you sustain the sun, the adorable chariot in the sky.

## Sūkta 64 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वरुणं व इति सप्तर्चमष्टमं सूक्तमर्चनानस आर्षं मैत्रावरुणं ।
> पंक्त्यंतपरिभाषयानुष्टुभं । अंत्या पंक्तिः । वरुणं पंक्त्यंतं त्वित्यनुक्रमणिका ।
> विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta, beginning वरुणं व (*varuṇaṃ va*), has seven Ṛks and is the eighth sūkta of the fifth anuvāka. Its seer is Arcanānas of the Atri line, its deity is Mitra and Varuṇa, and by the rule that a pāda-ending in paṅkti governs the metre, it is in Anuṣṭubh, with the last Ṛk in Paṅkti. The Anukramaṇikā says वरुणं पंक्त्यंतं (*varuṇaṃ paṃktyaṃtaṃ*). The use in ritual is by indication (laiṅgika).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning "Varuṇaṃ vaḥ", is the eighth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It has seven Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Arcanānā of the Atri line; the deity is Mitra and Varuṇa; the last Ṛk is in Paṅkti metre; the remaining Ṛks are in Anuṣṭubh metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Varuṇaṃ paṅktyantaṃ tu". The use in ritual is by indication.

##### Particulars

- Sūkta: 64
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 64
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 2
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 7
- Ṛṣi: Arcanānā Ātreya
- Devatā: Mitra and Varuṇa
- Chandas: 1-6 Anuṣṭubh; 7 Paṅkti

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वरु॑णं वो रि॒शाद॑समृ॒चा मि॒त्रं ह॑वामहे ।  
> परि॑ व्र॒जेव॑ बा॒ह्वोर्ज॑ग॒न्वांसा॒ स्व॑र्णरं ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 27

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वरुणं । वः । रिशादसं । ऋचा । मित्रं । हवामहे ।  
> परि । व्रजाइव । बाह्वोः । जगन्वांसा । स्वःनरं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वो युवयोर्मध्यॆ ॥ व्यत्ययेन बहुवचनं ॥ रिशादसं । रिशंति हिंसंतीति
> रिशाः शत्रवः । तेषां प्रेरकं स्वर्णरं स्वर्गस्य नेतारं । एतद्व्यवहितमप्युभयत्र
> संबध्यते । एवंविधं वरुणमुक्तलक्षणं मित्रं च ऋचा मंत्रेण हवामहे ।
> आह्वयामः । अथावयुत्योच्यते । व्रजेव गोयूथानीव बाहोर्बलेन परि
> जगन्वांसा परिगच्छंत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

वो (*vo*) means "of you two"; the plural stands by a change of number. रिशादसं (*riśādasaṃ*): the रिशाः (*riśāḥ*) are enemies, those who injure; he is their driver (impeller). स्वर्णरं (*svarṇaraṃ*) is the leader to heaven. Though these two words stand apart, they are connected with both deities. Such a Varuṇa, with the stated qualities, and Mitra we call with a ṛc, a mantra. Then the comparison is explained: व्रजेव (*vrajeva*) means "like herds of cattle", and जगन्वांसा (*jaganvāṃsā*) means "going around" by the strength of the arms. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- बाह्वोः (*bāhvoḥ*)—by his strength of arm
- परि (*pari*)—in front of themselves
- व्रजेव (*vrajeva*)—like herdsmen driving a herd of cattle along behind them **NOTE**
- जगन्वांसा (*jaganvāṃsā*)—going
- वः (*vaḥ*)—among you
- रिशादसं (*riśādasaṃ*)—the destroyer of enemies
- स्वर्णरं (*svarṇaraṃ*)—the one who takes the sacrificers and others to heaven
- वरुणं (*varuṇaṃ*)—Varuṇa
- मित्रं (*mitraṃ*)—Mitra (who has the same qualities)
- ऋचा (*ṛcā*)—with mantras
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—we call, praying

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, with mantras we call, praying, Varuṇa, the destroyer of enemies, who takes the sacrificers and others to heaven and who goes in front of you like herdsmen driving a herd of cattle along before them by the strength of their arms, and Mitra, who has the same qualities.

##### English translation
We invoke you, Mitra and Varuna, with this hymn; each the discomfiter of foes, the conductor to heaven, like (two herdsmen) driving, by (the strength of their) arms, the herds of cattle before them.

### Printed p. 28
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता बा॒हवा॑ सुचे॒तुना॒ प्र यं॑तमस्मा॒ अर्च॑ते ।  
> शेवं॒ हि जा॒र्यं॑ वां॒ विश्वा॑सु॒ क्षासु॒ जोगु॑वे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । बाहवा । सुचेतुना । प्र । यंतं । अस्मै । अर्चते ।  
> शेवं । हि । जार्यं । वां । विश्वासु । क्षासु । जोगुवे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता तौ युवां बाहवा बाहुना सुचेतुना सुष्टु प्रज्ञात्रा । प्रयासभूयस्त्वं जानतेत्यर्थः । यद्यप्यात्मेव ज्ञाता न हस्तस्तथापि तद्धर्ममत्रारोप्य
> सूयते । यद्वैतत्तावित्यस्य विशेषणं । सुज्ञानावित्यर्थः । शोभनहस्तेनास्मा
> अर्चते स्तुवते मह्यं प्र यंतं । अभिवतं सुखं प्रगमयतं ॥ अंतर्भावि
> तण्यर्थो द्रष्टव्यः । यमिर्दानकर्मा ना द्रष्टव्यः ॥ प्रयच्छतमित्यर्थः । जार्यं
> स्तुत्यं शेवं सुखं वां युवयोः संबंधि युवाभ्यां कृतं वा विश्वासु सर्वासु
> क्षासु भूमिषु जोगुवे । गच्छति । व्याप्नोति । अवते गवत इति गतिकर्मसु पाठादयमपि गतिकर्मा । अथवा जोगुवे सर्वत्र शब्दयामीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ता (*tā*) means "you two". बाहवा (*bāhavā*) is "by the arm", and सुचेतुना (*sucetunā*) is "well knowing", that is, "knowing with great effort". Although it is the self that knows and not the hand, the quality is transferred to the hand here. Or else सुचेतुना (*sucetunā*) is an epithet of ता (*tā*), meaning "you two of good knowledge". With the beautiful hand, to me, this one who praises, प्र यंतं (*pra yaṃtaṃ*): bring the desired happiness forward. The causative sense is to be understood within the verb; the root यमि (*yami*) is not to be seen as a verb of giving, but the meaning is "give". जार्यं (*jāryaṃ*) is "praiseworthy", शेवं (*śevaṃ*) is "happiness", and वां (*vāṃ*) is "belonging to you two" or "made by you two". In all the lands (क्षासु (*kṣāsu*), the earths) जोगुवे (*joguve*) means it goes, it pervades; since जोगुवे (*joguve*) is read among the verbs of motion (as in "avate, gavate"), this verb too means motion. Or else जोगुवे (*joguve*) means "I proclaim everywhere". **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ता (*tā*)—you two
- सुचेतुना (*sucetunā*)—possessing the wisdom that discerns the worthy from the unworthy
- बाहवा (*bāhavā*)—with your arms
- अर्चते (*arcate*)—the one who praises you
- अस्मै (*asmai*)—to me
- प्र यंतं (*pra yaṃtaṃ*)—fulfil (my desire)
- वां (*vāṃ*)—given by you
- जार्यं (*jāryaṃ*)—and praiseworthy
- शेवं (*śevaṃ*)—happiness
- विश्वासु (*viśvāsu*)—in all
- क्षासु (*kṣāsu*)—lands
- जोगुवे हि (*joguve hi*)—does it not pervade and spread?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

You two, with your arms that have the wisdom to discern the worthy from the unworthy, fulfil my desire, mine who praise you. The happiness given by you, which is praiseworthy, certainly pervades and spreads through all the lands.

### Printed p. 29

##### English translation
Do you two, with discriminating hand, bestow upon me, your worshipper, (what I desire); for the desirable felicity (that is given by you) spreads through all lands.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यन्नू॒ नम॒श्यां गतिं॑ मि॒त्रस्य॑ यायां प॒था ।  
> अस्य॑ प्रि॒यस्य॒ शर्म॒ण्यहिं॑सानस्य सश्चिते ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । नूनं । अश्यां । गतिं । मित्रस्य । यायां । पथा ।  
> अस्य । प्रियस्य । शर्मणि । अहिंसानस्य । सश्चिरे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यद्यदा नूनमिदानीं गतिं गमनमश्यां प्राप्नुयां॥ यद्योगादनिघातः॥
> तदा मित्रस्य मित्रभूतस्य पथा मार्गेण मित्रप्रापकेण मार्गेण यायां। गच्छेयं।
> मित्रो नयतु विद्वान् । ऋग्वे. १-९०-१ । इत्यादिषु मित्रस्यॆष्टदेशगमयितृत्वं
> प्रसिद्धं । न केवलमहमेक एव गंता किंतु सर्वॆऽपि तथागमन्नित्याह! आस्य प्रियस्य।
> मित्रस्येत्थर्थः । अहिंसानस्याहिंसतः । यतोऽयमहंतातः प्रिय इत्यर्थः । तस्य
> शर्मणि सुखे गृहे स्थाने वा सश्चिरे । संगताः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

यद्यदा (*yadyadā*) means "when": now may I obtain गतिं (*gatiṃ*), going. Because of the connection with यत् (*yat*) the verb does not lose its accent. Then may I go by the path of Mitra, the friend, that is, by the path that leads to Mitra. In passages such as मित्रो नयतु विद्वान् (*mitro nayatu vidvān*) (Ṛgve. 1-90-1) it is well known that Mitra leads to the desired place. **GLOSS** **NOTE** Not I alone shall go; all shall go in the same way, he says. अस्य प्रियस्य (*asya priyasya*): of this beloved Mitra. अहिंसानस्य (*ahiṃsānasya*) is "of the one who does not injure"; the sense is that he is dear like a father. In his happiness, that is, in his house or place, they are gathered together (सश्चिरे (*saścire*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यॆत् (*yèt*)—for which reason
- अहिंसानस्य (*ahiṃsānasya*)—of the one who causes violence itself **NOTE**
- प्रियस्य (*priyasya*)—dear to all
- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Mitra (given by Mitra)
- शर्मणि (*śarmaṇi*)—in the happiness
- सश्चिरे (*saścire*)—(all good fortunes) have gathered together (therefore)
- नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—surely
- गतिं (*gatiṃ*)—the good path
- अश्यां (*aśyāṃ*)—may I attain
- मित्रस्य (*mitrasya*)—of Mitra
- पथा (*pathā*)—by the path
- यायां (*yāyāṃ*)—may I walk

### Printed p. 30

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

All good fortunes have gathered together in the happiness given by this Mitra, who causes no violence and is dear to all. Therefore, in order to obtain his grace, surely keeping to the good path, I shall walk by the path of Mitra.

##### English translation
That I may now pursue the (right) direction, may I proceed by the path of Mitra, for all (good things) are aggregated in the happiness, (the gift) of that beloved and benignant (deity).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

मित्रस्य पथा-मित्रभूतस्य मार्गेण मित्रप्रापकेण मार्गेण गच्छेयं । (*mitrasya pathā-mitrabhūtasya mārgeṇa mitraprāpakeṇa mārgeṇa gaccheyaṃ |*). The commentator explains this sentence thus: "I shall walk by a path dear like Mitra, or by a path that leads to Mitra, that is, by the good path", and cites in this connection the verse यजुनीती नो वरुणो मित्रो नयतु विद्वान् । अर्यमा देवैः सजोषाः ॥ (*yajunītī no varuṇo mitro nayatu vidvān | aryamā devaiḥ sajoṣāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-90-1). **GLOSS** **NOTE**
#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒वाभ्यां॑ मित्रावरुणोप॒मं धे॑यामृ॒चा ।  
> यद्ध॒ क्षये॑ म॒घोनां॑ स्तोतृ॒णां च॑ स्पू॒र्धसे॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युवाभ्यां । मित्रावरुणा । उपमं । धेयां । यजा ।  
> यत् । ह । क्षये । मघोनां । स्तोत्वाणां । च । स्पर्धसे ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 31

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्रावरुणा युवाभ्यां दातव्यमुपममुप सविपे मीयमानं
> धनमृचा स्तुत्या साधनेन धेयां । धारयामि । यद्ध यत्खलु युवाभ्यां सकाशादाप्तं धनं मघोनां धनवतामदातृणां स्तोतृणां च क्षये गृहे स्पर्धसे
> स्पर्धनाय भवति । धनिकानां स्तोतृणां च धनाधिक्यविषये स्पर्धा भवतीत्यर्थः ।
> अपरिमितं धनं भवतीति भावः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra and Varuṇa, I hold (धेयां (*dheyāṃ*), "I bear") by ṛc, that is, by praise as the means, the wealth उपमं (*upamaṃ*) that is to be given by you, wealth measured out close at hand. The wealth that is obtained from you, यद्ध (*yaddha*) certainly, becomes a cause of rivalry (स्पर्धसे (*spardhase*)) in the house (क्षये (*kṣaye*)) of the wealthy मघोनां (*maghonāṃ*), who are non-givers, and of the praisers. The meaning is that rivalry arises between the rich and the praisers about the excess of wealth; the idea is that the wealth is immeasurable.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्रावरुणा (*mitrāvaruṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- स्तोतृणां (*stotṛṇāṃ*)—to the praisers (which you give)
- यत् ह (*yat ha*)—which wealth
- मघोनां (*maghonāṃ*)—of those who are merely rich (and not performers of sacrifice)
- क्षये (*kṣaye*)—in the house
- स्पर्धसे (*spardhase*)—is a cause of rivalry (gives rise to envy)
- उपमं (*upamaṃ*)—such wealth
- ऋचा (*ṛcā*)—as a reward for my praise
- युवाभ्यां (*yuvābhyāṃ*)—from you
- धेयां (*dheyāṃ*)—may I obtain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, the wealth that you give to the praisers is greater than the wealth of those who are merely rich, without performing sacrifice. Therefore it causes envy in the house of such rich men and becomes a cause of rivalry. Grant that I may obtain such wealth from you as a reward for my praise.

##### English translation
May I obtain from you, Mitra and Varuna, by my praise, such wealth as to excite envy in the dwellings of the rich and the devout.
#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ नो॑ मित्र सुदी॒तिभि॒र्वरु॑णश्च स॒दस्य॒ आ ।  
> स्वे क्षये॑ म॒घोनां॒ सखी॑नां च वृ॒धसे॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । मित्र । सुदीतिभिः । वरुणः । च । सधस्थे । आ ।  
> स्वे । क्षये । मघोनां । सखीनां । च । वृधसे ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 32

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मित्र त्वं च वरुणश्च सुदीतिभिः सुदीप्तियुक्तौ युवां नोऽस्मभ्यं
> सधस्थे सहस्थाने यज्ञ आ गच्छतं । द्वितीय आकारोऽनर्थक आदरार्थो वा ।
> किमर्थमित्युच्यते । मघोनां धनवतां हविष्मतां सखीनां च युवयोः
> सखिभूतानामस्माकं क्षये गृहे स्वे स्वकीये वृधसे वर्धनाय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Mitra, you and Varuṇa, you two, endowed with good radiance (सुदीतिभिः (*sudītibhiḥ*)), come to us at the sacrifice, the common place (सधस्थे (*sadhasthe*)). The second आ (*ā*) is either without meaning or expresses respect. The reason is now stated: for the growth (वृधसे (*vṛdhase*)) in our own house (स्वे क्षये (*sve kṣaye*)) of us, who are wealthy (मघोनां (*maghonāṃ*), that is, possessed of oblations) and are your friends (सखीनां (*sakhīnāṃ*)).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मित्र (*mitra*)—O Mitra (you)
- वरुणश्च (*varuṇaśca*)—and Varuṇa also
- मघोनां (*maghonāṃ*)—of us who possess wealth in the form of oblations
- सखीनां (*sakhīnāṃ*)—and are your friends, our
- स्वे क्षये (*sve kṣaye*)—in our own house
- वृधसे (*vṛdhase*)—to bring about growth
- सुदीतिभिः (*sudītibhiḥ*)—with your shining protections
- सदस्ये (*sadasye*)—to our house of sacrifice
- आ (*ā*) (गच्छतं (*gacchataṃ*))—be pleased to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra, you and Varuṇa also, be pleased to come to our sacrifice with your shining protections, in order to bring about growth in our own house, we who possess wealth in the form of oblations and are your friends.

##### English translation
Come, Mitra, (come), Varuna, with your splendour to our assembly, and augment (the prosperity) of the affluent (worshipper), and of (those who are) your friends in their respective abodes
#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒वं नो॒ येषु॑ वरुण क्ष॒त्रं बृ॒हच्च॑ बिभृ॒थः ।  
> उ॒रु णो॒ वाज॑सातये कृ॒तं रा॒ये स्व॒स्तये॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युवं । नः । येषु । वरुणा । क्षत्रं । बृहत् । च । बिभृथः ।  
> उरु । नः । वाजसातये । कृतं । राये । स्वस्तये ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 33

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वरुणा मित्रावरुणौ युवं युवां नोऽस्माकं क्षत्रं बलं बृहत् ब्रह्म
> परिवृधमन्नं च येषु स्तोत्रेषु निमित्तेषु बिभृथः धारयथः युवाभ्यां
> धृतमन्नं च नोऽस्मभ्यं वाजसातयेऽन्नस्य लाभाय यज्ञाय ना राये धनाय
> स्वस्तये क्षेमाय चोरु विस्तीर्णं कृतं । कुरुतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Varuṇa, that is, Mitra and Varuṇa, you two hold our strength (क्षत्रं (*kṣatraṃ*)), the great (बृहत् (*bṛhat*)) ब्रह्म (*brahma*) and food, in those hymns, which are the occasions (येषु (*yeṣu*)). **NOTE** The food held by you, for us, for the gain of food (वाजसातये (*vājasātaye*)), for the sacrifice, for wealth (राये (*rāye*)) and for welfare (स्वस्तये (*svastaye*)), make wide (उरु (*uru*)), that is, make spacious (कृतं (*kṛtaṃ*)). **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुणा (*varuṇā*)—O Mitra and Varuṇa
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- येषु (*yeṣu*)—as the reward of those same praises of ours
- क्षत्र्रं (*kṣatrraṃ*)—strength
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—and abundant food
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us (to give)
- बिभृथः (*bibhṛthaḥ*)—hold
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- वाजसातये (*vājasātaye*)—for the gain of food
- राये (*rāye*)—for increase of prosperity
- स्वस्तये (*svastaye*)—for the gain of well-being
- उरु (*uru*)—abundantly
- कृतं (*kṛtaṃ*)—show favour

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Mitra and Varuṇa, as the reward of our praises, hold strength and abundant food in order to give them to us. Show us abundant favour, for the gain of food, for increase of prosperity and for the gain of well-being.

##### English translation
You, Mitra and Varuna, bring us strength and abundant (food) for (those praises) which (we offer): be largely bountiful to us in food, in riches, in prosperity.
#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒च्छंत्यां॑ मे यज॒ता दे॒वक्ष॑ेत्रे॒ रुश॑द्गवि ।  
> सु॒तं सोमं॒ न ह॒स्तिभि॒रा प॒द्भिर्धा॑वतं नरा॒ जिभ्र॑तावर्च॒नान॑सं ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 34

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उच्छंत्यां । मे । यजता । देवक्षत्रे । रुशत्गवि ।  
> सुत । सोमं । न । हस्तिभिः । आ । पट्भिः । धावतं । नरा । बिभ्रता ।  
> अर्चनानसं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उच्छंत्यामुषसि । मे सोममिति संबंधः । यजता यष्टव्यौ रुशद्गवि
> रोचमानरश्मा प्रातःसवने देवक्षत्रे । देवानां क्षत्रं बलं यस्मिन् यज्ञे
> तद्देवक्षत्रं । तस्मिन्ने मदीयं सुतमभिषुतं सोमं न । नेति संप्रत्यर्थे । इदानीं
> हस्तिभिर्हस्तवद्धिः ॥ हंतेर्गतिकर्मणो हस्तशब्दः ॥ गमनसाधनपादवद्धिरित्यर्थः ।
> पद्धिः पादवद्धिश्च पादचतुष्टयोपेतैरश्वैरा धावतं आगच्छतं नरा हे नेतारौ
> मित्रावरुणौ ॥ '

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

उच्छंत्यां (*ucchaṃtyāṃ*) means "when the dawn is shining"; the construction is "my Soma". यजता (*yajatā*): you two who are to be worshipped; रुशद्गवि (*ruśadgavi*): when the rays are shining, at the morning pressing; देवक्षत्रे (*devakṣatre*): the sacrifice in which there is the strength (kṣatra) of the gods is the देवक्षत्रं (*devakṣatraṃ*). In that very sacrifice, my Soma that has been pressed. न (*na*) is used in the sense of "now". हस्तिभिः (*hastibhiḥ*) means "having hands"; the word hasta comes from the root han, a root of motion; the meaning is "having feet that are the means of going". पद्धिः (*paddhiḥ*) means "having feet", that is, with horses possessing four feet. Come, नरा (*narā*), leaders, Mitra and Varuṇa. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उच्छंत्यां (*ucchaṃtyāṃ*)—at the time of the appearing of the dawn
- रुशद्गवि (*ruśadgavi*)—when the ray of the sun is shining (at the time of the morning pressing)
- यजता (*yajatā*)—the ones who are worshipped
- नरा (*narā*)—the leaders
- अर्चनानासं (*arcanānāsaṃ*)—Arcanānas
- बिभ्रत् (*bibhrat*)—who nourish with favour, O Mitra and Varuṇa
- मे (*me*)—my (by me)
- सुतं सोमं (*sutaṃ somaṃ*)—this pressed Soma, wishing for it
- हस्तिभिः (*hastibhiḥ*)—possessing hands
- पड्भिः (*paḍbhiḥ*)—and possessing feet, with horses
- आ धावतं (*ā dhāvataṃ*)—come quickly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

At the time of the appearing of the dawn (in the morning), when the ray of the sun is shining, that is, at the time of the morning pressing, in the place of the sacrifice to the gods, O Mitra and Varuṇa, you who are worshipped, who are leaders and who nourish by your favour the seer called Arcanānas, wishing for this Soma pressed by me, come quickly with horses that have hands and feet.

##### English translation
Deities who are to be worshipped at the sacrifice to the gods, at the (first) shining ray (of light) at dawn, behold my Soma libation poured out : hasten, with rapid steeds, leaders (of rites), propitious to Archananas.

### Printed p. 35

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

हस्तिभिः । हस्तवद्भिः (*hastibhiḥ | hastavadbhiḥ*)—For horses, "hands" means the two forelegs, in the part of the body where hands would be; this is the meaning intended.

## Sūkta 65 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यश्चिकेतेति षडृचं नवमं सूक्तं रातहव्यस्यात्रेयस्यार्षं मैत्रावरुणं ।
> यश्चिकेत षड्रातहव्य इत्यनुक्रमणिका । पंक्त्यंतं त्वित्युक्तत्वात् पंक्त्यंतपरिभाषयानुष्टुभं । अंत्या पंक्तिः ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning यश्चिकेत (*yaściketa*) has six Ṛks and is the ninth sūkta. Its seer is Rātahavya of the Atri line, and its deity is Mitra and Varuṇa. The Anukramaṇikā says यश्चिकेत षड्रातहव्य (*yaściketa ṣaḍrātahavya*). Since it has been said that the ending is पंक्त्यंतं (*paṃktyaṃtaṃ*), by the rule of the paṅkti-ending the metre is Anuṣṭubh; the last Ṛk is Paṅkti. The use in ritual is by indication (laiṅgika).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning "Yaś cikete", is the ninth sūkta in the fifth anuvāka. It has six Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Rātahavya of the Atri line; the deity is Mitra and Varuṇa. The last Ṛk of this sūkta is in Paṅkti metre; the remaining Ṛks are in Anuṣṭubh metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: "Yaś cikete ṣaḍ rātahavyaḥ". The use in ritual is by indication.

##### Particulars

- Sūkta: 65
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 65
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 3
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 6
- Ṛṣi: Rātahavya Ātreya
- Devatā: Mitra and Varuṇa
- Chandas: 1-5 Anuṣṭubh; 6 Paṅkti
#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यश्चि॒केत॒ स सु॒क्रतु॑र्देव॒त्रा स ब्र॑वीतु नः ।  
> वरु॑णो॒ यस्य॑ दर्श॒तो मि॒त्रो वा॒ वन॑ते॒ गिरः॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यो॥ । चिकेत । सः । सुक्रतुः । देवत्रा । सः । ब्रवीतु । नः ।  
> वरुणः । यस्य । दर्शतः । मित्रः । वा । वनते । गिरः ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 36

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यः स्तोता चिकेत जानाति स्तुतिं युवयोः स सुक्रतुः स एव शोभनकर्मा
> देवत्रा देवेषु स्तोतृमध्यॆ । यद्वा । देवेषु मध्यॆ । युवयोरेव स्तुतिं
> यश्चिकेत स सुक्रतुरिति । स सुकर्मा नोऽस्माकं ब्रवीतु । स्तुतिं परमुपदिशतु ।
> यश्च दर्शतो दर्शनीयो वरुणो दर्शनीयो मित्रो वा मित्रश्च । वाशब्दश्चार्थे ।
> गिरोऽस्मदीयाः स्तुतीर्वनते संभजते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The praiser who knows (चिकेत (*ciketa*)) your praise, he is सुक्रतुः (*sukratuḥ*), one of good works, देवत्रा (*devatrā*), among the gods, or in the midst of the praisers. Or else: among the gods, whoever knows the praise of you two alone, he is सुक्रतुः (*sukratuḥ*). May that doer of good works speak to us (ब्रवीतु (*bravītu*)), and teach the praise further. And the lovely Varuṇa, or Mitra (the word वा (*vā*) is in the sense of "and"), who accept (वनते (*vanate*), share in) our praises.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the praiser who
- देवत्रा (*devatrā*)—firm among the gods
- चिकेत (*ciketa*)—knows (the order of worship of Mitra and Varuṇa)
- सः (*saḥ*)—he
- सुक्रतुः (*sukratuḥ*)—is a performer of excellent works
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of whichever praiser
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—the praises
- दर्शतः (*darśataḥ*)—the lovely in form
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- वा (*vā*)—and
- मित्रः (*mitraḥ*)—Mitra
- वनते (*vanate*)—accept
- सः (*saḥ*)—such a
- doer of good works
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us also
- ब्रवीतु (*bravītu*)—may he teach that best path

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The praiser who knows the order of worship of Mitra and Varuṇa, who are firm among the gods, he is the excellent performer. May such a doer of good works, whose praises the lovely Varuṇa and Mitra accept, teach that best path to us also.

##### English translation
He who knows (how to honour you two), amongst the gods, is the performer of good works: let him communicate (that knowledge) to us, he of whom the graceful Varuna or Mitra accepts the laudation.
#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता हि श्रेष्ठ॑वर्चसा॒ राजा॑ना दीर्घ॒श्रुत्त॑मा  
> ता सत्प॑ती ऋता॒वृध॑ ऋ॒तावा॑ना॒ जने॑जने ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 37

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । हि । श्रेष्ठवर्चसा । राजाना । दीर्घश्रुत्कतमा ।  
> ता । सत्पती इति सत्पती । ऋत्यवृधा । ऋत्यवाना । जनेजने ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता हि तौ खलु मित्रावरुणौ श्रेष्ठवर्चसा प्रशस्ततेजस्क् राजानेश्वरौ
> दीर्घश्रुत्तमा दूरदेशादाह्वानश्लोतृतमौ । ता तौ सत्पती सतां यजमानानां
> स्वामिनावृतावृधा यज्ञस्योदकस्य वा वर्धयितार् जने जने सर्वॆषु स्तोतृषु
> निमित्तेष्टृतावाना गमनवंत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ता हि (*tā hi*): those two, indeed, Mitra and Varuṇa, are श्रेष्ठवर्चसा (*śreṣṭhavarcasā*), of excellent splendour, राजाना (*rājānā*), lords, and दीर्घश्रुत्तमा (*dīrghaśruttamā*), best at hearing the call from a distant place. सत्पती (*satpatī*): they are the masters of the good, the sacrificers. ऋतावृधा (*ṛtāvṛdhā*): they cause the sacrifice, or the water, to grow. जने जने (*jane jane*): in all the praisers, on all occasions. ऋतावाना (*ṛtāvānā*): possessing motion.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- श्रेष्ठ वर्चसा (*śreṣṭha varcasā*)—possessing the most excellent splendour
- राजाना (*rājānā*)—lords
- दीर्घ श्रुत्वामा (*dīrgha śrutvāmā*)—able to hear the call even from a distant place
- ता (*tā*)—the well known
- सत्पती (*satpatī*)—protectors of the sacrificers
- ऋतावृधा (*ṛtāvṛdhā*)—causes of the growth of the sacrifice
- ता ह (*tā ha*)—those same Mitra and Varuṇa
- जने जने (*jane jane*)—for the good of every single person
- ऋतावाना (*ṛtāvānā*)—are moving about

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Those same Mitra and Varuṇa, who possess the most excellent splendour, are lords, are able to hear the call even from a distant place, are the well-known protectors of the sacrificers and are the causes of the growth of the sacrifice, move about for the good of every single person.

##### English translation
They two, verily excelling in radiance, royal (deities), who hear (invocation s) from the greatest distance, lords of the virtuous, favourers of the sacrifice, are in movement (for the good of) each individual man.
#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता वा॑मिया॒नोऽव॑से॒ पूर्वा॒ उप॑ ब्रुवे॒ सचा॑ ।  
> स्वश्वा॑सः॒ सु चे॒तुना॒ वाजा॑ अ॒भि प्र दा॒वने॑ ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 38

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । वां । इयानः । अवसे । पूर्व् । उप । ब्रुवे । सचा ।  
> सुअश्वासः । सु । चेतुना । वाजान् । अभि । प्र । दावने ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता तौ पूर्व पुरातन् नां युवामियानो गच्छन् अवसे रक्षणाय सचा
> सहोप ब्रुवे । उपेत्य सौमि । स्वश्वासः स्वश्वा वयमात्रेयाः सुचेतुना शोभनप्रज्ञानौ युवां वाजानभि अन्नान्यभिलक्ष्य प्र प्रकर्षण दावने दानाय स्तुम इति
> शेषः । यद्वा । स्वश्वासः सश्वा ॥ द्वितीयाद्विवचनस्य जसादेशः ॥ उप ब्रुव
> इति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

ता (*tā*), those two ancient (पूर्व (*pūrva*), old) ones; going (इयानः (*iyānaḥ*)) to you for protection (अवसे (*avase*)), I speak (उप ब्रुवे (*upa bruve*)) to you together, that is, I praise you, having approached. **NOTE** स्वश्वासः (*svaśvāsaḥ*): we of the Atri line, who have good horses; सुचेतुना (*sucetunā*): you two of good understanding. Aiming at food (वाजानभि (*vājānabhi*)), प्र (*pra*), greatly, for giving (दावने (*dāvane*)), we praise you; the verb स्तुम (*stuma*) is to be supplied. Or else स्वश्वासः (*svaśvāsaḥ*) means "having good horses", with the ending jas substituted for the accusative dual; it is connected with उप ब्रुव (*upa bruva*).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ता (*tā*)—the well known
- पूर्वौ (*pūrvau*)—and ancient
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you two
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protection
- इयानः (*iyānaḥ*)—approaching
- सचा (*sacā*)—both together
- उप ब्रुवे (*upa bruve*)—I praise
- स्वश्वासः (*svaśvāsaḥ*)—you who have excellent horses
- सुचेतुना (*sucetunā*)—and have excellent understanding, you
- वाजानभि (*vājānabhi*)—wishing for food
- दावने (*dāvane*)—for the giving of that food
- प्र (*pra*)—I praise exceedingly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)
