- पुरु (*puru*)—in many places
- यत् (*yat*)—not so, but
- अंतरिक्षे (*aṃtarikṣe*)—in the atmosphere
- आ गतं (*ā gataṃ*)—graciously come to us

### Printed p. 81

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, who accept a share in many sacrifices: whether at the time of this sacrifice you are in a very distant place, or in a near place, or in many places, or else in the atmosphere, graciously come to us.

##### English translation
Whether, Ashwins, you are at present far off, whether you are nigh, whether you are (straying) in many places, or whether you are in mid-air, do you, who partake of many offerings, come hither.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒ह त्या पु॑रु॒भूत॑मा पु॒रू दंसां॑सि॒ बिभ्र॑ता ।  
> व॒र॒स्या या॒म्यध्रि॑गू हु॒वे तु॒विष्ट॑मा भु॒जे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इह । त्या । पुरुभूतमा । पुरु । दंसांसि । बिभ्रता ।  
> वरस्या । यामि । अध्रिगू इत्यध्रिगू । हुवे । तुविःतमा । भुजे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इहास्मिन्यज्ञे त्या तौ पुरुभूतमा पुरूणां बहूनां यजमानानां
> भावयितृतमौ पुरु पुरूणि दंसांसि कर्माणि बिभ्रता धारयंतौ वरस्या वरणीयावध्रिगू अन्यैरधृतगमनकर्माण् यामि । उपागच्छामि । तुविष्टमा प्रभूततवौ
> भुजे भोगाय पालनाय वा हुवे । आह्वयामि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In this sacrifice, those two Aśvins are the greatest benefactors of the many sacrificers; they bear many deeds; they are to be chosen, and their movement and action are not held back by others. Sāyaṇa says: I approach them, and I invoke them, who are of abundant strength, for enjoyment or for protection.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुभूतमा (*purubhūtamā*)—you who have honoured many sacrificers with your favour
- पुरु (*puru*)—of many kinds
- दंसांसि (*daṃsāṃsi*)—deeds
- बिभ्रता (*bibhratā*)—you who bear
- वरस्या (*varasyā*)—you who are esteemed
- अध्रिगू (*adhrigū*)—you whose going is unobstructed
- त्या (*tyā*)—the renowned Aśvin deities

### Printed p. 82

- इह (*iha*)—at this time of sacrifice
- यामि (*yāmi*)—I approach
- तुविष्टमा (*tuviṣṭamā*)—the Aśvin deities of exceedingly abundant strength
- भुजे (*bhuje*)—for protecting us
- हुवे (*huve*)—I call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I approach, at this time of sacrifice, the renowned Aśvin deities, who have honoured many sacrificers with their favour, who have performed deeds of many kinds, who are esteemed, whose going is unobstructed. I call the Aśvin deities of exceedingly abundant strength for protecting us.

##### English translation
I approach you (to invite you) hither, you who are the encouragers of many; (who are) the achievers of many (great) exploits, most excellent and irresistible: I invoke you, who are most mighty, for protection.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ई॒र्मान्यद्वपु॑षे॒ वपु॑श्च॒क्रं रथ॑स्य येमथुः ।  
> पर्य॒न्या नाहु॑षा यु॒गा म॒ह्ना रजां॑सि दीयथः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ईर्मा । अन्यत् । वपुषे । वपुः । चक्रं । रथस्य । येमथुः ।  
> परि । अन्या । नाहुषा । युगा । मह्ना । रजांसि । दीयथः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ युवां रथस्य युष्मदीयस्यान्यच्चक्रमिर्मा ॥ अर्तेरीर्मति
> रूपं ॥ गंतर्यादित्ये वपुषे तस्य शोधायै वपुर्वपुष्मत् तेजोवत् चक्रं
> येमथुः । नियमितवंतौ स्थः । अन्यान्येन चक्रेण मह्ना महत्त्वेन स्वसामर्थ्येन
> नाहुषा युगा । नहुषा मनुष्याः । तेषां युगा युगोपलक्षितान् कालान्
> प्रातरादिसवनान् अहोरात्रादिकालान्वा रजांसि लोकांश्च परि दीयथः । परिगच्छथः।
> न्यंघ्यस्य मूर्धनि चक्रं रथस्य येमथुः । ऋग्वे. १-३०-१९ । इत्युक्तं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, of your chariot you have held back one wheel, a radiant one, for the sake of the Sun while he is on his course; **NOTE**. With the other wheel, by your greatness, that is by your own power, you go round the ages of men, that is the times marked by the yugas, such as the morning and other pressings, or the times of day and night and so on, and also the worlds. Sāyaṇa adds that this is stated in the passage न्यंघ्यस्य मूर्धनि चक्रं रथस्य येमथुः । (*nyaṃghyasya mūrdhani cakraṃ rathasya yemathuḥ |*) (Ṛgve. 1-30-19) **GLOSS** **NOTE**.

### Printed p. 83

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ईर्मा (*īrmā*)—(O Aśvin deities) at the time of the Sun's course
- वपुषे (*vapuṣe*)—to increase his radiance
- रथस्य (*rathasya*)—of your chariot
- वपुः (*vapuḥ*)—radiant
- अन्यत् चक्रं (*anyat cakraṃ*)—one wheel
- येमथुः (*yemathuḥ*)—you held back and made stand still
- अन्या (*anyā*)—with the other wheel
- मह्ना (*mahnā*)—with your greatness
- नाहुषा युगा (*nāhuṣā yugā*)—the day-and-night and other times of men (to regulate them)
- रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*)—in all the worlds
- परि दीयथः (*pari dīyathaḥ*)—you go round

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, in the course of the Sun's travel you held back and made stand still one radiant wheel of your chariot, to increase his radiance. With the other wheel, with your greatness, you go round and travel through all the worlds to regulate the day-and-night and other times of men.

##### English translation
You have arrested one luminous wheel of (your) car for illumining the form (of the sun), whilst with the other you traverse the spheres (to regulate) by your power the ages of mankind.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तदू॒ षु ना॑मे॒ना कृ॒तं विश्वा॒ यद्वा॒मनु॒ ष्टवे॑ ।  
> नाना॑ जा॒ताव॑रे॒पसा॒ सम॒स्मे बंधु॒मेय॑थुः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तत् । ऊं इति । सु । वां । एना । कृतं । विश्वा । यत् । वां । अनु । स्तवे ।  
> नाना । जातौ । अरेपसा । सं । अस्मे इति । बंधुं । आ । ईयथुः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे विश्वा व्याप्तौ यत् येन स्तोत्रेण वां युवामनु ष्टवे अनुस्तुवे
> तदु । उ इति पूरणः । एनानेन पौरेण सु सुष्टु वां युवाभ्यां कृतं संपादितं
> भवतु । नाना पृथगेव जात् समृद्धावरेपसापापावस्मे अस्यभ्यं बंधुमन्नं धनं
> ना समेयथुः । सम्यक् गमयथः । सम्यक् प्रयच्छतमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O all-pervading ones, whatever praise I now offer to you, let that be well accomplished for you by this Paura (worshipper). Separately born and grown, sinless, you are to bring together for us food and wealth, that is, to give it fully **NOTE**.

### Printed p. 84

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—O all-pervading Aśvin deities
- यत् (*yat*)—with whatever recitation of praise
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- अनुष्टुवे (*anuṣṭuve*)—I am praising
- तदु (*tadu*)—that same praise
- एना (*enā*)—by this Paura
- वां (*vāṃ*)—addressed to you
- सु (*su*)—exceedingly
- कृतं (*kṛtaṃ*)—has been accomplished
- नाना (*nānā*)—individually
- जाता (*jātā*)—you who were born and have grown
- अरेपसा (*arepasā*)—being free from sin
- आस्मे (*āsme*)—to us
- बंधुं (*baṃdhuṃ*)—food
- सं आ ईयथुः (*saṃ ā īyathuḥ*)—provide fully

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O all-pervading Aśvin deities, the praise with which I am now praising you has been accomplished exceedingly, addressed to you, by this Paura seer. Having been born individually and having grown, free from sin, provide us fully with food.

##### English translation
May the praise, universal (deities), wherewith I laud you, be agreeable to you, as offered by this (your worshipper); and do you, who are severally born, and free from blame bestow upon us food.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ यद्वां॑ सू॒र्या रथं॒ तिष्ठ॑द्रघु॒ष्यदं॒ सदा॑ ।  
> परि॑ वामरु॒षा वयो॑ घृ॒णा व॑रंत आ॒तपः॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । यत् । वां । सूर्या । रथं । तिष्ठत् । रघुस्यदं । सदा ।  
> परि । वां । अरुषाः । वयः । फृणा । वरंते । आतपः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ नां युवयोः सदा सर्वदा रघुष्यदं शीघ्रगं रथं सूर्या
> युवयोः पत्नि यद्यदा आ शिष्ठत् आस्थितवती तदा नां परि वरंते । परितो वृण्वंति।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, whenever Sūryā, your wife, mounts your chariot that always runs swiftly, then they surround you on every side.

### Printed p. 85

> के । अरुषा आरोचमाना फृणा दीप्ता आतप आतापनाः शत्रूणां वयोऽश्वाः ।
> अथवातप इति विशेष्यं । वयो गंतार उक्तलक्षणा आतपः सर्वतस्तापयित्र्यो
> दीप्तयः परि वरंते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Who are they? The bright, shining ones, the radiant ones, the scorchers of enemies; the word for horses is also given as वयः (*vayaḥ*). Or else आतपः (*ātapaḥ*) is the noun to be qualified: the वयः (*vayaḥ*) are the goers, of the character stated, and the आतपः (*ātapaḥ*) are the flames that heat on all sides; they surround you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वां (*vāṃ*)—(O Aśvin deities) your
- सदा (*sadā*)—at all times
- रघुष्यदं (*raghuṣyadaṃ*)—that travels swiftly
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—chariot
- सूर्या (*sūryā*)—Sūryā, your wife
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- आ तिष्ठत् (*ā tiṣṭhat*)—she mounts and sits (then)
- अरुषाः (*aruṣāḥ*)—with attractive radiance
- घृणा (*ghṛṇā*)—blazing
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—and trembling
- आतपः (*ātapaḥ*)—rays
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- परि (*pari*)—all around
- वरंते (*varaṃte*)—encompass and pervade

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, when Sūryā, your wife, mounts and sits in your chariot that travels swiftly at all times, then rays of attractive radiance, blazing and trembling, encompass and pervade you on all sides.

##### English translation
When Surya has ascended your ever-easy moving car, then bright-waving, resplendent rays (of light) encompass you.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

As to the matter that Sūryā, that is the daughter of the Sun, mounted and sat in the chariot of the Aśvin deities, see what we have written earlier, that is in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 9, page 365 and page 471.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यु॒वोरत्रि॑श्चिकेतति॒ नरा॑ सु॒म्नेन॒ चेत॑सा ।  
> घ॒र्मं यद्वा॑मरे॒पसं॒ नास॑त्या॒स्ना भु॑र॒ण्यति॑ ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 86

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> युवोः । अत्रिः । चिकेतति । नरा । सुम्नेन । चेतसा ।  
> घर्मं । यत् । वां । अरेपसं । नासत्या । आस्ना । भुरण्यति ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नरा नेतारावश्विन् युवोर्युवयोः । युवामित्यर्थः । अत्रिरस्मत्पिता
> ऋषिः सुम्मेन सुखेनाग्नि दाहोपशमरूपेण हेतुना चेतसादरयुक्तेन मनसा
> चिकेतति । जानाति । स्तुतवानित्यर्थः । सति दुःखे सुखमुक्षते । दुःखस्य कः प्रसंग
> इत्यत आह । यद्यस्मात् हे नासत्यौ वां युवयोरास्नास्येन तन्निष्टन्नेन स्तोत्रेण
> घर्मं दीप्तं दहंतमग्निमसुरैः स्वदाहाय प्रक्षिप्तमरेपसमपापं सुखकरं
> भुरण्यति प्राप्तवान् ॥ भुरण्यतिर्गतिकर्मा ॥ अत्रेरग्नेः सकाशाद्रक्षणं हिमेनाग्निं
> फुंसं । ऋग्वे. १-११६-८ । युवमृबीसमुत तप्तमत्रये । ऋग्वे. १०-३९-९ ।
> इत्यादिभिः स्पष्टं प्रदर्शितं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O leaders, Aśvins, Atri, our father the seer, knows you, that is, he has praised you, with a mind full of reverence, by reason of the happiness that consists in the quenching of the burning of the fire. If there was suffering, how is happiness spoken of? What occasion was there for suffering? Sāyaṇa answers: because, O Nāsatyas, by the praise that comes from his mouth and is directed to you, Atri obtained the blazing, burning fire that the Asuras had thrown in to burn him, harmless, free from sin, and giving comfort. The word भुरण्यति (*bhuraṇyati*) is a verb of motion. That Atri was protected from the fire is shown clearly by such passages as हिमेनाग्निं फुंसं (*himenāgniṃ phuṃsaṃ*) (Ṛgve. 1-116-8) **GLOSS** **NOTE** and युवमृबीसमुत तप्तमत्रये (*yuvamṛbīsamuta taptamatraye*) (Ṛgve. 10-39-9) **GLOSS** **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नासत्या (*nāsatyā*)—O Aśvin deities
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- वां (*vāṃ*)—who praise you
- आस्ना (*āsnā*)—by praise
- घर्मं (*gharmaṃ*)—the burning fire
- अरेपसं (*arepasaṃ*)—not harmful but giving comfort
- भुरण्यति (*bhuraṇyati*)—he obtained (then)
- नरा (*narā*)—O Aśvin deities, leaders
- अत्रिः (*atriḥ*)—Atri
- युवोः (*yuvoḥ*)—your power
- सुम्मेन (*summena*)—because he experienced happiness (with gratitude)
- चेतसा (*cetasā*)—with the mind
- चिकेतति (*ciketati*)—understood

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, when, by the greatness of the praise that extols you, Atri obtained the burning fire as harmless and comfort-giving, then, O Aśvin deities, leaders, because Atri unexpectedly experienced happiness, he understood your power with a mind full of gratitude.

##### English translation
Leaders (of rites), Atri recognized (your benevolence) with (a grateful) mind on account of the relief (you afforded him), when, Nastyas, through his praise of you, he found the (fiery) heat innocuous.

### Printed p. 87

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒ग्रो नां॑ ककु॒हो य॒यिः शृ॒ण्वे यामे॑षु संत॒निः ।  
> यद्वां॒ दंसो॑भिरश्वि॒नात्रि॑र्नराव॒वर्त॑ति ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उग्रः । नां । ककुहः । ययिः । शृण्वे । यामेषु । संतनिः ।  
> यत् । नां । दंसभिः । अश्विना । अत्रिः । नरा । आववर्तति ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उग्र उद्धूर्णबलो वां युवयोः ककुह उच्छ्रितो महान् ययिर्गंता संतनिः सततं गच्छन् रथो यामेषु गमनेषु यज्ञेषु वा-शृण्वे ।
> श्रूयते । हे अश्विना हे नरा यद्यस्माद्वां युवां दंसोभिः कर्मभिरत्रिरसृष्टिताववर्तति आवर्तयति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Your chariot, of lofty strength, high and great, a goer that goes constantly, is heard of in the goings, that is in the sacrifices. O Aśvins, O leaders, because Atri by your deeds is turned about, that is brought round **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—chariot of terrible strength
- ककुहः (*kakuhaḥ*)—great
- ययिः (*yayiḥ*)—and given to moving
- संतनिः (*saṃtaniḥ*)—the chariot
- यामेषु (*yāmeṣu*)—in sacrifices
- शृण्वे (*śṛṇve*)—is renowned
- नरा (*narā*)—O leaders
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- दंसोभिः (*daṃsobhiḥ*)—by deeds
- अत्रिः (*atriḥ*)—Atri, our father
- आववर्तति (*āvavartati*)—has been protected

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, leaders: your chariot, of terrible strength, great and given to moving, is renowned in sacrifices. O leaders, by your deeds Atri, our father, has been protected.

##### English translation
Your strong, lofty, moving, ever-progressing (car) has been renowned at sacrifices ever since; Aswins, leaders (of rites), Atri was rescued by your acts.

### Printed p. 88

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> मध्व॑ ऊ॒ षु म॑धूयुवा॒ रुद्रा॒ सिष॑क्ति पि॒प्युषी॑ ।  
> यत्स॑मु॒द्राति॒ पर्ष॑थः प॒क्वाः पृक्षो॑ भरंत वां ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> मध्वः । ऊं इति । सु । मध्वयुवा । रुद्रा । सिसक्ति । पि प्युषी ।  
> यत् । समुद्रा । अति । पर्षथः । पक्वाः । पृक्षः । भरंत । वां ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मधूयुवा मधुरस्य सोमादेर्मिश्रयितार् हे रुद्रा । रुत् स्तुतिः ।
> तया द्रवणीयौ रुद्रपुत्रौ ना वां मध्यो मधुररसेन सु पिप्युषी सुष्टु प्याययंती स्तुतिरस्मत्कृता सिषक्ति । सेवते । यद्यदा समुद्रा समुद्ध्रवणसाधनान्यंतरिक्षाण्यति पर्षथः अतिपारयथः । यज्ञं प्राप्नुथ इत्यर्थः । पक्वाः पृक्षोऽन्नानि
> हविर्लक्षणानि वां भरंत । भ्रियंते यजमानैः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O mixers of the sweet Soma and the like, O Rudras: the word रुत् (*rut*) means praise, and those who hasten to it are the two sons of Rudra. Our praise, nourishing you well with the sweet juice, serves you. When you cross the atmospheric regions, which are the means of passing over the waters, that is, when you reach the sacrifice, cooked foods, which are of the nature of oblation, are carried to you by the sacrificers.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मधुयुवा (*madhuyuvā*)—you who mix the sweet Soma and the like
- रुद्रा (*rudrā*)—O Aśvin deities, sons of Rudra
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- समुद्रा (*samudrā*)—the border regions of the atmosphere
- अति पर्षथः (*ati parṣathaḥ*)—you cross (that is, you come to the sacrifice; then)
- मध्वः (*madhvaḥ*)—with sweet Soma juice
- सु पिप्युषी (*su pipyuṣī*)—fully satisfying you
- सिषक्ति (*siṣakti*)—(our praise) serves you
- पक्वाः (*pakvāḥ*)—cooked
- पृक्षः (*pṛkṣaḥ*)—foods
- वां (*vāṃ*)—to you
- भरंत (*bharaṃta*)—are carried

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, mixers of the sweet Soma and the like, sons of Rudra: when you cross the border regions of the atmosphere and come to our sacrificial ground, our praise serves you, fully satisfying you with the sweet Soma juice. Cooked foods are carried to you.

### Printed p. 89

##### English translation
Mixers of the Soma-juice, Rudras, (our) nutritious (adoration) bedews you well with the libation, when you traverse (the limits) of the firmament, and the prepared viands (of the sacrifice) support you.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स॒त्यमिद्वा उ॑ अश्विना यु॒वामा॑हुर्मयो॒भुवा॑ ।  
> ता याम॑न्याम॒हूत॑मा॒ याम॒न्ना मृ॑ळ॒यत्त॑मा ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सत्यं । इत् । वै । ऊं इति । अश्विना । युवां । आहुः । मयःभुवा ।  
> ता । यामन् । यामहूतमा । यामन् । आ । मृळयत्कतमा ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना युवां सत्यमित् सत्यमेव । वा इति पादपूरणः । मयोभुवा
> सुखस्य भावयितारावाहुः पुराविदः। ता तौ सुखकरत्वेन प्रसिद्धौ युवां यामन्यज्ञे
> यामहूतमा गमनार्थं भृशमाह्वातव्यौ भवतमिति शेषः । यामन्नस्मद्यज्ञेऽस्मदर्थागमने वा मृळयत्तमा चातिशयेन सुखयितारौ भवतं । आकारश्चार्थे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, truly indeed; the word वा (*vā*) is only a filler of the foot. The men of old call you the producers of happiness. You, renowned as makers of happiness, are to be eagerly invoked in the sacrifice for your coming; this is the word to be supplied. And in our sacrifice, or at your coming for us, be exceedingly gladdening. The long ā stands in the sense of "and".

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- युवां (*yuvāṃ*)—you
- सत्यमित् (*satyamit*)—truly
- मयोभुवा (*mayobhuvā*)—givers of happiness
- आहुः (*āhuḥ*)—the seers of old have said
- ता (*tā*)—such as you are
- यामन् (*yāman*)—in our sacrifice
- यामहूतमा (*yāmahūtamā*)—you are prayed to in many ways for your coming
- यामन् (*yāman*)—in this sacrifice of ours
- मृळयत्तमा (*mṛḷayattamā*)—be most bountiful givers of happiness

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, the seers of old have praised you as truly the givers of happiness. Praying to such as you in many ways, we call you to this sacrifice. Come to this sacrifice of ours and here be givers of happiness in many ways.

### Printed p. 90

##### English translation
Truly have they called you, Aswins, the bestowers of felicity : such may you be when earnestly invoked to our sacrifice ; bountiful bestowers of felicity at our sacrifice.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मा ब्रह्मा॑णि॒ वर्ध॑ना॒श्विभ्यां॑ संतु॒ शंत॑मा ।  
> या तक्षा॑म॒ रथा॑ इ॒वावो॑चानु बृ॒हन्नमः॑ ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमा । ब्रह्माणि । वर्धना । अश्विभ्यां । संतु । शंतमा ।  
> या । तक्षाम । रथान् इव । अवोचाम । बृहत् । नमः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अथ स्तुतिं निगमयति । इमेमानीदानीं कृतानि ब्रह्माणि परिवृढानि स्तोत्राण्यश्विभ्यां वर्धना वर्धनानि समर्धकानि शंतमा सुखतमानि संतु । या यानि तक्षाम
> संपादयाम रथानिव शिल्पी तद्वत्तक्षाम तानि संतु । वयं बृहत् प्रभूतं प्रभूतफलप्रदं नमो नमस्कारोक्तिमवोचाम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Now he concludes the praise. Let these hymns, made now, full-grown, be for the Aśvins increasing and most pleasing. Those that we have fashioned, as a craftsman fashions chariots, let them be so. We have uttered great homage, which is abundant and yields abundant fruit.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रथानिव (*rathāniva*)—as a craftsman skilfully builds chariots
- या (*yā*)—which praises
- तक्षाम (*takṣāma*)—we fashion
- इमा ब्रह्माणि (*imā brahmāṇi*)—these praisers, such as these
- अश्विभ्यां (*aśvibhyāṃ*)—for the Aśvin deities
- वर्धना (*vardhanā*)—causing increase
- शंतमा (*śaṃtamā*)—causing happiness
- संतु (*saṃtu*)—let them be
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundantly
- नमः (*namaḥ*)—words of homage
- अवोचाम (*avocāma*)—we recite

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a craftsman skilfully builds chariots, so may these praises that we fashion be for the Aśvin deities causing increase and causing happiness. We recite, addressed to the Aśvin deities, abundant words of homage.

### Printed p. 91

##### English translation
May these praises exalting the Aswins be productive of happiness, the praises that we fabricate as (a wheelwright) a car: we proclaim aloud fervent adoration.

## Sūkta 74 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कूष्ठ इति दशर्च० द्वितीयं सूक्तं पौरस्यार्ष० । कूष्ठ इत्यनुक्रमणिका ।
> पूर्वत्र तुशब्दादिदमप्यानुष्टुभं । आश्विनं तदिति पूर्वोक्तत्वात् ॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोः पूर्वसूक्तेन सहोक्तो विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning कूष्ठः (*kūṣṭhaḥ*) has ten ṛcs and is the second sūkta; its seer is Paura. The Anukramaṇikā says: "Kūṣṭha". Because of the word "tu" in the earlier one, this too is in the Anuṣṭubh metre; and since the preceding one is spoken of as Aśvin ("tat"), this too is addressed to the Aśvins. Its use at the Prātaranuvāka and in the Aśvin śastra is stated together with the preceding sūkta.

##### Anuvāda

The sūkta "Kūṣṭhaḥ" is the second sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It has ten ṛcs. The seer of this sūkta is Paura Ātreya; the deity is the Aśvin deities; the metre is Anuṣṭubh. It has already been said, in the application of the preceding sūkta, that this sūkta is applied for the Prātaranuvāka and the Aśvinaśastra mantras.

##### Particulars

- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 74
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 13, 14
- Number of ṛcs in the sūkta: 10
- Seer: Paura Ātreya
- Deity: the Aśvins
- Metre: Anuṣṭubh

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कूष्ठो॑ देवावश्विना॒द्या दि॒वो म॑नावसू ।  
> तच्छ्र॑वथो वृषण्वसू॒ अत्रि॑र्वा॒मा वि॑वासति ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 92

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कूस्थः । देवौ । अश्विना । आद्य । दिवः । मनावसू इति ।  
> तत् । श्रवथः । वृषण्वसू इति वृषण्वसू । आत्रिः । नां । आ । विवासति ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देवौ हे मनावसू स्तुतिधनावश्विनाद्यास्मिन्यागदिने दिवो द्युलोकादागत्य
> कूष्ठः ॥ व्यत्ययेनैकवचनं ॥ कौ भूमौ तिष्ठंतौ संतौ तत्सोत्रं श्रवथः । शृणुथः।
> हे वृषण्वसू वर्षकधनौ वामत्रिरत्रेः पुत्र आ निवासति । सर्वतः परिचरति ॥ अथवा
> कू इत्यस्य निपातस्य स्थ इत्याख्यातेन सहेति योगविभागात्समासः । ऐकस्वर्यं
> चाध्यॆतृसमयप्राप्तं । अरा इव । ऋग्वे. ५-५८-५ । इत्यत्र यथेवशब्दः कूचिज्जायते।
> ऋग्वे. १०-४-५ । कूचित्संतं । ऋग्वे. १०-९३-११ । इत्यादिवदत्तापि द्रष्टव्यं ॥ क्वस्थिता
> भवथः । अत्रिर्युवां स्तौति तं शृणुथ इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O gods, O Manāvasū, Aśvins, who have praise for your wealth: today, on this day of the sacrifice, coming from the heavenly world and standing on the earth, hear that praise. The word कूष्ठः (*kūṣṭhaḥ*) is a singular put for the dual by interchange; कौ (*kau*) means "on the earth". O Vṛṣaṇvasū, showerers of wealth, Atri, the son of Atri, serves you on every side. Or else the particle कू (*kū*) is joined with the verb स्थ (*stha*) as a compound by division of the rule, and the single accent is what has come down in the reciters' tradition. As in अरा इव (*arā iva*) (Ṛgve. 5-58-5) **GLOSS**, कूचिज्जायते (*kūcijjāyate*) (Ṛgve. 10-4-5) **GLOSS** and कूचित्संतं (*kūcitsaṃtaṃ*) (Ṛgve. 10-93-11) **GLOSS**, so here too it is to be seen; where are you staying? Atri praises you; hear him **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवौ (*devau*)—divine beings
- मनावसू (*manāvasū*)—you whose wealth is praise
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- आद्य (*ādya*)—on this day of the sacrifice
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—coming from the heavenly world
- कूष्ठः (*kūṣṭhaḥ*)—and standing on the earth
- तत् (*tat*)—our praise
- श्रवथः (*śravathaḥ*)—hear
- वृषण्वसू (*vṛṣaṇvasū*)—showerers of wealth
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- अत्रिः (*atriḥ*)—the son of Atri
- आ विवासति (*ā vivāsati*)—worships everywhere

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, divine beings whose wealth is praise: on this day of the sacrifice come from the heavenly world and, standing on the earth, hear our praise. The son of Atri worships you everywhere, showerers of wealth.

##### English translation
Divine Adityas, affluent in praise, descended this day from heaven upon the earth, hear that (laudation) which, liberal showerers (of benefits), Atri ever addresses to you.

### Printed p. 93

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कुह॒ त्या कुह॒ नु श्रु॒ता दि॒वि दे॒वा नास॑त्या ।  
> कस्मि॒न्ना य॑तथो॒ जने॒ को वां॑ न॒दीनां॒ सचा॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कुह । त्या । कुह । नु । श्रुता । दिवि । देवा । नासत्या ।  
> कस्मिन् । आ । यतथः । जने । कः । वां । नदीनां । सचा ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> त्या तौ देवा देवौ नासत्याश्विन् कुह कुत्र तिष्ठतः । नु अद्य कुह श्रुता
> कुत्र श्रुत् दिवि द्युलोके निवसत इति शेषः । अथ प्रत्यक्षकृतः । हे देवौ युवां
> कस्मिन् जने यजमान आ यतथः । आगच्छधः । कः स्तोता वां युवयोर्नदीनां
> स्तुतीनां सचा सहायः स्यात् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Where are those two gods, the Nāsatyas, the Aśvins? Where are they heard of today? That they dwell in the heavenly world is the word to be supplied. Then he addresses them directly: O gods, to which sacrificer do you come? Which praiser would be the companion of your नदीनां (*nadīnāṃ*), that is, of your praises?

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- त्या (*tyā*)—the renowned
- देवा (*devā*)—divine beings
- नासत्या (*nāsatyā*)—Aśvin deities
- कुह (*kuha*)—where are they
- नु (*nu*)—now
- दिवि (*divi*)—in the heavenly world
- कुह (*kuha*)—in what region
- श्रुता (*śrutā*)—are they famed (O Aśvin deities)
- कस्मिन् जने (*kasmin jane*)—to favour which sacrificer
- आ यतथः (*ā yatathaḥ*)—you come
- कः (*kaḥ*)—which sacrificer
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- नदीनां (*nadīnāṃ*)—praises
- सचा (*sacā*)—helps

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Where are the renowned Aśvin deities, the divine beings, now? In what region of the heavenly world are they famed? O Aśvin deities, to favour which sacrificer do you come to the earth? Which sacrificer is a helper in the making of praises addressed to you?

##### English translation
The divine Nasatyas, where are they? where are they heard of in heaven? to what worshipper do you come? who may be the associate of your praises ?

### Printed p. 94

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कं या॑थः॒ कं ह॑ गच्छथः॒ कमच्छा॑ युंजाथे॒ रथं॑ ।  
> कस्य॒ ब्रह्मा॑णि रण्यथो व॒यं वा॑मुश्मसी॒ष्टये॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कं । याथः । कं । ह । गच्छथः । कं । अच्छ । युंजाथे इति । रथं ।  
> कस्य । ब्रह्माणि । रण्यथः । वयं । वां । उश्मसि । इष्टये ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ युवां कं यजमानं यज्ञं वा प्रति याथः । अध्वानमुल्लंघ्य
> याथः । कं ह कं च प्रति गच्छथः । गत्वा च केन सह संगतौ भवथः । कं चाच्छाभिप्राप्तं युंजाथे योजयथो रथमश्वैः । कस्य ब्रह्माणि परिवृढानि स्तोत्राणि
> रण्यथः । रमेथे । वयं च नां युवामिष्टये गमनाय्षणाय वोश्मसि ।
> कामयामहे । तस्मात् कस्य च यागं प्रति न गंतव्यमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, toward which sacrificer or sacrifice do you go, passing over the road? To whom do you go, and having gone, with whom do you meet? Toward whom, who has come near, do you yoke your chariot with horses? Whose abundant praises do you delight in? And we long for you, for your coming and seeking. Therefore you should not go to anyone's sacrifice; that is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे अश्विनौ (*he aśvinau*)—O Aśvin deities
- कं (*kaṃ*)—having in view which sacrificer
- याथः (*yāthaḥ*)—have you set out
- कं ह (*kaṃ ha*)—to whom
- गच्छथः (*gacchathaḥ*)—do you go
- कं अच्छ (*kaṃ accha*)—to go toward which sacrificer
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—your chariot
- युंजाथे (*yuṃjāthe*)—do you join with horses
- कस्य (*kasya*)—of which praiser
- ब्रह्माणि (*brahmāṇi*)—hearing the praises
- रण्यधः (*raṇyadhaḥ*)—do you delight
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- इष्टये (*iṣṭaye*)—for the coming
- उश्मसि (*uśmasi*)—desire

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, having in view which sacrificer have you set out? Toward which sacrificer do you yoke horses to your chariot to go? Hearing the praises of which praiser do you delight? We desire your coming.

##### English translation
To whom do you proceed!? to whom do you repair? to (go to) whose presence do you harness you car ? by whose prayers are you gratified ? we are anxious for your arrival.

### Printed p. 95

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पौ॒रं चि॒द्ध्यु॑द॒पुतं॒ पौर॑ पौ॒राय॒ जिन्न॑थः ।  
> यदीं॑ गृभी॒तता॑तये सिं॒हमि॑व द्रु॒हस्प॒दे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पौरं । चित् । हि । उदपुतं । पौर । पौराय । जिन्नथः ।  
> यत् । ईं । गृभीततातये । सिंहंइव । द्रुहः । पदे ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पौर । इदमश्विनोः संबोधनं । पौरेण स्तुत्यत्वेन संबंधादश्विनावपि
> पौरा ॥ उभयोश्चांदसमेकवचनं ॥ हे पौरसंबंधिनावश्विन् युवां पौरं ।
> चिदिति पूरणः । पौरेण वृष्ट्यर्थं प्रार्थ्यमानत्वेन संबंधान्मेघोऽपि पौरः ।
> तमुदप्रुतमुदकप्लावकं मेघं पौराय ऋषये मह्यं जिन्वथः । प्रेरयथः ।
> यद्या युवां गृभीततातये गृहीतयज्ञसंतानाय पौरायेमेनं मेघं
> प्रतिगच्छथो द्रुहो द्रोहस्य पदे स्थानेऽरण्यदेशे सिंहमिव । गर्जंतं प्रबलं
> सिंहं यथा बलाच्चावयंति शूराः तद्वज्जिन्वथ इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Paura" is a form of address to the Aśvins: because of their connection with Paura, who praises them, the Aśvins too are called Paura; the singular for both is Vedic usage. O Aśvins, who belong to Paura, you send to me, the seer Paura, the cloud that floods with water; the word चित् (*cit*) is a filler. Because the cloud too is prayed for by Paura for the sake of rain, it too is Paura. Or else, you go to this cloud for Paura, who has taken up the continuing of the sacrifice, as heroes forcibly drive away a roaring, powerful lion in a place of harm, that is, in a forest region; in the same way you urge it on.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पौर (*paura*)—O Aśvin deities, honoured by Paura
- पौराय (*paurāya*)—for me, who am named Paura
- उदपुतं (*udaputaṃ*)—full of water
- पौरं चित् (*pauraṃ cit*)—the cloud that pours rain, a benefit to Paura
- जिन्वथ (*jinvatha*)—send it, and urge it for the sake of rain
- द्रुहः पदे (*druhaḥ pade*)—in a forest, a place of harm
- सिंहं इव (*siṃhaṃ iva*)—as hunters strike and chase away a lion
- यत् ईं (*yat īṃ*)—that same cloud
- गृभीततातये (*gṛbhītatātaye*)—urge it to come quickly to me, who am engaged in the sacrificial rite

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, honoured by Paura, send for me, who am named Paura, the cloud that is full of water and pours rain, a benefit to Paura, and urge it to pour rain. As hunters strike and chase away a lion in a forest, a place of harm, urge that same cloud to come quickly, and send it to me, who am engaged in the sacrificial rite.

### Printed p. 96

##### English translation
Pauras, send to Paura the rain shedding cloud; drive it to him who is engaged in sccrifice, as (hunters chase) a lion in a forest,

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र च्यवा॑नाज्जुजु॒रुषो॑ व॒व्रिमत्कं॒ न मुं॑चथः ।  
> युवा॒ यदी॑ कृ॒थः पुन॒रा काम॑मृण्वे व॒ध्वः॑ ॥ ५

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । च्यवानात् । जुजुरुषः । वन्रिं । आत्कं । न । मुंचथः ।  
> युवा । यदि । कृथः । पुनः । आ । कामं । ऋण्वे । वध्वः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> च्यवानादृषेर्जुजुरुषो जरसा जीर्णात्सकाशाद्वन्विं हेयं पुराणं
> रूपमत्कं न कवचमिव प्र मुंचथः । प्रमुंचतं । प्रामुंचतं द्रापिमिव च्यवानादित्यादि मंत्रांतरं । ऋग्वे. १-११६-१० । यदि यदा पुनर्युवा कृधः । युवानं
> कुरुथ इत्यर्थः । तदा वध्वः सुरूपायाः स्त्रीयः कामं कमनीयं रूपमा
> ऋण्वे । प्राप्तवान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

From Cyavāna the seer, worn out by old age, you strip off the old, discardable form, like a coat of mail. Another mantra, प्रामुंचतं द्रापिमिव (*prāmuṃcataṃ drāpimiva*) (Ṛgve. 1-116-10) **GLOSS**, and so on, says the same. When you again made him young, then he obtained a desirable form for fair women **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- जुजुरुषः (*jujuruṣaḥ*)—who was aged
- च्यवानात् (*cyavānāt*)—from the body of the seer Cyavana
- वव्रिं (*vavriṃ*)—the unbearable form of an old man
- अत्कं न (*atkaṃ na*)—as one tears off an old coat of mail
- प्र मुंचथः (*pra muṃcathaḥ*)—you destroyed
- यदि (*yadi*)—when
- पुनः (*punaḥ*)—again
- कृधः (*kṛdhaḥ*)—you made him a youth, then he
- वध्वः (*vadhvaḥ*)—so as to be attractive to women
- कामं (*kāmaṃ*)—a charming
- ऋण्वे (*ṛṇve*)—form he obtained

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, as one tears off an old coat of mail, you destroyed the unbearable form of old age from the body of the aged seer Cyavana. When you again made him a youth, he obtained a charming form, attractive to women.

### Printed p. 97

##### English translation
You stripped off (his aged form) like a cuirass from the decrepid Chyavana, so that when you had rendered him again a youth, he attracted the desires of women.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अस्ति॒ हि वा॑मि॒ह स्तो॒ता स्मसि॑ वां सं॒दृशि॑ श्रि॒ये ।  
> नू श्रु॒तं म॒ आ ग॑त॒मवो॑भिर्वाजिनीवसू ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्ति । हि । वां । इह । स्तोतु । स्मसि । वां । संदृशि । श्रिये ।  
> नु । श्रुतं । मे । आ । गतं । अवःभिः । वाजिनीवसू इति वाजिनीवसू ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विन्ौ वां स्तोता पौरोऽस्ति हि । वर्तते खलु । हे देवौ वां संदृशि
> संदर्शने संनिधाने स्मसि । स्मः । भवेम । किमर्थं । श्रिये । नु अद्य मे मदीयमाह्वानं श्रुतं । श्रुणुतं । श्रुत्वा आ गतं । आगच्छतं । किं तूष्टीं नेत्याह ।
> अवोभी रक्षणैः सह वाजिनीवसू अन्नवसू ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, your praiser Paura is indeed here. O gods, may we be in your sight, in your presence. For what purpose? For prosperity. Today hear my invocation and, having heard, come. Will you be silent? He says: no; come with your protections, O you whose wealth is food.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- स्तोता (*stotā*)—praiser
- इह हि (*iha hi*)—here itself
- अस्ति (*asti*)—is
- श्रिये (*śriye*)—for the increase of our prosperity
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- संदृशि (*saṃdṛśi*)—for being worthy of your sight
- स्मसि (*smasi*)—may we be
- नु (*nu*)—at the time of this sacrifice
- मे (*me*)—my praise
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—hear
- वाजिनीवसू (*vājinīvasū*)—you who are rich in food
- अवोभिः (*avobhiḥ*)—with your protections
- अगतं (*agataṃ*)—come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, Paura, your praiser, is here itself. For the increase of our prosperity, may we remain worthy of your sight. At the time of this sacrifice hear our praise. You who are rich in food, come to us with your protections.

### Printed p. 98

##### English translation
A glorifier of you both is here : may we be (retained) in your sight for the sake of prosperity : hear to day (my invocation) : come hither with your protections, you who are affluent in food-

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> को वा॑म॒द्य पु॑रू॒णामा नन्॑ने॒ मर्त्या॑नां ॥  
> को विप्रो॑ विप्रवाहसा॒ को य॒ज्ञैर्वा॑जिनीवसू ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कः । वां । अद्य । पुरूणां । आ । वव्रे । मर्त्यानां ।  
> कः । विप्रः । विप्रवाहसा । कः । यज्ञैः । वाजिनीवसू इति वाजिनीवसू ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वाजिनीवसू अन्नवसू वां युवामद्यास्मिन्निने पुरूणां मर्त्यानां
> मध्येक आ वन्ने । सर्वतो भजति । हे विप्रवाहसा विप्रैर्मेधाविभिर्वहनीयौ को
> विप्रो मेधावी वन्ने । को यजमानो यज्ञैर्वष्टे ! आत्मं लंबयुगमानः
> पुनः पुनर्ब्रूते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O you whose wealth is food, who among the many mortals serves you today on all sides? O you who are to be borne by the wise, which wise man, which man of intelligence, serves you? Which sacrificer serves you with sacrifices? **NOTE**.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वाजनीवसू (*vājanīvasū*)—O gods, who have food for wealth
- अद्य (*adya*)—on this day of sacrifice
- नां (*nāṃ*)—you
- पुरूणां (*purūṇāṃ*)—of the many
- मर्त्यानां (*martyānāṃ*)—among men
- कः (*kaḥ*)—who **NOTE**
- आ वन्ने (*ā vanne*)—is worshipping abundantly
- विप्रवाहसा (*vipravāhasā*)—O Aśvin deities, worshipped by the best of seers
- को विप्रः (*ko vipraḥ*)—which wise seer has praised and worshipped
- यज्ञैः (*yajñaiḥ*)—with sacrificial rites
- कः (*kaḥ*)—which sacrificer has worshipped you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, who have food for wealth, who among all these men has worshipped you most on this day of sacrifice? O Aśvin deities, who are praised by excellent seers

### Printed p. 99

> देवतॆगळे, मेधावियाद याव ऋषियु निम्मन्नु स्तुतिसि आराधिसिद्दानॆ? याव यजमाननु
> निम्मन्नु यज्ञकर्मगळिंद पूजिसिद्दानॆ?

O gods, which wise seer has praised and worshipped you? Which sacrificer has worshipped you with sacrificial rites?

##### English translation
Who among many mortals has this day (best) propitiated you? what wise man (has best propitiated you) who are reverenced by the wise ? What (worshipper has best propitiated you) by sacrifice, you who are affluent in food ?

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ वां॒ रथो॒ रथा॑नां॒ येष्णो॑ यात्वश्विना ।  
> पु॒रू चि॑दस्म॒युस्ति॒र आं॑गू॒षो मर्त्ये॒ष्वा ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । वां । रथः । रथानां । येष्णः । यातु । अश्विना ।  
> पुरु । चित् । अस्मयुः । तिरः । आंगूषः । मर्त्येषु । आ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनाश्विनौ वां युवयो रथ एको रथो रथानां येष्ण इतरदेवरथानां मध्येऽतिशयेन गंता सन् आ यातु । कीदृशः सन् । पुरू चिद्वहून्यप्यस्मद्विरोधिनस्तिरो हिंसकस्तिरस्कर्ता वास्मयुरस्मान् कामयमानो मर्त्येषु सर्वेषु
> यजमानेषु मध्य अंगूषः स्तुत्यः । आकारः पूरणः । अथवा अंगूषः स्तोमः ।
> स्तवोऽस्मयुरस्मान् बलैर्योक्तुं कामयमानो मर्त्येषु मत्यैरस्मदीयैर्युत्विग्भिः प्रेरितः सन् । उक्तलक्षणाश्विनो रथस्य ना स्तुतिरा सर्वतः पुरू चित्
> पुरुण्यपि शत्रुबलानि तिरः । तिरस्करोति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, may your chariot, one among the chariots, the one that goes most excellently among the chariots of the other gods, come hither. Of what kind is it? It overcomes and defeats even many enemies of ours, or it desires us; among all mortals, that is, among the sacrificers, it is praised. The syllable of the verse's ending is a filler. Or else the word for "praised" means the hymn itself: the hymn, desiring to join us with strength, is urged among mortals by our own priests. The praise of the chariot of the Aśvins, of the kind described, comes from all sides; even many hostile forces it overcomes. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- पुरू चित् (*purū cit*)—even enemies of many kinds
- तिरः (*tiraḥ*)—which overcomes
- अस्मयुः (*asmayuḥ*)—which desires to come to us
- मर्त्येषु (*martyeṣu*)—among all men

### Printed p. 100

- आंगूषः (*āṃgūṣaḥ*)—which is exceedingly praised
- रथानां (*rathānāṃ*)—among chariots
- श्रेष्ठः (*śreṣṭhaḥ*)—the most excellent
- नां रथः (*nāṃ rathaḥ*)—your chariot
- आ यातु (*ā yātu*)—may it come to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, may your chariot, which overcomes even enemies of many kinds, desires to come to us, is exceedingly praised among all men and is the most excellent among chariots, come to us.

##### English translation
May your car, Aswins, the swiftest of the cars (of the gods), come hither well-disposed towards us, the discomfiter of numerous (foes), glorified amongst men

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> शमू॒षु वां॑ मधूयुवा॒स्माक॑मस्तु चर्कृ॒तिः ।  
> अ॒र्वा॒ची॒ना वि॑चेतसा॒ विभिः॑ श्ये॒नेव॑ दीयतं ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> शं । ऊं इति । सु । वां । मधुयुवा । आस्माकं । अस्तु । चर्कृतिः ।  
> अर्वाचीना । विचेतसा । विभिः । श्येनाइव । दीयतं ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मधूयुवा मधुमंतौ वां युवाभ्यां चर्कृतिः पुनःपुनः करणं
> युवाभ्यामर्थाय पुनःपुनः क्रियमाणं स्तोत्रं युवयोरागमनस्याभ्यासो
> नास्माकं सु सुष्टु शमु सुखकरमेवास्तु । भवतु । हे अर्वाचीनार्वागस्मदभिमुखांचनौ हे विचेतसा विशिष्टप्रज्ञॆ श्येनेव श्येनाविव विभिर्गंतृभिरश्वैर्दीयतं ।
> गच्छतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, you who are possessed of honey: the repeated making of praise for you, that is, the praise made again and again for your sake, the repetition of your coming, may it be well and happily productive for us. O you who are turned towards us, O you of distinguished wisdom, go like two falcons with your swift-going horses.

### Printed p. 101

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मधूयुवा (*madhūyuvā*)—O Aśvin deities, joined with honey
- वां (*vāṃ*)—addressed to you
- चर्कृतिः (*carkṛtiḥ*)—made and recited again and again
- आस्माकं (*āsmākaṃ*)—our praises
- सु (*su*)—exceedingly
- शमु अस्तु (*śamu astu*)—may they be productive of happiness
- आर्वाचीना (*ārvācīnā*)—you who descend towards us
- विचेतसा (*vicetasā*)—you who have special wisdom
- श्येनेव (*śyeneva*)—like a falcon
- विभिः (*vibhiḥ*)—with swift-going horses
- दीयतं (*dīyataṃ*)—come quickly

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, joined with honey, may our praises, made and recited again and again for you, be exceedingly productive of happiness. You who descend towards us and have special wisdom, come here quickly, like a falcon, with your swift-going horses.

##### English translation
May our repeated adoration of you two, who are desirous of the libation, be productive of felicity: descending to our presence, and exceeding in wisdom, travel with rapid (steeds), swift as to falcons.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अश्वि॑ना॒ यद्ध॒ कर्हि॑ चिच्छुश्रू॒यात॑मि॒मं हवं॑ ।  
> वस्थि॑रू॒ षु वां॒ भुजः॑ पृं॒चंति॒ सु वां॒ पृचः॑ ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्विना । यत् । ह । कर्हि । चित् । शुश्रुयातं । इमं । हवं ।  
> वस्वीः । ऊं इति । सु । वां । भुजः । पृंचंति । सु । वां । पृचः ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना युवां यद्ध यत्र कर्हि चित् स्थितवंतौ भवथः शत्रेमं मे
> हवमाह्वानं शुश्रुयातं । शृणुतं । श्रुत्वागच्छतमित्यर्थः । किमर्थमित्युच्यते ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, wherever you may be, hear this invocation of mine; that is, hear it and come. For what purpose, he states next.

### Printed p. 102

> वस्वीः प्रशस्या भुजो धनानि हविर्लक्षणानि सु सुष्टु नां युवां पृंचंति । मिश्रयंति । प्राप्नुवंति । उ इति पूरणः । कीदृशो भुजः । नां युवां सु सुष्टु पृचः
> संपर्चनाः । युवां प्राप्तुं कामयमाना इत्यर्थः । अथवा सु पृचः सुसंपर्चनाः स्तोतारो वस्वीर्भुजो नां सुष्टु पृंचंति । संपर्चयंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The excellent, praiseworthy riches in the form of oblations reach you well; that is, they mingle with you and attain you. The syllable that follows is a filler. Of what kind are these riches? They are well-connecting, desiring to reach you. Or else the praisers, who are well-connecting, mingle well with you the excellent riches.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- यद्धकर्हिचित् (*yaddhakarhicit*)—in whatever region you may be
- इमं हवं (*imaṃ havaṃ*)—this hymn recited by me
- शुश्रुयातं (*śuśruyātaṃ*)—listen
- सु पृचः (*su pṛcaḥ*)—those that wish to join you with regard
- वस्वीः (*vasvīḥ*)—the praiseworthy
- भुजः (*bhujaḥ*)—riches in the form of oblations
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- सु (*su*)—in an exceeding manner
- पृंचंति (*pṛṃcaṃti*)—come and join

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, wherever you may be, listen to this praise recited by me. The praiseworthy riches in the form of oblations, which wish to join you with regard, come and join you in an exceeding manner. **NOTE**

##### English translation
Aswins, wherever you may be, hear this invocation : the excellent sacrificial offerings, longing for your proximity, reach you.

## Sūkta 75 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रति प्रियतममिति नवर्चं तृतीयं सूक्तं अवस्युर्नामात्रेय ऋषिः ।
> पंक्तिश्चंदः । अश्विनौ देवता । अनुक्रांतं च । प्रति नवावस्युः पांक्तमिति ॥ प्रातरनुवाक
> आश्विने क्रत् पांक्ते छंदस्याश्विनशस्त्रे चेदं सूक्तं । प्रति प्रियतममिति पांक्तं ।
> आ. ४-१५ । इति हि सूत्रितं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning with the words "prati priyatamam" has nine ṛks and is the third sūkta. Its seer is Avasyu of the Atri line, its metre is Paṅkti and its deity is the Aśvins. The Anukramaṇī says: प्रति नवावस्युः पांक्तं (*prati navāvasyuḥ pāṃktaṃ*). This sūkta is used in the Prātaranuvāka, in the Āśvina-kratu, in the mantras in the Paṅkti metre, and in the Āśvina-śastra. This is stated in the sūtra of Āśvalāyana, Ā. 4-15.

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning with prati priyatamam is the third sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It contains nine ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Avasyu of the Atri line; the deity is the Aśvins; the metre is Paṅkti. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: प्रति नवावस्युः पांक्तं (*prati navāvasyuḥ pāṃktaṃ*).

### Printed p. 103

The application of this sūkta is for the Prātaranuvāka and for the mantras in the Paṅkti metre relating to the Āśvina-kratu; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, प्रति प्रियतम मिति पांक्तं (*prati priyatama miti pāṃktaṃ*) (Ā. 4-15).

##### Particulars

- Sūkta: 25 **NOTE**
- Maṇḍala: 5
- Anuvāka: 6
- Sūkta: 75
- Aṣṭaka: 4
- Adhyāya: 4
- Varga: 15, 16
- Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 9
- Ṛṣi: Avasyu of the Atri line
- Devatā: the Aśvins
- Chandas: Paṅkti

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रति॑ प्रि॒यत॑मं॒ रथं॒ वृष॑णं वसु॒वाह॑नं ।  
> स्तो॒ता वा॑मश्विना॒वृषिः॒ स्तोमे॑न॒ प्रति॑ भूषति॒ माध्वी॒ मम॑ ॒ ॑
> श्रुतं हवं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रति । प्रियतमं । रथं । वृषणं । वसुवाहनं ।  
> स्तोता । वां । अश्विन् । ऋषिः । स्तोमन । प्रति । भूषति । माध्वी इति । मम । श्रुतं ।  
> हवं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ । एकः प्रतिशब्दोऽनुवादः । वां युवयोः प्रियतमं रथं
> स्तोता ऋषिरवस्युः स्तोमेन प्रति भूषति । अलंकरोति । कीदृशं रथं । वृषणं
> वर्षितारं फलानां वसुवाहनं धनानां वाहकं । ईदृशं रथमागमनाय
> स्तोतीत्यर्थः । तस्मात् हे माध्वी मधुविद्यावेदितार् मम हवमाह्वानं श्रुतं
> शृणुतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins. The one word "prati" is a mere repetition. The seer Avasyu, the praiser, adorns with praise your dearest chariot. Of what kind is the chariot? It is the showerer of fruits and the bearer of wealth. He praises such a chariot so that it may come. Therefore, O you who know the honey-lore, hear my invocation.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विनौ (*aśvinau*)—O Aśvin deities
- वां (*vāṃ*)—to you
- प्रियतमं (*priyatamaṃ*)—the one that is most dear
- वृषणं (*vṛṣaṇaṃ*)—the one that showers desires
- वसुवाहनं (*vasuvāhanaṃ*)—the one that carries riches
- रथं (*rathaṃ*)—the chariot

### Printed p. 104

- स्तोता (*stotā*)—the one named Avasyu, the praiser
- ऋषिः (*ṛṣiḥ*)—and the seer, I
- स्तोमेन (*stomena*)—with praises
- प्रति भूषति (*prati bhūṣati*)—adorn
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvin deities who know the honey-lore
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—invocation
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, I, the praiser, the seer, named Avasyu, adorn with praises your chariot, which is most dear to you, which showers desires and carries riches. O Aśvin deities who know the honey-lore, listen to my invocation.

##### English translation
The Rishi, your worshipper. Aswins, graces your beloved chariot, the showerer (of benefits), the vehicle of wealth, with praises : masters of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

We have already described in detail the distinctive features of the chariot of the Aśvin deities. It is also said in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-34 that their chariot has three wheels. The meaning of the epithets त्रिचक्रः (*tricakraḥ*), त्रिवंधुरः (*trivaṃdhuraḥ*), मनसः जवीयान् (*manasaḥ javīyān*) and so on was explained earlier. In the present context we must examine in what manner the epithets त्रिचक्रः (*tricakraḥ*) and त्रिवंधुरः (*trivaṃdhuraḥ*) show the all-pervading nature of the Aśvin deities. Just as the pervasiveness of Viṣṇu is understood from sentences such as त्रिषु विक्रमणेषु (*triṣu vikramaṇeṣu*) **GLOSS** and so on, and the all-pervading nature of Agni from sentences such as त्रीणि जाना परिभूषंति (*trīṇi jānā paribhūṣaṃti*) **GLOSS** and so on, so the nature of the Aśvin deities is understood from the epithets त्रिचक्रः (*tricakraḥ*) and so on. It is noteworthy that the descriptions of Agni, Viṣṇu, Savitṛ and the Aśvin deities all explain a common principle, and in all contexts explain an element common to all these deities. The English scholar Hopkins, explaining this point, has stated as follows.

A comparison with the abodes of other Vedic Deities who are said to traverse the whole Universe like Vishnu confirms the same view. One of these deities is Savitṛ. The second deity that traverses or measures the Universe is Agni. He has three stations. His light is spoken of as three-fold. Although these three stations do not seem to be always conceived alike, yet, one of them at any rate can be clearly identified with the third step of Vishnu. Agni sometimes represents the Sun in the Rig-Veda and Vishnu is also the same Sun under

### Printed p. 105

a different name and the third or the hidden abode of Agni can therefore, be easily recognised as identical in character. The third Deity that traverses the Universe is the Aswins to whom the epithet परिज्मन् (*parijman*) is applied several times in the Rig-Veda. The Aswins are said to have three stations and their chariot which is to go over both the worlds alike has three wheels one of which is represented as deposited in a cave or a secret place like the third step of Vishnu which is beyond the ken of mortals. This coincidence between the third stations of three different world-traversing Gods cannot be treated as accidental. (J. A. O. S. Vol. 16. p. CXLIX to CL.)

Two points are clearly understood from the above passage. First: 1. The three worlds, the earth, the heavenly world and the atmosphere, are the dwelling-places of Agni, Viṣṇu and the Aśvin deities; and secondly, 2. All these three deities have three steps, and the third step of each of the deities occupies the world that lies beyond the reach of man. Both these points are noteworthy. If we examine only some of the verses that describe the first point:

प्र तद्विष्णुः स्तवते वीर्येण मृगो न भीमः कुचरो गिरिष्ठाः । यस्योरुषु त्रिषु विक्रमणेष्वधिक्षियंति भुवनानि विश्वा ॥ (*pra tadviṣṇuḥ stavate vīryeṇa mṛgo na bhīmaḥ kucaro giriṣṭhāḥ | yasyoruṣu triṣu vikramaṇeṣvadhikṣiyaṃti bhuvanāni viśvā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-154-2)

यस्य त्री पूर्णा मधुना पदान्यक्षीयमाणा स्वधया मदंति । य उ त्रिधातु पृथिवीमुत द्यामेको दाधार भुवनानि विश्वा ॥ (*yasya trī pūrṇā madhunā padānyakṣīyamāṇā svadhayā madaṃti | ya u tridhātu pṛthivīmuta dyāmeko dādhāra bhuvanāni viśvā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-154-4)

त्रीणि जाना परि भूषंत्यस्य समुद्र एकं दिव्येकमप्पु । पूर्वामनु प्र दिशं पार्थिवानामृतून्प्रशासद्वि दधावनुष्टु ॥ (*trīṇi jānā pari bhūṣaṃtyasya samudra ekaṃ divyekamappu | pūrvāmanu pra diśaṃ pārthivānāmṛtūnpraśāsadvi dadhāvanuṣṭu ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-95-3)

अग्निस्त्रीणि त्रिधातून्या क्षेति विदथा कविः । स त्रीरेकादशा इह यक्षच्च पिप्रयच्च नो विप्रो दूतः परिष्कृतो नभंतामन्यके समे ॥ (*agnistrīṇi tridhātūnyā kṣeti vidathā kaviḥ | sa trīrekādaśā iha yakṣacca piprayacca no vipro dūtaḥ pariṣkṛto nabhaṃtāmanyake same ‖*) **NOTE**

त्रीणि पदान्यश्विनोराविः सांति गुहा परः । कवी ऋतस्य पत्मभिरर्वाग्जीवेभ्यस्परि ॥ (*trīṇi padānyaśvinorāviḥ sāṃti guhā paraḥ | kavī ṛtasya patmabhirarvāgjīvebhyaspari ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-8-23)

### Printed p. 106

Of these ṛks the first two describe the all-pervasiveness of Viṣṇu. Accordingly, all created beings stand firmly settled in the casts of Viṣṇu's exceedingly extensive steps, three in number. The three steps of Viṣṇu, which are filled with sweet nectar and are imperishable, delight those who take refuge in them with the gift of pure food. And that same Viṣṇu has upheld the seven lower worlds and the seven upper worlds, thus all the fourteen worlds, as though joined together with the three elements (dhātu) that have the forms of earth, water and fire: such is the description of the pervasiveness of Viṣṇu's three steps. In the third and fourth ṛks the nature of Agni is stated. Agni has three birthplaces: one in the sea in the form of the submarine fire, one in the heavenly world in the form of the sun, and one in the atmosphere in the form of lightning. And this same Agni has pervaded the three elements (tridhātu); thus Agni's pervading of the three worlds is described. In the same way, in the fifth ṛk the three steps of the Aśvin deities are described, as are the dwelling-places of Viṣṇu and Agni; and likewise:

इदं हि नां प्रदिवि स्थानमोक इमे गृहा अश्विनेदं दुरोणं । आ नो दिवो बृहतः सर्वतादाद्भो यातमिषमूर्ज० वहंता ॥ ( (*idaṃ hi nāṃ pradivi sthānamoka ime gṛhā aśvinedaṃ duroṇaṃ | ā no divo bṛhataḥ sarvatādādbho yātamiṣamūrja0 vahaṃtā ‖ (*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-76-4)

here it is described that for the Aśvin deities also the three worlds, earth, atmosphere and the heavenly world, are dwelling-places. One more point must be noted in these descriptions. In the ṛk 1-154-4 that describes the steps of Viṣṇu, in the ṛk 8-39-9 that describes the pervasiveness of Agni, and in the ṛk (1-183-1) that describes the chariot-journey of the Aśvin deities, the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) is a word that indicates the common nature of these three deities.

Secondly, the description that the third step (or abode) of all of them lies in a secret place beyond reach shows the nature common to all of them.

विष्णुरित्था परममस्य विद्वाञ्चातो बृहन्नभि पाति तृतीयं । आसा यदस्य पयो अक्रत स्वं सचेतसो अभ्यर्चंत्यत्र ॥ (*viṣṇuritthā paramamasya vidvāñcāto bṛhannabhi pāti tṛtīyaṃ | āsā yadasya payo akrata svaṃ sacetaso abhyarcaṃtyatra ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-1-3)

कं इमं वो निण्यमा चिकेत वत्सो मात्रर्जनयत स्वधाभिः । (*kaṃ imaṃ vo niṇyamā ciketa vatso mātrarjanayata svadhābhiḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-95-4)

द्वे इदस्य क्रमणे स्वर्दृशोऽभिख्याय मत्योF भुरण्यति । तृतीयमस्य नकिरा दधर्षति वयश्चन पतॆयंतः पतत्रिणः ॥ (*dve idasya kramaṇe svardṛśo 'bhikhyāya matyoF bhuraṇyati | tṛtīyamasya nakirā dadharṣati vayaścana patèyaṃtaḥ patatriṇaḥ ‖*) **NOTE** (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-155-29)

### Printed p. 107

In ṛks such as these it is stated that the third place of Agni and the occupation of the third step of Viṣṇu cannot be known by any man. As it is said, "Man knows only, by praising, the earthly world and the atmospheric world, the two steps of Viṣṇu, who looks upon the heavenly world; no man can know the world in which the third step of this Viṣṇu is placed", so also the course of the three-wheeled chariot of the Aśvin deities and its journey beyond reach are stated:

यदयातं शुभस्पती वरेयं सूर्यामुप । क्वैकं चक्रं नामासीत्त्व देष्ट्राय तस्थथुः ॥ द्वे ते चक्रे सूर्ये ब्रह्माण ऋतुथा विदुः । अथैकं चक्रं यद्दुहा तदद्धातय इद्दिदुः ॥ (*yadayātaṃ śubhaspatī vareyaṃ sūryāmupa | kvaikaṃ cakraṃ nāmāsīttva deṣṭrāya tasthathuḥ ‖ dve te cakre sūrye brahmāṇa ṛtuthā viduḥ | athaikaṃ cakraṃ yadduhā tadaddhātaya iddiduḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-85-15, 16)

Here it is described that of the three wheels of the chariot of the Aśvin deities only one is visible to no eye and is known only to seers who have the power of vision. In this way, in the description of their extraordinary nature, a common quality of the three deities is shown in two main points. It was also stated earlier that all three deities are of the nature of the sun. In the epithets त्रिचक्रः (*tricakraḥ*), त्रिबर्हिः (*tribarhiḥ*), त्रिषधस्थः (*triṣadhasthaḥ*), त्रिवंधुरः (*trivaṃdhuraḥ*), त्रिविक्रम (*trivikrama*) and so on, a symbolic meaning is indicated. The same matter is stated in the present ṛk also. And since, as said above, their all-pervasiveness is conveyed by the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*), it must be understood what the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) means.

त्रिधातुना—त्रिप्रकारस्थानोपेतेन । सुवर्णरजतताम्रधातुनोपेतेन वा रथेन (*tridhātunā—triprakārasthānopetena | suvarṇarajatatāmradhātunopetena vā rathena*) — thus he has explained the meaning. In many places the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) is explained as त्रयाणां धातूनां समाहारस्त्रिधातु । पृथिव्यप्तेजोरूपधातुत्रयविशिष्टं । (*trayāṇāṃ dhātūnāṃ samāhārastridhātu | pṛthivyaptejorūpadhātutrayaviśiṣṭaṃ |*), that is, the combined principle of the three elements: earth, water and fire. In explaining the purport of this word, Sāyaṇa in some places quotes the sentence

तासां त्रिवृतं तिवृतमेकैकां करवाणीति सेयं देवतेमास्तिस्रो देवता अनेनैव जीवेनात्मानुप्रविश्य नामरूपे व्याकरोत् । (*tāsāṃ trivṛtaṃ tivṛtamekaikāṃ karavāṇīti seyaṃ devatemāstisro devatā anenaiva jīvenātmānupraviśya nāmarūpe vyākarot |*) (Bhā. U. 6-3-2-3) **GLOSS** **NOTE**

and says that here too is praised the greatness of the Supreme Brahman, who created the threefold principle of the forms of food, water and fire and sustained them by his glory. This explanation is only one of the many explanations of the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*). In some other places Sāyaṇa has said त्रिधातु कालत्रयं गुणत्रयं वा (*tridhātu kālatrayaṃ guṇatrayaṃ vā*): that it is understood either as the three times, namely past, future and present, or as the qualities of the forms sattva, rajas and tamas.

### Printed p. 108

अग्निरस्मि जन्मना जातवेदा धृतं मे चक्षुरमृतं म आसन् । आर्कस्त्रिधातू रजसो विमानोऽजस्रो घर्मो हविरस्मि नाम ॥ (*agnirasmi janmanā jātavedā dhṛtaṃ me cakṣuramṛtaṃ ma āsan | ārkastridhātū rajaso vimāno 'jasro gharmo havirasmi nāma ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-26-7) **GLOSS**

In this ṛk the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) is explained as प्राणापानव्यानात्मना त्रिधा वर्तमानोऽर्कोर्चनीयो यः प्राणोऽस्ति सोऽपि अहमेवास्मि । (*prāṇāpānavyānātmanā tridhā vartamāno 'rkorcanīyo yaḥ prāṇo 'sti so 'pi ahamevāsmi |*), that is, the principle of life-breath in the three forms of prāṇa, apāna and vyāna; and

यद्देवाः शर्म शरणं यद्भद्रं यदनातुरं । त्रिधातु यद्वरूथ्यं १ तदस्मासु वि यंतनानेहसो व ऊतयः सु ऊतयो व ऊतयः ॥ (*yaddevāḥ śarma śaraṇaṃ yadbhadraṃ yadanāturaṃ | tridhātu yadvarūthyaṃ 1 tadasmāsu vi yaṃtanānehaso va ūtayaḥ su ūtayo va ūtayaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 8-47-10) **GLOSS**

here it is explained as the three qualities in the forms of sattva, rajas and tamas. Moreover:

आ धर्णसिर्बृहद्दिवो रराणो विश्वेभिर्गन्वोमभिर्हुवानः । ग्ना वसान ओषधीरमृध्रस्त्रिधातुशृंगो वृषभो वयोधाः ॥ (*ā dharṇasirbṛhaddivo rarāṇo viśvebhirganvomabhirhuvānaḥ | gnā vasāna oṣadhīramṛdhrastridhātuśṛṃgo vṛṣabho vayodhāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-43-13)

Here there is a description of the flame of Agni. Here the word त्रिधातुशृंगः (*tridhātuśṛṃgaḥ*) is explained as त्रिप्रकारशृंगवदुन्नतलोहितशुक्ल कृष्णवर्णज्वालः (*triprakāraśṛṃgavadunnatalohitaśukla kṛṣṇavarṇajvālaḥ*), that is, one who has flames that rise high in three ways, white, black and red.

त्रिरा दिवः सवितर्नार्याणि दिवेदिव आ सुव त्रिर्नो अह्नः । त्रिधातु राय आ सुवा वसूनि भग त्रातर्धिषणे सातये धाः ॥ (*trirā divaḥ savitarnāryāṇi divediva ā suva trirno ahnaḥ | tridhātu rāya ā suvā vasūni bhaga trātardhiṣaṇe sātaye dhāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-56-6)

Here, as the qualifier of वसूनि (*vasūni*) धनानि (*dhanāni*), it is explained as त्रिधातूनि पशुकनकरत्नभेदेन त्रिप्रकाराणि (*tridhātūni paśukanakaratnabhedena triprakārāṇi*): wealth of three kinds, namely cattle, gold and jewels.

तव त्रिधातु पृथिवी उत द्यौर्वैश्वानर व्रतमग्ने सचंत । त्वं भासा रोदसी आ ततंथाजस्रेण शोचिषा शोशुचानः ॥ (*tava tridhātu pṛthivī uta dyaurvaiśvānara vratamagne sacaṃta | tvaṃ bhāsā rodasī ā tataṃthājasreṇa śociṣā śośucānaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-5-4)

Here, in describing that the three worlds follow the vow of Vaiśvānara, the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) is used, with पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*) and द्याः (*dyāḥ*), as a synonym for the atmosphere.

तस्य यो योनिराशय आस । तमनु परामृश्य संलुप्याच्छिनत्सैषेष्टिरभवत्तद्यदेतस्मिन्नाशये त्रिधातुरिवैषा विद्या तस्मात्त्रैधातवी नाम । (*tasya yo yonirāśaya āsa | tamanu parāmṛśya saṃlupyācchinatsaiṣeṣṭirabhavattadyadetasminnāśaye tridhāturivaiṣā vidyā tasmāttraidhātavī nāma |*) (Śa. Brā. 5-5-5-6)

### Printed p. 109

From the sentences of the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa it is understood that, because at the time of the slaying of the asura Vṛtra there was the support of the threefold knowledge, the sacrificial offering (iṣṭi) performed on that occasion became famous by the name Traidhātavī iṣṭi.

ततो वै स इमान् लोकाग् स्त्रेधादुहत्तत्रिधातोस्त्रिधातुत्वं यं कामयेतान्नादस्स्यादिति तस्मा एतं त्रिधातुं निर्वपेदिंद्राय राज्ञे पुरोडाशमेकादशकपालं ॥ (*tato vai sa imān lokāg stredhāduhattatridhātostridhātutvaṃ yaṃ kāmayetānnādassyāditi tasmā etaṃ tridhātuṃ nirvapediṃdrāya rājñe puroḍāśamekādaśakapālaṃ ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 2-3-6-1)

Sāyaṇa has explained the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) in these sentences of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā in the following way.

तिस्र एतास्त्रिधातोर्देवता लोकत्रयात्मिकाः । ततः खलु त्रिधातु देवातानां त्रित्वात्प्रजापतिः लोकत्रयव्यापी व्याप्तानिमान् लोकांस्त्रेधा दुहत् त्रिप्रकारमदुहत् । त्रयो धातवो धनान्यस्मिन्निति त्रिधातुः । त्रेधा धातव्यत्वात्पुरोडाशस्य । यद्वा सर्वाधारलोकत्रयात्मकत्वात् त्रिधातुः ॥ (*tisra etāstridhātordevatā lokatrayātmikāḥ | tataḥ khalu tridhātu devātānāṃ tritvātprajāpatiḥ lokatrayavyāpī vyāptānimān lokāṃstredhā duhat triprakāramaduhat | trayo dhātavo dhanānyasminniti tridhātuḥ | tredhā dhātavyatvātpuroḍāśasya | yadvā sarvādhāralokatrayātmakatvāt tridhātuḥ ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. Bhāṣya)

Prajāpati arranged for the gods who dwell in the three worlds and exist in three forms, at three times, food in the form of the puroḍāśa; thus for the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) the meanings world, food, the three elements and so on are given. Thus the word त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) is used in different meanings in different places. In the present context it can be said that, in the ṛks praising the deities Agni, Aśvin and Viṣṇu, this word is used as a synonym that denotes the three worlds, earth, atmosphere and the heavenly world. The explanation of words such as त्रिचक्र (*tricakra*) and त्रिधातु (*tridhātu*) is given in Ṛ. Saṃ. Part 4, in the 34th sūkta.

माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—This word is used to address the Aśvin deities who know the honey-lore (madhuvidyā). It is necessary to explain here what the madhuvidyā is. Skandasvāmin and Sāyaṇa have told a legend here. This legend is well known in the Vājasaneya Brāhmaṇa.

आथर्वणाय दधीचे मध्वाख्यं परं ब्रह्म इंद्र उवाच । प्रोच्य च यदि वक्ष्यसि शिरस्ते पतिष्यतीति । तमश्विन् मध्वर्थिनावागत्य ऊचतुः । प्र ब्रूहि नौ परं ब्रह्मेति । स तौ प्रत्युवाच । शिरःपातभयान्न ब्रवीमीति । तौ तं प्रत्यूचतुः । अन्यत् ते आवां शिर आदध्वहे । तेन प्रब्रूहि । स तथेति प्रपेद । तस्याश्विनौ शिरश्चित्वा अश्वशिर आदधाते । तेनासौ तानध्यापयांचकारेति । तदेतदिहोच्यते दध्यज् ह यदित्यादिना ॥ (*ātharvaṇāya dadhīce madhvākhyaṃ paraṃ brahma iṃdra uvāca | procya ca yadi vakṣyasi śiraste patiṣyatīti | tamaśvin madhvarthināvāgatya ūcatuḥ | pra brūhi nau paraṃ brahmeti | sa tau pratyuvāca | śiraḥpātabhayānna bravīmīti | tau taṃ pratyūcatuḥ | anyat te āvāṃ śira ādadhvahe | tena prabrūhi | sa tatheti prapeda | tasyāśvinau śiraścitvā aśvaśira ādadhāte | tenāsau tānadhyāpayāṃcakāreti | tadetadihocyate dadhyaj ha yadityādinā ‖*)

### Printed p. 110

Indra gave to the sage Dadhīci, the son of Atharvan, the well-known instruction in the Supreme Brahman called Madhu (the madhuvidyā), and said, "If you teach this to anyone else, your head will fall off." The Aśvin deities, desiring to obtain the madhuvidyā, came near the sage Dadhīci and asked him, "Teach us the Supreme Brahman, which the madhuvidyā sets forth." Then he said to them that he would not teach, from fear that his head would fall off. Then the Aśvin deities said, "We shall attach another head to you; therefore teach," and on hearing those words he agreed to it. Then the Aśvin deities cut off his head and attached a horse's head. He taught them the madhuvidyā. Such is the legend in the commentary of Skandasvāmin.

तदिंद्रो ज्ञात्वा वज्रेण तच्छिरो अच्चिनत् । अथाश्विनौ तस्य स्वकीयं मानुषं शिरः प्रत्यधत्तां ॥ (*tadiṃdro jñātvā vajreṇa tacchiro accinat | athāśvinau tasya svakīyaṃ mānuṣaṃ śiraḥ pratyadhattāṃ ‖*)

Indra learned of this and cut off the head of the sage Dadhīci with his thunderbolt. Afterwards the Aśvin deities attached to the body of the sage Dadhīci his former head, of human form, and protected him. This part of the legend (story) is told in the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya.

अश्वस्य शीर्ष्णा वां ईं यदुवाच-युष्मत् सामर्थ्येन प्रतिहितेन शिरसा युवाभ्यां इमां मधुविद्यां यदा च उक्तर्वा । (*aśvasya śīrṣṇā vāṃ īṃ yaduvāca-yuṣmat sāmarthyena pratihitena śirasā yuvābhyāṃ imāṃ madhuvidyāṃ yadā ca uktarvā |*) The meaning is: "I shall proclaim here your power, who brought and attached the horse's head, and the fact that the sage Dadhīci taught the madhuvidyā, and that you received instruction from him through the exchange of heads and protected him." Thus it is described in this ṛk.

The sage called Dadhyañc or Dadhīci is well known in the Purāṇas and elsewhere. The commentators tell a story here. While the sage Dadhyañc, the son of one named Atharvan, was alive, the asuras, frightened by the sight of him, had fled. After he died and went to heaven, the asuras fearlessly spread over the whole earth. Then Indra, unable to fight those asuras, searched for that sage. It came to be known that the sage had gone to heaven. Then Indra asked those who were near whether any remnant of the limbs of that sage remained; they said that only the skull of the horse's head, with which Dadhīci had once taught the madhuvidyā to the Aśvin deities, remained, and that where it was no one knew. Then Indra told them to search for it, and they searched and brought it, found lying in the lake called Śaryaṇāvat near Kurukṣetra. Indra conquered the asuras with the bones of that skull; that is, nine times ninety, eight hundred, ten times the magical net of the asuras

### Printed p. 111

made futile. In explaining here the number nine times ninety, the commentator says: In order to conquer the gods who dwell in the three worlds, the asuras at first had three kinds of divisions of magic. Since each of them was threefold, as present, past and future, there were nine kinds in all. Again, those nine magics, joined with the three powers beginning with enthusiasm, became 9 × 2 = 18 **NOTE**. Again, joined with the three qualities beginning with sattva, they became 27 × 3 = 81. Since these eighty-one magics are practised in the ten directions, they became 81 × 10 = 810 in all, or nine times ninety, 9 × 90 = 810. Indra made futile the magical nets of the asuras, who were of the form of Vṛtra, in this way; so the commentator explains. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-80-16)

Skandasvāmin tells another legend. In former times there were asuras called Kālakañjas. They became powerful, killed the gods and caused them great trouble. Then the gods went near Brahmā and prayed that he should tell them a means of destroying these asuras. Brahmā said, "O gods, there is a sage named Vadhyañc **NOTE**. Go near him. He will tell you a suitable means for the destruction of the asuras." The gods, as Brahmā had said, went near the sage Dadhīci and submitted that Brahmā had sent them. That sage, knowing by yogic vision the purpose for which they had come, gave up his life-breaths in yogic trance. Then Indra destroyed the asuras with the bones of that sage. Skandasvāmin says that it is just this matter that is described in this ṛk. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-80-16)

It is the tradition that the head of the sage Dadhīci was like a horse's head and that it had been given to him by the Aśvin deities; it is stated in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-84-13 that after the sage Dadhīci died, Indra searched for his head, which was like a horse's head, and found it behind a mountain, in the lake called Śaraṇāvatī.

Regarding how the sage Dadhīci came to have a horse's head, Skandasvāmin tells an earlier legend. Indra taught to the sage Dadhīci, the son of Atharvan, the form of Brahman called Madhu **NOTE**, and told him not to tell it to anyone, and that if he did tell it, his head would fall off. After some time the Aśvin deities came near that sage Dadhīci and asked him to explain the form of Brahman that Indra had told him. The sage Dadhīci said to them that Indra had said that if he told them this matter, his head would fall off; for fear of that he would not tell it. The Aśvin deities said, "Do not worry about that; if your head falls off, we shall give you another head." Dadhīci agreed to it and told them. Thereupon the head of the sage Dadhīci fell off, and the Aśvin deities cut off a horse's head, brought it and attached it

### Printed p. 112

to the body of the sage Dadhīci. Because of that, the head of the sage Dadhīci is like a horse's head, as already described. The sage called Kakṣīvān describes this matter in the ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-116-12 and 1-117-22.

The name of this sage is recited in the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā in the ṛks 1-80-16; 1-84-13; 1-84-14; 1-116-12; 1-117-22; 1-119-9 and others; and in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā 5-1-4-4; 5-6-6-3; in the Kāṭhaka Saṃhitā 19-4; in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa 4-1-5-18; 6-4-2-3; 14-1-1-18; 14-1-1-20; 14-1-1-25; 14-1-4-13; in the Bṛhadāraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2-5-22; 4-5-28 and other places.

The Western scholar Wilson writes as follows on this matter.

We have here rather obscure allusions to a legend which was probably afterwards modified by the Puranas in which the name also occurs as Dadhicha. In the Mahabharata, Vana Parva, Vol. I, it is merely related, that the gods, being oppressed by the kalakeya Asuras, solicited from the sage Dadhicha his bones, which he gave them, and from which Twashtri fabricated the thunderbolt with which Indra slew Vritra and routed the Asuras. The legend of the text differs from this : Indra--having taught the sciences called Pravargya vidya and Madhu-vidya to Dadhyanch, threatened that he would cut off his head if ever he taught them to any one else : the Aswins prevailed upon him, nevertheless, to teach them the prohibited knowledge, and, to evade Indra's threat, took off the head of the sage, replacing it by that of a horse: Indra, apprized of Dadhyanch's breach of faith, struck off his equine head with the thunderbolt : on which the Aswins restored to him his own. The Pravargya vidya is said to imply certain verses of the Rik, Yajur, and Sama Vedas, and the Madhu-vidya the Brahmana.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒त्याया॑तमश्विना ति॒रो विश्वा॑ अ॒हं सना॑ ।  
> दस्रा॒ हिर॑ण्यवर्तनी॒ सुषु॑म्ना॒ सिंधु॑वाहसा॒ माध्वी॒ मम॑ ॒ ॑
> श्रुतं हवं ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 113

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अतिआयातं । अश्विना । तिरः । विश्वाः । अहं । सना ।  
> दस्रा । हिरण्यवर्तनी इति हिरण्यवर्तनी । सुसुम्मा । सिंधुवाहसा । माध्वी इति ।  
> मम । श्रुतं । हवं ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना अत्यायातं । सर्वान्यजमानानतिक्रम्यागच्छतं । यथाहमृषिर्विश्वाः
> सर्वा अस्मद्विरोधिप्रजाः सना सदा तिरस्करोमि । अथवाहं तिरः । तिरः सत इति
> प्राप्तस्येति निरुक्तं । ३-२० । प्राप्ता विश्वाः सर्वाः क्रिया युष्मदीया अनुतिष्ठेयमित्यर्थः । सना सनातनौ दस्रा शत्रूणामुपक्षपयितार् हिरण्यवर्तनी हिरण्यरथौ
> सुषुम्ना सुधन् सिंधुवाहसा नदीनां प्रवाहयितार् वृष्टिप्रेरणेन । तादृशौ
> युवामत्यायातं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, come, passing by all the other sacrificers, so that I, the seer, may always overcome all the offspring hostile to us. Or else: "tiraḥ" has the sense of "attained" (Ni. 3-20); the meaning is that I may perform all your rites that have come to me. The two are everlasting; they are destroyers of enemies; they ride in a golden chariot; they have good wealth; they make the rivers flow by impelling the rain. Such as you are, come passing by the others.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दस्रा (*dasrā*)—destroyers of enemies
- हिरण्यवर्तनी (*hiraṇyavartanī*)—who have a chariot made of gold
- सुषुम्ना (*suṣumnā*)—who have excellent wealth
- सिंधुवाहसा (*siṃdhuvāhasā*)—who impel the flow of the rivers
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- अत्यायातं (*atyāyātaṃ*)—leaving all the other sacrificers, favour me alone
- अहं (*ahaṃ*)—I
- सना (*sanā*)—always
- विश्वाः (*viśvāḥ*)—all adversaries
- तिरः (*tiraḥ*)—having rejected (help me to scorn them)
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvins, perfect in the madhuvidyā
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—praise (invocation)
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, destroyers of enemies, who have a golden chariot, excellent wealth, and who impel the flow of the rivers, leave all the other sacrificers and favour me alone. Help me so that I may always reject and drive far away all adversaries. O Aśvins, perfect in the madhuvidyā, listen to my praise.

##### English translation
Passing by (other worshippers), come, Aswins, hither, so that I may ever overcome all (adversaries): Dasras, riding in a golden chariot,

### Printed p. 114

distributors of wealth, propellers of rivers, masters of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ नो॒ रत्ना॑नि॒ बिभ्र॑ता॒वश्वि॑ना॒ गच्छ॑तं यु॒वं ।  
> रुद्रा॒ हिर॑ण्यवर्तनी जुषा॒णा वा॑जिनीवसू॒ माध्वी॒ मम॑श्रुतं॒ हवं॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । नः । रत्नानि । बिभ्रतौ । अश्विना । गच्छतं । युवं ।  
> रुद्रा । हिरण्यवर्तनी इति हिरण्यवर्तनी । जुषाणा । वाजिनीवसू इति वाजिनीऽवसू।  
> माध्वी इति । मम । श्रुतं । हवं ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना नोऽस्मभ्यं रत्नानि रमणीयानि धनानि बिभ्रत् धारयंत् युवं
> युवां नोऽस्मान्वा आ गच्छतं । हे रुद्रा रुद्रपुत्रौ स्तुत्या वा हे वाजिनीवसू
> वाजिनधन् युवां हिरण्यवर्तनी हिरण्यरथौ जुषाणा यज्ञं सेवमान् संतावा
> गच्छतमिति । माध्वी इत्यादि गतं ॥
> ॥ श्रीराम ॥
> रुद्रा—रुद्रपुत्ररू हिरण्यवर्तनी—सुवर्णनिर्मितवाद रथवुळ्ळवरू, जुषाणा—
> यज्ञवन्नु सेविसुववरू वाजिनीवसू—अन्नसंपद्युक्तरू आद अश्विना—ऎलै अश्विनी
> देवतॆगळे । युवं—नीवु नः—नमगोस्कर रत्नानि—रत्नगळन्नु ! बिभ्रत्—हॊंदि
> दवरागि ' आ गच्छतं—नम्मल्लिगॆ दयमाडिरि माध्वी—मधुविद्यापारंगतराद ऎलै अश्विनी
> देवतॆगळे , मम—नन्न हवं—स्तुतियन्नु (आह्वानवन्नु) । श्रुतं—केळिरि.

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, you two, bearing for us delightful wealth, come to us. O Rudras: sons of Rudra, or else those worthy of praise. O you who have wealth of food, you who ride in a golden chariot, taking pleasure in the sacrifice, serving it, come. The meaning of "mādhvī" and the rest has been given.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रुद्रा (*rudrā*)—sons of Rudra
- हिरण्यवर्तनी (*hiraṇyavartanī*)—who have a chariot made of gold
- जुषाणा (*juṣāṇā*)—who partake of the sacrifice
- वाजिनीवसू (*vājinīvasū*)—who are endowed with the wealth of food
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- रत्नानि (*ratnāni*)—jewels
- बिभ्रत् (*bibhrat*)—having obtained
- आ गच्छतं (*ā gacchataṃ*)—favour us with your presence
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvin deities, perfect in the madhuvidyā
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—praise (invocation)
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen

### Printed p. 115

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, sons of Rudra, who have a chariot made of gold, who partake of the sacrifice and are endowed with the wealth of food, having obtained delightful riches for us, favour us with your presence. O Aśvin deities, perfect in the madhuvidyā, listen to my praise.

##### English translation
Come, Aswins, bring for us precious treasures: Rudras, riding in a golden chariot, propitiated (by sacrifice), affluent with food, masters of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

रुद्रा (*rudrā*)—How the Aśvin deities are sons of Rudra has not been explained by the commentator.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सु॒ष्टुभो॑ वां वृषण्वसू॒ रथे॒ वाणी॒च्याहि॑ता ।  
> उ॒त वां॑ ककु॒हो मृ॒गः पृक्षः॑ कृणोति वापु॒षो माध्वी॒ ॑ ॒॑
> मनु श्रुतं हवं ॥४॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सुस्तुभः । नां । वृषण्वसू इति वृषण्वसू । रथे । वाणीची । आहिता ।  
> उत । नां । ककुहः । मृगः । पृक्षः । कृणोति । वापुषः । माध्वी इति । मम । श्रुतं ।  
> हवं ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वृषण्वसू वर्षधनौ वर्षितारौ ना वसूनां हे अश्विन् सुष्टुभः सुस्तोतुर्मम वाणीची वाग्रूपा स्तुतिर्वां युवयो रथ आहिता । स्थापिता । आस्माभिः
> कृतेत्यर्थः । उतापि च वां युवाभ्यां ककुहो महान् मृगोमृगयिता वापुषो
> वपुष्मान्यजमानः पृक्षोऽन्नं हविः कृणोति । करोति । तस्मान्मम हवं श्रुतमिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O showerers of riches, O Aśvins, the praise in the form of speech of me, the good praiser, is placed on your chariot; the meaning is that it has been made by us. Moreover, the great seeker, the embodied sacrificer, makes for you the food, the oblation. Therefore hear my invocation.

### Printed p. 116

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृषण्वसू (*vṛṣaṇvasū*)—O Aśvin deities, who provide riches
- सुष्टुभः (*suṣṭubhaḥ*)—of me, the excellent praiser
- वाणीची (*vāṇīcī*)—the praise
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- रथे (*rathe*)—addressed to the chariot
- आहिता (*āhitā*)—is made and recited
- उत (*uta*)—and
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- मृगः (*mṛgaḥ*)—the one who seeks
- ककुहं (*kakuhaṃ*)—who has special qualities
- वापुषः (*vāpuṣaḥ*)—who has a human body, the sacrificer
- पृक्षः (*pṛkṣaḥ*)—the oblation
- कृणोति (*kṛṇoti*)—offers
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvin deities, endowed with the madhuvidyā
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—invocation
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, who provide riches, the praise of me, the excellent praiser, is made and recited addressed to your chariot. And the sacrificer, who seeks you alone, who has special qualities and a human body, offers the oblation. O Aśvin deities, endowed with the madhuvidyā, listen to my invocation in the form of praise.

##### English translation
Showerers of wealth, the praise of your worshipper is addressed to your chariot; (to it), as well as to you, does this distinguished, devoted, embodied (adorer) offer sacrificial food: masters of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बो॒धिन्म॑नसा र॒थ्ये॑षि॒रा ह॑वन॒श्रुता॑ ।  
> विभि॒श्चवा॑नमश्विना॒ नि या॑थो॒ अद्व॑याविनं॒ माध्वी॒ मम॑ ॒ ॑
> श्रुतं हवं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बोधित्मनसा । रथ्या । इषिरा । हवनश्रुता ।  
> विभिः । च्यवानं । अश्विना । नि । याथः । अद्वयाविनं । माध्वी इति । मम । श्रुतं ।  
> हवं ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 117

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना बोधिन्मनसा बुध्यमानमनस्कौ रथ्या रथस्वामिनाविषिरा शीघ्रगंता रौ
> सर्वैर्गंतव्यौ वा हवनश्रुता हवनस्य स्तुतेः श्रोतारौ युवां विभिरश्चैश्चवानमृषिमद्वयाविनं मायारहितं नि याथः । यथा तम्मृषिं प्राप्तवंतौ तद्वन्मां प्रति
> पथि नि याथः । नितरामागच्छतं । प्र च्यवानाज्जु जुरुषो वव्रिं । ऋग्वे. ५-७४-५
> इति ह्युक्तं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, you whose minds are attentive, who are lords of the chariot, swift-going or else to be approached by all, who hear the invocation, that is, the praise: you go with your horses to the ṛṣi Cyavāna, who is free of duplicity. Just as you came to that ṛṣi, so come on the way to me; come down to me fully. Sāyaṇa says that this is also stated in "प्र च्यवानाज्जु जुरुषो वव्रिं (*pra cyavānājju juruṣo vavriṃ*)" (Ṛgve. 5-74-5)**NOTE****GLOSS**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- बोधिन्मनसा (*bodhinmanasā*)—having minds that understand our praise
- रथ्या (*rathyā*)—being lords of the chariot
- इषिरा (*iṣirā*)—swift-going
- हवनश्रुता (*havanaśrutā*)—who hear our invocation
- विभिः (*vibhiḥ*)—with your horses
- अद्वयाविनं (*advayāvinaṃ*)—who, single-minded, was meditating on you alone
- च्यवानं (*cyavānaṃ*)—to the sage Cyavana
- नि याथः (*ni yāthaḥ*)—you set out
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—invocation
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, having minds that understand our praise, being lords of the chariot, swift-going, hearers of our invocation, you set out with your horses to the sage Cyavana, who, single-minded, was meditating on you alone. O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā, listen to my invocation.

##### English translation
With mind attentive (to praise), riding in cars, swif-tmoving, listening to invocations, you hastened with your steeds to the single-purposed Chyavana : masters of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The sage Cyavana was old. His body was full of the marks of old age, such as grey hair and a wrinkled body. Because of this, the sons and others of this sage had abandoned him. Then the sage Cyavana praised the Aśvin deities and pleased them, lost his old age through them and obtained youth: this history is told here by this Ṛk. Skandasvāmin has told this history in a different way. This history is seen in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa.

### Printed p. 118

Here, in the Śatapatha (Śa.
