Brā. 4-1-5): अधीतमितिहासमाचक्षते । शर्यातो नाम राजा संक्रीडद्भिः कृतापराधाय कुपिताय जीर्णाय च्यवनाय प्रत्यापत्त्यर्थं सुकन्यां नाम कन्यकां दुहितरं ददौ । तां तत्रासीनां दृष्ट्वा अश्विनौ पुनः पुनरूचतुः । किं किं जीर्णीकृतरूपं कामयसे । आवां संभजेति । सा तौ प्रत्युवाच । यस्मै मां पिता अदात् नाहं तं जीवंतं हास्यामीति । तदृषिज्ञात्वा सुकन्यामुवाच । यदि त्वामेतौ पुनरेवं ब्रूयातां ततस्त्वमेनौ प्रतिब्रूयाः । आसर्वौ वसुसमृद्धौ संतौ पतिं मे निंदथ इति। तौ यदि त्वां प्रतिब्रूयातां केनासर्वौ वसुसमृद्धौ चेति तत एनौ प्रतिब्रूयाः पतिं मे पुनः युवानं कुरुतं । अथ नां वक्ष्यामीति । सा तौ तथोवाच । तामश्विन् प्रत्यूचतुः एतं ह्रदमवतरतु आतो यत् कामयते तेन वयसा युक्त उत्तरिष्यतीति । स ऋषिस्तं ह्रदमवतीयोत्ततार युवाभूत्वेति । तदेतदितिहासेनोच्यते जुजुरुषो नासत्येत्यादिना ॥ (*adhītamitihāsamācakṣate | śaryāto nāma rājā saṃkrīḍadbhiḥ kṛtāparādhāya kupitāya jīrṇāya cyavanāya pratyāpattyarthaṃ sukanyāṃ nāma kanyakāṃ duhitaraṃ dadau | tāṃ tatrāsīnāṃ dṛṣṭvā aśvinau punaḥ punarūcatuḥ | kiṃ kiṃ jīrṇīkṛtarūpaṃ kāmayase | āvāṃ saṃbhajeti | sā tau pratyuvāca | yasmai māṃ pitā adāt nāhaṃ taṃ jīvaṃtaṃ hāsyāmīti | tadṛṣijñātvā sukanyāmuvāca | yadi tvāmetau punarevaṃ brūyātāṃ tatastvamenau pratibrūyāḥ | āsarvau vasusamṛddhau saṃtau patiṃ me niṃdatha iti| tau yadi tvāṃ pratibrūyātāṃ kenāsarvau vasusamṛddhau ceti tata enau pratibrūyāḥ patiṃ me punaḥ yuvānaṃ kurutaṃ | atha nāṃ vakṣyāmīti | sā tau tathovāca | tāmaśvin pratyūcatuḥ etaṃ hradamavataratu āto yat kāmayate tena vayasā yukta uttariṣyatīti | sa ṛṣistaṃ hradamavatīyottatāra yuvābhūtveti | tadetaditihāsenocyate jujuruṣo nāsatyetyādinā ‖*) (Śa. Brā. 4-1-5)

The king named Śaryāti, angered because his sons were playing, gave his daughter named Sukanyā in marriage to the old sage Cyavana. When Sukanyā was living in the hermitage of the sage Cyavana, the Aśvin deities came there and said to her many times: "Abandon this old husband and marry us." To this she said: "My father gave me to this sage. As long as the one who married me is alive, I shall not abandon him." The sage Cyavana heard this and told her: "When they question you again in this way, ask them: 'Though you are endowed with every prosperity, what is the reason that you reproach my husband?' In answer they will ask you: 'How are we rich in wealth?' Then tell them: 'Give my husband youth; afterwards I shall tell you.'" Thus the sage Cyavana taught her what to say to them. Sukanyā spoke to the Aśvin deities as the sage Cyavana had said. Hearing her words, the Aśvin deities said: "If your husband descends into this pool and bathes, your wish will be fulfilled; your husband will become possessed of youth." Having heard the words spoken by the Aśvin deities, he bathed in the pool they had named and came up a young man. This history is told by the Ṛk beginning "jujuruṣo nāsatya". The matter of the Aśvin deities making the old sage Cyavana young is described in many places in the Ṛgveda:

### Printed p. 119

युवं च्यवानं जरसोऽमुमुक्तं नि पेदव ऊहधुराशुमश्वं । (*yuvaṃ cyavānaṃ jaraso 'mumuktaṃ ni pedava ūhadhurāśumaśvaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-71-5)**GLOSS**

प्र च्यवानाज्जु जुरुषो वव्रिमत्कं न मुंचथः । (*pra cyavānājju juruṣo vavrimatkaṃ na muṃcathaḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-74-5)**GLOSS**

निष्पौग्र्यं पारयथः समुद्रात्पुनश्च त्वापानं चक्रधुर्युवानं । (*niṣpaugryaṃ pārayathaḥ samudrātpunaśca tvāpānaṃ cakradhuryuvānaṃ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-118-6)**GLOSS**

युवं च्यवानं सनयं यथा रथं पुनर्युनानं चरथाय तक्षथुः । (*yuvaṃ cyavānaṃ sanayaṃ yathā rathaṃ punaryunānaṃ carathāya takṣathuḥ |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-39-4)**GLOSS**

This matter is mentioned in these Ṛks, and in many other Ṛks such as Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-74-5 and 7-68-6.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आवां॑ नरा मनो॒युजोऽश्वा॑सः पुषि॒तप्स॑वः ।  
> वयो॑ वहंतु पी॒तये॑ स॒ह सु॒म्नेभि॑रश्विना॒ माध्वी॒ मम॑ ॒ ॑
> श्रुतं हवं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । वां । नरा । मनःयुजः । आश्वासः । प्रुषितप्सवः ।  
> वयः । वहंतु । पीतये । सह । सुम्नेभिः । अश्विना । माध्वी इति । मम । श्रुतं ।  
> हवं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे नरा कर्मणां नेतारौ हे अश्विनौ वां युवां मनोयुजो
> मनोमात्रेण युज्यमानाः । सुशिक्षिता इत्यर्थः । प्रुषितप्पवो विचित्ररूपा वयः
> शीघ्रगंतारोऽश्वासोऽश्वा आ वहंतु पीतये सोमपानाय । सुम्नेभिः सह
> अस्मभ्यं देयैः सुखैस्तत्साधनैर्धनादिभिर्वा सह वहंतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Naras, leaders of rites, O Aśvins, let your horses, which are yoked by mere thought, that is, are well trained, of variegated form, swift-going, bring you for the drinking of Soma. Let them bring you together with comforts, that is, with the pleasures to be given to us, or with wealth and the like, which are the means of them.

### Printed p. 120

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- नरा (*narā*)—leaders
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- मनोयुजः (*manoyujaḥ*)—those that join the chariot by mere resolve
- पुषिताप्सवः (*puṣitāpsavaḥ*)—of wondrous form
- वयः (*vayaḥ*)—swift-going
- आश्वासः (*āśvāsaḥ*)—horses
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- सुम्मेभिः सह (*summebhiḥ saha*)—with wealth that is to be given to us and gives comfort
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for the drinking of Soma
- आ वहंतु (*ā vahaṃtu*)—let them bring
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—invocation
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, leaders, let your horses, which join the chariot by mere resolve, are of wondrous form and are swift-going, bring you for the drinking of Soma, together with wealth that is to be given to us and gives comfort. O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā, listen to my invocation.

##### English translation
Aswins, leaders (of rites), may your horses, harnessed at will, of wondrous beauty, and of rapid course, bring you hither with good gifts to drink (of the proffered beverage): masters of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अश्वि॑ना॒वेह ग॑च्छतं॒ नास॑त्या॒ मा वि वे॑नतं ।  
> ति॒रश्चि॑दर्य॒या परि॑ व॒र्तिया॑र्तमदाभ्या॒ माध्वी॒ मम॑ श्रुतं॒  ॑
> हवं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्वन् । आ । इह । गच्छतं । नासत्या । मा । वि । वेनतं ।  
> तिरः । चित् । अर्यया । परि । वर्तिः । यातं । अदाभ्या । माध्वी इति । मम ।  
> श्रुतं । हवं ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 121

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनाविहास्मिन्यज्ञ आ गच्छतं । हे नासत्या मा वि वेनतं । विगतकामौ
> मा भवतं ॥ वेनतिः कांतिकर्मा ॥ हे आदाभ्याहिंस्यौ पूज्यौ आर्ययार्यौ
> स्वामिनौ युवां ॥ सुपां सुलुगित्यादिना सुपो याजादेशः ॥ तिरश्चित् अंतर्हिताद्दूरदेशादपि युवां वर्तिरस्मद्यज्ञगृहं परि यातं । आगच्छतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, come here, to this sacrifice. O Nāsatyas, do not be without desire: do not become ones whose desire has gone. "Venati" is a word for a verb of desire. O inviolable, worshipful, masters, you two: from a distant place, even one hidden from sight, come to our house of sacrifice, that is, approach it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विनौ (*aśvinau*)—O Aśvin deities
- इह (*iha*)—to this sacrifice
- आ गच्छतं (*ā gacchataṃ*)—come
- नासत्या (*nāsatyā*)—O Nāsatyas
- मा वि वेनतं (*mā vi venataṃ*)—do not be unwilling to come to us
- अदाभ्या (*adābhyā*)—who are unharmed
- आरया (*ārayā*)—and worshipful lords, you
- तिरश्चित् (*tiraścit*)—even from a distant place hidden from the eye
- वर्तिः (*vartiḥ*)—to our house of sacrifice
- परि यातं (*pari yātaṃ*)—come
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvins, skilled in the madhuvidyā
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—invocation
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, come to this sacrifice. O Nāsatyas, do not be unwilling to come to us. You who are unharmed and are worshipful lords, come to our house of sacrifice even from a distant place hidden from the eye. O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā, listen to my invocation.

##### English translation
Aswins, come hither : Nasatyas, be not unpropitious : invincible lords, come from hidden (regions) to our sacrificial hall : master of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒स्मिन्न्य॒ज्ञे अ॑दाभ्या जरि॒तारं॑ शुभस्पती ।  
> अ॒व॒स्युम॑श्विना यु॒वं गृ॒णंत॒मुप॑ भूषथो॒ माध्वी॒ मम॑ ॒ ॑
> श्रुतं हवं ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 122

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्मिन् । यज्ञे । अदाभ्या । जरितारं । शुभः । पती इति ।  
> अवस्युं । अश्विना । युवं । गृणंतं । उप । भूषथः । माध्वी इति । मम । श्रुतं  
> हवं ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे आदाभ्या अहिंस्यौ हे शुभस्पती उदकस्य स्वामिनावश्विना युवं युवामस्मिन्यज्ञे जरितारं स्तोतारमवस्युं गृणंतं स्तुवंतं मामुप भूषथः ।
> उपप्रापुतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O inviolable, that is, unharmable ones, O Śubhaspatis, lords of water, O Aśvins: you at this sacrifice approach me, the praiser, who am eager for help and who extol you; that is, come near.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आदाभ्या (*ādābhyā*)—who are free from harm
- शुभस्पती (*śubhaspatī*)—who are lords of water
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- अस्मिन् यज्ञे (*asmin yajñe*)—in this sacrifice
- जरितारं (*jaritāraṃ*)—the maker of praise
- गृणंतं (*gṛṇaṃtaṃ*)—the reciter of praise, who extols you
- अवस्युं (*avasyuṃ*)—named Avasyu, me
- उप भूषथः (*upa bhūṣathaḥ*)—favour
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—praise
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, who are free from harm and are lords of water, favour me in this sacrifice, me who am the maker of praise and extol you with praises. O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā, listen to my praise.

##### English translation
Invincible Aswins, lords of water, favour Avasyu, glorifying you at this sacrifice : masters of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अभू॑दु॒षा रुश॑त्पशु॒राग्नि र॑धाय्य॒त्वियः॑ ।  
> आयो॑जि वां वृषण्वसू॒ रथो॑ दस्रा॒वम॑र्त्यो॒ माध्वी॒ मम॑ ॒ ॑
> श्रुतं हवं ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 123

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभूत् । उषाः । रुशत्पशुः । आ । अग्निः । अधायि । ऋत्वियः ।  
> अयोजि । वां । वृवण्वसू इति वृषण्वसू । रथः । दस्त्रौ । अमत्यर्घः । माध्वी इति ।  
> मम । श्रुतं । हवं ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उषा व्युष्टिरभूत् । अग्निश्च रुशत्पशुर्दिप्तपशुमान् । प्रकाशितहविरित्यर्थः ।
> अथवा पशवः किरणाः । रुशद्रश्मिः सन् आधायि । वेद्यामधीयत अग्निराहवनीय
> ऋत्विय ऋत् काले भवः । हे वृषण्वसू प्रदत्तधनौ हे दस्रौ नां युवयोरमर्त्योऽमरणधर्मा ध्रुवो रथोऽयोजि । अश्वैर्युक्तो भवत्त्व्यर्थः ॥ छंदसि
> लुङ्लज्जिट इत्यनेकेष्वर्थेषु लुज् भवति। इह प्रार्थनायां॥ माध्वी इत्यादि गतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The dawn, that is, the break of day, has come. Agni too is रुशत्पशुः (*ruśatpaśuḥ*): he has shining animals, that is, his oblation is made bright. Or else, "animals" are rays: having shining rays, he has been placed on the altar. This is the Āhavanīya fire, born at the proper season. O showerers of wealth, who give wealth, O Dasras, your immortal chariot, whose nature is not to die, and which is steady, has been yoked; the meaning is that it should be yoked with horses. Here the aorist is used in the sense of a prayer. The meaning of "mādhvī" and the rest has been given.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उषाः (*uṣāḥ*)—the goddess Dawn
- अभूत् (*abhūt*)—has appeared
- ऋत्वियः (*ṛtviyaḥ*)—who appears in accordance with the season
- रुशत्पशुः (*ruśatpaśuḥ*)—who has a shining flame; Agni
- अधायि (*adhāyi*)—has been established on the altar
- वृषण्वसू (*vṛṣaṇvasū*)—givers of wealth
- दस्रौ (*dasrau*)—who have a form fair to see, O Aśvin deities
- वां (*vāṃ*)—your
- अमत्यर्‍यः (*amatyaryaḥ*)—indestructible
- रथः (*rathaḥ*)—chariot
- अयोजि (*ayoji*)—is yoked with horses
- माध्वी (*mādhvī*)—O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā
- मम (*mama*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—invocation
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—listen, giving ear

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The goddess Dawn has appeared. Agni, who appears in accordance with the season and has a shining flame, has been established on the altar. O Aśvin deities, givers of wealth, who have a form fair to see, your steady chariot is yoked with horses. O Aśvin deities, skilled in the madhuvidyā, give ear to my invocation.

##### English translation
The dawn has come: the Agni of the season, blazing with the oblation, has been placed (upon the altar): showerers of wealth, subduers of foes, your immortal chariot has been harnessed: masters of mystic lore, hear my invocation.

### Printed p. 124

## Sūkta 76 (Maṇḍala 5)

**NOTE**

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आ भात्यग्निरिति पंचर्चं चतुर्थं सूक्तं भौमस्यात्रेरार्षं त्रैष्टुभमाश्विनं।
> अत्रानुक्रमणिका । आ भाति पंचात्रिरिति ॥ प्रवर्ग्येऽभिष्टवार्धमिदं सूक्तं । सूत्रितं
> च । आ भात्यग्निर्ग्राणेव । आ. ४-६ । इति ॥ प्रातरनुवाक अश्विने क्रत्वा त्रैष्टुभे
> छंदस्याश्विनशस्त्रे चेदमादिके द्वे सूक्ते । सूत्यते हि । आ भात्यग्निरिति सूक्ते ।
> आ. ४-१५ । इति ॥ अपोर्यामे होतुरतिरिकोक्ष्य इदं सूक्तं । सूत्रितं च ।
> आ भात्यग्निः क्षेत्रस्य पतिना वयमिति परिधानीया । आ. ९-११ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta, beginning आ भात्यग्निः (*ā bhātyagniḥ*), is the fourth sūkta; it has five Ṛks. It belongs to Atri Bhauma as seer; its deity is the Aśvins and its metre is Triṣṭubh. The Anukramaṇikā says आ भाति पंचात्रिः (*ā bhāti paṃcātriḥ*). This sūkta is used in the Pravargya for the praise; and it is prescribed in the sūtra आ भात्यग्निर्ग्राणेव (*ā bhātyagnirgrāṇeva*) (Ā. 4-6). In the morning recitation and in the Aśvina-śastra, in the Triṣṭubh metre of the Aśvina rite, this and the next are the two sūktas; it is prescribed in the sūtra आ भात्यग्निरिति सूक्ते (*ā bhātyagniriti sūkte*) (Ā. 4-15). In the Aporyāma it is to be recited by the Hotṛ as the atiriktokthya; and the sūtra says आ भात्यग्निः क्षेत्रस्य पतिना वयमिति परिधानीया (*ā bhātyagniḥ kṣetrasya patinā vayamiti paridhānīyā*) (Ā. 9-11).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning आ भात्यग्निः (*ā bhātyagniḥ*), is the fourth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka; it contains five Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is a man named Atri Bhauma, who saw it in the maṇḍala; the deity is the Aśvins; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said आ भाति पंचात्रिः (*ā bhāti paṃcātriḥ*). The use of this sūkta in the sacrifice called Pravargya is for the praise; this is explained by the sūtra आ भात्यग्निर्ग्राणेव (*ā bhātyagnirgrāṇeva*) (Ā. 4-6) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra. For the Prātaranuvāka and for the Triṣṭubh mantras connected with the Aśvina rite, this sūkta and the next sūkta have a use; this is explained by the sūtra आ भात्यग्निरिति सूक्ते (*ā bhātyagniriti sūkte*) (Ā. 4-15) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra. And for the recitation of the mantra called atiriktokthya, which the Hotṛ recites in the sacrifice called Aporyāma, this sūkta has a use; this is explained by the sūtra आ भात्यग्निः क्षेत्रस्य पतिना वयमिति परिधानीया (*ā bhātyagniḥ kṣetrasya patinā vayamiti paridhānīyā*) (Ā. 9-11) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra.

##### Particulars of the sūkta

- Sūkta 76
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 76
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 17
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5
- Seer: अत्रिः (*atriḥ*)
- Deity: आश्विनौ (*āśvinau*)
- Metre: त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*)

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ भा॑त्य॒ग्नि रु॒षसा॒मनी॑क॒मुद्वि प्रा॑णां देव॒या वाचो॑ अस्थुः ।  
> अ॒र्वांचा॑ नू॒नं र॑थ्ये॒ह या॑तं पीपि॒वांस॑मश्विनाघ॒र्ममच्छ॑ ॥

### Printed p. 125

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । भाति । अग्निः । उषसां । अनेकं । उत् । पिप्राणां । देवयाः । वाचः । अस्तुः ।  
> अर्वांचा । नूनं । रथ्या । इह । यातं । पीप्सिवांसं । अश्विना । घर्म० । अच्छ ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उषसामनीकमनीकभूतः । अनीकं मुखं । उषसि प्रबुध्यमान इत्यर्थः ।
> तादृशोऽग्निरा भाति । दीप्यते । यद्वा । उषसां मुखमा भाति । दीपयति । उषःकाले ह्यग्नयः प्रतिबोध्यंते । किंच विप्राणां मेधाविनां स्तोत्कृणां देवया
> देवकामा वाचः स्तोत्राण्युदस्थुः । उत्तिष्ठंति । यस्मादेवं तस्मात् हे रथ्या
> रथस्वामिनावश्विनावर्वांचास्मदभिमुखांचन् नूनमद्यास्मिन्यागदिन इहा यातं । कं
> प्रति । पीपिवांसं स्वांगैः परिवृढं घर्मं प्रदीप्तं यज्ञं । यद्वा । पीपिवांसमाप्यायितं वसतीवरीभिः क्षरद्रूपं सोमरसं । अथवा धृतादिना पीपिवांसं
> घर्मं प्रवर्ग्यमच्छाभिलक्ष्येह यातं । प्रवर्ग्येऽस्य सूक्तस्य विनियोगः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Agni, who is the face of the dawns, that is, who is awakened at dawn, shines; or else he lights up the face of the dawns, for at the time of dawn the fires are kindled. Moreover, the speeches, that is, the hymns, of the wise praisers, which long for the gods, have risen up. Since this is so, O Aśvins, lords of the chariot, turned toward us, come here today, to this day of the sacrifice. Come to what? To the घर्मं (*gharmaṃ*) that is kindled, the sacrifice, which is full with all its parts; or else to the Soma juice that has swollen, flowing with the vasatīvarī waters; or else, swollen with ghee and the like, to the Pravargya, the ghārma. The use of this sūkta is in the Pravargya.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्निः (*agniḥ*)—Agni
- उषसां (*uṣasāṃ*)—of the dawn goddesses
- अनेकं (*anekaṃ*)—the divine faces
- आ भाति (*ā bhāti*)—has made to shine
- विप्राणां (*viprāṇāṃ*)—of the wise poets
- देवयाः (*devayāḥ*)—which praise the gods
- वाचः (*vācaḥ*)—the hymns
- उत् अस्थुः (*ut asthuḥ*)—have shone forth
- रथ्या (*rathyā*)—who are lords of the chariot
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—in this sacrifice**NOTE**
- पीपिवांसं (*pīpivāṃsaṃ*)—which is full with all its materials
- घर्म० (*gharma0*)—which is kindled
- इह (*iha*)—to this sacrifice
- अच्छ अर्वांचा (*accha arvāṃcā*)—turned toward us
- यातं (*yātaṃ*)—come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Agni has appeared and has made the divine faces of the dawn goddesses shine. The wise poets have made and brought to light hymns that praise the gods. O Aśvin deities, lords of the chariot, on this day of the sacrifice come, turned toward us, to this sacrifice, which is full with all its materials and kindled.

##### English translation
Agni lights up the face of the dawns: the devout praisers of the pious have risen up : therefore, Aswins, lords of the chariot, descending, come hither to-day to the splendid sacrifice, perfect (in all its parts).

### Printed p. 126

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न सं॑स्कृ॒तं प्र मि॑मातो॒ गमि॒ष्ठांति॑ नू॒नम॒श्विनोप॑स्तु ते॒ह ।  
> दिवा॑भिपि॒त्वेऽव॒साग॑मिष्ठा॒ प्रत्यव॑र्तिं दा॒शुषे॒ शंभ॑विष्ठा ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । संस्कृतं । प्र । मिमातः । गमिष्ठा । अंति । नूनं । अश्विना । उपस्तुता । इह ।  
> दिवा । अभिपित्वे । अवसा । आगमिष्ठा । प्रति । अवर्तिं । दाशुषे । शंभविष्ठा ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अश्विनौ संस्कृतं घर्मं न प्र मिमितः । न हिंसां । किंतु अंत्यंतिके
> घर्मसमीपे नूनमिदानीमिह यज्ञे गमिष्णा गंतृतमावश्विनाश्विनावुपस्तुतोपस्तुतौ भवतः । दिवाभिपित्वे दिवसस्याभिपतने प्रातःकालेवसा रक्षणेन सहागमिष्ठागंतृतमौ । अवर्तिं प्रति द्वंद्वभूत् । वर्तिर्जीवनं । तदभावोऽवर्तिः । तद्रहितमन्नं
> यथा भवति तथागंतृतमौ । आगत्य च दाशुषे हविर्दत्तवते यजमानाय शंभविषा
> सुखस्य भावयितारौ भवतां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Aśvins do not diminish the perfected ghārma; they do not harm it. Rather, near it, now, here in the sacrifice, may they, O Aśvins, who are most inclined to come, be praised. At the break of the day, that is, at the morning time, may you be most inclined to come with protection. Toward अवर्तिं (*avartiṃ*)**NOTE**: "varti" is livelihood, and "avarti" is its absence; come so that food is free of it. And having come, be producers of happiness for the sacrificer who has given the oblation.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- संस्कृतं (*saṃskṛtaṃ*)—this ghārma, which has been prepared and made ready
- न प्र मिमीतः (*na pra mimītaḥ*)—do not destroy
- इह अंति (*iha aṃti*)—in this part of the sacrifice
- नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—now
- गमिष्ठा (*gamiṣṭhā*)—having come
- उपस्तुता (*upastutā*)—be worthy of praise
- दिवा अभिपित्वे (*divā abhipitve*)—at the beginning of the day
- अवर्तिं प्रति (*avartiṃ prati*)—which checks poverty
- अवसा (*avasā*)—with protection
- आगमिष्ठा (*āgamiṣṭhā*)—having come
- दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)—to the sacrificer who gives the oblation
- शंभविष्ठा (*śaṃbhaviṣṭhā*)—be givers of happiness

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, do not destroy this ghārma (the sacrifice called Pravargya), which has been prepared and made ready. Come to this sacrifice and be worthy of our praise. At the beginning of the day, come with protection that checks poverty and be givers of happiness to the sacrificer who gives the oblation.

### Printed p. 127

##### English translation
Harm not, Aswins, the perfected (rite), but coming now most quickly, be glorified on this occasion : be present at the opening of the day, with protection against destitution, and be prompt to bestow happines upon the donor (of the offering).

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒ता या॑तं संग॒वे प्रा॒तरह्नो॑ म॒ध्यंदि॑न॒ उदि॑ता॒ सूर्य॑स्य ।  
> दिवा॒ नक्त॒मव॑सा॒ शंत॑मेन॒ नेदानीं॑ पी॒तिर॒श्विना त॑तान ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । आ । यातं । संगवे । प्रातः । अह्नः। मध्यंदिने । उत् इता । सूर्यस्य ।  
> दिवा । नक्तं । अवसा । शंतमेन। न। इदानीं । प्रीतिः । अश्विना । आ । ततान ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अहो द्वेधा त्रेधा पंचधा पंचदशधा समाना विभागाः संति । इह पंचधा
> विभाग आत्तः ॥ उतापि चा यातं । आगच्छतं । कदा । संगवे संगवकाले । संगच्छंते
> गावो दोहनभूमिं यस्मिन्काले स संगवः । रात्र्यपरकाले हि गावो वने हिमतृणानि भक्षयित्वा पुनर्दोहाय संगवे प्रतिनिवर्तंते । तथाः प्रातःकालेऽपि । तथा
> मध्यंदिनेऽह्नो मध्यकाले सूर्यस्योदितोदितावभ्युदयेऽत्यंतप्रवृद्धसमये।
> अपराह्न इत्यर्थः । एतत्सायाह्नस्याप्युपलक्षणं । न केवलमुक्तेष्टेव कालेषु
> किंतर्हि दिवा नक्तं सर्वदा शंतमेन सुखतमेनावसा रक्षणेन हविषा वा निमित्तेना
> यातं। किमर्थवागम्यते पूर्वमेवानैर्देवैः स्वीकृतत्वात् नेत्याह। इदानीमपि
> पीतिरितरदेवानां पानं ना ततान न तनोत्यश्विनाश्विनौ विहायेति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The day has equal divisions in two, three, five and fifteen ways; here the fivefold division is taken. "Utāpi ca": also come. When? At the time of saṃgava, the time when the cows gather at the milking place. For in the latter part of the night the cows eat the frosty grass in the forest and return at saṃgava to be milked again; so also in the morning. So also at midday, in the middle of the day, and at the sun's rising and rising, that is, at the time of the sun's greatest growth, which means the afternoon; this also indicates the evening. Not only at the times mentioned, but by day and by night, always, come with most pleasant protection, or on account of the oblation. For what purpose would you come, since the sacrifice had already been accepted before by the other gods? **NOTE** He says "no": even now the drinking of the other gods has not been extended, leaving the Aśvins aside; this is what is to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- संगवे (*saṃgave*)—at the time when the cows are milked
- प्रातः (*prātaḥ*)—at the morning time
- मध्यंदिने (*madhyaṃdine*)—at the midday time
- सूर्यस्य (*sūryasya*)—of the sun
- उदिता (*uditā*)—at the evening time, the time of the rising prosperity
- दिवा नक्तं (*divā naktaṃ*)—by day or by night, whichever time it may be
- शंतमेन (*śaṃtamena*)—full of happiness
- अवसा (*avasā*)—with protection
- आ यातं (*ā yātaṃ*)—come
- इदानीं (*idānīṃ*)—at this time of the sacrifice
- पीतिः (*pītiḥ*)—the invitation to the drinking of Soma
- न आ ततान (*na ā tatāna*)—has not been extended to any other deity, leaving the Aśvin deities aside (it has not been appointed)

### Printed p. 128

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, whether at the time when the cows are milked, at the morning time, at midday, or at the evening time, the time of the sun's rising prosperity, by day or by night, whichever time it may be, come with protection full of happiness. At this time of the sacrifice the invitation to the drinking of Soma has not been extended to any other deity, leaving the Aśvin deities aside.

##### English translation
Whether you come at the (milking time) of the cattle, at the dawn of day, at noon, when the sum is high, or by day or by night, (come) with felicitious protection : the drinking of the Soma has not now extended beyond the Aswins.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒दं हि वां॑ प्र॒दिवि॒ स्थान॒मोक॑ इ॒मे गृ॒हा अ॑श्विने॒दं दु॑रो॒णं ।  
> आ नो॑ दि॒वो बृ॑ह॒तः पर्व॑ता॒दाद्यो या॑त॒मिष॒मूर्जं॒  ॑
> वहंता ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इदं । हि । वां । प्रदिवि । स्थानं । ओकः । इमे । गृहाः। अश्विना । इदं । दुरोणं ।  
> आ । नः । दिवः । बृहतः । पर्वतात् । आ । अत्भ्यः । यातं। इषं। ऊर्जं। वहंता॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विना नां युवयोः प्रदिवि पुराणमिदं ह्योको निवासयोग्यं
> स्थानमुत्तरवेद्याख्यं । अथ सौमिकवेद्यभिप्रायेणाह । इमे गृहाः प्राचीनवंशा-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, this is your ancient dwelling from of old, a place fit for residence, called the northern altar. Then, with the Saumika altar in view, he says: "These houses, the eastern halls, …" (the sentence continues in the next window).

### Printed p. 129

> दयः । इदं दुरोणं देवयजनगृहं । नोऽस्मदर्थ० दिवो द्युलोकाद्बृहतः
> पर्वतात् पर्ववतो मेघादद्भ्योऽपां कारणादंतरिक्षादा यातं । आगच्छतं । किं
> तूष्णीं । न । इषमूर्जं चान्नं बलं च । बलसाधनमन्नं सारभूतमित्यर्थः ।
> तादृशं वहंता वहंत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"These houses, the eastern halls and the rest": this is the house of divine sacrifice, the dwelling. For us, from the sky, from the great mountain, that is, from the cloud, the cause of waters, from the atmosphere: come, that is, arrive. **NOTE** Bringing food and strength: strength means the power, and food is the means of power, the essential thing; bearing such a thing, come.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- इदं हि (*idaṃ hi*)—this very region
- नां (*nāṃ*)—to you
- प्रदिवि (*pradivi*)—ancient
- ओकः (*okaḥ*)—fit for residence
- स्थानं (*sthānaṃ*)—is the place
- इमे (*ime*)—these
- गृहाः (*gṛhāḥ*)—are the houses
- इदं (*idaṃ*)—this
- दुरोणं (*duroṇaṃ*)—is the place of sacrifice
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—great
- अद्भ्यः (*adbhyaḥ*)—filled with water
- पर्वतात् (*parvatāt*)—covered with clouds
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from the heavenly world
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- ऊर्जं (*ūrjaṃ*)—and strength
- वहंता (*vahaṃtā*)—bearing
- आ यातं (*ā yātaṃ*)—come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, this very region is for you an ancient place fit for residence. These are your houses, this is your place of sacrifice. From the great heavenly world, filled with water and covered with clouds, come for us, bearing food and strength.

##### English translation
This station, Aswins, is your ancient abode; these are your mansions, this your dwelling: come from the vast firmament, (overspread) by clouds (filled) with water, bringing to us food and strength.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सम॒श्विनो॒रव॑सा॒ नूत॑नेन मयो॒भुवा॑ सु॒प्रणी॑ती गमेम ।  
> आ नो॑ र॒यिं व॑हत॒मोत वी॒राना विश्वा॑न्यमृता॒ सौभ॑गानि ॥५॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं ! अश्विनोः । अवसा । नूतनेन । मयःभुवा । सुप्रनीती । गमेम ।  
> आ । नः । रयिं । वहतं । आ । उत । वीरान् । आ । विश्वानि । अमृता । सौभगानि ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

### Printed p. 130

> वयं सं गमेम संगच्छेमाश्विनोर्नूतनेनावसानन्यकृतेन रक्षणेन
> मयोभुवा सुखस्य भावयित्र्या सुप्रणीती सुप्रणीत्या सुष्टागमनेन च । अपि
> वैते उभे अवसो विशेषणे । उक्तलक्षणेन सं गमेम । नोऽस्मभ्यं रयिमा
> वहतं । उत वीरान् पुत्रादीना वहतं । हे अमृतामरणधर्माणौ विश्वानि सौभगानि
> सुभगत्वान्या वहतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May we be united with the new protection of the Aśvins, not made by another, which is the producer of happiness, and with their good guidance, that is, their good coming; or else both are qualifiers of the protection. Bring wealth to us, and bring heroes too, sons and the rest. O immortals, you whose nature is not to die, bring all good fortunes.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- अश्विनोः (*aśvinoḥ*)—of the Aśvin deities
- नूतनेन (*nūtanena*)—new
- मयोभुवा (*mayobhuvā*)—which brings happiness
- सुप्रणीता (*supraṇītā*)—which is excellent and a guide
- अवसा (*avasā*)—with protection
- सं गमेम (*saṃ gamema*)—may we be united
- अमृता (*amṛtā*)—O Aśvins, free from the nature of death
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- विश्वानि (*viśvāni*)—all
- सौभगानि (*saubhagāni*)—things that bring good fortune
- आ वहतं (*ā vahataṃ*)—bring
- रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth; provide it
- उत (*uta*)—and
- वीरान् (*vīrān*)—heroic sons and the rest; favour us with them

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May we be united with the new protection of the Aśvin deities, which brings happiness and is an excellent guide. O Aśvins, free from the nature of death, bring about for us all good fortunes. Provide wealth. Favour us with heroic sons and the rest.

##### English translation
May we be united with the Aswins by their special protection, which is the source of happiness and guide to good: bestow upon us, immortals, wealth and posterity. and all good things.

## Sūkta 77 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रातर्यावाणेति पंचर्चं पंचमं सूक्तमात्रेयं त्रैष्टुभमाश्विनं ।
> प्रातर्यावाणेति ह्यनुक्रांतं॥ प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोः पूर्वसूक्तेन सहोक्तो
> विनियोगः ॥ अप्पोर्यामे मैत्रावरुणातिरिक्तोक्ष्य इदमेव शस्यं । तथा च
> सूत्रितं । प्रातर्यावाणा क्षेत्रस्य पते मधुमंतमूर्मिं । आ. ९-११ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta, beginning प्रातर्यावाणा (*prātaryāvāṇā*), has five Ṛks and is the fifth sūkta; it is Ātreya, in Triṣṭubh metre, addressed to the Aśvins. The Anukramaṇikā has it as listed under प्रातर्यावाणेति (*prātaryāvāṇeti*). Its use, together with the previous sūkta, in the morning recitation and the Aśvina-śastra has already been stated. In the Aporyāma, at the atiriktokthya of the Maitrāvaruṇa, this alone is the recited piece; and so it is prescribed by the sūtra प्रातर्यावाणा क्षेत्रस्य पते मधुमंतमूर्मिं (*prātaryāvāṇā kṣetrasya pate madhumaṃtamūrmiṃ*) (Ā. 9-11).

### Printed p. 131

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning प्रातर्यावाणा (*prātaryāvāṇā*), is the fifth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It contains five Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta, who saw it in the maṇḍala, is Atri; the deity is the Aśvin deities; the metre is Triṣṭubh. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said, beginning with प्रातर्यावाणा (*prātaryāvāṇā*). The use of this sūkta for the Prātaranuvāka and for the recitation of the mantras of the Aśvina-śastra has been stated in the use of the preceding sūkta. For the mantras called atiriktokthya, which the priest called Maitrāvaruṇa recites in the sacrifice called Aptoryāma, this sūkta has a use; this is explained by the sūtra प्रातर्यावाणा क्षेत्रस्य पते मधुमंतमूर्मिं (*prātaryāvāṇā kṣetrasya pate madhumaṃtamūrmiṃ*) (Ā. 9-11) of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra.

##### Particulars of the sūkta

- Sūkta 77
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 77
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 18
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 5
- Seer: आत्रिः (*ātriḥ*)
- Deity: अश्विनौ (*aśvinau*)
- Metre: त्रिष्टप् (*triṣṭap*)

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रातर्यावाणा प्रथमा यजध्वंपुरा गृध्रादररुषः पिबातः।  
> प्रातर्हि यज्ञमश्विना दधाते प्र शंसंति कवयः पूर्वभाजः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रातःयावाना । प्रथमा । यजध्वं । पुरा । गृध्रात् । अररुषः । पिबातः ।  
> प्रातः । हि । यज्ञं । अश्विना । दधाते इति । प्र । शंसंति । कवयः । पूर्वभाजः ॥१॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मदीया ऋत्विजः प्रातर्यावाणा प्रातरेव यज्ञगंतार् प्रथमात
> एवेतरेभ्यो देवेभ्यः पूर्वभाविनौ प्रकृष्टतमौ वाश्विनौ यजध्वं । किमर्थं ।
> प्रथमयाग नियोग उच्यते । गृध्रादभिकांक्षतोऽररुषोऽदातु रक्षःप्रभृतेः
> पुरा पूर्वं पिबातः । पिबतां । अनागतौ कथं यष्टुं शक्यावित्यत आह । अश्विनाश्विनौ

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O my priests, worship the two Aśvins who come at dawn, who are first, that is, earlier than the other gods, or the most excellent. For what purpose? The command to sacrifice to them first is stated: let them drink before the greedy one who desires, the non-giver, the demon and the like. If they have not yet come, how can they be worshipped? Therefore he says: the Aśvins

### Printed p. 132

> प्रातर्हि यज्ञं दधाते । धारयतः । संभजतः । पूर्वभाजः पूर्वकालीनाः
> कवयोऽनूचाना ऋषयः प्रातरेवैत् प्र शंसंति । ये वा अनूचानास्ते कवयः ।
> ऐ. ब्रा. २-३८ । इति हि निगमः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

at dawn indeed bear the sacrifice, that is, they share in it. The former seers, the sharers of old, the poets, that is, the ṛṣis who are anūcāna, praise them at dawn alone. He cites ये वा अनूचानास्ते कवयः (*ye vā anūcānāste kavayaḥ*) (Ai. Brā. 2-38)**GLOSS** as the Vedic passage.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- प्रथमा (*prathamā*)—earlier than the other gods
- प्रार्यावाणा (*prāryāvāṇā*)—addressing the Aśvin deities who come at dawn
- यजध्वं (*yajadhvaṃ*)—perform the sacrifice
- गृध्रात् (*gṛdhrāt*)—than the greedy
- अररुषः (*araruṣaḥ*)—and obstructing demon
- पुरा (*purā*)—before
- पिबातः (*pibātaḥ*)—let them drink the Soma
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—the Aśvin deities
- प्रातर्हि (*prātarhi*)—at dawn indeed
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—the share of the sacrifice
- दधाते (*dadhāte*)—they bear
- पूर्वभाजः (*pūrvabhājaḥ*)—belonging to former times
- कवयः (*kavayaḥ*)—the sages also
- प्र शंसंति (*pra śaṃsaṃti*)—praise them at dawn alone

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Perform the sacrifice addressing the Aśvin deities, who come at dawn, earlier than the other gods. Let them drink the Soma before the greedy, obstructing demon. The Aśvin deities bear the share of the sacrifice at dawn alone. The sages of former times also praise them at dawn alone.

##### English translation
Worship the two who come first (of the gods) at dawn : let them drink before the greedy withholders (of the offering); for the Aswins verily claim the morning sacrifice: the ancient sages praised them (at dawn).

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रातर्यजध्वमश्विना हिनोतन सायमस्ति देवया अजुष्टं।  
> उतान्यो अस्मद्यजते वि चावः पूर्वःपूर्वो यजमानो  
> वनीयान् ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 133

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रातः । यजध्वं । अश्विना । हिनोत । न । सायं । अस्ति । देवयाः । अजुष्टं ।  
> उत । अन्यः । अस्मत् । यजते । वि । च । अवः । पूर्वःपूर्वः । यजमानः । वनीयान् ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मदीयाः पुरुषा अश्विनाश्विन् प्रातः प्रातरेव यजध्वं । पूजयध्वं ।
> स्तुध्वं । हिनोत । प्रहिणुत हवींषि । सायं सायंकाले हविर्देवया देवगामि
> नास्ति । न विद्यते । देवा न स्वीकुर्वंतीत्यर्थः । अजुष्टमसेव्यं तद्भवति । पूर्वाणो
> वै देवानामिति हि श्रुतिः । उतास्मदस्मत्तोऽन्यः कोऽपि यजते यजेत सोमेन वि
> चावः वितर्पयेच्च हविषा । अतोऽस्मास्वन्येषु च मध्यॆ पूर्वः पूर्वो
> यजमानो यः पूर्वः पूर्वो यष्टा भवति स वनीयान् देवानां संभजनीयः
> संभाव्यो भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O my men, worship the Aśvins at dawn alone; that is, honour and praise them. Send them oblations. In the evening, the oblation does not go to the gods, it is not; that is, the gods do not accept it. It is unfit to be served. The śruti says पूर्वाणो वै देवानामिति (*pūrvāṇo vai devānāmiti*)**GLOSS**. Moreover, if anyone other than ourselves sacrifices with Soma and satisfies with the oblation, then among us and the others the sacrificer who is earlier and earlier is the most to be honoured, the one whom the gods hold in regard.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—addressing the Aśvin deities
- प्रातः (*prātaḥ*)—at dawn alone
- यजध्वं (*yajadhvaṃ*)—offering worship
- हिनोत (*hinota*)—offer oblations as well
- सायं (*sāyaṃ*)—the oblation offered in the evening
- देवयाः (*devayāḥ*)—pleasing to the gods
- न अस्ति (*na asti*)—is not
- अजुष्टं (*ajuṣṭaṃ*)—the oblation offered at that time is not fit to be enjoyed
- उत (*uta*)—moreover
- अस्मत् (*asmat*)—than ourselves
- अन्यः (*anyaḥ*)—another man
- यजते (*yajate*)—even if he worships
- वि चावः (*vi cāvaḥ*)—or offers an oblation (whether among us or among others)
- पूर्वः पूर्वः (*pūrvaḥ pūrvaḥ*)—who offers before all
- यजमानः (*yajamānaḥ*)—the sacrificer
- वनीयान् (*vanīyān*)—is dear to the gods

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Addressing the Aśvin deities, offer at dawn alone a praise worthy of worship, and an oblation. An oblation offered in the evening is not pleasing to the gods; an oblation offered at that time is not fit to be enjoyed. Moreover, even if another than ourselves worships, or offers an oblation, whether among us or among others, the sacrificer who offers before all becomes worthy of the love of these gods.

##### English translation
Worship the Aswins at early dawn; offer them oblations: the evening is not for the gods; it is unacceptable to them; and whether it be

### Printed p. 134

any other than ourselves who worships them or propitiates them, the worshipper who is foremost (in his dovotion) is the most approved of.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> हिरण्यत्वज् मधुवर्णो घृतस्नुः पृक्षो वहन्ना रथोवर्तते  
> नां ।  
> मनोजवा अश्विना वातरंहा येनातियाथो दुरितानि विश्वा ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> हिरण्यत्वक् । मधुवर्णः । धृतस्नुः । पक्षः । वहन् । आ । रथः । वर्तते । नां ।  
> मनःजवाः । अश्विना । वातरंहाः । येन । अति याथः । दुःइतानि । विश्वा ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हिरण्यत्वगित्येषाश्विने पश् हविषो याज्या । हिरण्यत्वङ्मधुवर्णो धृतस्सुरभि
> क्रत्वेंद्र भूरथ ज्मन् । आ. ३-८ । इति हि सूत्रितं ॥
> वां युवयो रथः प्रक्षोऽन्नममृतं वहन्ना वर्तते । अस्मदभिमुखमागच्छति, केदृशो रथः । हिरण्यत्पक् हिरण्याच्छादितरूपो हिरण्यावृतो मधुवर्णो
> मनोहरवर्णो घृतस्सुरुदकस्य प्रसन्नवो मनोजवा मनोवेगो नातरंहा
> वातसदृशवेगः । हे अश्विना येन रथेन विश्वा सर्वाणि दुरितानि दुर्गमनान्मार्गानतियाधः अतिक्रम्य गच्छथः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This verse, "hiraṇyatvak", is the yājyā of the Aśvina offering **NOTE**; so it is prescribed in the sūtra beginning हिरण्यत्वङ्मधुवर्णो (*hiraṇyatvaṅmadhuvarṇo*) (Ā. 3-8). Your chariot, bearing food, the nectar, comes toward us. What kind of chariot? हिरण्यत्वक् (*hiraṇyatvak*): covered with gold, enveloped in gold; of charming colour; streaming with water **NOTE**; as swift as the mind; swift as the wind. O Aśvins, with which chariot you pass over all difficult roads, going beyond them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- येन (*yena*)—by which chariot
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- दुरितानि (*duritāni*)—even the roads that others cannot cross
- अतियाधः (*atiyādhaḥ*)—you go beyond them; such a chariot
- हिरण्यत्वक् (*hiraṇyatvak*)—which has a golden covering**NOTE**
- मधुवर्णः (*madhuvarṇaḥ*)—which has a charming form
- धृतस्नुः (*dhṛtasnuḥ*)—which streams water
- मनोजवाः (*manojavāḥ*)—which has the speed of the mind
- नातरंहाः (*nātaraṃhāḥ*)—which is swift like the wind
- वां रथः (*vāṃ rathaḥ*)—your chariot
- पक्षृ (*pakṣṛ*)—food
- वहन् (*vahan*)—bearing
- आ वर्तते (*ā vartate*)—comes toward us

### Printed p. 135

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, the chariot with which you go beyond all the roads that others cannot cross, that chariot of yours, which has a covering of gold, which streams water, which has the speed of the mind, which is swift as the wind, comes toward us bearing food.

##### English translation
Your car, Aswins, approaches, coated with gold, honey-tinted,
water-shedding, laden with ambrosia, as quick as thought. as rapid as the
wind, wherewith you pass over all obstacles.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This Ṛk beginning हिरण्यत्वक् (*hiraṇyatvak*) is to be recited as the yājyā mantra when the oblation is offered in the animal sacrifice made to the Aśvin deities; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra हिरण्यत्वङ्मधुवर्णो धृतस्सुरभि क्रत्वेंद्र भूरध ज्मन् (*hiraṇyatvaṅmadhuvarṇo dhṛtassurabhi kratveṃdra bhūradha jman*) (Ā. Sū. 3-8).

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यो भूयि॑ष्ठं॒ नास॑त्याभ्यां वि॒वेष॒ चनि॑ष्ठं पि॒त्वो रर॑ते  ॒
> विभा॒गे ।  
> स तो॒कम॑स्य पीपर॒च्छमी॑भि॒रनू॑र्ध्वभासः॒ सद॒मित्तु॑ तुर्यात्॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । भूयिष्ठं । नासत्याभ्यां । विवेष । चनिष्ठं । पित्वः । ररते । विभागे ।  
> सः । तोकं । अस्य । पीपरत् । शमीभिः । अनूर्ध्वऽभासः । सदं । इत् । तुतुर्यात् ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो यजमानो विभागे हविर्विभागवति यागे नासत्याभ्यामश्विभ्यां
> भूयिष्ठं चनिष्ठं । चन इत्यन्ननाम । नि. ६-१६ । बह्वन्नं कर्म विवेष करोति ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificer who, in a sacrifice in which the oblations are divided, performs for the Nāsatyas, the two Aśvins, a most abundant चनिष्ठं (*caniṣṭhaṃ*) rite. He explains: चन (*cana*) is a name for food (Ni. 6-16); so this is a rite rich in food, and विवेष (*viveṣa*) means he performs it.

### Printed p. 136

> पित्वः ॥ कर्मणि षष्ठी ॥ अन्नं च ररते ददाति स यजमानोऽस्यात्मन एव शोकं
> पुत्रं पीपरत् पालयेत् शमीभिः कर्मभिः । अनूर्ध्वभासोऽनुन्नततेजस्कान् ।
> यद्वा । ऊर्ध्वभासोऽग्नयः । अग्निरहितानयष्टन् सदमित् सदैव तुतुर्यात् ।
> हिंस्यात् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

He also offers food. That sacrificer protects his own son by his rites. Sāyaṇa explains अनूर्ध्वभासः (*anūrdhvabhāsaḥ*) as those whose splendour is not lofty. Or else, ऊर्ध्वभासोऽग्नयः (*ūrdhvabhāso 'gnayaḥ*): the fires are the lofty-splendoured ones, and the word then means those who have no fires, the non-sacrificers; such persons he is able to harm always.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which sacrificer
- विभागे (*vibhāge*)—in the sacrifice in which the oblations are divided
- नासत्याभ्यां (*nāsatyābhyāṃ*)—addressed to the Aśvin deities
- भूयिष्ठं (*bhūyiṣṭhaṃ*)—abundantly
- चनिष्ठं (*caniṣṭhaṃ*)—the rite full of food
- विवेष (*viveṣa*)—performs
- पित्वः (*pitvaḥ*)—food
- ररते (*rarate*)—offers
- सः (*saḥ*)—that same sacrificer
- शमीभिः (*śamībhiḥ*)—by his rites
- अस्य (*asya*)—his own
- तोकं (*tokaṃ*)—son
- पीपरत् (*pīparat*)—protects (moreover)
- अनूर्ध्वभासः (*anūrdhvabhāsaḥ*)—those fallen from the rites, who do not worship Agni of lofty splendour
- सदमित् (*sadamit*)—always
- तुतुर्यात् (*tuturyāt*)—becomes able to harm

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificer who, in a sacrifice in which the oblations are divided, performs abundantly, for the Aśvin deities, a rite full of food, that same sacrificer protects his own son by his rites. Moreover, he becomes able always to harm those who have fallen from the rites and do not worship Agni of lofty splendour.

##### English translation
He who, in the appointment (of the offerings), presents to the Nasatyas the most ample (share) of (the sacrificial) food, who gives (them) the largest portion of the viands, secures, by his acts, the welfare of his, son, and ever has the advantage of those who light no sacred fires.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सम॒श्विनो॒रव॑सा॒ नूत॑नेन मयो॒भुवा॑ सु॒प्रणी॑ती गमेम ।  
> आ नो॑ र॒यिं व॑हत॒मोत वी॒राना विश्वा॑न्यमृता॒ सौभ॑गानि ॥५॥

### Printed p. 137

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सं । अश्विनोः । अवसा । नूतनेन । मयःभुवा । सुप्रनीति । गमेम ।  
> आ । नः । रयिं । वहतं । आ । उत । वीरान् । आ । विश्वानि । अमृता । सौभगानि ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ई मुक्किन भाष्यवु 130 ने सेजिनल्लिदॆ.

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The bhāṣya of this Ṛk is on page 130.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- अश्विनोः (*aśvinoḥ*)—of the Aśvin deities
- नूतनेन (*nūtanena*)—which is new
- मयोभुवा (*mayobhuvā*)—which brings happiness
- सुप्रणीता (*supraṇītā*)—which is an excellent guide
- आवसा (*āvasā*)—with protection
- सं गमेम (*saṃ gamema*)—may we be joined together
- अमृता (*amṛtā*)—O Aśvins, free from mortality
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- विश्वानि (*viśvāni*)—all
- सौभगानि (*saubhagāni*)—things that bring auspicious good fortune
- आ वहतं (*ā vahataṃ*)—bring
- रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth
- आ(वहतं) (*ā(vahataṃ)*)—provide
- उत (*uta*)—and
- वीरान् (*vīrān*)—heroic sons and the like
- आ (वहतं) (*ā (vahataṃ)*)—bestow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May we be joined together with the protection of the Aśvin deities, which is new, brings happiness and is an excellent guide. O Aśvin deities, free from mortality, bring us all things that bring auspicious good fortune, provide wealth. And bestow heroic sons and the like.

##### English translation
May we be united with the Aswins, by their special protection, which is the source of happiness, the guide to good: bestow upon us, immortals, wealth and all good things.

## Sūkta 78 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अश्विनावेहेति नवर्चं षष्ठं सूक्तं । अत्रानुक्रमणिका । अश्विनौ नव सप्तवध्रिस्त्रिष्टिगादि चतुर्थी त्रिष्टुप्पंचानुष्टुभ इति सप्तवध्रिर्नामात्रेयै यषिः । आदितस्तिस्र
> उष्णिहश्चतुर्थी त्रिष्टुप् शिष्टाः पंचानुष्टुभः ॥ प्रातरनुवाक आश्विने क्रतावौष्ठि हे

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta beginning अश्विनावेह (*aśvināveha*) has nine Ṛks and is the sixth sūkta. The Anukramaṇikā says that the deity is the two Aśvins, that the number of Ṛks is nine, that the seer is Saptavadhri of the Atri line, that the first three Ṛks are in Uṣṇih, the fourth in Triṣṭubh, and the remaining five in Anuṣṭubh. Its use: in the morning recitation (prātaranuvāka), in the Āśvina rite, in the Uṣṇih metre,

### Printed p. 138

> छंदस्याश्विने शस्त्रे चाद्यस्तृचो विनियुक्तः । सूत्रितं च । अश्विना वर्तिरस्मदाश्विनावेह
> गच्छतमिति तृचौ । आ. ४-१५ । इति ॥ दशरात्रे तृतीयेऽहनि प्रउगशस्त्रेयमाश्विनस्तृचः । सूत्रितं च । अश्विनावेह गच्छतमा याह्यद्रिभिः सुतं । आ. ७-१० । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

and in the Āśvina śastra, the first tṛca is employed. This is laid down by the sūtra that the two tṛcas begin with अश्विना वर्तिरस्मदाश्विनावेह गच्छतमिति तृचौ (*aśvinā vartirasmadāśvināveha gacchatamiti tṛcau*) (Ā. Sū. 4-15). On the third day of the Daśarātra, in the Praüga-śastra, this is the Āśvina tṛca; this is laid down by the sūtra अश्विनावेह गच्छतमा याह्यद्रिभिः सुतं (*aśvināveha gacchatamā yāhyadribhiḥ sutaṃ*) (Ā. Sū. 7-10).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning अश्विनावेह (*aśvināveha*) is the sixth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It contains nine Ṛks. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said अश्विन् नव सप्तवध्रिस्त्रिष्टिगादि चतुर्थीं त्रिष्टुप्पंचानुष्टुभः (*aśvin nava saptavadhristriṣṭigādi caturthīṃ triṣṭuppaṃcānuṣṭubhaḥ*). The seer of this sūkta is the one called सप्तवध्रिरात्रेयः (*saptavadhrirātreyaḥ*); the Aśvin deities are the deity. The first three Ṛks of this sūkta are in the Uṣṇik metre, the fourth is in the Triṣṭup metre, and the remaining five Ṛks are in the Anuṣṭup metre. In the recitation of the Prātaranuvāka mantras, the first three Ṛks of this sūkta have a use for the Uṣṇik-metre mantras connected with the Āśvina rite and for the mantras of the Āśvina śastra; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra अश्विना वर्तिरस्मदाश्विनावेह गच्छतमिति तृचौ (*aśvinā vartirasmadāśvināveha gacchatamiti tṛcau*) (Ā. Sū. 4-15). And on the third day of the sacrifice called Daśarātra, at the time of reciting the mantras of the Praüga-śastra, the first three Ṛks of this sūkta are to be used as the tṛca recited to the Aśvin deities, called the Āśvina tṛca; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra अश्विनावेह गच्छतमा याह्यद्रिभिः सुतं (*aśvināveha gacchatamā yāhyadribhiḥ sutaṃ*) (Ā. Sū. 7-10).

##### Particulars of the sūkta

- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 78
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 19, 20
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9
- Seer: सप्तवध्रिरातेयः (*saptavadhrirāteyaḥ*)
- Deity: अश्विनौ (*aśvinau*)
- Metre: 1-3 उष्णिक् (*uṣṇik*); 4 त्रिष्टुप् (*triṣṭup*); 5-9 अनुष्टुप् (*anuṣṭup*)

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अश्वि॑ना॒वेह ग॑च्छतं॒ नास॑त्या॒ मा वि वे॑नतं ।  
> हं॒सावि॑व पतत॒मा सु॒ता उप॑ ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्विनौ । आ । इह । गच्छतं । नासत्या । मा । वि । वेनतं ।  
> हंसौइव । पततं । आ । सुतान् । उप ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 139

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनाविहास्मिन्याग आ गच्छतं । हे नासत्या मा वि वेनतं । वेनतिः कांतिकर्मा । मा विकामौ भवतं । निःस्पृहावकाम् वा न भवतमित्यर्थः । हंसाविव
> तौ यथा निर्मलोदकमागच्छतस्तद्वत् सुतानभिषुतान् सोमानुपा पपतं ।
> उपागच्छतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, come here, to this sacrifice. O Nāsatyas, do not be unwilling: वेनतिः कांतिकर्मा (*venatiḥ kāṃtikarmā*), that is, the verb denotes desire; so do not be without desire, do not be without longing or lacking in wish. Just as the two swans come to clear water, so come to the pressed Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विन् (*aśvin*)—O Aśvin deities
- इह (*iha*)—to this sacrifice
- आ गच्छतं (*ā gacchataṃ*)—deign to come
- नासत्या (*nāsatyā*)—O Aśvin deities
- मा वि वेनतं (*mā vi venataṃ*)—do not be unwilling to come to us
- हंसाविव (*haṃsāviva*)—like two swans flying in
- सुशान् (*suśān*)—the pressed Soma, with it in view **NOTE**
- उप आ पततं (*upa ā patataṃ*)—deign to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, deign to come to this sacrifice. O Aśvin deities, do not be unwilling to come to us. Like two swans flying in, deign to come with the pressed Soma in view.

##### English translation
Aswins. come hither : Nasatyas. be not ill-disposed: alight like two
swans upon the effused libations.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अश्वि॑ना हरि॒णावि॑न गौ॒रावि॒वानु॒ यव॑सं ।  
> हं॒सावि॑न पतत॒मा सु॒ता उप॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्विना । हरिणौ इव । गौरौ इव । अनु । यवसं ।  
> हंसौ इव । पततं । आ । सुतान् । उप ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 140

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अश्विनौ हरिणाविव । गौराविव गौरमृगाविव च । तौ यथा यवसं घासमनु
> धावतः तानिव हंसाविव च सोममागच्छतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Aśvins, come to the Soma like two deer, and like two gaura animals; just as those two run after the यवसं (*yavasaṃ*), the fodder, so you, like them and like two swans, come.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- हरिणाविव (*hariṇāviva*)—like two deer
- यवसं (*yavasaṃ*)—fresh grass
- अनु (*anu*)—following, running
- गौराविव (*gaurāviva*)—like two gaura animals
- हंसाविव (*haṃsāviva*)—like two swans flying in
- सुतान् उप (*sutān upa*)—with the pressed Soma in view
- आ पततं (*ā patataṃ*)—deign to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, like two deer, like two gaura animals that run following fresh grass, and like two swans flying in, deign to come with the pressed Soma in view.

##### English translation
Like two deer, Aswins, like two wild cattle on (fresh) pasture ; like
two swans alight upon the effused libation.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

गौरविव (*gauraviva*)—The word गौर (*gaura*) is included, together with the word गवय (*gavaya*), in the list of animals stated for the Aśvamedha sacrifice. On this subject, in the Taittirīya Saṃhitā:
इंद्राय राजे सूकरो वरुणाय राजे कृष्णो यमाय राज ऋश्य ऋषभाय राजे गवयः शार्दूलाय राजे गौरः पुरुषराजाय मर्कटः क्षिप्रश्चेनस्य वर्तिका नीलंगोः क्रिमिः सोमस्य राजः कुलुंगः सिंधोः शिग्ंशुमारो हिमवतो हस्ती ॥ (*iṃdrāya rāje sūkaro varuṇāya rāje kṛṣṇo yamāya rāja ṛśya ṛṣabhāya rāje gavayaḥ śārdūlāya rāje gauraḥ puruṣarājāya markaṭaḥ kṣipraścenasya vartikā nīlaṃgoḥ krimiḥ somasya rājaḥ kuluṃgaḥ siṃdhoḥ śigṃśumāro himavato hastī ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 5-5-11-1). **GLOSS**
And in another place also of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā of the Yajurveda:
गवयमारण्यमनु ते दिशामि तेन चिन्वानस्तनुवो निषीद । (*gavayamāraṇyamanu te diśāmi tena cinvānastanuvo niṣīda |*) (Trai. Saṃ. 4-2-0-3) **NOTE** **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 141

Thus it is said of the offering of the forest animal called गवय (*gavaya*). The animals called गौर (*gaura*) and गवय (*gavaya*) (Gayal and Bos gavaeu) are, respectively, a kind of deer and an ox living in the forest. In the list of about 81 forest animals used in the Aśvamedha sacrifice, they are stated in the anuvākas from Tai. Saṃ. 5-5-11 to 28. In Kannada there are no proper names for these. गवय (*gavaya*) means a kind of wild ox. Its name is stated in the Ṛgveda only in a few places. But the use of this word गवय (*gavaya*) is found in such places as Mai. Saṃ. 3-14-10; Kāṭhaka Saṃhitā 16-17; Vā. Saṃ. 24-28; Ai. Brā. 2-8; 3-34; Śa. Brā. 1-2-3-9; Sāṅkhyāyana Śrauta Sūtra 3-14. This word is recited together with the word गौर (*gaura*) in the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-21-8, together with the words गौर (*gaura*) and महिष (*mahiṣa*) in Vā. Saṃ. 24-28; 13-49, and in Mai. Saṃ. 2-7-17; Kāṭhaka Saṃhitā 16-17.

गौर (*gaura*) also is a kind of wild ox (Bos gaurus). It is commonly stated together with the word गवय (*gavaya*) in the Ṛgveda 1-16-5, 4-21-8; 4-58-2; 5-78-2; 7-69-6; 7-98-1 and such places. The use of this word is found also in Mai. Saṃ. 3-14-10; Vā. Saṃ. 24-28; Ai. Brā. 3-34 and such places. That it is a forest animal is said in Vā. Saṃ. 13-48. The name of the female animal गौरि (*gauri*) is in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-84-10; 4-12-6; 9-12-3; 1-164-11. The name गौरमृग (*gauramṛga*) also is found in Vā. Saṃ. 24-32; Ai. Brā. 2-8.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अश्वि॑ना वाजिनीवसू जु॒षेथां॑ य॒ज्ञमि॒ष्टये॑ ।  
> हं॒सावि॑व पतत॒मा सु॒ता उप॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अश्विना । वाजिनीवसू इति वाजिनीवसू । जुपेथां । यज्ञं । इष्टये ।  
> हंसौइव । पततं । आ । सुतान् । उप ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वाजिनीवसू अन्नार्थं वासयितारावश्विना यज्ञमस्मदीयमिष्टयेऽभीष्पायैषणाय वा जुषेथां । सेवेथां ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Vājinīvasū, you two Aśvins who provide dwelling for the sake of food, accept our sacrifice, for the sake of what is desired or for the seeking of it; serve it.

### Printed p. 142

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वाजिनीवसू (*vājinīvasū*)—endowed with the wealth of food
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*)—our sacrifice
- इष्टये (*iṣṭaye*)—for your satisfaction
- जुषेथां (*juṣethāṃ*)—partake
- हंसाविव (*haṃsāviva*)—like two swans flying in
- सुतान् उप (*sutān upa*)—with the pressed Soma in view
- आ पततं (*ā patataṃ*)—come flying

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, endowed with the wealth of food, partake of our sacrifice for your satisfaction. Like two swans flying in, come flying with the pressed Soma in view.

##### English translation
Aswins, affluent in food, be propitiated at your pleasure by the sacrifice: alight like two swans upon the effused libation.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अत्रि॒र्यद्वा॑मव॒रोह॑न्नृ॒बीस॒मजो॑हवी॒न्नाध॑मानेव॒  ॑
> योषा ।  
> श्ये॒नस्य॑ चि॒ज्जव॑सा॒ नूत॑ने॒नाग॑च्छतमश्विना॒ शंत॑मेन ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अत्रिः । यत् । वां । अवरोहन् । ऋबीसं । अजोहवीत् । नाधमानाइव ।  
> योषा ।  
> श्येनस्य । चित् । जवसा । नूतनेन । आ । आगच्छतं । अश्विना । शंतमेन ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्रिरस्मत्पिता यद्यदा नां युवामजोहवीत् आस्तौषीत् नाधमाना याच
> माना योषा योषिदिव । सा यथा पतिं प्रीणयति तद्वत्कुर्वन् ऋबीसं तुषाग्नि-

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Atri, our father, when he called upon you and praised you, imploring like a woman who asks and who, as she pleases her husband, acts accordingly, **NOTE** while he was thrown into the ऋबीसं (*ṛbīsaṃ*), the chaff-fire, in the fire-pit,

### Printed p. 143

> मग्नि कुंडे क्षिप्तः सन् अवरोहन् विमुंचन् अजोहवीत् । श्येनस्य चिज्जवसा
> श्येनस्य गरुत्मत इव वेगेन ! चिदित्युपमार्थे । अथवा श्येनस्य जवसा वेगेन
> नूतनेन प्रथमोत्पन्नेन सततं गच्छतः श्येनस्य प्रथमजवेन शंतमेनास्माकं
> सुखतमेन च रथेन तं रक्षितुमागच्छतं हे आश्विना ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

and was releasing himself from it (अवरोहन् (*avarohan*)), called upon you. Sāyaṇa explains the next words: with the speed of a falcon, that is, of Garutmat, with its speed; the particle चित् (*cit*) has the sense of comparison. Or else: with the new speed, the speed freshly arisen, of a falcon that is constantly flying, the first speed of the falcon, and with a chariot that is the most auspicious, the most pleasant for us, come, O Aśvins, to protect him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अशिना (*aśinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- यबीसं (*yabīsaṃ*)—fallen into the fire-pit
- अत्रिः (*atriḥ*)—Atri
- अवरोहन् (*avarohan*)—(with your help) escaping from there
- यत् (*yat*)—with which devotion full of gratitude
- नाधमाना (*nādhamānā*)—who begs for the love of her husband
- योषा (*yoṣā*)—like a woman
- वां (*vāṃ*)—you
- अजोहवीत् (*ajohavīt*)—called, praying (for that reason)
- श्येनस्य चित् (*śyenasya cit*)—of the falcon
- नूतनेन (*nūtanena*)—newly arisen
- जवसा (*javasā*)—with a speed like the speed
- शंतमेन (*śaṃtamena*)—that brings happiness, with your chariot
- आ अगच्छतं (*ā agacchataṃ*)—deign to come

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, Atri, fallen into the fire-pit, escaped from there with your help, and with the devotion full of gratitude with which he prayed to you and called you, like a woman who begs for the love of her husband, for that reason deign to come with your chariot that brings happiness, with a speed like the newly arisen speed of the falcon.

##### English translation
Inasmuch as Atri, escaping by your aid from the fire of chaff, conciliations you, like a wife soliciting (the affection of a husband), therefore come with (your) propitious cars, with the new-born rapidity of the falcon.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अश्विना ऋबीसं अत्रिः अवरोहन् (*aśvinā ṛbīsaṃ atriḥ avarohan*)—O Aśvin deities, the seer Atri, who had fallen into the fire-pit, escaped from the fire with your help. On this subject:
युवं ह घर्म० मधुमंतमत्रयेऽपो न क्षोदोऽव्वणीतमेषे । (*yuvaṃ ha gharma0 madhumaṃtamatraye 'po na kṣodo 'vvaṇītameṣe |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-180-4) **GLOSS**
In Ṛks such as this, the matter of the Aśvin deities saving Atri, who had fallen into the fire and was suffering, is stated.
अत्रेतिहासमाहुः । प्रवर्ग्येण प्रचरंतमत्रिं राक्षसास्तप्ते घर्मेऽपातयन् स च तस्यमानोःश्विन् तुष्टाव । त्वा च स्तुत्या हृष्टमनसावरक्षतामिति ॥ (*atretihāsamāhuḥ | pravargyeṇa pracaraṃtamatriṃ rākṣasāstapte gharme 'pātayan sa ca tasyamānoḥśvin tuṣṭāva | tvā ca stutyā hṛṣṭamanasāvarakṣatāmiti ‖*) (Sā. Bhā.) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 144

The rākṣasas threw the seer Atri, who was engaged in the sacrificial rite called Pravargya, into a blazing fire. Then Atri took refuge in the Aśvin deities and prayed that they protect him. They, satisfied, protected him, so it says. Further:
याभिः शुचंतिं धनसां सुषंसदं तप्तं घर्ममोम्यावंतमत्रये । (*yābhiḥ śucaṃtiṃ dhanasāṃ suṣaṃsadaṃ taptaṃ gharmamomyāvaṃtamatraye |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-112-7) **GLOSS**
हिमेनाग्निं फ्रंसमवारयेथां पितुमतीमूर्जमस्मा अधत्तं । यबीसे अत्रिमश्विनावनीतमुन्निन्यथुः सर्वगणं स्वस्ति ॥ (*himenāgniṃ phraṃsamavārayethāṃ pitumatīmūrjamasmā adhattaṃ | yabīse atrimaśvināvanītamunninyathuḥ sarvagaṇaṃ svasti ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-116-8) **GLOSS**
This allusion is found also in these Ṛks:
अत्रिर्नामषिः । तमसुरा अग्निकूटे प्रचिक्षिपुः । सोऽश्विनौ तुष्टाव तमश्विना वागत्य वृष्टा निशमय्याग्निं तत उज्जह्रतुः । बलं चासुरप्रतिबंध समर्थमस्मै सहान्नेन ददतुरिति ॥ (*atrirnāmaṣiḥ | tamasurā agnikūṭe pracikṣipuḥ | so 'śvinau tuṣṭāva tamaśvinā vāgatya vṛṣṭā niśamayyāgniṃ tata ujjahratuḥ | balaṃ cāsurapratibaṃdha samarthamasmai sahānnena dadaturiti ‖*) (Sā. Bhā.)
Once upon a time the asuras threw the seer called Atri into a heap of fire. He prayed to the Aśvin deities. They came, put out the fire with rain-water and lifted him up. Afterwards they gave him strength to hold back the asuras, and food. So the story goes.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि जि॑हीष्व वनस्पते॒ योनिः॒ सूष्यं॑त्या इव ।  
> श्रु॒तं मे॑ अश्विना॒ हवं॑ स॒प्तव॑ध्रिं च मुंचतं ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वि । जिहीष्व । वनस्पते । योनिः । सूष्यंत्याः इव ।  
> श्रुतं । मे । अश्विना । हवं । सप्तवध्रिं । च । मुंचतं ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 145

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्र ब्रुवंतीतिहासं सप्तवध्रेः पुराविदः ॥ भ्रातृव्याः पेटिकायां तमृषिं
> प्रक्षिप्य यत्नतः । मुद्रां कृत्वा गृहे स्वीये रक्षयित्वा न्यवेशयन् ॥ न संगच्छेत
> भार्या० स्वां यथा रात्र्य तथा कृतं । प्रातः प्रातः समुद्घात्य निर्मथिष्यंति तं
> मुनिं ॥ एवं स्थित्वा चिरं कालं पेटायां दुःखितः कृशः । उपायं चिंतयामास
> निर्गमस्य चिरं मुनिः ॥ हृदि निश्चित्य नासत्यावस्तौषीद्धृष्टमानसः । तमश्विन् समागत्य
> समुद्घात्य च पेटिकां ॥ उद्धृत्य तमृषिं शीघ्रं तावदृष्टौ बभूवतुः । स ऋषिर्भार्यया सार्धं रमित्वाथ पुनर्भयात् ॥ प्रातरेत्य पुनः पेटां प्रविश्य प्रागिव
> स्थितः । पेटानिवाससमये दृष्टवानृद्वयं तदा ॥
> हे वनस्पते वनस्पतिविकाररूपे पेटके वि जिहीष्ट । विगच्छ । विवृता भव ।
> सूष्यंत्या इव प्रसवोन्मुख्याः स्त्रिया योनिरिव भगो यथा विव्रियते तथा त्वमपि
> विवृता भव । तदर्थं हे अश्विनाश्विन् मे हवमाह्वानं श्रुतं । शृणुतं । श्रुत्वा च
> सप्तवध्रिं च मामृषिं मुंचतं । मोचयतं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa tells the old story of Saptavadhri. His rivals, his brother's sons, put the seer into a chest with care, sealed it and kept it in their own house, so arranged that he should not unite with his wife at night. Every morning they opened it and tormented that sage. Having lived thus a long time in the chest, unhappy and lean, the sage thought of a means of escape; having decided in his heart, with a glad mind he praised the Nāsatyas. The two Aśvins came, opened the chest, lifted the seer out and quickly became invisible. The seer, after being with his wife, afraid, came back in the morning and entered the chest again, staying as before. While he was staying in the chest he saw this pair of Ṛks. Then the explanation of the verse: O Vanaspati, that is, the chest, which is a modification of a tree, open wide, be opened; as the womb of a woman about to give birth is opened, so be opened. For that, O Aśvins, hear my call; and having heard, release me, the seer Saptavadhri.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वनस्पते (*vanaspate*)—O Vanaspati (O chest)
- सूष्यंत्याः (*sūṣyaṃtyāḥ*)—of a woman at the time of delivery
- योनिः इव (*yoniḥ iva*)—like the organ of birth
- वि जिहीष्ट्व (*vi jihīṣṭva*)—open your mouth wide
- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- मे (*me*)—my
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—call
- श्रुतं (*śrutaṃ*)—hear
- सप्तवध्रिं च (*saptavadhriṃ ca*)—and the seer Saptavadhri
- मुंचतं (*muṃcataṃ*)—release

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Vanaspati (O chest), open your mouth wide, like the organ of birth of a woman at the time of delivery. O Aśvin deities, hear my call. Release Saptavadhri also.

##### English translation
Open, Vanaspati. like the womb of a parturient female: hear,
Aswins, my invocation : set Saptavadhri free.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

On this Ṛk and the next there is a story concerning the seer Saptavadhri, who saw this sūkta:
अत्र ब्रुवंतीतिहासं सप्तवध्रेः पुराविदः । भ्रातृव्याः पेटिकायां तमृषिं प्रक्षिप्य यत्नतः ॥ (*atra bruvaṃtītihāsaṃ saptavadhreḥ purāvidaḥ | bhrātṛvyāḥ peṭikāyāṃ tamṛṣiṃ prakṣipya yatnataḥ ‖*)

### Printed p. 146

मुद्रां कृत्वा गृहे स्विये रक्षयित्वा न्यवेशयन् । न संगच्छेत भार्यां स्वां यथा रात्रौ तथा कृतं ॥ प्रातःप्रातः समुद्भाट्य निर्मथिष्यंति तं मुनिं । एवं स्थित्वा चिरं कालं पेटायां दुःखितः कृशः ॥ उपायं चिंतयामास निर्गमस्य चिरं मुनिः । हृदि निश्चित्य नासत्यावस्तौषीद्वृष्टमानसः ॥ तमश्विन् समागत्य समुद्भाट्य च पेटकां । उद्धृत्य तम्मषिं शीघ्रं तावदृष्टौ बभूवतुः ॥ स ऋषिर्भार्यया सार्धं रमित्वाथ पुनर्भयात् । प्रातरेत्य पुनः पेटां प्रविश्य प्रागिव स्थितः ॥ पेटानिवाससमये दृष्टवानृग्वयं तदा ॥ (*mudrāṃ kṛtvā gṛhe sviye rakṣayitvā nyaveśayan | na saṃgaccheta bhāryāṃ svāṃ yathā rātrau tathā kṛtaṃ ‖ prātaḥprātaḥ samudbhāṭya nirmathiṣyaṃti taṃ muniṃ | evaṃ sthitvā ciraṃ kālaṃ peṭāyāṃ duḥkhitaḥ kṛśaḥ ‖ upāyaṃ ciṃtayāmāsa nirgamasya ciraṃ muniḥ | hṛdi niścitya nāsatyāvastauṣīdvṛṣṭamānasaḥ ‖ tamaśvin samāgatya samudbhāṭya ca peṭakāṃ | uddhṛtya tammaṣiṃ śīghraṃ tāvadṛṣṭau babhūvatuḥ ‖ sa ṛṣirbhāryayā sārdhaṃ ramitvātha punarbhayāt | prātaretya punaḥ peṭāṃ praviśya prāgiva sthitaḥ ‖ peṭānivāsasamaye dṛṣṭavānṛgvayaṃ tadā ‖*)
Those who know the ancient history tell a story about the seer Saptavadhri. The sons of the brothers of this Saptavadhri, thinking that sons should not be born to the seer, so that he should not unite with his wife at night, used to put him every night, in his own house, into a wooden chest, close the door and lock it. Then, rising in the morning, they would open the door of the chest and beat the seer well. When night came, they would again put him in the chest and lock it. It seems that they did so probably out of the desire that if he had no children his property would come to them. When this had gone on for a long time, Saptavadhri, grown lean with sorrow, thought how he might escape from it, and praised the Aśvin deities. They, satisfied, came there, opened the door of the chest, released the seer and at once became invisible. The seer, delighted by this, enjoyed himself freely with his wife all night, and when morning came he entered the chest again as before, that is, like one who knew nothing. So it went on for a long time. While he was in the chest, this Ṛk and the next Ṛk were seen by this seer Saptavadhri. Thus the bhāṣyakāra has narrated this story.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> भी॒ताय॒ नाध॑मानाय॒ ऋष॑ये स॒प्तव॑ध्रये ।  
> मा॒याभि॑रश्विना यु॒वं वृ॒क्षं सं च॒ वि चा॑चथः ॥ ६ ॥

### Printed p. 147

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> भीताय । नाधमानाय । ऋषये । सप्तवध्रये ।  
> मायाभिः । अश्विना । युवं । वृक्षं । सं । च । वि । च । अचथः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> भीताय निर्गमाद्बिभ्युषे नाधमानाय विमोक्षं याचमानाय सप्तवध्रय
> ऋषये हे अश्विनाश्विन् युवं युवां मायाभिर्वृक्षं वृक्षविकारां पेटिकां सं
> चाचथः । मम निर्गमार्थं संगच्छथः । व्यचथश्च । विभक्तं कुरुथः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

For the seer Saptavadhri, who is afraid of coming out and who begs for release, O Aśvins, you two, by your powers, come together for my release to the chest, which is a modification of a tree, and separate it, that is, make it open.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अश्विना (*aśvinā*)—O Aśvin deities
- भीताय (*bhītāya*)—who is afraid
- नाधमानाय (*nādhamānāya*)—who begs for release
- सप्तवध्रये (*saptavadhraye*)—by name Saptavadhri
- ऋषये (*ṛṣaye*)—for the help of me, the seer
- युवं (*yuvaṃ*)—you
- मायाभिः (*māyābhiḥ*)—by your extraordinary power
- वृक्षं (*vṛkṣaṃ*)—to the chest, a modification of a tree
- सं चाचथः (*saṃ cācathaḥ*)—come
- वि चाचथः (*vi cācathaḥ*)—open its mouth (its door)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Aśvin deities, for the help of the seer (me), who is afraid, who begs for release and who is named Saptavadhri, come with your extraordinary power to the chest, which is a modification of a tree. Open its mouth (its door).

##### English translation
Aswins, by your devices sunder the wicker-work for (the liberation
of the) terrified, imploring Rishi, Saptavadhri.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यथा॒ वातः॑ पुष्क॒रिणीं॑ समिं॒गय॑ति स॒र्वतः॑ ।  
> ए॒वा ते॒ गर्भ॑ एजतु नि॒रैतु॒ दश॑मास्यः ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 148

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यथा । वातः । पुष्करिणीं । संइंगयति । सर्वतः ।  
> एव । ते । गर्भः । एजतु । निःऐतु । दशमास्यः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एतदाद्यक्रयं गर्भस्राविण्युपनिषत् ॥ एतदाद्यक्रयेणासौ सप्तवध्रिः
> स्वयोषितः । गर्भिण्याः प्रसवायाशु स्तुतवानश्विनावृषिः ॥ यथा वातः पुष्करिणीं
> सर आदिकं सर्वतः समिंगयति सम्यक् चालयति एवैवं तव गर्भ विजतु । कंपतां ।
> इतस्ततः संचरतु । दशमास्को दशमासान् गर्भे स्थितो निरैतु । निर्गच्छतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Ṛk and the next two form the Garbhasrāviṇī Upaniṣad. With these three Ṛks the seer Saptavadhri praised the two Aśvins for the quick delivery of his pregnant wife. Sāyaṇa explains: as the wind moves a lake, a pond and the like on every side, shaking it thoroughly, so may your embryo move, tremble, go about here and there; and the one who has stayed in the womb ten months, let him come out, let him go forth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- वातः (*vātaḥ*)—the wind
- पुष्करिणीं (*puṣkariṇīṃ*)—the lake
- सर्वतः (*sarvataḥ*)—all around
- समिंगयति (*samiṃgayati*)—makes it move
- एव (*eva*)—in the same manner
- गर्भः (*garbhaḥ*)—(your) embryo
- विजतु (*vijatu*)—let it move
- दशमास्यः (*daśamāsyaḥ*)—the child that has been in the womb for ten months
- निरैतु (*niraitu*)—let it come out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the manner in which a lake shaken by the wind moves all around with waves, in the same manner let your embryo move. Let the child that has been in the womb for ten months come out.

##### English translation
As the wind, ruffles the lake on every side, so may your womb be stimulated, and the conception of the months come forth.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

This Ṛk and the next two Ṛks are the Garbhasrāviṇī Upaniṣad, the bhāṣyakāra has said:
एतदाद्यक्रयेणासौ सप्तवध्रिः स्वयोषितः । गर्भिण्याः प्रसवायाशु स्तुतवानश्विनावृषिः ॥ (*etadādyakrayeṇāsau saptavadhriḥ svayoṣitaḥ | garbhiṇyāḥ prasavāyāśu stutavānaśvināvṛṣiḥ ‖*)
It says that the seer Saptavadhri, wishing an easy delivery for his wife, praised the Aśvin deities with these three Ṛks.

### Printed p. 149

Here and in the following Ṛks the child in the womb is described as staying in the womb for ten months. Now, ordinarily, it is the custom that the child stays in the mother's womb only up to nine months. But it is seen that in that time it was common for a child to stay in the womb for ten months. Even now there are many instances of delivery not taking place until ten months. And in the Ṛgveda and in the Ekāgni Kāṇḍa of the Yajurveda:
तं ते गर्भं हवामहे दशमे मासि सूतवे । (*taṃ te garbhaṃ havāmahe daśame māsi sūtave |*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-184-3) **GLOSS**
यथेयं पृथिवी मही तिष्ठंती गर्भमादधे । एवं त्वं गर्भमाधत्स्व दशमे मासि सूतवे । पुमाग्ंसं गर्भमाधेहि दशमे मासि सूतवे । तं माता दशमासो बिभर्तु स जायतां वीरतमः स्वानां । आ वीरो जायतां पुत्रस्ते दशमास्यः ॥ (*yatheyaṃ pṛthivī mahī tiṣṭhaṃtī garbhamādadhe | evaṃ tvaṃ garbhamādhatsva daśame māsi sūtave | pumāgṃsaṃ garbhamādhehi daśame māsi sūtave | taṃ mātā daśamāso bibhartu sa jāyatāṃ vīratamaḥ svānāṃ | ā vīro jāyatāṃ putraste daśamāsyaḥ ‖*) (Tai. E. Anu. 13) **NOTE** **GLOSS**
It is said that the child stays in the womb for ten months, and that the son who is born after being so becomes a hero. It is a matter known from experience that the child who is born after staying ten months in the womb will in future be endowed with strength and health and be long-lived.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यथा॒ वातो॒ यथा॒ वनं॒ यथा॑ समु॒द्र एज॑ति ।  
> ए॒वा त्वं द॑शमास्य स॒हावे॑हि ज॒रायु॑णा ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यथा । वातः । यथा । वनं । यथा । समुद्र । विजति ॥  
> एव । त्वं । दशमास्य । सह । अव । इहि । जरायुणा ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 150

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वातो यथा कंपमानो वनं कंपयति स्वयं । यथा समुद्रश्चलति
> चाल्यते वाथ वायुना ॥ स्थित्वा दशैव मासांस्त्वं गर्भोल्पेन सुवेष्टितः । निर्गच्छ
> जठरान्मातुर्जरायुजस्ततः पतेत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

As the wind, itself shaking, makes the wood shake, and as the ocean moves or is moved by the wind, so you, having stayed only ten months, well enveloped by the membrane of the womb **NOTE**, come out of the mother's belly; then, born with the afterbirth, may you fall forth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- वातः (*vātaḥ*)—the moving (blowing) wind
- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- वनं (*vanaṃ*)—makes the trees tremble
- यथा (*yathā*)—in what manner
- समुद्रः (*samudraḥ*)—the ocean (moved by the wind)
- विजति (*vijati*)—is agitated
- एव (*eva*)—in the same manner
- दशमास्य (*daśamāsya*)—O child that has been in the womb for ten months
- जरायुणा सह (*jarāyuṇā saha*)—with the membrane of the womb
- अवेहि (*avehi*)—come out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the manner in which the blowing wind makes the trees and the ocean tremble, in the same manner, by the movement of the womb, O child that has been in the womb for ten months, come out with the membrane of the womb.

##### English translation
As the wind, as the wood, as the ocean are agitated, so do you,
gestation of ten months, invested with the uterine membranes, descend.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दश॒ मासा॑ञ्छशया॒नः कु॑मा॒रो अधि॑ मा॒तरि॑ ।  
> नि॒रैतु॑ जी॒वो अक्ष॑तो जी॒वो जीवं॑त्या॒ अधि॑ ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दश । मासान् । शशयानः । कुमारः । अधि । मातरि ॥  
> निःऐतु । जीवः ! अक्षतः । जीवः । जीवंत्याः । अधि ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 151

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> दश मासानुषित्वासौ जननीजठरे सुखं । निर्गच्छतु सुखं जीवो जननी
> चापि जीवतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Having lain ten months in the mother's womb, may he come out happily, alive, and may the mother also live.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- दश मासान् (*daśa māsān*)—for ten months
- मातरि अधि (*mātari adhi*)—in the mother's womb
- शशयानः (*śaśayānaḥ*)—who lay
- कुमारः (*kumāraḥ*)—the child
- अक्षतः (*akṣataḥ*)—unharmed
- जीवंत्याः (*jīvaṃtyāḥ*)—from the womb of the mother who is living
- जीवः (*jīvaḥ*)—himself living
- निरैतु (*niraitu*)—let him come out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Let the child who lay ten months in the mother's womb come out, unharmed, himself living, from the womb of the mother who is living.

##### English translation
May the boy who has reposed for ten months in the bosom of his mother come forth, alive, unharmed, living, from a living (parent).

## Sūkta 79 (Maṇḍala 5)
**A brief introduction to the deity Uṣas**
The deity of this sūkta and of the next sūkta is Uṣas. Concerning the deity Uṣas, the great sage Yāska has said:
उषा? कस्मादुच्छतीति सत्या रात्रेरपरः कालः (*uṣā? kasmāducchatīti satyā rātreraparaḥ kālaḥ*) (Ni. 2-19). That is, examining questions such as who the deity Uṣas is, what her nature is, and what is the reason for her having this name, he says:
उषः कस्मात् । उच्छतीति सत्याः । उच्छि विवासे । विवासयति हीयं तमांसि तस्मादुच्छतीत्येवमस्या एतस्मिन्कर्मणि सत्याः कर्तरि कारके उषा इत्येतदभिधानं भवति । का पुनरियमुषा इति । रात्रेरपरःकालः स उषा इत्युच्यते ॥ (*uṣaḥ kasmāt | ucchatīti satyāḥ | ucchi vivāse | vivāsayati hīyaṃ tamāṃsi tasmāducchatītyevamasyā etasminkarmaṇi satyāḥ kartari kārake uṣā ityetadabhidhānaṃ bhavati | kā punariyamuṣā iti | rātreraparaḥkālaḥ sa uṣā ityucyate ‖*)
That is, he explains that the word Uṣas is derived from the root उच्छि-विवासे (*ucchi-vivāse*), which has the meaning "to drive out". If one asks what is driven out, the meaning is darkness. Because she drives out darkness and spreads light, this deity is called उषाः (*uṣāḥ*). If one asks what that time is, it is the last part of the night. The time after the night is over and before sunrise, when there is no darkness and it is light, is called the Uṣas time; thus they have explained the meaning. The night is over

### Printed p. 152

and the Uṣas time follows; therefore it is customary to say that the Uṣas time is born of night. To support this, Yāska quoted the Ṛk
इदं श्रेष्ठं ज्योतिषां ज्योतिरागाच्चित्रः प्रकेतो अजनिष्ट विभ्वा । यथा प्रसूता सवितुः सवाय एवा रात्रुषसे योनिमारैक् ॥ (*idaṃ śreṣṭhaṃ jyotiṣāṃ jyotirāgāccitraḥ praketo ajaniṣṭa vibhvā | yathā prasūtā savituḥ savāya evā rātruṣase yonimāraik ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-113-1) **GLOSS**
And following this order, since sunrise comes after the Uṣas time, in the matter of saying that the sun is born in Uṣas, Yāska quoted for support the Ṛk
रुशद्वत्सा रुशती श्वेत्यागादारैगु कृष्णा सदनान्यस्याः । समानबंधू अमृते अनूची द्यावा वर्णं चरत आमिनाने ॥ (*ruśadvatsā ruśatī śvetyāgādāraigu kṛṣṇā sadanānyasyāḥ | samānabaṃdhū amṛte anūcī dyāvā varṇaṃ carata āmināne ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-113-2) **GLOSS**
(Ni. 2-20).

The seers have described the nature of this deity Uṣas in various ways. In accordance with these descriptions they have used various epithets. Of these, sixteen principal words apply to the deity Uṣas. Yāska has stated them thus (Ni. 2-19):
- विभावरी (*vibhāvarī*)—विशेषप्रभावयुक्ता । विशिष्टप्रकाशयुक्ता (*viśeṣaprabhāvayuktā | viśiṣṭaprakāśayuktā*): having special power; having distinct light; very radiant—Resplendent. This word occurs in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-30-21; 1-48-1; 1-48-10; 1-92-14; 4-52-6; 5-79-4; 5-79-11; 8-47-14.
- सूनरी (*sūnarī*)—सुष्टु नेत्री (*suṣṭu netrī*): a good leader; beautiful—Beautiful. This word occurs in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-48-10; 1-48-5; 1-48-8; 4-52-1; 7-81-1.
- भास्वती (*bhāsvatī*)—तेजस्सती। विशिष्टप्रकाशनयुक्ता (*tejassatī| viśiṣṭaprakāśanayuktā*): possessing brilliance; having distinct radiance; shining—Shining. This word is recited in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-92-7; 1-113-4.
- ओदती (*odatī*)—उंदी क्लेदने । उनत्ति सर्वं नीहारेणेत्योदती (*uṃdī kledane | unatti sarvaṃ nīhāreṇetyodatī*): flowing, gushing, streaming, wetting everything with mist—flowing. gushing & c. This word is only in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-46-6; 8-69-2.
- चित्रमघा (*citramaghā*)—विचित्रधना । विविधरश्माख्यधना वा (*vicitradhanā | vividharaśmākhyadhanā vā*): having various wealth, or radiant wealth—possessed of brilliant riches. This word occurs in Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-75-5; 7-77-3; 8-58-3.

### Printed p. 153

- अर्जुनी (*arjunī*)—शुभ्रवर्णा (*śubhravarṇā*): of bright colour; white—white. This word is in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-49-3; 5-84-2.
- वाजिनी (*vājinī*)—or वाजिनीवती (*vājinīvatī*)—वाजोऽन्नमस्या अस्तीति । बह्वन्ना (*vājo 'nnamasyā astīti | bahvannā*): having plenty of food; endowed with food, or bringing food—the bringer of food. These words are in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-6-1; 3-60-1; 6-61-6; and Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-3-10; 6-61-4; 6-75-5; 7-96-3; 10-75-8; 1-48-6; 1-48-16; 1-92-13; 1-92-15; 2-41-18; 4-55-9; 6-61-3; 8-24-28.
- सुम्मावरी (*summāvarī*)—सुम्ममिति सुखनाम । तद्वती (*summamiti sukhanāma | tadvatī*): सुम्म (*summa*) is a name for happiness; she who has it; producing joy—the giver of joy. This word is only in the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-113-12.
- अहना (*ahanā*), द्योतना (*dyotanā*)—कृत्स्नं जगद्द्योतनशीला । तद्वती (*kṛtsnaṃ jagaddyotanaśīlā | tadvatī*): she whose nature is to illumine the whole world; she who has it; radiant—bright. These words are in the Ṛk Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-123-4.
- श्वेत्या (*śvetyā*)—श्वेतवर्णोषा । श्वेता वर्णॆ (*śvetavarṇoṣā | śvetā varṇè*): Uṣas of white colour; having a bright colour—the fair-caloured. This word is in Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-113-2; 10-75-6.
- अरुषी (*aruṣī*)—आरोचमाना । रक्तवर्णा ना (*ārocamānā | raktavarṇā nā*): red-coloured, having a slightly reddish shade—the ruddy. This word is recited in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-55-11; 4-52-2; 8-98-8 and others.
- सूनृता (*sūnṛtā*), सूनृतावती (*sūnṛtāvatī*), सूनृतावरी (*sūnṛtāvarī*)—प्रियसत्यरूपा । प्रियसत्यात्मिका वाक् । तादृश्या वाचा युक्ता (*priyasatyarūpā | priyasatyātmikā vāk | tādṛśyā vācā yuktā*): who speaks pleasant and beneficial words—the utterer of pleasent or sacred speech. These words are recited in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-8-8; 1-30-5; 1-43-3 and others, and in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-92-14; 1-22 3; 7-81-6; and Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-52-4.

Although these words are generally used with reference to the deity Uṣas, in some contexts they are used also with reference to other deities.

Yāska has given this explanation of the Ṛk quoted above:
इदं श्रेष्ठं ज्योतिषां ज्योतिरागमत् । चित्रं प्रकेतनं प्रज्ञाततममजनिष्ट विभूततमं यथा प्रसूता सवितुः प्रसवाय रात्रिरादित्यस्य । एवं रात्र्युषसे योनिमरिचत् स्थानं । स्त्रीयोनिरभियुत एनां गर्भः ॥ (*idaṃ śreṣṭhaṃ jyotiṣāṃ jyotirāgamat | citraṃ praketanaṃ prajñātatamamajaniṣṭa vibhūtatamaṃ yathā prasūtā savituḥ prasavāya rātrirādityasya | evaṃ rātryuṣase yonimaricat sthānaṃ | strīyonirabhiyuta enāṃ garbhaḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**
According to the statement रात्रेरपरः काल उष इत्युच्यते (*rātreraparaḥ kāla uṣa ityucyate*), the last part of the night, that is, the time before sunrise, is called the Uṣas time. The brilliance of this Uṣas time, that is, the light

### Printed p. 154

is the best of all lights. In the world the sun, the moon and the stars are luminous. Among these the light of the sun is very sharp. The light of the stars illumines only the stars themselves and does not illumine other things; that is, other objects cannot be seen by the light of the stars. Although the light of the moon is cool, it is not very bright, and so objects are not seen clearly by it. But the light of the Uṣas time is neither sharp like the light of the sun, nor cool like the radiance of the moon, nor dim. It has the power of illumining all things very well. For this very reason it is said here that the light of this Uṣas time is far better than the light of the sun, the moon, the stars and the rest. Moreover, the light of the Uṣas time spreads over all regions at one and the same time and is very pleasing to the mind. The rising of such an Uṣas time has now come about. This is the translation of the first half of this Ṛk.

In the world, night comes after the sun has set. After the night has passed, the Uṣas time comes, and after the Uṣas time, the sunrise. Because they come one after another in this order, one is said to be the cause of the birth of the other.
