> अस्य॒ हि स्वय॑शस्तरं सवि॒तः कच्च॒न प्रि॒यं ।  
> न मि॒नंति॑ स्व॒राज्यं॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अस्य । हि । स्वयशःतरं । सवितुः । कत् । चन । प्रियं ।  
> न । मिनंति । स्वराज्यं ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 191

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्य सवितुः स्वयशस्तरं स्वयमसाधारणं यशो यस्यातिशयेन भवति
> तत्तादृशं प्रियं सर्वेषां प्रियभूतं स्वराज्यं स्वयमेव राजमानत्वमैश्वर्य्यं
> कच्चन केचिदप्यसुरादयो न मिनंति । न हिंसंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of this Savitṛ, whose fame is exceedingly his own and uncommon, such a dear sovereignty, स्वराज्यं (*svarājyaṃ*), that is, shining by himself, lordship, dear to all, no one, no asuras and the like, impairs; they do not injure it.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आस्य (*āsya*)—of the well-known
- सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—god Savitṛ
- स्वयशस्तरं (*svayaśastaraṃ*)—that which is itself the cause of his uncommon fame
- प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—and dear to the whole world
- स्वराज्यं (*svarājyaṃ*)—sovereignty
- कच्चन (*kaccana*)—no adversary even
- न मिनंति (*na minaṃti*)—can injure

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

No adversary can injure the sovereignty and the wealth of Savitṛ, which is the cause of his uncommon fame by his own greatness and is dear to the whole world.

##### English translation
Nothing impairs the sovereignty of this Savitri, which is most especially renowned and beloved.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स हि रत्ना॑नि दा॒शुषे॑ सु॒वाति॑ सवि॒ता भगः॑ ।  
> तं भा॒गं चि॒त्रमी॑महे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । हि । रत्नानि । दाशुषे । सुवाति । सविता । भगः ।  
> तं । भागं । चित्रं । ईमहे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स हि स खलु सविता भगो भजनीयो देवो दाशुषे हविर्दात्रे मह्यं
> रत्नानि रमणीयानि धनानि सुवाति । प्रेरयति । प्रयच्छति । तं देवं भागं भजनीयं
> चित्रं चायनीयं धनमीमहे । याचामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

He, indeed, Savitṛ Bhaga, the adorable god, gives, that is, sends and bestows, jewels, delightful riches, on me, the giver of oblation. That god's share, the adorable one, the wonderful wealth, चायनीयं (*cāyanīyaṃ*), to be gathered, we ask for; we beg.

### Printed p. 192

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- भगः (*bhagaḥ*)—the adorable one
- स हि सविता (*sa hi savitā*)—that same god Savitṛ
- दाशुषे (*dāśuṣe*)—to the sacrificer, the giver of oblation
- रत्नानि (*ratnāni*)—delightful riches
- सुवाति (*suvāti*)—gives
- चित्रं (*citraṃ*)—attractive
- तं भागं (*taṃ bhāgaṃ*)—a share of that wealth (that praiseworthy wealth)
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we desire and pray for

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The adorable Savitṛ gives delightful riches to the sacrificer who is the giver of oblation. We desire and pray for that wealth, which is attractive and worthy of worship.

##### English translation
That savitri, who is Bhaga,bestows precious treasure on the donor
of the offering: we solicit (of him) a valuable portion.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒द्या नो॑ देव सवितः प्र॒जाव॑त्सावीः॒ सौभ॑गं ।  
> परा दुःष्वप्नं सुव ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अद्य । नः । देव । सवितरिति । प्रजावत् । सावीः । सौभगं ।  
> परा । दुःस्वप्नं । सुव ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अद्या न इति चतुर्थीपंचमीभ्याममनोज्ञस्वप्नदर्शने प्रत्यृचं जुहुयात् । अद्या नो देव सवितरिति द्वाभ्यां । आ, गृ. ३-६-६ । इति सूत्रितं ॥
> हे सवितर्देव नोऽस्मभ्यमध्यासि न्यागदिने प्रजावत्पुत्राद्युपेतं सौभगं
> धनं सावीः । प्रेरय । दुष्स्वप्नं दुःस्वप्नं दुःस्वप्नवद्दुःखकरं दारिद्रं परासुव
> दूरे प्रेरय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa states the ritual use: when an unpleasant dream has been seen, one should offer oblation with each Ṛk, with the fourth and the fifth Ṛk, as the Gṛhya-sūtra ordains: अद्या नो देव सवितरिति द्वाभ्यां (*adyā no deva savitariti dvābhyāṃ*) (Ā. Gṛ. 3-6-6). He then explains the verse. O god Savitṛ, to us, today, in this sacrificial day, send wealth, सौभगं (*saubhagaṃ*), that is endowed with offspring, sons and the like. Send far away the bad dream, दुःस्वप्नं (*duḥsvapnaṃ*), that is, poverty, which is painful like a bad dream. **NOTE**

### Printed p. 193

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—adorable
- सवितः (*savitaḥ*)—O god Savitṛ
- अद्य (*adya*)—on this sacrificial day
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- प्रजावत् (*prajāvat*)—endowed with sons and the like
- सौभगं (*saubhagaṃ*)—wealth
- सावीः (*sāvīḥ*)—provide
- दुःस्वप्नं (*duḥsvapnaṃ*)—the troubles such as poverty, painful like a bad dream
- परा सुव (*parā suva*)—drive far away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O adorable god Savitṛ, provide us in this sacrifice with wealth endowed with sons and the like. Drive far away the troubles such as poverty, which are painful like a bad dream.

##### English translation
Grant us to-day, divine Savitri, affluence with progeny, and drive away evil dreams.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

When a bad dream (a nightmare) has occurred, for the removal of its evil one should offer oblation of ghee in the fire with this Ṛk beginning अद्या नः (*adyā naḥ*) and with the next Ṛk beginning विश्वानि देव (*viśvāni deva*). This is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Gṛhya-sūtra, अद्या नो देव सवितरिति द्वाभ्यां (*adyā no deva savitariti dvābhyāṃ*) (Ā. Gṛ. 3-6-6).

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> विश्वा॑नि देव सवितर्दुरि॒तानि॒ परा॑ सुव ।  
> यद्भ॒द्रं तन्न॒ आ सु॑व ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> विश्वानि । देव । सवितः । दुःइतानि । परा । सुव ।  
> यत् । भद्रं । तत् । नः । आ । सुव ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे सवितर्देव त्वं विश्वानि दुरितानि परा सुव । यद्भद्रं प्रजापशुगृहादिकं
> तन्नोऽस्मभ्यमा सुव । अस्मदभिमुखं प्रेरय । प्रजा पै भद्रं पशवो भद्रं गृहं
> भद्रमिति हि श्रुतिः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O god Savitṛ, drive away all evils, दुरितानि (*duritāni*). Whatever is auspicious, भद्रं (*bhadraṃ*), such as offspring, cattle and house, that bring to us, आ सुव (*ā suva*), that is, send it towards us. For the scripture says that offspring is auspicious, cattle are auspicious, the house is auspicious: प्रजा पै भद्रं पशवो भद्रं गृहं भद्रमिति हि श्रुतिः ॥ (*prajā pai bhadraṃ paśavo bhadraṃ gṛhaṃ bhadramiti hi śrutiḥ ‖*) **GLOSS**

### Printed p. 194

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—adorable
- सवितः (*savitaḥ*)—O Savitṛ
- विश्वानि (*viśvāni*)—all
- दुरितानि (*duritāni*)—sins (misfortunes)
- परा सुव (*parā suva*)—drive far away and destroy
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- यत् (*yat*)—whatever
- भद्रं (*bhadraṃ*)—is auspicious
- तत् (*tat*)—that
- आ सुव (*ā suva*)—provide

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O adorable Savitṛ, drive far away and destroy all our sins and misfortunes. Provide us with whatever is auspicious for us.

##### English translation
Remove from us, divine Savitri, all misfortunes : bestow upon us
that which is good.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अना॑गसो॒ अदि॑तये दे॒वस्य॑ सवि॒तुः स॒वे ।  
> विश्वा॑ ना॒मानि॑ धीमहि ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अनागसः । अदितये । देवस्य । सवितुः । सवे '  
> विश्वा । नामानि । धीमहि ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वयमनुष्ठातारः सवितुः प्रेरकस्य देवस्य सवेऽनुज्ञायां सत्यामदितयेऽखंडनीयायै देव्यै भूम्या अनागसः स्याम । अनपराधिनो भवेम ।
> भूम्यां हि पापाः संभवंति । असगतेष्वागःसु विश्वा सर्वाणि वानीयानि
> धनानि धीमहि । धारयाम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

We, the performers, when Savitṛ the impelling god gives his sanction, may be free from offence, अनागसः (*anāgasaḥ*), that is, without fault, towards Aditi, the indivisible goddess, who is the Earth; for it is on the earth that sins arise. **NOTE** We hold, धीमहि (*dhīmahi*), that is, we bear, all desirable riches.

### Printed p. 195

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देवस्य (*devasya*)—of the divine
- सवितुः (*savituḥ*)—of Savitṛ
- सवे (*save*)—obedient to the command
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- आदितये (*āditaye*)—in regard to the goddess Aditi
- अनागसः (*anāgasaḥ*)—let us be free from sin
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- वामानि (*vāmāni*)—also the desired riches
- धीमहि (*dhīmahi*)—let us obtain

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Being obedient to the command of the divine Savitṛ, may we be free from sin in regard to the goddess Aditi. May we obtain all the desired riches.

##### English translation
Let us be void of offence towards Aditi, according to the will of the divine Savitri: may we be possessed of all-desired (riches).

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ वि॒श्वदे॑वं॒ सत्प॑तिं सू॒क्तैर॒द्या वृ॑णीमहे ।  
> सत्यसनं सवितारं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आ । विश्वदेवं । सर्पपतिं । सुउक्तैः । अद्य । वृणेमहे ।  
> सत्यसवं । सवितारं ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> चातुर्मास्येषु वैश्वदेवे पर्वणि सावित्रस्य द्वादशकपालस्या विश्वदेवमित्यनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं वाममद्य सवितः । आ. २-१६ । इति ॥
> प्रायणीय एषैव सवितुरनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं य इमा विश्वा
> जातानि । आ. ४ ३ । इति ॥ देवसुवां हविष्वेष्टैव सवितुरनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च । आ
> विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं न प्रमिये सवितुर्द्वैव्यस्य तत् । आ. ४-११ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa gives the ritual use in three places. In the Cāturmāsya sacrifice, at the Vaiśvadeva parvan, this Ṛk, beginning आ विश्वदेवं (*ā viśvadevaṃ*), is the anuvākyā for the oblation to Savitṛ on twelve potsherds; the sūtra says so (Ā. 2-16). In the Prāyaṇīya offering this same Ṛk is the anuvākyā to Savitṛ; the sūtra says so (Ā. 4 3). **NOTE** In the offering of the oblations called Devasū this same Ṛk is again the anuvākyā to Savitṛ; the sūtra says so (Ā. 4-11).

### Printed p. 196

> विश्वदेवं । विश्वे देवा यस्य वशे भवंति तं तादृशं । तं हि सर्वात्मत्वादिंद्रं
> मित्रं वरुणमग्निमाहुरित्यादिश्रुतेरितरेषां तद्विभूतित्वात् । सत्पतिं सतामनुष्ठात्याणां पालकं सत्यसवं सत्यानुज्ञं सवितारं देवमद्यास्मिन्यागदिने सूक्तैरावृणीमहे । संभजामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

विश्वदेवं (*viśvadevaṃ*): he under whose control all the gods stand, such a one. He is all-pervading and the self of all; for the scripture says that they call him Indra, Mitra, Varuṇa, Agni, and so on, the others being his manifestations. सत्पतिं (*satpatiṃ*): the protector of the good, of those who perform the rites. सत्यसवं (*satyasavaṃ*): whose sanction is true. We today, on this sacrificial day, choose and serve the god Savitṛ with hymns, सूक्तैः (*sūktaiḥ*). **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- विश्वदेवं (*viśvadevaṃ*)—who is the inner controller in all the gods
- सत्पतिं (*satpatiṃ*)—the protector of the good
- सत्यसवं (*satyasavaṃ*)—who has a regulation of the nature of truth
- सवितारं (*savitāraṃ*)—the god Savitṛ
- अद्य (*adya*)—on this day of the sacrifice
- सूक्तैः (*sūktaiḥ*)—with excellent praises
- आ वृणीमहे (*ā vṛṇīmahe*)—we praise and worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I praise and worship the god Savitṛ, who is the inner controller in all the gods, the protector of the good, and who has a regulation of the nature of truth, on this day of the sacrifice, with excellent praises.

##### English translation
We glorify to-day with hymns Savitri, the protector of the good,
the observer of truth, (identical with) all the gods.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the sacrifice called Cāturmāsya, in the Vaiśvadeva parvan, in the oblation on twelve potsherds made with Savitṛ as the deity, this Ṛk beginning आ विश्वदेवं (*ā viśvadevaṃ*) is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं वाममद्य सवितः (*ā viśvadevaṃ satpatiṃ vāmamadya savitaḥ*) (Ā. 2-16). In the Prāyaṇīya offering also, in the oblation made with Savitṛ as the deity, this same Ṛk is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं य इमा विश्वा जातानि (*ā viśvadevaṃ satpatiṃ ya imā viśvā jātāni*) (Ā. 4-3). And in the oblations called Devasū, in the oblation made to Savitṛ as the deity, this Ṛk is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra आ विश्वदेवं सत्पतिं न प्रमिये सवितुर्दैव्यस्य तत् (*ā viśvadevaṃ satpatiṃ na pramiye saviturdaivyasya tat*) (Ā. 4-11).

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य इ॒मे उ॒भे अह॑नी पु॒र एत्यप्र॑युच्छन् ।  
> स्वा॒धीर्दे॒वः स॑वि॒ता ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 197

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । इमे इति । उभे इति । अहनी इति । पुरः । एति । अप्रयुच्छन् ।  
> सुआधीः । देवः । सविता ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यः सविता देवः स्वाधीः शोभनाध्यानः सुकर्मा वा सन् इमे अहनी
> रात्र्यहनी तयोः पुरः पुरस्तावप्रयुच्छन् अप्रमाद्यन्नेति गच्छति तमा वृणीमह
> इति संबंधः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The god Savitṛ, who, being स्वाधीः (*svādhīḥ*), of good meditation or of good works, goes in front of these two, day and night, अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*), that is, without negligence: him we choose. Thus the construction is completed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Savitṛ who
- इमे उभे अहनी (*ime ubhe ahanī*)—of both these day and night
- पुरः (*puraḥ*)—in front (as a guide)
- अप्रयुच्छन् (*aprayucchan*)—very carefully
- एति (*eti*)—moves about (such)
- देवः (*devaḥ*)—divine
- सविता (*savitā*)—Savitṛ
- स्वाधीः (*svādhīḥ*)—is the object of excellent meditation

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Savitṛ who moves about as a guide in front of both day and night, free from negligence and very careful, that same god is the object of excellent meditation.

##### English translation
The divine object of meditation, Savitri, who, ever vigilant, precedes
both night and day.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य इ॒मा विश्वा॑ जा॒तान्या॑श्रा॒वय॑ति॒ श्लोके॑न ।  
> प्र च॑ सु॒वाति॑ सवि॒ता ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यॆः । इमा । विश्वा । जातानि । आश्रवयति । श्लोकेन ।  
> प्र । च । सुवाति । सविता ॥ ९ ॥

### Printed p. 198

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रायणीये य इमा विश्वेति सवितुर्याज्या । सूत्रितं च । य इमा विश्वा
> जातानि सुत्रमाणं पृथिवीं द्यामनेहसं । आ. ४-३ । इति ॥ अश्वमेधेश्वमुत्सृज्यानु
> सवनं सावित्रीष्टिः कार्या । तस्यामेषा माध्यंदिने सवनेऽनुवाक्या । सूत्रितं च ।
> य इमा विश्वा जातान्या देवो यातु सविता सुरत्नः । आ. १०-६ । इति ॥
> यो देव इमेमानि विश्वा सर्वाणि जातान्युत्पन्नान् प्राणिनः । जंगमानित्यर्थः । तान् श्लोकेन यशसाश्रावयति । सर्वेऽप्यस्य स्तुतिं शृण्वंतीत्यर्थः । अथवा
> गर्जनशब्देन सर्वाणीमान्युत्पन्नान्याश्रावयति वृष्ट्युन्मुखः सन् । किंच सविता प्र
> सुवाति प्रेरयति तं वृणीमह इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa gives three ritual uses. In the Prāyaṇīya offering the Ṛk beginning य इमा विश्वा (*ya imā viśvā*) is the yājyā for Savitṛ; the sūtra says: य इमा विश्वा जातानि सुत्रमाणं पृथिवीं द्यामनेहसं (*ya imā viśvā jātāni sutramāṇaṃ pṛthivīṃ dyāmanehasaṃ*) (Ā. 4-3). In the Aśvamedha, after the horse has been let loose, a Sāvitrī offering is to be performed at each pressing; in it this Ṛk is the anuvākyā at the midday pressing; the sūtra says: य इमा विश्वा जातान्या देवो यातु सविता सुरत्नः (*ya imā viśvā jātānyā devo yātu savitā suratnaḥ*) (Ā. 10-6). Then the sense of the verse: the god who makes all these born beings, that is, creatures, meaning moving ones, hear श्लोकेन (*ślokena*), that is, by fame; the meaning is that all hear his praise. Or else, eager to give rain, he makes all these born beings hear by the sound of his thunder. And further, Savitṛ impels, sends forth; him we choose.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which
- सविता (*savitā*)—god Savitṛ
- विश्वा (*viśvā*)—all
- इमा जातानि (*imā jātāni*)—these living beings
- श्लोकेन (*ślokena*)—by his own greatness
- अश्रावयति (*aśrāvayati*)—makes manifest
- च (*ca*)—and
- प्र सुवाति (*pra suvāti*)—gives life to all living beings (such a god Savitṛ we worship)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The god Savitṛ who by his own greatness makes all living beings manifest, and who gives life to all living beings, such a god Savitṛ we worship.

##### English translation
Savitri, who proclaims his glory to all these living beings. and gives them life.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In the Prāyaṇīya offering, this Ṛk beginning य इमा विश्वा (*ya imā viśvā*) is to be recited as the yājyā mantra in the oblation made with Savitṛ as the deity; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra य इमा विश्वा जातानि सुत्रामाणं पृथिवीं द्यामनेहसं (*ya imā viśvā jātāni sutrāmāṇaṃ pṛthivīṃ dyāmanehasaṃ*) (Ā. 4-6). **NOTE** And in the Aśvamedha sacrifice, after the horse has been let loose to wander, the after-pressing offering called सावित्रिपति (*sāvitripati*) is to be performed. In that offering, at the time of the midday pressing, this Ṛk is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra य इमा विश्वा जातान्या देवो यातु सविता सुरत्नः (*ya imā viśvā jātānyā devo yātu savitā suratnaḥ*) (Ā. 10-6). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 199

## Sūkta 83 (Maṇḍala 5)

**Introduction**

**A brief account of the deity Parjanya**

Although the subject of the deity Parjanya is indicated in many Ṛks of the Ṛgveda, the sūktas that praise this deity alone are only three. Among them the 83rd sūkta, which is the present subject, is very important. There are ten Ṛks in all in it. In these Ṛks the nature of the deity Parjanya is well described. On the question who this deity Parjanya is, Yāska has given the etymology: पर्जन्यः । पर्जनस्यपेराद्यंतविषरीतस्य तर्पयिता जन्यः । परो जेता ना । परो जनयिता वा । प्रार्जयिता वा रसानां ॥ (*parjanyaḥ | parjanasyaperādyaṃtaviṣarītasya tarpayitā janyaḥ | paro jetā nā | paro janayitā vā | prārjayitā vā rasānāṃ ‖*) (Ni. 10-10). That is, the word parjanya is formed from the root तृप (*tṛpa*) (to satisfy). By transposing the letters of this root and by adding the (qualifying) word जन्य (*janya*), it becomes पर्जन्य (*parjanya*). How: in तृप (*tṛpa*), if the ṛ-letter (र् (*r*)) of the first syllable is added at the end, and the last letter, प (*pa*), at the beginning, there results पर् (*par*). If जन्य (*janya*) is added to this, पर्जन्य (*parjanya*) results. Because he pours down the rain (vṛṣṭi), which is most necessary to people and productive of food and the like, he satisfies the people: this is the component meaning of the word Parjanya. Or परो जेता वा (*paro jetā vā*): it may also mean one who is excellent and victorious. Or परो जनयिता वा (*paro janayitā vā*): or it may mean that this god, by pouring rain, is the producer of plants and the like and of food and the like. Or प्रार्थयिता वा रसानां (*prārthayitā vā rasānāṃ*): rasa means water; since he provides water to the people by means of rain, this god is named Parjanya. In this way Yāska has explained the formation of the word Parjanya. To explain this meaning he has given the second Ṛk of this very sūkta as an example and has also stated its etymology; we have explained it in the particulars of that Ṛk. The main purport is that in this sūkta the god who is the cause of rain, accompanied by thunder, lightning, flashes and storm, is praised as Parjanya.

This Parjanya is praised not only in this sūkta but also in the 101st and 102nd sūktas of the seventh Maṇḍala; in the 101st there are only six Ṛks and in the 102nd only three Ṛks. But the nature, qualities and so on of Parjanya are not set forth so clearly as in the present sūkta. In the seventh

### Printed p. 200

Maṇḍala, although the deity of the 12th sūkta is the Maṇḍūkas (frogs), by option (in meaning) that sūkta also is concerned with Parjanya.

Parjanya is the lord of all moving beings. (यो विश्वस्य जगतो देव ईशे (*yo viśvasya jagato deva īśe*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-101-2); in this god all the worlds, and the three worlds of the atmosphere, are established. That is, this god is the support of all the worlds. (यस्मिन्विश्वा भुवनानि तस्थुस्तिस्रो द्यावस्त्रेधा सस्तुरापः (*yasminviśvā bhuvanāni tasthustisro dyāvastredhā sasturāpaḥ*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-101-4); in this god the self of all beings abides. That is, this god is the impeller of all beings, moving and unmoving. (तस्मिन्नात्मा जगतस्तस्थुषश्च (*tasminnātmā jagatastasthuṣaśca*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-101-6); this god is said to be the son of heaven or of the heaven-deity (दिवस्पुत्राय (*divasputrāya*), Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-102-1). The subject of the deity Parjanya is stated not only in the sūktas mentioned above but also in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-57-8; 5-63-4, 6; 6-52-6; 7-55-10; 8-6-1; 8-21-18; 9-2-9; 9-22-2; 10-66-6; 10-98-1, 8; 10-169-2. And in the Ṛks Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-49-6; 6-50-12; 10-65-9; 10-66-10 he is praised together with the deity Vāta (Vāyu), and in Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-52-16 together with Agni, as a dual deity: पर्जन्यावाता (*parjanyāvātā*), वातापर्जन्या (*vātāparjanyā*), अग्नीपर्जन्यौ (*agnīparjanyau*). And in Ṛ. Saṃ. 9-82-3 there is the description that Parjanya is the father of the Soma plant (पर्जन्य पिता महिषस्य पर्णिनः (*parjanya pitā mahiṣasya parṇinaḥ*)).

The place of this deity in the Ṛgveda is very unimportant: this deity is praised in only three sūktas, and this name occurs only thirty times. In the Atharvaveda also (4-15) one sūkta has Parjanya as its deity, but the Ṛks in that sūkta are for the most part chosen from the Ṛgveda. In the passages that follow, Parjanya generally means simply the familiar cloud. The rain-clouds (Parjanya) quicken the earth (1-164-51). The Maruts make darkness even by day with the water-bearing clouds (Parjanya) (1-38-9). They spread the clouds in both worlds (5-53-6). Brhaspati is prayed to make the rain fall and to send the rain-cloud (10-98-1 and 8). Soma flows like a rain-cloud (9-2-9), and the Soma drops fall as rain falls from a cloud (9-22-2). In the Atharvaveda, in praising the rain-pouring cow called Vaśā, it is said: "O Parjanya, your udder" (A. Ne. 10-10-7). But in (Vā. Saṃ. 12-6) Parjanya means Stanayitnu (lightning, thunder), and in (Śa. Brā. 14-5-5-10) it is used in the sense of Dyauḥ (Vā. Saṃ. 12-6). **NOTE** In some contexts it is difficult to say whether the conventional meaning or the deity is intended. Like Parjanya, Agni's power gives an echo (8-91-5). Awakened by Parjanya the frogs croak (7-103-1). That Parjanya means the deity is clearly seen in many passages.

### Printed p. 201

In such contexts the cloud is called by names such as udder, pot, water-bag (5-83-8 and 9; 7-101-4). To some extent the deity Parjanya is described as an animal such as a bull. The bull, full of swiftly flowing water and bellowing loudly (compare 5-83-1; 5-83-6 and 9; A. Ve. 4-15-1); the water that comes from the hissing, watery bull and roars gladdens the earth (A. Ve. 4-15-1). There are descriptions that Parjanya at times is a barren cow, at times a milch cow, and that Parjanya uses his body as he wishes (7-101-3). Pouring rain is the main work of Parjanya. Moving about in a chariot made of water (5-83-7), he unties the water-bag and holds it mouth downwards. As a charioteer urges on his horses, Parjanya shows forth his rain-messengers; when the rain falls, a lion's roar is heard from afar. Pouring rain, the roaring Parjanya comes like our heavenly father (Asura) (5-83-3 and 6). He is prayed to pour rain (7-101-5) and, when he has poured, to stop (5-83-10). Parjanya and the Maruts pour rain. While remaining subject to Mitra and Varuṇa (5-63-3 to 6), it is also said that he often roars (5-83). Roaring, he overthrows trees, demons, evil-doers, all. The whole world is afraid of his weapon (5-83-2). He and Vāta are both the ones who wield that weapon (10-66-10). There is also some connection with the thunderbolt. When Parjanya quickens the earth with seeds, the wind blows and lightning strikes (5-83-4). Parjanya, together with the lightning, thunders in the ocean (A. Ve. 19-30-4).

Because he pours rain, Parjanya is the cause of the growth of plants. When seed is sown in the earth, the plants spring up. All kinds of plants come within his sphere of action; for nourishment he has produced the plants (5-83-4, 5 and 10; compare 6-52-6; A. Ve. 4-15-2, 3 and 15; 8-7-21). He is the one who makes plants increase and bear fruit. Protected by that deity, the plants bear good fruit (7-101-1 and 5). By his action reeds and grass grow (7-102-1; compare 5-75-15; A. Ve. 1-2-1; 1-3-1; 9-30-5). Parjanya places the seed (the embryonic germ) not only in plants but also in cows, mares and women (7-102-2), and he is prayed to for fertility (5-83-7; compare 6-52-16). He is the bull that makes everything fruitful; in him is the self of all things moving and unmoving in the world (7-101-6; compare 1-15-1).

### Printed p. 202

He is the ruler of the whole world, a king of complete independence; in him all beings and the three worlds are established. In him the threefold water flows (7-101-2, 4 and 5). Because of this generative power the epithet "father" is applied to him many times (7-101-3; 9-82-3; A. Ve. 4-15-12; 12-1-12). He is our (king, father) heavenly father (5-83-6).

The earth is suggested to be his wife (5-83-4; 7-101-3; compare 1-160-3). In the Atharvaveda (12-1-12) the earth is the mother and Parjanya the father, and in another place (11-10-6) it is stated clearly that one called Vaśā is his wife. In calling himself a bull and in being connected with thunder, lightning and rain, Parjanya is equal to the heaven-deity (compare 10-45 4; 2-4-6; 2-27-15); in one place it is said that Parjanya is the son of the heaven-deity (7-102-1). Parjanya himself produces a calf; this is the sprout of the plants (7-101-1; compare 5-83-1). This calf may be the lightning or Soma. In one place Parjanya is said to be the father of Soma (9-82-3), and that it grew through Parjanya (9-113-3).

Parjanya has connection with many other deities, but the connection is greatest with Vāta. Except for one occasion with Agni, it is with Parjanya alone that Vāta is spoken of as a dual deity. The Maruts also are praised together with Parjanya (5-63-6; 5-83-5); they praise Parjanya (A. Ve. 4-15-4). In two mantras Agni is praised together with him (6-52-6 and 16). Parjanya, who gives rain, and Indra have many qualities in common (8-6-1); the same part of nature is the basis of both deities, but in the case of Parjanya this is more evident.

The derivation of this word is not clear. In the Ṛgveda, by this form both the conventional meaning, the cloud, and the deity presiding over the cloud are intended. In the Brāhmaṇas too both these meanings appear to be current. In the Mahābhārata Indra and Parjanya are both regarded as one.

The main purport of this sūkta has been described by the Western scholar Muir in the following way:

##### English translation
1. "Address the powerful (god) with these words; laud Parjanya; worship him
with reverenc: the procreative and stimulating fructifier, resounding, sheds his seed and
impregnates the plants.
2. He splits the trees, he destroys the Rakshasas ; the whole creation is afraid of
the mighty stroke ; even the innocent man flees before the vigorous god, when Parjanya
thundering smites the evil doers.

### Printed p. 203

1. Like a charioteer urging forward his horses with a whip, the god brings into view his showery scouts. From afar the lion's roarings arise, when Parjanya charges the clouds with rain.
4. The winds blow. the lightnings fall, the plants shoot up ; the heaven fructifies; food is produced for all created things, when Parjanya, thundering, replenishes the earth with moisture.
5. Parjanya, before whose agency the earth bows down, at whose operation all hoofed cattle quiver ; by whose action plants (spring up) of every form; grant your mighty protection.
6. Gran to us, Maruts, the rain of the sky ; replenish the streams of the procreative horse ; come hither with this your thunder, our divine father, shedding waters.
7. Resound, thunder, Impregnate, rush hither and thither with your watery chariot. Draw on forward with you. your opened and inverted water-skin : let the hills and dale be levelled
8. Raise aloft your vast water-vessel, and pour down showers ; let the discharged rivulets roll on forward : mosisten the heaven and earth with fatness ; lat there be well-filled drinking places for the cows.
9. When you, Parjanya, resounding and thundering, slay the evil-doers, the whole universe rejoices, whatever lives upon the earth.
10. You have shed down rain : now desist ; you have made the waterless wastes fit to be traversed ; you have generated plants for food, and you have fulfilled the desires of living creatures.

**NOTE**

(The introduction ends.)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अच्छा वदेति दशर्चमेकादशं सूक्तं भौमस्यात्रेरार्षं । अत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका । अच्छ दशात्रिः पार्जन्यमुपाद्यास्तिस्रो जगत्य उपांत्यानुष्टुबिति । यत्पर्जन्येत्येषा नवम्यनुष्टुप् वि वृक्षानित्याद्यास्तिस्रो जगत्यः शिष्टाः षट् त्रिष्टुभः ।
> पर्जन्यो देवता ॥ अनेन सूक्तेन प्रत्यृचं वा दिश उपस्थेयाः । सूत्रितं च ।
> संस्थितायां सर्वा दिश उपतिष्ठेताच्छा वद तवसं गीर्भिराभिरिति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं
> सूक्तेन वा । आ- २-१३ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning अच्छा वद (*acchā vada*) has ten Ṛks and is the eleventh sūkta; its seer is Atri, son of Bhūmi. On it the Anukramaṇikā says: अच्छ दशात्रिः पार्जन्यमुपाद्यास्तिस्रो जगत्य उपांत्यानुष्टुबिति । (*accha daśātriḥ pārjanyamupādyāstisro jagatya upāṃtyānuṣṭubiti |*). The deity is Parjanya, and three Ṛks, beginning with the first, are Jagatī, the penultimate being Anuṣṭubh. The ninth Ṛk, यत्पर्जन्य (*yatparjanya*), is Anuṣṭubh; the three beginning वि वृक्षानि (*vi vṛkṣāni*) are Jagatī; the remaining six are Triṣṭubh. The deity is Parjanya. Ritual use: with this sūkta, or with each Ṛk, the directions are to be addressed. The sūtra says: संस्थितायां सर्वा दिश उपतिष्ठेताच्छा वद तवसं गीर्भिराभिरिति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं सूक्तेन वा । (*saṃsthitāyāṃ sarvā diśa upatiṣṭhetācchā vada tavasaṃ gīrbhirābhiriti catasṛbhiḥ pratyṛcaṃ sūktena vā |*) (Ā. 2-13). **NOTE**

### Printed p. 204

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning अच्छा वद (*acchā vada*) is the eleventh sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It has ten Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Atri, the seer of the Maṇḍala. The deity is Parjanya. The ninth Ṛk of this sūkta, beginning यत्पर्जन्य (*yatparjanya*), is in the Anuṣṭubh metre. The three Ṛks beginning विवृक्षान् (*vivṛkṣān*) (Ṛks 2-4) are in the Jagatī metre. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: अच्छ दशात्रिः पार्जन्यमुपाद्यास्तिस्रो जगत्त उपांत्यानुष्टुप् (*accha daśātriḥ pārjanyamupādyāstisro jagatta upāṃtyānuṣṭup*). With each Ṛk of this sūkta, or with the whole sūkta, an address (prayer) to the directions is to be made; this is set forth by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra संस्थितायां सर्वा दिश उपतिष्ठेताच्छा वद तवसं गीर्भिराभिरिति चतसृभिः प्रत्यृचं सूक्तेन वा (*saṃsthitāyāṃ sarvā diśa upatiṣṭhetācchā vada tavasaṃ gīrbhirābhiriti catasṛbhiḥ pratyṛcaṃ sūktena vā*) (Ā. 2-13).

##### Particulars of the sūkta

- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Sūkta 83
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 27, 28
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 10
- Seer: Atri
- Deity: Parjanya
- Metre: Ṛks 1, 5-8 Triṣṭubh; Ṛks 2-4 Jagatī; Ṛk 9 Anuṣṭubh **NOTE**

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अच्छा॑ वद त॒वसं॑ गी॒र्भिरा॒भिः स्तु॒हि प॒र्जन्यं॒ नम॒सा वि॑वास ।  
> कनि॑क्रदद्वृष॒भो जी॒रदा॑नू॒ रेतो॑ दधा॒त्योष॑धीषु॒ गर्भं॑ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अच्छ । वद । तवसं । गीःभिः । आभिः । स्तुहि । पर्जन्यं । नमसा । आ । विवास ।  
> कनिक्रदत् । वृषभः । जीरदानुः । रेतः । दधाति । ओषधीषु । गर्भं ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे स्तोतस्तवसं बलवंतं पर्जन्यमच्छाभिप्राप्य वद । प्रार्थय ॥ पर्जन्यशब्दो यास्केन बहुधा निरुक्तः । पर्जन्यस्तृपेराद्यंतविपरीतस्य तर्पयिता जन्यः
> परो जेता ना जनयिता वा प्रार्जयिता वा रसानां । नि. १०-१० । इति ॥ आभिर्गिर्भिः

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O praiser, go to the mighty Parjanya and speak, that is, pray. Sāyaṇa notes that the word parjanya has been explained in many ways by Yāska: पर्जन्यस्तृपेराद्यंतविपरीतस्य तर्पयिता जन्यः परो जेता ना जनयिता वा प्रार्जयिता वा रसानां । (*parjanyastṛperādyaṃtaviparītasya tarpayitā janyaḥ paro jetā nā janayitā vā prārjayitā vā rasānāṃ |*) (Ni. 10-10). आभिः (*ābhiḥ*) means with these words of praise (continued below).

### Printed p. 205

> स्तुतिवाग्भिः स्तुहि । नमसान्नेन हविर्लक्षणेना विवास । सर्वतः परिचर। यः पर्जन्यो
> वृषभोपां वर्षिता जीरदानुः क्षिप्रदानः कनिक्रदत् गर्जनशब्दं कुर्वन्नोषधीषु
> गर्भं गर्भस्थानीयं रेत उदकं दधाति स्थापयति तं स्तुहि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Praise him with these words of praise. With नमसा (*namasā*), that is, with food in the form of oblation, serve him on all sides. Parjanya, the bull, the one who rains the waters, जीरदानुः (*jīradānuḥ*), quick in giving, making the sound of thunder, कनिक्रदत् (*kanikradat*), places in the plants the germ, that is, the seed-like water standing in the place of an embryo: praise him.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे स्तोतः (*he stotaḥ*)—O praiser
- तवसं (*tavasaṃ*)—mighty
- पर्जन्यं (*parjanyaṃ*)—the god Parjanya
- अच्छ (*accha*)—approaching
- वद (*vada*)—pray
- अभिः गीर्भिः (*abhiḥ gīrbhiḥ*)—with these praises
- स्तुहि (*stuhi*)—praise
- नमसा (*namasā*)—with food in the form of oblation
- आ विवास (*ā vivāsa*)—serve fully
- वृषभः (*vṛṣabhaḥ*)—who pours rain
- जीरदानुः (*jīradānuḥ*)—who quickly gives what is desired; such Parjanya
- कनिक्रदत् (*kanikradat*)—making the sound of thunder
- ओषधीषु (*oṣadhīṣu*)—in the plants (herbs and the like)
- गर्भं (*garbhaṃ*)—standing in the place of an embryo
- रेतः (*retaḥ*)—water
- दधाति (*dadhāti*)—places

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O praiser, approach the mighty god Parjanya and pray. Praise him with these praises. Serve him fully with food in the form of oblation. Parjanya, who pours rain and quickly gives what is desired, making the sound of thunder places in the plants (herbs and the like) the water that stands in the place of an embryo.

##### English translation
Address the mighty Parjanya who is present : praise him with these hymns ; worship him with reverence, him who is the thunderer, the showerer, the bountiful. who impregnates the plants with rain.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि वृ॒क्षान् हं॑त्यु॒त हं॑ति र॒क्षसो॒ विश्वं॑ बिभाय॒ भुव॑नं  ॒॑
> महानधात् ।  
> उ॒ताना॑गा ईषते॒ वृष्णा॑वतो॒ यत्प॒र्जन्यः॑ स्त॒नय॒न् हंति॑  ॒॑
> दुष्कृतः ॥ २ ॥

### Printed p. 206

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वि । वृक्षान् । हंति । उत । हंति । रक्षसः । विश्वं । बिभाय । भुवनं । महावधात् ।  
> उत । अनागाः । ईषते । वृष्ट्यवतः । यत् । पर्जन्यः । स्तनयन् । हंति । दुःकृतः॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयं मंत्रो निरुक्ते स्पष्टं व्याख्यातः । तदेवात्र लिख्यते । पर्जन्यो विहंति
> वृक्षान्विहंति च रक्षांसि सर्वाणि चास्माद्भूतानि बिभ्यति महावधात् महान् ह्यस्य वधः।
> अप्यनपराधो भीतः पलायते वर्षकर्मवतो यत्पर्जन्यः स्तनयन्यंति दुष्कृतः
> पापकृतः । नि. १०-११ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sāyaṇa says that this mantra is clearly explained in the Nirukta and writes down that same explanation here: पर्जन्यो विहंति वृक्षान्विहंति च रक्षांसि सर्वाणि चास्माद्भूतानि बिभ्यति महावधात् महान् ह्यस्य वधः। अप्यनपराधो भीतः पलायते वर्षकर्मवतो यत्पर्जन्यः स्तनयन्यंति दुष्कृतः पापकृतः । (*parjanyo vihaṃti vṛkṣānvihaṃti ca rakṣāṃsi sarvāṇi cāsmādbhūtāni bibhyati mahāvadhāt mahān hyasya vadhaḥ| apyanaparādho bhītaḥ palāyate varṣakarmavato yatparjanyaḥ stanayanyaṃti duṣkṛtaḥ pāpakṛtaḥ |*) (Ni. 10-11). Parjanya strikes down trees and strikes down demons; all beings fear him because of his great weapon, for great is his stroke; even the innocent man, afraid, runs away from the one who does the work of rain, when Parjanya, thundering, strikes the evil-doer, that is, the one who does sinful deeds.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पर्जन्यः (*parjanyaḥ*)—Parjanya
- वृक्षान् (*vṛkṣān*)—trees
- वि हंति (*vi haṃti*)—throws down
- उत (*uta*)—and
- रक्षसः (*rakṣasaḥ*)—demons
- हंति (*haṃti*)—destroys
- महावधात् (*mahāvadhāt*)—with his great weapon
- विश्वं भुवनं (*viśvaṃ bhuvanaṃ*)—the whole world
- बिभाय (*bibhāya*)—frightens
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- पर्जन्यः (*parjanyaḥ*)—Parjanya
- स्तनयन् (*stanayan*)—thundering
- दुष्कृतः (*duṣkṛtaḥ*)—sinners
- हंति (*haṃti*)—kills (then)
- वृष्ट्यवतः (*vṛṣṭyavataḥ*)—by this Parjanya, the maker of rain
- उत अनागाः (*uta anāgāḥ*)—even the innocent
- ईषते (*īṣate*)—(frightened) run away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Parjanya throws down trees and destroys demons. With his great weapon he rebukes the whole world. When Parjanya, thundering, kills sinners, then even the innocent, frightened by this Parjanya, the maker of rain, run away.

##### English translation
He strikes down the trees, he destroys the Rakshasas, he terrifies the whole world by his mighty weapon : even the innocent man flies from the sender of rain, when Parjanya, thundering, slays the wicked.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

For this mantra Yāska has given the explanation: विहंति वृक्षान् विहंति च रक्षांसि सर्वाणि चास्मद्भूतानि बिभ्यति महावधान्महान् ह्यस्य वधोऽप्यनपराधो भीतः पलायते वर्षकर्मवतः यत्पर्जन्यः स्तनयन् हंति दुष्कृतः पापकृतः ॥ (*vihaṃti vṛkṣān vihaṃti ca rakṣāṃsi sarvāṇi cāsmadbhūtāni bibhyati mahāvadhānmahān hyasya vadho 'pyanaparādho bhītaḥ palāyate varṣakarmavataḥ yatparjanyaḥ stanayan haṃti duṣkṛtaḥ pāpakṛtaḥ ‖*) (Ni. 10-11).

### Printed p. 207

This god Parjanya strikes the trees and throws them down. And he also smites the demons. Seeing this kind of devastation (work of destruction) all beings are afraid. This work of slaying is very terrible. Even the innocent man, in great fear, seeing this terrible deed, runs away. The chief work of this Parjanya is indeed the pouring of rain. While pouring rain, appearing terrible with lightning and flashes and with a great roar, he strikes and kills those who do sinful deeds: this is the main purport. Here, because the thunderbolt strikes trees and beings and they are destroyed, this very matter is described here by the sentence that Parjanya strikes and kills the doers of sinful deeds.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> रथीव कशयाश्वा अभिक्षिपन्ना विर्दूतान्‌कृणुते वर्ष्यां ३ अह।  
> दू॒रात्सिं॒हस्य॑ स्त॒नथा॒ उदी॑रते॒ यत्प॒र्जन्यः॑ कृणु॒ते व॒र्ष्य०१॒॑ १  ॑
> नभः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> रथीऽइव । कशया । आश्वान् । अभिक्षिपन् । आविः । दूतान् । कृणुते । वर्ष्यान् ।  
> अह ।  
> दूरात् । सिंहस्य । स्तनथाः । उत् । ईरते । यत् । पर्जन्यः । कृणुते । वर्ष्यं ।  
> नभः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> रथीव रथस्वामीव । स यथा कशयाश्वानभिक्षिपन् दूतान् भटानाविष्करोति
> तद्वदसौ पर्जन्योऽपि कशयाश्वान्मेघानभिक्षिपन् अभिप्रेरयन् वर्ष्यान् वर्षकान्
> दूतान् दूतवद्वृष्टिप्रेरकान्मेघान् मरुतो नाविष्कृणुते । प्रकटयति । अहेति
> पूरणः । एवं सति सिंहस्य ॥ सहतेर्हिंसतेर्वा शब्दकर्मणः सिंहशब्दः ॥ अवर्षणेनाभिभवितुः शब्दयितुर्वा मेघस्य स्तनथा गर्जनशब्दा दूरादुदीरते । उद्दच्छंति ।
> कदा ! यद्यदा पर्जन्यो नभोऽंतरिक्षं वर्ष्यं वर्षोपेशं कृणुते करोति तदा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Like the master of a chariot: as he, urging the horses with the whip, makes his messengers, that is, his warriors, manifest, so this Parjanya also, urging with the whip the clouds, which are his horses, makes manifest the rain-giving messengers, that is, the clouds that like messengers impel the rain, the Maruts. अह (*aha*) is an expletive. This being so, from afar rise the thundering sounds of the cloud, which overpowers or sounds by not raining and which is called the lion, the word सिंह (*siṃha*) being from a root meaning to be strong or to injure, a root of the action of sound. When? When Parjanya makes the sky, नभः (*nabhaḥ*), that is, the atmosphere, rain-bearing.

### Printed p. 208

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- रथीव (*rathīva*)—as a charioteer who drives the chariot strikes the horses with his whip and, bringing the warriors who are in the chariot, makes them visible to all, so (Parjanya also)
- कशया (*kaśayā*)—with his whips
- अश्वान् (*aśvān*)—the clouds
- अभिक्षिपन् (*abhikṣipan*)—urging
- वर्ष्यान् (*varṣyān*)—rain-making
- दूतान् (*dūtān*)—those that are like messengers, the clouds
- आविष्कृणुते (*āviṣkṛṇute*)—makes visible to all
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- पर्जन्यः (*parjanyaḥ*)—Parjanya
- नभः (*nabhaḥ*)—the atmosphere
- वर्ष्यं (*varṣyaṃ*)—filled with rain
- कृणुते (*kṛṇute*)—makes (then)
- सिंहस्य (*siṃhasya*)—of the clouds that sound like a lion
- स्तनथाः (*stanathāḥ*)—the sounds of thunder
- दूरात् (*dūrāt*)—from afar
- उदीरते (*udīrate*)—become manifest (are heard)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As a charioteer who drives the chariot strikes the horses with his whip and, bringing the warriors who are in the chariot, makes them visible to all, so Parjanya also, urging the clouds with his whips, makes visible to all the clouds that make rain and are like messengers. When Parjanya fills the atmosphere with rain, then the thundering sounds of the clouds that sound like a lion are heard from afar.

##### English translation
As a charioteer, urging his horses with his whip, brings into view
the messenger (of war), so Parjanya. (driving the clouds before him),
make manifest the messengers of the rain: the roaring of the lion (like
cloud) proclaims from afar that Parjanya overspreads the sky with
rainy clouds.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र वाता॒ वांति॑ प॒तयं॑तिवि॒द्युत॒ उदोष॑धी॒र्जिह॑ते॒ पिन्व॑ते॒ स्वः॑।  
> इता॒ विश्व॑स्मै॒ भुव॑नाय जायते॒ यत्प॒र्जन्यः॑ पृथि॒वीं रेत॒साव॑ति॥

### Printed p. 209

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । वाताः । वांति । पतयंति । विद्युतः । उत् । ओषधीः । जिहते । पिन्वते । स्वगरिति स्वः ।  
> इरा । विश्वस्मै । भुवनाय । जायते । यत् । सर्जन्यः । पृथिवीं । रेतसा । अवति ॥४॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्रवाता इति चतुर्थी पर्जन्यस्य चरोर्याज्या । सूत्रितं च । प्र वाता वांति
> पतयंति विद्युत् इत्यग्न्याधेयप्रकृति । आ. २-१५ । इति ॥
> प्रवांति वाता वृष्ट्यर्थं । पतयंति गच्छंति समंतात्संचरंति विद्युतः ।
> ओषधीरोषधय उज्जिहते । उद्दच्छंति । प्रवर्धंते । स्वरंतरिक्षं पिन्वते । क्षरति ।
> इरा भूमिर्विश्वस्यॆ सर्वस्मै भुवनाय सर्वजगद्धिताय जायते । समर्था भवति ।
> कदैवमिति । यद्यदा पर्जन्यो देवः पृथिवीं रेतसोदकेनावति रक्षति अभिगच्छति ना
> तदैवं भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The fourth Ṛk, beginning प्रवाता (*pravātā*), is the yājyā in Parjanya's caru offering. The sūtra says: प्र वाता वांति पतयंति विद्युत् इत्यग्न्याधेयप्रकृति (*pra vātā vāṃti patayaṃti vidyut ityagnyādheyaprakṛti*) (Ā. 2-15). The winds blow forth for the sake of rain; the lightnings fly, that is, move about everywhere; the plants spring up, that is, rise and grow; the heaven, स्वः (*svaḥ*), the atmosphere, swells, that is, pours; Irā, the earth, becomes capable for the whole world, for the benefit of the whole universe. When is this so? When the god Parjanya protects the earth with seed, that is, with water; at that time it is so. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- पर्जन्यः (*parjanyaḥ*)—the god Parjanya
- पृथिवीं (*pṛthivīṃ*)—the earth
- रेतसा (*retasā*)—with water
- अवति (*avati*)—makes fruitful and protects (then)
- वाताः (*vātāḥ*)—the winds
- प्र वांति (*pra vāṃti*)—blow strongly
- विद्युतः (*vidyutaḥ*)—the lightnings
- पतयंति (*patayaṃti*)—dart down from the atmosphere
- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—the plants
- उत् जिहते (*ut jihate*)—come forth and grow
- स्वः (*svaḥ*)—the atmosphere
- पिन्वते (*pinvate*)—becomes liquid
- इरा (*irā*)—the earth
- विश्वस्मै (*viśvasmai*)—for all
- भुवनाय (*bhuvanāya*)—the world
- जायते (*jāyate*)—becomes fit (as a dwelling-place)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

When the god Parjanya makes the earth fruitful with water and protects it, then the winds blow strongly. The lightnings dart down from the atmosphere. The plants come forth. The atmosphere becomes liquid. The earth becomes a fit dwelling-place for the whole world.

##### English translation
The winds blow strong, the lightning flash, the plants spring up, the
the firmament dissolves : earth becomes (fit) for all creatures when Parjanya
fertilizes the soil with showers.

### Printed p. 210

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

The fourth Ṛk of this sūkta, beginning प्रवाता (*pravātā*), is to be recited as the yājyā mantra when a cooked offering (caru) is made to the deity Parjanya. This is made clear by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, in the sūtra प्र वाता वांति पतयंति विद्युत् इत्यग्नाधेयप्रकृति (*pra vātā vāṃti patayaṃti vidyut ityagnādheyaprakṛti*) (Ā. 2-15).

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यस्य॑ व्र॒ते पृ॑थि॒वी नंन॑मीति॒ यस्य॑ व्र॒ते श॒फव॒ज्ज र्भु॑रीति ।  
> यस्य॑ व्र॒त ओष॑धीर्वि॒श्वरू॑पाः॒ स नः॑ पर्जन्य॒ महि॒ शर्म॑ यच्छ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यस्य । व्रते । पृथिवी । नंनमीति । यस्य । व्रते । शफवत् । जर्भुरीति ।  
> यस्य । व्रते । ओषधीः । विश्वरूपाः । सः । नः । पर्जन्य । महि । शर्म । यच्छ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यस्य पर्जन्यस्य व्रते कर्मणि पृथिवी नंनमाति अत्यर्थं नमति सर्वॆषामधो भवति । यस्य व्रते शफवत् पादोपेतं गवादिकं जर्भुरीति भ्रियते
> पूर्यते गच्छतीति वा । यस्य व्रते कर्मण्योषधीरोषध्यो विश्वरूपा नानारूपा
> भवंति हे पर्जन्य स महांस्त्वं नोऽस्मभ्यं महि शर्म महत् सुखं यच्छ ।
> प्रयच्छ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the vrata, that is the action, of Parjanya the earth bows down exceedingly, that is, becomes lower than all. In his vrata the hoofed creatures such as cattle are nourished (or are filled, or move about). In his vrata, in his action, the plants take all forms, that is, many forms. O Parjanya, such a great one as you, grant us great happiness.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यस्य (*yasya*)—of you, whose
- व्रते (*vrate*)—by the power of the action
- पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*)—the earth
- नंनमाति (*naṃnamāti*)—is full of humility
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of you, whose
- व्रते (*vrate*)—by the greatness of the actions
- शफवत् (*śaphavat*)—hoofed animals and the like
- जर्भुरीति (*jarbhurīti*)—grow stout
- यस्य (*yasya*)—of you, whose
- व्रते (*vrate*)—by the power of the action
- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—the plants
- विश्वरूपाः (*viśvarūpāḥ*)—grow in many forms
- सः (*saḥ*)—such a one as you
- पर्जन्य (*parjanya*)—O god Parjanya
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- महि (*mahi*)—abundantly (plentifully)
- शर्म (*śarma*)—happiness
- यच्छ (*yaccha*)—grant

### Printed p. 211

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O god Parjanya, by the power of whose action the earth is full of humility, by the greatness of whose actions hoofed animals and the like grow stout, by the power of whose action the plants grow in many forms, such a one as you, grant us happiness in abundance.

##### English translation
O Parjanya, through whose function the earth is bowed down :
through whose function hoofed cattle thrive ; through whose function plants
assume all kinds of forms, grant us great felicity.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दि॒वो नो॑ वृ॒ष्टिं म॑रुतो ररीध्वं॒ प्र पि॑न्वत॒ वृष्णो॒ अश्व॑स्य॒  ॑
> धाराः ।  
> अ॒र्वाजे॒तेन॑ स्तनयि॒त्नु नेह्य॒पो नि॑षिं॒चन्न सु॑रः पि॒ता नः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दिवः । नः । वृष्टिं । मरुतः । ररीध्वं । प्र । पिन्वत । वृष्णः । अश्वस्य । धाराः ।  
> अर्वाज् । एतेन । स्तनयित्नुना । आ । इहि । अपः । निसिंचन् । असुरः । पिता । नः ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतो यूयं दिवोऽंतरिक्षसकाशात् नोऽस्मदर्थं वृष्टिं ररीध्वं ।
> दत्त । वृष्णो वर्षकस्याश्वस्य व्यापकस्य मेघस्य संबंधिन्नो धारा उदकधारा प्र
> पिन्वत । प्रक्षरत । हे पर्जन्य त्वमेतेन स्तनयित्सुना गर्जता मेघेन सहार्वाज्
> अस्मदभिमुखमेहि । आगच्छ । किं कुर्वन् । अपोऽंभांसि निषिंचन् स देवोऽसुर
> उदकानां निरसितापि सन् नोऽस्माकं पिता पालकश्च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Maruts, you give us rain from the sky (the atmosphere). Cause the streams of water belonging to the rain-giving, pervading horse, that is, the cloud, to flow forth. O Parjanya, come toward us together with this thundering, roaring cloud. What are you doing? Pouring down water. That god, though he is the one who casts down the waters (the asura), is also our father, our protector.

### Printed p. 212

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from the atmosphere
- नः (*naḥ*)—for us
- वृष्टिं (*vṛṣṭiṃ*)—the rain
- ररीध्वं (*rarīdhvaṃ*)—pour down and send
- वृष्णः (*vṛṣṇaḥ*)—of the rain-making
- अश्वस्य (*aśvasya*)—and pervading cloud, belonging to it
- धाराः (*dhārāḥ*)—the streams of water
- प्र पिन्नत (*pra pinnata*)—increase and pour down abundantly (O Parjanya)
- एतेन (*etena*)—with this thundering cloud
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—water
- निषिंचन् (*niṣiṃcan*)—pouring down in the form of rain
- अर्वाक् (*arvāk*)—turned toward us
- ऐहि (*aihi*)—be pleased to come
- असुरः (*asuraḥ*)—you, the mighty one (the maker of rain)
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- पिता (*pitā*)—a protector, you are

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, pour down rain for us from the atmosphere. Increase the streams of water belonging to the rain-making, pervading cloud, and pour them down abundantly. O Parjanya, be pleased to come, pouring down water in the form of rain from this thundering cloud. You, the mighty one, as the maker of rain, are a protector to us.

##### English translation
Send down for us, Maruts, the rain from heaven : drops of the rainy
charger descend : come down Parjanya, sprinkling water by this thundering
(cloud) ; you who are the sender of rain, our protector.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒भि क्रं॑द स्त॒नय॒ गर्भ॒मा धा॑ उद॒न्वता॒ परि॑ दीया॒ रथे॑न ।  
> दृतिं॒ सु क॑र्ष॒ विषि॑तं॒ न्यं॑चं स॒मा भ॑वंतू॒द्वतो॑ निपा॒दाः ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अभि । क्रंद । स्तनय । गर्भं । आ । धाः । उदन्वता । परि । दीय । रथेन ।  
> दृतिं । सु । कर्ष । विसितं । न्यंचं । समाः । भवंतु । उत्वतः । निपादाः ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 213

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अभि भूम्यभिमुखं क्रंद । शब्दय । तदेव पुनरुच्यते दार्ढ्याय । स्तनय ।
> गर्ज । गर्भ० गर्भस्थानीयमुदकमोषधीष्वा धाः । आधेहि । तदर्थमुदन्वतोदकवता रथेन परि दीय । परितो गच्छ । दृतिं दृतिवदुदकधारकं मेघं विषितं
> विशेषेण सितं बद्धं न्यंचं नृक् अधोमुखं सु सुष्टु कर्ष । आकर्ष वृष्ट्यर्थं ।
> यद्वा । विषितं विमुक्तबंधनमेवं कर्ष । एवं कृत उद्धत ऊर्ध्ववंत उन्नतप्रदेशा निपादा न्यग्भूतपादा निकृष्णपादा वा निम्मोन्नतप्रदेशाः समा एकस्था भवंतु ।
> उदकपूर्णा भवंत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Cry out toward the earth, make sound. The same is repeated for emphasis. Thunder, roar. Place the embryo, that is, the water that stands in the place of an embryo, in the plants. For that purpose go about on every side with a chariot filled with water. Draw down properly, for the sake of rain, the cloud, which is like a leather bag holding water, specially bound and turned with its mouth downward. Or else: draw it thus, with its binding loosened. When this is done, the elevated places and the low places (those whose feet are lowered or drawn down) become level, together in one state; the meaning is that they become filled with water.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अभि (*abhi*)—toward the earth
- क्रंद (*kraṃda*)—make sound
- स्तनय (*stanaya*)—thunder
- गर्भं (*garbhaṃ*)—the water in the form of an embryo
- आ धाः (*ā dhāḥ*)—place it in the plants and fill them
- उदन्वता (*udanvatā*)—filled with water
- रथेन (*rathena*)—with a chariot
- परि दीय (*pari dīya*)—move about everywhere
- दृतिं (*dṛtiṃ*)—the cloud, which is like a leather bag
- विषितं (*viṣitaṃ*)—having made it exceedingly well prepared
- न्यंचं (*nyaṃcaṃ*)—with its mouth downward
- सु कर्ष (*su karṣa*)—drive it for the sake of rain
- उद्वतः (*udvataḥ*)—the elevated places
- निपादाः (*nipādāḥ*)—the low places
- समाः (*samāḥ*)—in one and the same manner
- भवंतु (*bhavaṃtu*)—let them become filled with water

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Parjanya, make sound toward the earth. Thunder well, and place in the plants, which are the embryo-water of them, and fill them. Move about everywhere with a chariot filled with water. Make the cloud, which is like a leather bag, well prepared, and drive it with its mouth downward for the sake of rain. May the elevated places and the low places alike be filled with water.

##### English translation
Cry aloud over (the earth) ; thunder ; impregnate the plants ; traverse
(the sky) with your water-laden chariot, draw open the tight-fastened,
downward-turned water bag, and may the low places be made level.

### Printed p. 214

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> म॒हांतं॒ कोश॒मुद॑चा॒ नि षिं॑च॒ स्यंदं॑तां कु॒ल्या विषि॑ताः  ॒॑
> पुरस्तात् ।  
> घृ॒तेन॒ द्यावा॑पृथि॒वी व्युं॑धि सुप्रपा॒णं भ॑वत्व॒घ्न्याभ्यः॑ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> महांतं । कोशं । उत् । अच । नि । सिंच । स्यंदंतां । कुल्याः । विसिताः ।  
> पुरस्तात् ।  
> धृतेन । द्यावापृथिवी इति । वि । उंधि । सुप्रपानं । भवतु । अघ्न्याभ्यः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पर्जन्य त्वं महांतं प्रवृद्धं कोशं कोशस्थानीयं मेघमुदच ।
> उद्गच्छ । उद्गमय वा । तथा कृत्वा नि षिंचॆ । नीचैः क्षारय । कुल्या नद्यो
> विषिता विष्यूताः सत्यः स्यंदंतां प्रवहंतु पुरस्तात् पूर्वाभिमुखं । प्रायेण
> नद्यः प्राच्य स्यंदंते । घृतेनोदकेन द्यावापृथिवी दिवं च पृथिवीं च व्युंधि ।
> क्षेदयात्यधिकं । अघ्न्याभ्यो गोभ्यः सुप्रपाणं सुष्टु प्रकर्षेण पातव्यमुदकं
> भवतु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Parjanya, raise up the great, swollen sheath, that is, the cloud which stands in the place of a sheath, or cause it to rise. Having done so, pour down, make it flow downward. Let the channels, that is, the rivers, released, flow forward, toward the east; for the most part rivers flow eastward. With water (ghṛta) drench heaven and earth thoroughly, make them very wet. For the cows let there be water that is well fit for drinking in abundance.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- महांतं (*mahāṃtaṃ*)—of great form
- कोशं (*kośaṃ*)—the cloud, which is like a bag
- उदच (*udaca*)—lift it up and hold it
- निषिंच (*niṣiṃca*)—pour down (the water) below
- कुल्याः (*kulyāḥ*)—the rivers
- विषिताः (*viṣitāḥ*)—without hindrance
- पुरस्तात् (*purastāt*)—toward the east
- स्यंदंतां (*syaṃdaṃtāṃ*)—let them flow
- धृतेन (*dhṛtena*)—with water
- द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*)—heaven and earth
- व्युंधि (*vyuṃdhi*)—make them wet
- अघ्नाभ्यः (*aghnābhyaḥ*)—for the cows
- सुप्रसाणं (*suprasāṇaṃ*)—water fit for drinking
- भवतु (*bhavatu*)—let it overflow

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Parjanya, lift up and hold the cloud, which is of great form and like a bag. Pour the water down. Let the rivers flow eastward without hindrance. Make heaven and earth wet with water. For the cows, let water fit for drinking overflow.

### Printed p. 215

##### English translation
Raise on high the mighty sheath (of rain), pour down (its contents); let the rivers flow unimpeded to the east ; saturate with water both heaven and earth, and let there be abundant beverage for the cows.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यत्प॑र्जन्य॒ कनि॑क्रदत् स्त॒नय॒न् हंसि॑ दु॒ष्कृतः॑ ।  
> प्रती॒दं विश्वं॑ मोदते॒ यत्किं च॑ पृथि॒व्यामधि॑ ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यत् । पर्जन्य । कनिक्रदत् । स्तनयन् । हंसि । दुःकृतः ।  
> प्रति । इदं । विश्वं । मोदते । यत् । किं । च । पृथिव्यां । अधि ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पर्जन्य यद्यदा त्वं कनिक्रददत्यर्थं शब्दयन् स्तनयन् दुष्कृतः
> पापकृतो मेघान् हंसि विदारयसि तदानीमिदं विश्वं जगत् प्रति मॊदते । विश्वं
> विशेष्यते । यत्किंच पृथिव्यामधि भूम्यामधिष्ठितं यच्चराचरात्मकं तदिदं विश्वं
> मोदते । हृष्यति । वृष्टेः सर्वजगत्प्रीतिकारणत्वं प्रसिद्धं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Parjanya, when you, sounding exceedingly loud and thundering, strike down the wicked (those who do evil) clouds, that is, tear them apart, then this whole world rejoices. The word "whole" is specified: whatever stands upon the earth, whatever is moving or unmoving, this whole world rejoices, is glad. That rain is the cause of the delight of the entire world is well known.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पर्जन्य (*parjanya*)—O Parjanya
- यत् (*yat*)—when
- कनिक्रदत् (*kanikradat*)—you make a sound in the form of roaring (and)
- स्तनयन् (*stanayan*)—thundering
- दुष्कृतः (*duṣkṛtaḥ*)—the wicked
- हंसि (*haṃsi*)—you kill (then)
- इदं विश्वं (*idaṃ viśvaṃ*)—this whole world
- प्रति मोदते (*prati modate*)—rejoices
- पृथिव्यां अधि (*pṛthivyāṃ adhi*)—upon the earth
- यत्किंच (*yatkiṃca*)—whatever living beings there are (all of them rejoice)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Parjanya, when you make a sound in the form of roaring, and thundering kill the wicked, then the whole world rejoices. All living beings that are upon the earth rejoice.

### Printed p. 216

##### English translation
When, Parjanya, sounding loud and thundering, you destroy the wicked (clouds), this whole (world) rejoices, and all that is upon the earth.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अव॑र्षीर्व॒र्षमुदु॒ षू गृ॑भा॒याक॒र्धन्वा॒न्यत्ये॑त॒वा उ॑ ।  
> अजी॑जन॒ ओष॑धी॒र्भोज॑नाय॒ कमु॒त प्र॒जाभ्यो॑ऽविदो मनी॒षां ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अवर्षीः । वर्षं । उत् । ऊं इति । सु । गृभाय । अकः । धन्वानि । अशिएतवै ।  
> ऊं इति ।  
> अजीजनः । ओषधीः । भोजनाय । कं । उत । प्रजाभ्यः । अविदः । मनीषां ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इयमतिवृष्टिविमोचनी ॥ हे पर्जन्य त्वमवर्षिः । वृष्टवानसि । वर्षमुदु
> षू गृभाय । उत्कृष्टं सु सुष्टु गृभाय । गृहाण । परिहरेत्यर्थः । धन्वानि निरुदकप्रदेशानकः । जलवतः कृतवानसि । किमर्थं । अत्येतवा उ । अतिक्रम्य गंतुं ।
> ओषधीरजीजनः । उदपादयः । किमर्थं । भोजनाय धनाय भोगाय वा ।
> कमित्ययं शिशिरं जीवनाय कं । नि. १-१० । इतिवत्पादपूरणः । उतापि च प्रजाभ्यः
> सकाशाक् मनीषां स्तुतिमविदः । प्राप्तवानसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Ṛk is for the release from excessive rain. O Parjanya, you have rained; you have poured down rain. Hold back well the rain, taking it up; the meaning is, withhold it. You have made the waterless regions, the deserts, watery. For what purpose? In order to pass across them. You have brought forth the plants. For what purpose? For food, that is, for wealth or enjoyment. The word कं (*kaṃ*) is merely a filler of the verse-quarter, as in कमित्ययं शिशिरं जीवनाय कं (*kamityayaṃ śiśiraṃ jīvanāya kaṃ*) (Ni. 1-10). And also, from the creatures you have obtained praise (manīṣā), that is, adoration.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अवर्षिः (*avarṣiḥ*)—you have poured down rain (O Parjanya)
- वर्षमुदु (*varṣamudu*)—the same rain that falls on without end **NOTE**
- हू गृभाय (*hū gṛbhāya*)—hold back (so that it may not be excessive) **NOTE**
- धन्वानि (*dhanvāni*)—the waterless deserts
- अत्तेतवा उ (*attetavā u*)—so as to be easy to cross
- अकः (*akaḥ*)—you have made them filled with water
- ओषधीः (*oṣadhīḥ*)—the plants
- भोजनाय (*bhojanāya*)—so as to be fit for the enjoyment of men
- अजीजनः (*ajījanaḥ*)—you have produced
- प्रजाभ्यः (*prajābhyaḥ*)—from men such as the priests
- मनीषां (*manīṣāṃ*)—praise
- अविदः (*avidaḥ*)—you have obtained

### Printed p. 217

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Parjanya, you have poured down rain. Hold back that same rain, which falls on without end, so that it does not fall in a way that troubles us. You have made the waterless deserts full of water so that they may be easy to cross. You have produced the plants so that they may be fit for the enjoyment of men. You have also obtained praise from men such as the priests.

##### English translation
You have rained : now check well the rain : you have made the deserts capable of being crossed : you have given birth to plans for (man's) enjoyment : verily you have obtained laudation from the people.

## Sūkta 84 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> बळित्थेति तृचात्मकं द्वादशं सूक्तं भौमस्यात्रेरार्षं पृथिवीदेवताकमानुष्टुभं । अनुक्रातं च i बळित्था तृचं पार्थिवमानुष्टुभमिति ॥ सूक्तविनियोगो
> लैंगिकः । आद्या देवानां हविषु पृथिव्या अनुवाक्या । बळित्था पर्वतानां दृळ्हा
> चित्या वनस्पतीन् । आ. ६-१४ । इति हि सूत्रितं ॥ भूमिस्तोमे चैषा मरुत्वतीयशस्त्रे
> सूक्तमुखीया । आ. ९-५ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta बळित्था (*baḷitthā*) (बळित्थेति (*baḷittheti*)) is the twelfth sūkta, consisting of three Ṛks. Its seer is Atri, the son of Bhūmi; its deity is Pṛthivī; its metre is Anuṣṭubh. It is stated in the Anukramaṇī: बळित्था तृचं पार्थिवमानुष्टुभमिति (*baḷitthā tṛcaṃ pārthivamānuṣṭubhamiti*). The use of the sūkta is indicated by its characteristic mark (liṅga). The first Ṛk is the anuvākyā for the Pṛthivī in the offerings to the gods; for it is laid down in the sūtra बळित्था पर्वतानां दृळ्हा चित्या वनस्पतीन् (*baḷitthā parvatānāṃ dṛḷhā cityā vanaspatīn*) (Ā. 6-14). And in the Bhūmi-stoma, in the Marutvatīya śastra, this Ṛk is the opening Ṛk of the sūkta (Ā. 9-5).

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta, beginning बळित्था (*baḷitthā*), is the twelfth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It contains three Ṛks. Atri is the seer of this sūkta. The deity is Pṛthivī, that is, the deity of the middle region (the atmosphere). The metre is Anuṣṭup. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: Baḷitthā, a triad, belonging to Pṛthivī, in Anuṣṭubh. The use of the sūkta is according to its mark. It is made clear by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, in the sūtra बळित्था पर्वतानां दृळ्हा चिद्या वनस्पतीन् (*baḷitthā parvatānāṃ dṛḷhā cidyā vanaspatīn*) (Ā. 6-14), that when an oblation is offered to the gods, the first Ṛk of this sūkta is to be recited as the anuvākyā mantra in the offering to the deity Pṛthivī. It is also specified (Ā. 9-5) that among the stoma mantras recited in honour of the earth, this first Ṛk is to be recited as the first Ṛk of the mantras of the Marutvatīya śastra.

### Printed p. 218

##### Particulars

- Sūkta 84
- Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6
- Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 29
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 3
- Seer: Atri
- Deity: Pṛthivī
- Metre: Anuṣṭup

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बळि॒त्था पर्व॑तानां खि॒द्रं बि॑भर्षि पृथिवि ।  
> प्र या भूमिं॑ प्रवत्वति म॒ह्ना जि॒नोषि॑ महिनि ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बट् । इत्था । पर्वतानां । खिद्रं । बिभर्षि । पृथिवि ।  
> प्र । या । भूमिं । प्रवत्वति । मह्ना । जिनोषि । महिनि ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> द्विरूपा पृथिवी चैषा प्रत्यक्षदेवतापि च । मध्यस्थाना देवतोक्ता सात्र
> संबोध्य वर्ण्यते ॥ हे पृथिवि प्रथनवति मध्यस्थानदेवते । त्वमित्थेत्मममुश्रांतरिक्षे
> वा बट् सत्यं पर्वशानां मेघानां वा खिद्रं खेदनं भेदनं बिभर्षि । धारयसि ।
> हे महनि महति हे प्रवत्तति प्रकर्षवति प्रवणोदकवति वा या त्वं भूमिं प्रत्यक्षां
> पृथिवीं मह्ना महत्वेन महतोदकेन वा जिनोषि प्रकर्षणेण प्रीणयसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Pṛthivī is of two forms: this one, which is visible, and also a deity; the one declared to be a deity of the middle region is the one addressed and described here. O Pṛthivī, extensive deity of the middle region, you truly bear the shattering, the splitting, of the mountains, or of the clouds. **NOTE**. O great one, O one of exceeding eminence, or one with abundant flowing water, you who by your greatness, or by great water, delight the visible earth exceedingly.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पृथिवि (*pṛthivi*)—O deity of the middle region
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- बट् (*baṭ*)—truly
- इत्था (*itthā*)—in this manner
- पर्वतानां (*parvatānāṃ*)—of the clouds
- खिद्रं (*khidraṃ*)—the openings
- बिभर्षि (*bibharṣi*)—you bear
- प्रवश्वति (*pravaśvati*)—O you of exceeding qualities
- महिनि (*mahini*)—O goddess of greatness
- या (*yā*)—you, the very one who
- मह्ना (*mahnā*)—by your greatness
- भूमिं (*bhūmiṃ*)—the earth
- प्र जिनोषि (*pra jinoṣi*)—you delight exceedingly

### Printed p. 219

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O deity of the middle region, you bear the pieces of the clouds exceedingly. O goddess of excellent qualities and of greatness, by the same qualities you delight the earth exceedingly.

##### English translation
Verily you sustain here, Prithivi, the fracture of the mountains : mighty and most excellent. you are she who delights the earth by your greatness.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Concerning this sūkta the Bhāṣyakāra has said: द्विरूपा पृथिवी चैषा प्रत्यक्षा देवतापि च । मध्यस्थाना देवतोक्ता सात्र संबोध्य वक्ष्यते ॥ (*dvirūpā pṛthivī caiṣā pratyakṣā devatāpi ca | madhyasthānā devatoktā sātra saṃbodhya vakṣyate ‖*) **NOTE**. That is to say, Pṛthivī is the deity of this sūkta. This Pṛthivī is of two kinds. One kind is the Pṛthivī or Bhūmi that is visible to our eyes. The deity who presides over this earth is well known as a deity of the middle region; this is the second kind. The intention is that it is this second kind, the deity of the middle region, that is addressed and praised in this sūkta.

Pṛthivī: the word पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*) or पृथ्वि (*pṛthvi*) is read among the twenty-one names of Pṛthivī (the earth) (Ni. 2-5) and among the sixteen names of the atmosphere (Ni. 2-10). And the word पृथ्वी (*pṛthvī*) (a word in the dual) is read among the names of heaven and earth (Ni. 3-22).

The form of the word Pṛthivī is derived from the root पृथु विस्तीर्णे (*pṛthu vistīrṇe*). Pṛthivī means a very wide region. This meaning applies to the earth, or Bhūmi, and also to the atmosphere (the sky). Since the atmosphere is wider than the earth, the word Pṛthivī is used in many places in the Ṛgveda to indicate the atmosphere. In the present sūkta it is the middle deity, who dwells in the atmosphere, who is praised. Yāska has given an explanation of this Ṛk: सत्यं त्वं पर्वतातां मेघानां खेदनं भेदनं बलममुत्र धारयसि पृथिवि प्रजिन्वसि या भूमिं प्रणवति महत्त्वेन महतीत्युदकवतीति वा ॥ (*satyaṃ tvaṃ parvatātāṃ meghānāṃ khedanaṃ bhedanaṃ balamamutra dhārayasi pṛthivi prajinvasi yā bhūmiṃ praṇavati mahattvena mahatītyudakavatīti vā ‖*) (Ni. 11-37).

### Printed p. 220

O deity of the middle region, you in truth, staying in the atmosphere, strike or pierce the clouds and display your strength in the atmosphere. O goddess of great form (of vast shape), being of the form of water or bearing water, you make the earth moist by your extraordinary greatness and delight it. This is the main intention.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्तोमा॑सस्त्वा विचारिणि॒ प्रति॑ ष्टोभंत्य॒क्तुभिः॑ ।  
> प्र या वाजं॒ न हेषं॑तं पे॒रुमस्य॑स्यर्जुनि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्तोमासः । त्वा । विचारिणि । प्रति । स्तोभंति । अक्तुभिः ।  
> प्र । या । वाजं । न । हेषंतं । पेरुं । आस्यसि । अर्जुनि ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे विचारिणि विविधंचरणशीले पृथि वि देवि त्वा त्वां स्तोमासः स्तोतारोऽकुभिर्गमनशीलैः स्तोत्रैः प्रति प्वोभंति । अभिष्टुवंति । किंच या त्वं
> हेषंतं शब्दयंतं वाजं न अश्वमिवोद्वृत्तं पेरुं पूरकं मेघां प्रास्यसि प्रक्षिपसि हे अर्जुनि शुभ्रवर्णे गमनशीले वा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Pṛthivī, goddess who move in various ways, the worshippers praise you with their moving hymns. Further, you who cast the filling cloud, which is neighing, that is, roaring, like a horse excited, O bright-coloured one, or O one who goes about, hurl it down.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अर्जुनि (*arjuni*)—O you of bright colour
- विचारिणि (*vicāriṇi*)—O goddess who move in many ways
- या (*yā*)—you, the very one who
- हेषंतं (*heṣaṃtaṃ*)—roaring
- वाजं न (*vājaṃ na*)—as one makes a horse fall downward
- पेरुं (*peruṃ*)—the cloud full of water
- प्रास्यसि (*prāsyasi*)—you make fall downward
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you, the same
- स्तोमासः (*stomāsaḥ*)—the praisers
- अक्तुभिः (*aktubhiḥ*)—by their own hymns
- प्रति ष्टोभंति (*prati ṣṭobhaṃti*)—praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O goddess of the middle region, of bright colour and of manifold movement, as one makes a roaring horse fall downward, you make the cloud, full of water and filled with the sound of thunder, fall downward; the praisers glorify and worship you, so, with their own hymns.

### Printed p. 221

##### English translation
Wanderer in various ways your worshipers hymn you with (sacred)
songs ; you who, bright-hued, toss the swollen (cloud) like a neighing horse.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> दृ॒ळ्हा चि॒द्या वन॒स्पती॑न्क्ष्म॒या दर्ध॒र्ष्योज॑सा ।  
> यत्ते॑ अ॒भ्रस्य॑ वि॒द्युतो॑ दि॒वो वर्षं॑ति वृ॒ष्टयः॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> दृळ्हा । चित् । या । वनस्पतीन् । क्ष्मया । दर्धर्षि । ओजसा ।  
> यत् । ते । अभ्रस्य । विद्युतः । दिवः । वर्षंति । वृष्टयः ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> देवानां हविषु दृळ्ता चिदति पृथिव्या याज्या । एषैव भूमिस्तोमे निष्केवल्ये सूक्तमुखीया । सूत्रं तु पूर्वमेवोदाहृतं ॥
> हे पृथिवि या त्वं दृळ्हा चित् ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति तृतीयाया आकारः ॥ दृढया
> क्ष्मया भूम्या सह वनस्पतीन् वृक्षानोजसा बलेन दर्धर्षि धारयसि । अथवा
> दृढेति विधेयविशेषणं । वनस्पतीन् दृढान् कृत्वा धारयसीत्यर्थः । यद्यस्यास्ते
> तव संबंधिनो वृष्टयो वर्षका मेघा विद्युतो विद्योतमानादभ्रस्य॥ पंचम्यर्थे षष्ठी ॥ अभ्रादपां हर्तुद्दिवोऽंतरिक्षादादित्याद्वा वर्षंति । अथवा यद्यस्यास्ते तवाभ्रस्य मेघस्य वृष्टय उदकसंघाता विद्युतो विद्योतमानाया दिवः
> सकाशाद्वर्षंति पतंति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Ṛk is the yājyā for Pṛthivī in the offerings to the gods. The same Ṛk is the opening Ṛk of the sūkta in the Bhūmi-stoma, in the Niṣkevalya śastra; the sūtra was quoted earlier. O Pṛthivī, you who hold the trees by strength together with the firm earth; or, taking दृढ (*dṛḍha*) as a predicative adjective, you hold the trees, making them firm. When the showers belonging to you, that is, the rain-giving clouds, rain from the shining cloud (the genitive being used in the sense of the ablative), from the one that carries off the waters, from the atmosphere or from the sun; or else, when the showers, the masses of water of your cloud, fall from the shining sky.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—when
- ते (*te*)—from your
- अभ्रस्य (*abhrasya*)—cloud
- वृष्टयः (*vṛṣṭayaḥ*)—streams of rain
- विद्युतः (*vidyutaḥ*)—shining
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—from the atmosphere
- वर्षंति (*varṣaṃti*)—pour down (then)
- या (*yā*)—you, the well-known one
- दृढा चित् (*dṛḍhā cit*)—very firm
- क्षया (*kṣayā*)—together with the earth
- ओजसा (*ojasā*)—by your strength
- वनस्पतीन् (*vanaspatīn*)—the trees
- दर्धर्षि (*dardharṣi*)—you make stand firm

### Printed p. 222

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O goddess of the middle region, when from your cloud the streams of rain pour down from the shining atmosphere, then you, the well-known one, by your strength together with the firm earth make the trees stand firm.

##### English translation
You who, with solid earth, sustain by your strength the forest lords, when the showers of your cloud fall from the shining sky.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

It is said in the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra that when an oblation is offered to the gods, this Ṛk दृळ्हा चित् (*dṛḷhā cit*) is to be used as the yājyā mantra in the offering to the deity Pṛthivī, and that at the time of reciting the stoma in praise of the earth it is to be recited at the beginning of the sūkta among the mantras of the Niṣkevalya śastra. That detail has been given earlier.

At the end of this sūkta there is a supplementary mantra to be recited: वर्षंतु ते विभावरि दिवो अभ्रस्य विद्युतः । रोहंतु सर्वबीजान्यव ब्रह्मद्विषो जहि ॥ (*varṣaṃtu te vibhāvari divo abhrasya vidyutaḥ | rohaṃtu sarvabījānyava brahmadviṣo jahi ‖*). O shining deity of the middle region, by your favour may the clouds in the atmosphere, together with thunder and lightning, pour down rain. May all the seeds on the earth, refreshed by that rain, sprout and grow as seedlings. O goddess, destroy the haters of the brahman: this is the prayer stated here.

## Sūkta 85 (Maṇḍala 5)

**Brief introduction to the god Varuṇa**

Varuṇa is a most eminent deity, equal to Indra. The number of mantras addressed to Varuṇa is very small. The sūktas that praise him alone are twelve. Judged by the number of mantras, he would be a deity of the third rank. Or, if the twenty-four sūktas are taken in which the deity Mitra, his companion, and he are praised together, he comes in the fifth place in the order of rank. His place is far below the Aśvins and equal to that of the **NOTE**. The number of mantras addressed to Varuṇa shows neither Varuṇa's true position nor his eminence.

### Printed p. 223

Varuṇa has a human body and human activities. But more than to his physical body and activities, attention seems to be directed to his moral activities. The description of his body and equipment is very small; the description of his deeds is much greater. He has a face, eyes, arms, hands and feet. He moves his hands; he drives a chariot; he sits; he eats and drinks. The face (अनीकं (*anīkaṃ*)) of Varuṇa is taken to be the face of Agni (7-88-2; compare 7-87-6). The sun is the eye of Mitra and Varuṇa (1-115-1; 6-51-1; 7-61-1; 7-63-1; 10-37-1). Since the fact that the sun is like an eye to them occurs in the very first mantra of the sūktas addressed to Mitra and Varuṇa, it seems that this is the point that comes to mind first whenever Mitra and Varuṇa are mentioned. When it is said, in a sūkta addressed to the sun (1-50-6), that Varuṇa watches men with his eye, that eye must be the sun itself. **NOTE** Mitra and Varuṇa have the sun as their eye (7-66-10). The epithet is applied to all the gods. Varuṇa is far-sighted (1-25-5 to 16; 8-90-2) and thousand-eyed (7-34-10). Mitra and Varuṇa stretch out their arms (5-64-2; 7-62-5) and drive their chariot, making the rays of the sun their arms. Like Savitṛ and Tvaṣṭṛ, they have beautiful hands (सुपाणी (*supāṇī*)). Mitra and Varuṇa go swiftly on their feet (5-64-7), and Varuṇa tramples evil deeds with shining feet (8-41-8). He sits on the darbha grass spread in the sacrificial hall (1-26-4; 5-72-2) and, with Mitra, drinks soma like the other gods (4-4-3 and others). Varuṇa wears a golden mantle or robe (द्रापि (*drāpi*)) and shining raiment (1-25-13). Where he is said in some places to have shining raiment made of ghee (5-62-4; 7-64-1), this must refer to the offering of ājya made in the sacrifice. The "glittering garments" worn by both of them must be in this same sense (1-15-2-1). Only in one place in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa (13-3-6-5) is Varuṇa depicted as a handsome old man with a bald head and yellow eyes. The most important of his equipment is his chariot. It is radiant like the sun (1-112-15). It has a seat and whips (5-62-7), and firmly yoked horses draw it (5-62-4). Mitra and Varuṇa mount their chariot in a very high region of the sky (5-63-1). The poet has a longing to see that chariot on the earth (5-63-2).

### Printed p. 224

The dwelling of Mitra and Varuṇa is golden, and it is in the upper world (5-67-2; 1-136-2). And Varuṇa, seated in his palace, sees everything that goes on in the world (1-25-10 and 11). The seat (सदस् (*sadas*)) of Varuṇa and Mitra is great, very high, resting on a thousand pillars, and firm (5-68-5; 2-41-5); and their house has a thousand doors (7-88-5). The all-seeing sun, setting out from his own dwelling, goes to the house of Mitra and Varuṇa in order to report the doings of men (7-60-1 and 3), and he enters their dear house (1-152-4). It is in the highest heaven itself that the fathers behold Varuṇa (11-14-8). In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa there is the statement that Varuṇa, the lord of the whole world, sits in the middle of the sky and from there surveys the regions around him in which punishment is inflicted (1-1-6-1).

In some places it is said that Varuṇa has spies. They sit around him (1-24-13). They look at both heaven and the earth. Well informed about the matter of the sacrifices, they become inciters of praise (7-87-3). The spies of Mitra and Varuṇa enter houses separately (7-61-3); they are not deceived; they are wise (6-67-5). In the Atharvaveda (A. Ve. 4-16-4) **NOTE** it is said that the emissaries of Varuṇa descend from heaven, go all around the world and watch with thousands of eyes. Here the emissaries must be the stars. But in the Ṛgveda there is no basis for saying that the stars act as emissaries of Varuṇa and the other gods and inform them of what happens in the world. Probably it is a description that these gods too, like worldly kings, have spies. Or else: spies belong not only to Mitra and Varuṇa; Agni (4-4-3), Soma (9-73-4 and 7), the demons who fought with Indra (1-33-8) and other gods, for all of them (10-10-8) there are spies. It is said that the Ādityas watch from a high region like spies (8-47-11). Varuṇa's "messenger with golden wings" (10-123-6) must be the sun.

Varuṇa, alone or together with Mitra, like Yama and other gods, is called king in many places (1-24-7, 8 and so on). He is king of gods and men, of all (10-132-4; 2-27-10), king of the whole world (5-85-3) and of all things moving and unmoving (7-87-6). In general it is only Indra who is described as a self-dependent king; but Varuṇa too is self-dependent (2-28-1). In more instances than for "king", Varuṇa or Mitra and Varuṇa are called संराट् (*saṃrāṭ*). This epithet is found also for Agni. But for the most part it belongs to Indra. Twice as many times as for Indra, the use of संराट् (*saṃrāṭ*) for Varuṇa or Mitra and Varuṇa is found, so that

### Printed p. 225

there is the use. Since for every eight or ten sūktas addressed to Indra there is only one sūkta addressed to Varuṇa, it may be said that this epithet belongs to Varuṇa in particular.

The epithet क्षत्र (*kṣatra*) is applied generally to Varuṇa together with Mitra, and twice together with Aryaman. Besides, it is found used only once each for Agni, Bṛhaspati and the Aśvins. In the same way, of the five instances in which (क्षत्रिय (*kṣatriya*)) "ruler" occurs, four times it is said of Varuṇa or the Ādityas, and only once of other gods. The word असुर (*asura*) is used of Varuṇa or of Mitra and Varuṇa more often than of Indra or Agni. Taking into account the small number of sūktas addressed to Varuṇa, it must be said that the epithet असुर (*asura*) too applies chiefly to Varuṇa. They are called, among the gods, ones of mysterious nature, worthy of worship (असुराः आर्याः (*asurāḥ āryāḥ*)) (7-6-2).

The divine power of Mitra and Varuṇa is described by the word "माया (*māyā*)". Applied to these gods it means the mysterious power they have to do good; in the context where it is applied to the demons, the same word means the mysterious power they have to do evil. In the good sense the word suits Mitra and Varuṇa; in the bad sense it applies to the demons. In the same way the word "असुर (*asura*)" suits the exalted gods in the sense "one who cannot be known by ordinary people", and the demons in the sense "those who are not gods". By this mysterious power of माया (*māyā*) that is hidden in him, Varuṇa, standing in the atmosphere, measures the earth with the sun as with a measuring rod (5-85-5); Mitra and Varuṇa send forth the dawn (3-61-7); they make the sun cross the atmosphere and make it dim with rain clouds, and then sweet drops (mixed with honey) fall (5-63-4); they make the sky rain, and by the hidden strength of the Asura (the deity of the sky, or Parjanya) they cause all the above-mentioned works to be done. Therefore this word "माया (*māyā*)" is used among the gods chiefly of Varuṇa (6-48-14; 7-25-4; 10-99-10; 10-147-5).

There are many stories connected with Indra. But in the case of Varuṇa it is quite the opposite. There is not even one story. He is much praised as the regulator of physical and moral laws. It is Varuṇa who lays down the laws of nature. It is he who established earth and heaven, and he dwells in all the worlds (8-42-1). The three upper worlds and the three earthly worlds are in him (7-87-5). Varuṇa and Mitra rule the whole world (5-63-7), or they encompass both worlds (7-61-4). They are the protectors of the whole world (2-27-4 and so on). It is by the command of Varuṇa that heaven and earth are kept apart

### Printed p. 226

(6-72-1; 7-86-1; 8-81-10). With the help of Mitra, Varuṇa is the support of heaven and earth (5 62-3), or of heaven, earth and sky (5-69-1 and 4). He has made the golden ball (the sun) shine in the sky (7-87-5); he has placed fire in the water, the sun in the sky, and soma on the mountain (5-85-2); he has made a broad path for the sun (1-24-8; 7-87-1). Varuṇa, Mitra and Aryaman **NOTE** open the way for the sun (7-60-4). Wherever the horses of the sun run, the authority of Mitra and Varuṇa is established (5-62-1). The wind that blows roaring in the sky is the breath of Varuṇa (7-87-2).

It is by the commands of Varuṇa (व्रतानि (*vratāni*)) that the moon shines in the night, and the stars, which are very high in the atmosphere, appear only at night and disappear by day (1-24-10). Varuṇa embraces the nights (परिषस्वजे (*pariṣasvaje*)); by his mysterious power the morning or the day (उस्राः (*usrāḥ*)) comes about (8-41-3). From this it is not indicated that the relation of Varuṇa to the night is closer than his relation to the day. In truth it is the sun who is always with Varuṇa, and not the night or the moon. On the whole, Varuṇa is the regulator both of the light of day and of the light of night, whereas the sun (Mitra) is the regulator only of the light of day.
