In the Brāhmaṇas a special relation of Varuṇa to the night, or to the sky at the time of night, is stated. Mitra created the day and Varuṇa the night (Tai. Saṃ. 6-4-8-3); the day belongs to Mitra and the night to Varuṇa (Tai. Saṃ. 2-1-7-4). In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa it is stated clearly that the earth is Mitra and the heaven is Varuṇa.

In one place it is said that Varuṇa divided the seasons. He knows the twelve months (1-25-8); Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman divided the autumn, the months, the day and the night (7-66-11).

In many places it is said that it is he who arranges the waters. It is he who made the rivers flow; by his command they flow on without ceasing (2-28-4). That the rivers, though they pour their water swiftly into the sea, do not fill it, is due to his hidden power (5-85-6). Varuṇa and Mitra are the lords of the rivers (7-64-2). That Varuṇa is the lord of the oceans is an established point, yet it occurs very rarely in the Ṛgveda. Probably the reason is that it is very unimportant. Varuṇa, who goes into the water of the sea, is compared to the Maruts in the atmosphere, to Agni on the earth and to Vāyu in the air

### Printed p. 227

(1-161-14). That the seven rivers plunge into the mouth of Varuṇa, which is like a roaring deep gorge (8-58-12), must refer to the ocean. Varuṇa descends into the sea like the sky (द्याः (*dyāḥ*)) (7-87-6). In general, everything that concerns Varuṇa is connected with the water that is in the atmosphere. Like a hidden ocean, Varuṇa ascends to the sky (8-41-8). Watching the truth and untruth of men, Varuṇa moves in the midst of the water that falls down sweetly as fresh drops (7-49-3). Varuṇa wears a garment of water itself (9-90-2; compare 8-69-11 and 12). Among the gods who are prayed to for rain, Varuṇa and Mitra are the foremost. When all the mountains are covered with clouds, it is Varuṇa who, from the overturned vessel (of the cloud), pours water on heaven, earth and sky and makes the whole earth wet (5-85-3 and 4). Mitra and Varuṇa have milk-yielding cows and rivers of honey and ghee (5-69-2). They have the sky full of rain and flowing rivers (5-68-5). They wet the pastures with ghee (rain) and the other regions with honey (3-62-16); they send from the sky rain and things that refresh the weary (7-64-2). The rain of heavenly water comes from them (8-25-6). A whole sūkta praises their power of raining (5-63). The fact that the name of Varuṇa occurs in both the lists of the deities of the atmosphere and of heaven must be due to his close connection with water and rain.

In the Brāhmaṇas Mitra and Varuṇa are also deities of rain. In the Atharvaveda sovereignty (संराजत्व (*saṃrājatva*)) is not stated of Varuṇa; but his authority over water remains just the same. As Soma has kinship with the mountain, so Varuṇa has kinship with water (A. Ve. 4-15-12). His golden house is in the water (A. Ve. 7-83-1). He is the lord of all water; he and Mitra are the lords of rain (A. Ve. 5-24-4 and 5). In the Yajurveda it is said that he is the child of rain and has made his home in the water, which is like a mother (Vā. Saṃ. 10-7). The waters are the wives of Varuṇa (Tai. Saṃ. 5-5-4-1). Mitra and Varuṇa are the leaders of the water (Tai. Saṃ. 6-4-3-2).

It is repeatedly said that the ordinances of Varuṇa are well established. For this very reason he has the name धृतव्रत (*dhṛtavrata*). This epithet is applied to both Mitra and Varuṇa. The gods too obey the commands of Varuṇa (8-41-7), or of Varuṇa, Mitra and Aryaman. Even the primeval gods cannot transgress their धृतव्रत (*dhṛtavrata*) ordinances (5-69-4; compare 5-63-2). Mitra and Varuṇa are the regulators of law (ऋत (*ṛta*)) and of light. It is by the strength of these ordinances that they are able to support the laws (1-23-5). This quality is found especially in these two, and sometimes in the Ādityas or in other ordinary gods. They are the ones who promote (ऋत (*ṛta*)) truth and justice (1-2-5).

### Printed p. 228

Varuṇa or the Ādityas are called protectors of the laws (ऋतस्य गोपाः (*ṛtasya gopāḥ*)). This is applied also to Agni and Soma. The epithet ऋतावन् (*ṛtāvan*), "one who observes the laws", is generally used of Agni. It is found that it is often used of Mitra and Varuṇa as well.

The power of Varuṇa is immeasurable; neither flying birds nor flowing rivers can reach the boundary of his kingdom; they cannot surpass his power; they cannot withstand his anger (1-24-6). Even the sky and the rivers have not reached the limit of Mitra and Varuṇa (1-151-9). Everything is contained in Varuṇa itself; the dwellings of all creatures are in him (8-41 and 7). The three heavenly worlds and the three earthly worlds are placed in him (7-83-5). Varuṇa is all-knowing. The flight of birds in the sky, the course of ships on the sea, the path of the far-travelling wind, all this is known to him. He sees all things that have passed or are to come (1-25-7, 9, 11). He is the witness of the truth and untruth of men (7-49-3). Without him creatures cannot even wink their eyes (2-28-6). The number of a man's winkings is known to him; all the works, plans and devices that every man makes are known to Varuṇa (A. Ve. 4-16-2 and 5). He can grasp everything in heaven and on earth and what lies beyond them. However far one may run beyond the sky, no one can leave the range of Varuṇa's sight (A. Ve. 4-16-4 and 5). That this all-knowing quality is a personal quality of Varuṇa is supported by the fact that in this matter Agni has been compared to him.

As regulator of morality, Varuṇa stands in a position far superior to all the other gods. He becomes very angry at sin. He is angry also at the transgression of his laws. For both he inflicts very severe punishment (7-86-3 and 4). The noose that binds sinners is mentioned in many places (1-24-15; 1-25-11; 6-74-4; 10-85-24). Cast in sevenfold or threefold manner, that noose binds the speaker of untruth and releases the speaker of truth (A. Ve. 4-16-6). Keeping many fetters, Mitra and Varuṇa are the restrainers of untruth (7-65-3). In one place it is said that Varuṇa, accompanied by Indra, binds with bonds that are not ropes (7-84-2). In the whole Veda, only in one place does this word "noose" (पाश (*pāśa*)) occur in a mantra addressed to Agni, and that too in the context of praying that the noose that binds his devotees be loosened. Therefore these poems of binding with the noose and of release are Varuṇa's own. Some are of the opinion that this noose and the binding by it relate to the holding back of water, and others that they are bonds relating to the night. But it may be said that it is said ironically of bonds that bind the morally fallen. Varuṇa together with Mitra is the one who drives away, hates and punishes untruth (1-152-1; 7-60 5;

### Printed p. 229

6-66-13). They punish those who are negligent in their worship (1-122-9). But Varuṇa forgives those who repent; he unties them like a rope, and removes their sin (2-28 5; 5-85-7 and 8). He releases a man not only from the sin he himself has committed but also from the sin committed by his ancestors (7-86-5). Those who daily transgress the laws laid down by himself and afterwards repent, he also saves (1-25-1); and he shows compassion to those who have transgressed his commands through forgetfulness (7-89-5). It may be said that there is no hymn to other gods without a prayer for worldly things. In the same way there is no mantra addressed to Varuṇa without a prayer for forgiveness of offence.

In Varuṇa there are hundreds and thousands of medicines; he drives death far away and removes sin (1-25-9). He can take away life; he can also give life (1 2 -11; 1-25-12; 7-88-4; 7-89-1) **NOTE**. He is very skilful in protecting immortality (8- 2 2) **NOTE**. The worthy wish to see the two kings, Varuṇa and Yama, who rule in bliss, in the आमुष्टिरलोक (*āmuṣṭiraloka*) **NOTE** (10-14-7).

In his dwelling in the heavenly world his worshippers have dealings with him. They see him with the mental eye (5-25-18; 7-88-2). With such people Varuṇa behaves as an equal (7-88-4, 6).

Let us understand what Varuṇa's real position is and what his nature is. From what is known so far, this much is certain: Varuṇa and Mitra are connected with the sun. Varuṇa is connected with it more than Mitra. Compared with Varuṇa, Mitra has, as it were, no personality at all. Mitra is a deity-planet belonging to the solar system. Varuṇa must reflect another aspect of nature. The root व्य (*vya*) **NOTE** which is the basis of the word वरुण (*varuṇa*) means "to cover". From that, Varuṇa means one who covers, or the act of covering. Therefore by the word Varuṇa the visible vault (of the sky) can be understood. If this object is understood by the word Varuṇa, there is room for all the works done by Varuṇa. It is a great thing, far vaster than the sun. Between the sun and Varuṇa there is a close kinship; without space one cannot even think of the sun's disc. The sun is the eye of Varuṇa; this eye (the sun) informs Varuṇa of everything that goes on in the world; Varuṇa is far-sighted; the world is within Varuṇa's view both by day and by night; and all such things fit. Moreover, no one else can attain sovereignty (संराजत्व (*saṃrājatva*)) as easily as the sky; for the sun and other masses of radiance

### Printed p. 230

all of them have it as their support. That the sky, which lies far above the earth, and which by day and night keeps in view all things moving and unmoving, is a deity who is a ruler whose commands cannot be transgressed, is not hard to imagine.

(The introduction is concluded.)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र सम्राज इत्यष्टर्चं त्रयोदशं सूक्तमात्रेयं त्रैष्टुभं वारुणं ।
> अनुक्रम्यते च । प्र सम्राजेऽष्ट् वारुणमिति । विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This sūkta, beginning प्र सम्राज (*pra samrāja*), has eight Ṛks and is the thirteenth sūkta. Its seer is Atri (Ātreya), its metre is Triṣṭubh, and its deity is Varuṇa. In the Anukramaṇī it is described thus: प्र सम्राजेऽष्ट् वारुणमिति (*pra samrāje 'ṣṭ vāruṇamiti*). Its ritual use (viniyoga) is by indication (laiṅgika).

Translation: The sūkta प्र सम्रेजे (*pra samreje*) is the thirteenth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It has eight Ṛks. Atri is the seer of this sūkta; Varuṇa is the deity; Triṣṭubh is the metre. In the Anukramaṇī it is said: प्र सम्रेजेऽष्ट् वारुणं (*pra samreje 'ṣṭ vāruṇaṃ*). The ritual use is by indication.

Sūkta 85. Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6. Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 30, 31. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 8. Seer: Atri. Deity: Varuṇa. Metre: Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र स॒म्राजे॑ बृ॒हद॑र्चा गभी॒रं ब्रह्म॑ प्रि॒यं वरु॑णाय  ॒॑
> श्रुताय ।  
> वि यो ज॒घान॑ शमि॒तेव॒ चर्मो॑प॒स्तिरे॑ पृथि॒वीं सूर्या॑य ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । संराजे । बृहत् । अर्च । गभीरं । ब्रह्म । प्रियं । वरुणाय , श्रुताय ।  
> वि । यः । जघान । शमिताइव । चर्म । उपस्थिरे । पृथिवीं । सूर्याय ॥ १ ॥

### Printed p. 231

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अत्रिः स्वात्मानं संबोध्य ब्रवीति । हे अत्रे त्वं सम्राजे सम्यग्राजमानायेश्वराय श्रुताय सर्वत्र श्रूयमाणाय वरुणायोपद्रवस्य निवारकाय
> जनानामावरकायैतन्नामकाय देवाय बृहत् प्रभूतं गभीरं दुरवगाहं
> बह्वर्थोपेतं प्रियं प्रियभूतं ब्रह्म स्तोत्ररूपं कर्म प्रार्थ । प्रार्थय ।
> प्रोच्चारयेत्यर्थः । यो वरुणः शमितेव चर्म शमिता पशुविशसनकर्ता यथोपस्तरणायि चर्म हंति तद्वत्पृथिवीं विस्तीर्णमंतरिक्षं ॥ तृतीयस्यां पृथिव्यामिति
> श्रुतेरंतरिक्षस्यापि पृथिवीशब्दवाच्यत्वं ॥ सूर्याय सूर्यस्कोपस्तिय आस्तरणाय
> वि जघान विस्तारयामास । विस्तृतं हि व्यवहारयोग्यं भवति तस्माद्व्यवहारायांतरिक्षं विस्तारितवान् । उरुं हि राजा वरुणश्चकार । ऋग्वे. १-२४-८ । इति ह्युक्तं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Atri addresses himself: "O Atri, offer a prayer to Varuṇa." Varuṇa is the sovereign, that is, the lord who shines well; renowned, that is, heard of everywhere; the warder-off of calamity, or, in the other sense, the one who covers people, this being the meaning of his name. The prayer to be offered is a great, that is, abundant, profound, that is, hard to fathom and rich in meanings, dear, that is, pleasing, act of praise in the form of a hymn; "offer" means "pray" or "utter aloud". This is the Varuṇa who, as the slaughterer who cuts up the animal spreads out a hide as a spread, has spread out the earth, that is, the broad firmament. On the strength of the śruti that speaks of "the third earth", the word for earth can also denote the firmament. For the sun's spreading he has stretched it out. What is spread out becomes fit for use, so he spread out the firmament for use. This is stated in the verse उरुं हि राजा वरुणश्चकार (*uruṃ hi rājā varuṇaścakāra*) (Ṛgve. 1-24-8). **NOTE** **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शमिता (*śamitā*)—the śamitṛ, who cuts up animals
- चर्म इव (*carma iva*)—as one spreads out a hide
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Varuṇa
- सूर्याय (*sūryāya*)—of the sun
- उपस्थिरे (*upasthire*)—for the movement
- पृथिवीं (*pṛthivīṃ*)—the firmament
- वि जघान (*vi jaghāna*)—has spread out and extended
- सम्रजे (*samraje*)—who shines as a sovereign
- श्रुताय (*śrutāya*)—and is renowned
- वरुणाय (*varuṇāya*)—to the god Varuṇa
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundant
- गभीरं (*gabhīraṃ*)—endowed with exceedingly profound meanings
- प्रियं (*priyaṃ*)—pleasing
- ब्रह्म (*brahma*)—the hymn of praise
- प्र अर्च (*pra arca*)—recite and offer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Atri, to the god Varuṇa, who shines as a sovereign and is renowned, and who has spread out and extended the firmament for the sun's movement, as the slaughterer who cuts up animals spreads out a hide, recite and offer a hymn of praise that is abundant, endowed with exceedingly profound meanings, and pleasing.

##### English translation
Offer a solemn, profound, and acceptable prayer to the imperial and renowned Varuna, who has spread the firmament as a bed for the sun, as the immolator (spreads) the skin of the victim.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

शमिता चर्म इव (*śamitā carma iva*)—as the slaughterer who cuts up animals spreads out the hide of the animal. In a sacrifice, the one who kills the animal and cuts it up is called the slaughterer (śamitṛ). After an animal has been killed in the sacrifice,

### Printed p. 232

the slaughterer spreads out that animal's hide wide in the sun, in order that it may dry. Putting this spreading wide as the simile, the text describes Varuṇa's unequalled power: he spread out the firmament wide, so that it became vast, for the sun's movement. In support of this the Ṛk उरुं हि राजा वरुणश्चकार सूर्याय पंथामन्वेतवा उ॥ (*uruṃ hi rājā varuṇaścakāra sūryāya paṃthāmanvetavā u‖*) is cited. (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-24-8)

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वने॑षु॒ व्य१॒ं॑ंतरि॑क्षं ततान॒ वाज॒मर्व॑त्सु॒ पय॑ उ॒स्रिया॑सु ।  
> हृ॒त्तु क्रतुं॒ वरु॑णो अ॒प्स्व१॒॑ग्निं दि॒वि सूर्य॑मदधा॒त्सोम॒मद्रौ॑ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वनेषु । वि । अंतरिक्षं । ततान । वाजं । अर्वत्सु । पयः । उस्रियासु ।  
> हृत्सु । क्रतुं । वरुणः । अप्सु । अग्निं । दिवि । सूर्यं । अदधात् । सोमं ।  
> ।  
> आद्रॆ ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अयं वरुणो वनेषु वृक्षाग्रेष्ट्यंतरिक्षं वि ततान । विस्तारितवान् । तथा
> वाजसनेयकं । वनेषु हीदमेषु वृक्षाग्रेष्ट्यंतरिक्षं विततं तस्मादाह वनेषु
> व्यंतरिक्षं ततानेति । वाजं बलमर्वत्स्वश्वेषु वि ततान । पयः क्षीरमुस्रियासु
> गोसु । उस्रियेति गोनाम । उत्साविणोऽस्यां भोगा इति तद्व्युत्पत्तिः । हृत्तु
> हृदयेषु क्रतुं कर्मसंकल्पं । यद्दि मनसा ध्याययति तत्कर्मणा करोति ।
> अप्सूदकेष्टग्निं वैद्युतमौर्वं वा । सर्वत्र वि ततानेति संबंधः । किंच दिवि
> द्युलोके सूर्यमदधात् । स्थापितवान् । सोमं चाद् पर्वतेऽवधात् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Varuṇa has spread out the firmament over the tops of the trees (the woods). The Vājasaneyaka text has: वनेषु हीदमेषु वृक्षाग्रेष्ट्यंतरिक्षं विततं तस्मादाह वनेषु व्यंतरिक्षं ततानेति । (*vaneṣu hīdameṣu vṛkṣāgreṣṭyaṃtarikṣaṃ vitataṃ tasmādāha vaneṣu vyaṃtarikṣaṃ tatāneti |*) **GLOSS** Strength he has spread in the horses; milk, that is, the liquid, in the cows. The word for cow here is a name of the cow, and a derivation of it is given. In the hearts he has spread the resolve, that is, the purpose of action; for what one meditates on in the mind, one does in act. In the waters he has placed fire, that is, the lightning fire or the submarine fire. "Has spread out" is to be connected with all of these. Moreover, in heaven, the world of the sky, he has set the sun, and on the mountain he has placed Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—that famous god Varuṇa
- वनेषु (*vaneṣu*)—in the upper parts of trees
- अंतरिक्षं (*aṃtarikṣaṃ*)—the firmament
- वि ततान (*vi tatāna*)—has spread out
- आर्वत्सु (*ārvatsu*)—in horses
- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—strength (has spread out)

### Printed p. 233

- उस्रियासु (*usriyāsu*)—in cows
- पयः (*payaḥ*)—milk
- हृत्सु (*hṛtsu*)—in hearts
- क्रतुं (*kratuṃ*)—the resolve that prompts action
- अप्पु (*appu*)—in the waters
- अग्निं (*agniṃ*)—fire
- दिवि (*divi*)—in the heaven
- सूर्यं (*sūryaṃ*)—the sun
- आदधात् (*ādadhāt*)—has placed
- अदौ (*adau*)—on the mountain
- सोमं (*somaṃ*)—Soma (has placed)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In the upper parts of the well-known trees he has spread out the firmament, in horses strength, in cows milk, and in hearts the resolve that prompts action. In the waters he has placed fire, in the heaven the sun, and on the mountain Soma.

##### English translation
He has extended the firmament over the tops of the trees, has given strength to horses, milk to cows, determination to the heart : he has placed fire in the waters, the sun in heaven, the Soma-plant in the mountain.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

वनेषु वि अंतरिक्षं ततान (*vaneṣu vi aṃtarikṣaṃ tatāna*)—he made the firmament spread out over the tops of the trees. That is, to the people on the earth the firmament appears to rest on the tops of the trees. It is described as his having made it spread out. And the one who has set strength in the horses, milk in the cows, resolve in the mind, fire in the water, the sun in the firmament, and the Soma plants in the mountain region is Varuṇa himself. Therefore it is described that Varuṇa's power is exceedingly great.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नी॒चीन॑बारं॒ वरु॑णः॒ कबं॑धं॒ प्र स॑सर्ज॒ रोद॑सी अं॒तरि॑क्षं ।  
> तेन॒ विश्व॑स्य॒ भुव॑नस्य॒ राजा॒ यवं॒ न वृ॒ष्टिव्यु॑र्वनत्ति॒ भूम॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नीचीनबारं । वरुणः । कबंधं । प्र । ससर्ज । रोदसी इति । अंतरिक्षं ।  
> तेन । विश्वस्य । भुवनस्य । राजा । युवं । न । वृष्टिः । वि । उनत्ति । भूम ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 234

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> नीचीनबारमधोमुखबिलं प्र ससर्ज । कृतवान् । कं । कवंधं । कवनमुदकं । तद्धीयतेऽत्रेति कवंधो मेघः । तं तथाकरोत् । अथवा कवंधमुदकं ।
> तदुदकं नीचीनबारं नीचीननिर्गमनबिलं चकार । मेघं विदार्योदकमधोमुखं चकारेत्यर्थः । किं प्रतीत्युच्यते । रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यावंतरिक्षं च प्रति ।
> लोकत्रयहितायेत्यर्थः । तेनोदकेन विश्वस्य सर्वस्य भुवनस्य भूतजातस्य राजा
> स्वामी वरुणो भूम भूमिं व्युनत्ति । क्लेदयति । वृष्टिः सेक्ता पुमान्यवं न
> यवमिव यवान्यथा प्ररोहाय सर्वत्र प्रसारयति तद्वद्दूमिं सर्वत्तोनत्ति ।
> अथवा वृष्टिर्वरुण इति संबंधः । वर्षकः पर्जन्य इत्यर्थः । अस्मिन्पक्षे यवं पुरुष
> इवेति पुरुषशब्दोऽध्याहार्यः । अत्त नीचीनबारं वरुणः कवंधं । नि. १०-४ ।
> इत्यादिनिरुक्तं द्रष्टव्यं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Varuṇa has made the cloud, whose opening is turned downward, pour forth. What is it? It is the कवंधं (*kavaṃdhaṃ*): water is called kavana, and that in which it is held is the cloud; he made it so. Or else the water itself is meant: he made that water have a downward outlet; that is, splitting the cloud, he made the water face downward. For whom? It is said: for the heaven-and-earth and the firmament, that is, for the welfare of the three worlds. By that water Varuṇa, the lord of the whole world, that is, of all that has come to be, wets the earth, that is, drenches it. As the rain, the sprinkling male, spreads barley everywhere for its sprouting, so he wets the earth everywhere. Or else the rain is Varuṇa himself, that is, the rainer, Parjanya; on this view the word "man" is to be supplied: as a man (sows) barley. The Nirukta (Ni. 10-4) and the like are to be seen. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- रोदसी (*rodasī*)—for the sake of heaven-and-earth
- अंतरिक्षं (*aṃtarikṣaṃ*)—and for the sake of the firmament
- कबंधं (*kabaṃdhaṃ*)—the cloud
- नीचीनबारं (*nīcīnabāraṃ*)—facing downward
- प्रससर्ज (*prasasarja*)—made it pour out water
- वृष्टिः (*vṛṣṭiḥ*)—the rain
- यवं न (*yavaṃ na*)—as it drenches barley grain
- विश्वस्य भुवनस्य (*viśvasya bhuvanasya*)—of the whole world
- राजा (*rājā*)—Varuṇa, the lord
- तेन (*tena*)—with this same water
- भूम (*bhūma*)—the earth
- व्युनत्ति (*vyunatti*)—drenches

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Varuṇa, for the sake of heaven-and-earth and of the firmament, made the cloud pour out water downward. As the rain thoroughly drenches barley grain, so Varuṇa, the lord of the whole world, drenches the earth with this same water.

##### English translation
Varuna has set free the (water of the) downward opening cloud for the (benefit of the) heaven, the earth, and the firmament; thence is ne monarch of all the world, watering the soil as the rain bedews the barley.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

Yāska has given an explanation of this Ṛk: नीचीनद्वारं वरुणः कबंधं मेघं । कवनमुदकं भवति तदस्मिन् धीयते उदकमपि कबंधमुच्यते बंधिरनिभृतत्वे कमनिभृतं च प्रसृजति द्यावा पृथिव्यौ चांतरिक्षं च महत्वेन तेन सर्वस्य भुवनस्य राजा यवनिव वृष्टिर्व्युनत्ति भूमिं ॥ (*nīcīnadvāraṃ varuṇaḥ kabaṃdhaṃ meghaṃ | kavanamudakaṃ bhavati tadasmin dhīyate udakamapi kabaṃdhamucyate baṃdhiranibhṛtatve kamanibhṛtaṃ ca prasṛjati dyāvā pṛthivyau cāṃtarikṣaṃ ca mahatvena tena sarvasya bhuvanasya rājā yavaniva vṛṣṭirvyunatti bhūmiṃ ‖*) (Ni. 10 4)

### Printed p. 235

नीचीनबारं (*nīcīnabāraṃ*) means नीचीनद्वारं (*nīcīnadvāraṃ*), facing downward; since the cloud, being in the lofty firmament, pours rain upon the earth below, it is described that the mouth or door of the cloud faces downward. कवन (*kavana*) means water; the place in which such water is bound and kept is called कबंध (*kabaṃdha*), that is, the cloud. Or कबंध (*kabaṃdha*) may also be understood to mean water. For the state in which water, whose nature is to spread out or flow away, is bound and made to remain in one place is called water or cloud. Varuṇa makes a door in the lower part of such a cloud and makes its waters fall downward upon the earth. Besides, that same Varuṇa creates (builds) heaven-and-earth and the firmament. That lord of all the worlds, by his own great majesty, drenches the earth with waters, as water soaks the seedlings of barley. This is the main idea.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒नत्ति॒ भूमिं॑ पृथि॒वीमु॒त द्यां य॒दा दु॒ग्धं वरु॑णो॒  
> वष्ट्यादित् ।  
> सम॒भ्रेण॑ वसत॒ पर्व॑तासस्तविषी॒यंतः॑ श्रथयंत वी॒राः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उनत्ति । भूमिं । पृथिवीं । उत । द्यां । यदा । दुग्धं । वरुणः । वष्टि । आत् ।  
> इत् ।  
> सं । अभ्रेण । वसत । पर्वतासः । तविषीयंतः । श्रथयंत । वीराः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वरुणो भूमिं पृथिवीं प्रथितमंतरिक्षं । अंतरिक्षस्यापि पृथिवीशब्दवाच्यत्वं
> पूर्वमुक्तं । उतापि च द्यां म्युलोकमुनत्ति । क्लेदयति । यदायं दुग्धमुदकपूरणं वष्टि कामयतेः । अथवा दुग्धमुदकं तेन तत्कार्यमोषध्यादिकं लक्ष्यते!
> ओषध्यादयः प्रवर्धंतामिति यदा कामयते आदिदनंतरमेव सं वसत ! समाच्छादयंति । के । पर्वतासः पर्ववंतोऽद्रयः । केन । अभ्रेण मेघेन । किंचॆ
> तनिषीयंतो बलमिच्छंतो वीरा विशेषेण वृष्टेः प्रेरयितारो मरुतः
> श्रथयंत । श्रथयंति मेघान् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Varuṇa wets the earth, that is, the broad firmament; that the firmament too can be denoted by the word for earth has been stated before. And he wets also heaven, the world of the sky. When he desires the milked, that is, the fullness of water, or else when "milked" means the water, and by it its product, plants and the like, is indicated; when he desires that the plants and the like may grow, then immediately after that, the mountains, that is, the rocky heights that have ridges, cover themselves with the cloud. Moreover, the Maruts, the heroes, desiring strength, **NOTE**, and specially the impellers of the rain, loosen the clouds.

### Printed p. 236

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यदा (*yadā*)—when
- वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*)—Varuṇa
- दुग्धं (*dugdhaṃ*)—to milk the cloud (to pour out rain)
- वष्टि (*vaṣṭi*)—desires (then)
- भूमिं (*bhūmiṃ*)—the earth
- पृथिवीं (*pṛthivīṃ*)—and the firmament
- उत (*uta*)—and
- द्यां (*dyāṃ*)—the heaven
- उनत्ति (*unatti*)—he drenches with water
- आदित् (*ādit*)—at that very moment
- पर्वतासः (*parvatāsaḥ*)—the mountains
- अभ्रेण (*abhreṇa*)—with clouds
- सं वसंत (*saṃ vasaṃta*)—cover themselves
- तविषीयंतः (*taviṣīyaṃtaḥ*)—who have gained zeal through strength
- वीराः (*vīrāḥ*)—the Maruts
- श्रथयंत (*śrathayaṃta*)—make the clouds move

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as Varuṇa desires to pour out rain, he drenches the earth, the firmament and the heaven with water. At that very moment the mountains cover their peaks with clouds. The Maruts, who have gained zeal through strength, make the clouds move.

##### English translation
Varuna waters earth, mid-air, and heaven, when he pleases (to send forth) the milk (of the cloud): thereupon the mountains clothe (their summits) with the rain-cloud, and the hero, (Maruts), exulting in their strength, compel (the clouds) to relax.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मामू॒ प्वा॑सु॒रस्य॑ श्रु॒तस्य॑ म॒हीं मा॒यां वरु॑णस्य॒ प्र  ॑
> वोचं ।  
> माने॑नेव तस्थि॒वा अं॒तरि॑क्षे॒ वि यो म॒मे पृ॑थि॒वीं  ॑
> सूर्येण ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमां । ऊं इति । सु । आसुरस्य । श्रुतस्य । महीं । मायां । वरुणस्य । प्र । वोचं ।  
> मानेन इव । तस्थिवान् । अंतरिक्षे । वि । यः । ममे । पृथिवीं । सूर्येण ॥ ५ ॥

### Printed p. 237

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आसुरस्यासुरसंबंधिनः । आसुराणामस्य च वध्यघातकभावः संबंधः ! आसुरहंतुरित्यर्थः । अथवासुरो मेघः । प्राणदानात्तत्संबंधिनः श्रुतस्य विश्रुतस्य
> महीं महतीमिमां मायां प्रज्ञां प्र वोचं । प्रब्रवीमि । कैषा मायेति
> सोच्यते । यो वरुणोऽंतरिक्षे तस्थिवान् तिष्ठन्मानेनेव दंडेनेव सूर्येण
> पृथिवीमंतरिक्षं वि ममे परिच्छिन्नत्ति तस्मैषा माया ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The word for "of the asuras" means one related to the asuras; the relation between the asuras and him is that of the slain and the slayer; that is, he is the slayer of the asuras. Or else the asura is the cloud, because it gives life; then it means one related to it. "Of the renowned" means of the famed. "This great", that is, mighty, device, that is, wisdom, I proclaim, that is, I declare. What is this device? It is said: the Varuṇa who, standing in the firmament, measures out the earth, that is, the firmament, by the sun, as if with a measuring rod, that is, a staff, has this device. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- अंतरिक्षे (*aṃtarikṣe*)—in the firmament
- तस्थिवान् (*tasthivān*)—standing
- मानेनेव (*māneneva*)—as if with a measuring rod
- सूर्येण (*sūryeṇa*)—by the sun
- पृथिविं (*pṛthiviṃ*)—the firmament
- वि ममे (*vi mame*)—measures out
- आसुरस्य (*āsurasya*)—the slayer of the asuras
- श्रुतस्य (*śrutasya*)—and renowned
- महीं (*mahīṃ*)—great
- इमां मायां (*imāṃ māyāṃ*)—this supernatural power
- प्र वोचं (*pra vocaṃ*)—I praise and recite

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

I praise and recite the great supernatural power of Varuṇa, the slayer of the asuras and the renowned, who, standing in the firmament, measures out the firmament by the sun as if with a measuring rod.

##### English translation
I proclaim this great device ef the renowned Varuna, the destroyer of the Asuras, who abiding in the mid-heaven, has meted the firmament by the sun, as if by a measure.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒मामू॒ नु क॒वित॑मस्य मा॒यां म॒हीं दे॒वस्य॒ नकि॒रा  ॑
> दधर्ष ।  
> एकं॒ यदु॒द्ना न पृ॒णंत्येनी॑रासिं॒चंती॑र॒वन॑यः समु॒द्रं ॥

### Printed p. 238

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इमां । ऊं इति । नु । कवीतमस्य । मायां । महीं । देवस्य । नकिः । आ । दधर्ष ।  
> एकं । यत् । उद्ना । न । पृणंति । एनीः । आसिंचंतीः । अवनयः । समुद्रं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कवितमस्य प्रकृष्णप्रज्ञस्य देवस्य द्योतमानस्य स्तुत्यस्य वा वरुणस्येमां
> सर्वप्रसिद्धां महीं महतीं मायां प्रज्ञां नकिर्नैवा दधर्ष । न हिनस्ति कश्चिदपि ।
> ऊ न्विति पूरणौ । यद्यस्मादेकं समुद्रमुद्योदकेन न पृणंति न पूरयंति ।
> काः । एनीरेन्यः शुभ्रा गमनशीला वासिंचंतीरुदकमासेचयंत्योऽवनयो
> नद्यः । बह्वो नद्यः सर्वदोदकेन पूरयंत्योऽपि नैकमपि समुद्रं पूरयंतीति । इदं वरुणस्य महत्कर्मेति । अत्रांतरिक्षविस्तारादिसमुद्रापूरणपर्यंतं
> कर्म परमेश्वरस्यॆवोचितं न वरुणस्येति न वाच्यं तस्य वरुणादिरूपावसा नात् ।
> एष ब्रह्मेत्यादि श्रुतेः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of Varuṇa, the one of surpassing wisdom, the god, that is, the shining or the praiseworthy, this universally known great device, that is, wisdom, no one ever assails; no one at all harms it. The two particles are mere fillers. The reason is this: the rivers, bright and swift-flowing, pouring water, that is, many rivers always filling it with water, do not fill even the one ocean. This is Varuṇa's great work. It must not be said that the work extending from the spreading out of the firmament to the non-filling of the ocean befits the Supreme Lord alone and not Varuṇa, since he has the form of Varuṇa and the rest **NOTE**; for there is the śruti एष ब्रह्मेत्यादि श्रुतेः (*eṣa brahmetyādi śruteḥ*) **GLOSS**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यत् (*yat*)—by the greatness of which Varuṇa
- एनीः (*enīḥ*)—flowing
- आ सिंचंतीः (*ā siṃcaṃtīḥ*)—filled with water
- अवनयः (*avanayaḥ*)—(many) rivers also
- एकं समुद्रं (*ekaṃ samudraṃ*)—a single ocean
- उद्ना (*udnā*)—with water
- न पृणंति (*na pṛṇaṃti*)—cannot fill
- कवितमस्य (*kavitamasya*)—who has the most excellent wisdom
- देवस्य (*devasya*)—and is resplendent, of Varuṇa
- इमां (*imāṃ*)—famous
- महीं (*mahīṃ*)—and great
- मायां (*māyāṃ*)—wisdom
- नकिः (*nakiḥ*)—no one at all
- आ दधर्ष (*ā dadharṣa*)—can resist

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

That the ocean has such a depth that many rivers, though they come flowing full of floods, cannot fill it, is Varuṇa's greatness itself. No one at all can resist the famous and great wisdom of Varuṇa, who has such most excellent wisdom and is resplendent.

##### English translation
No one has counteracted the divice of the most sagacious divinity, whereby the lucid water-shedding rivers do not fill the ocean with water.

### Printed p. 239

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒र्य॒म्यं॑ वरुण मि॒त्र्यं॑ वा॒ सखा॑यं वा॒ सद॒मिद्भात॑रं  
> वा ।  
> वे॒शं वा॒ नित्यं॑ वरु॒णार॑णं वा॒ यत्सी॒माग॑श्चकृ॒माति॒श्रथ॒स्तत् ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अर्यम्यं । वरुण । मित्र्यं । वा । सखायं । वा । सदं । इत् । भ्रातरं । वा ।  
> वेशं । वा । नित्यं । वरुण । अरुणं । वा । यत् । सीं । आगः । चक्रम । शिश्रथः । तत् ॥७॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वरुण अर्यम्यं । अर्यमैवार्यम्यः ॥ स्वार्थिको यत् । आर्तेरिदं
> रूपं ॥ प्रदातारमित्यर्थः । अथवेरणान्मननाच्च शास्त्रीत्यर्यमा गुरुः । तं । वा
> अथवा मित्र्यं ॥ इमिदा स्नेहन इत्यस्मान्मित्रं । स्वार्थिको यत्॥ आनुरक्तमित्यर्थः।
> वा अथवा सखायं समानख्यानं सदमित्सर्वदैव भ्रातरं वा । नित्यं निरंतरं वेशं
> निकटनिकेतनवर्तिनं वा । अरणं वा । अशब्दमित्यर्थः । अथवारणमदातारं वा नित्यं
> निरंतरं । एतान्नृति यत्सिं यदेतदागोऽपराधं चक्रम तच्छिश्रथः । अस्यत्तो
> विनाशय ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"O Varuṇa", whether to अर्यम्यं (*aryamyaṃ*), that is, to Aryaman himself, the giver; or else, because he guides and reflects, one who instructs: Aryaman, the teacher; to him; or to मित्र्यं (*mitryaṃ*), the friend, that is, one who is attached to us in affection; or to the companion, that is, one of the same fame; or to the brother, always; constantly to the neighbour, that is, one who dwells in a house close by; or to अरणं (*araṇaṃ*), that is, one without speech; or else to अरणं (*araṇaṃ*), one who does not give, continually. Whatever offence we have committed against any of these, loosen that, destroy it from us. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa
- अरम्यं (*aramyaṃ*)—whether against one who has done us a service
- मित्रं (*mitraṃ*)—or against a friend
- वा (*vā*)—or
- स खायं (*sa khāyaṃ*)—against a companion
- वा (*vā*)—or
- सदमित् (*sadamit*)—always
- भ्रातरं (*bhrātaraṃ*)—against a brother
- नित्यं (*nityaṃ*)—always
- वेशं वा (*veśaṃ vā*)—or against one who lives next door
- अरणं वा (*araṇaṃ vā*)—or against a mute person
- यत् सीं (*yat sīṃ*)—of whatever kind
- आगः (*āgaḥ*)—sin or injury
- चक्रम (*cakrama*)—we have done
- तत् (*tat*)—all those sins
- शिश्रथः (*śiśrathaḥ*)—destroy

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Varuṇa, whether it be against one who has done us a service, or against our friend, companion, brother or a mute person, whatever kind of injury we may have done, destroy all those sinful acts, and so deal that that sin may not cling to us, and forgive us.

### Printed p. 240

##### English translation
If, Varuna, we have ever committed on offence against a benefactor, a friend, a companion, a brother, a near neighbour, or Varuna, a dumb man, remove it from us.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कि॒त॒वासो॒ यद्रि॑रि॒पुर्न दी॒पि यद्वा॑ घा स॒त्यम॒त यन्न वि॒द्म ।  
> सर्वा॒ ता वि ष्य॑ शिथि॒रेव॑ दे॒वाधा॑ ते स्याम वरुण प्रि॒यासः॑ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कितवासः । यत् । रिरिपुः । न । दीवि । यत् । वा । घ । सत्यं । उत । यत् । न । विष्म ।  
> सर्वा । ता । वि । स्य । शिथिराइव । देव । अध । ते । स्याम । वरुण । प्रियासः ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> कितवासः कितवा द्यूतकृतः । किं तवास्ति सर्वं मया जितमिति वदतीति कितवः ।
> न दीवि न देवने द्योते यथा यद्रिरिपुर्लोकयंति पापमारोपयंति तथास्मासु
> द्वेष्टारो मयि त्वमेतत्पापमकरोरित्याक्षिपंति ॥ अत्र पुरस्तादुपचारोऽपि नकार
> उपमार्थीयः ॥ ना अथवा । घेति पूरणः । यत्पापं सत्यमारोपमंतरेण
> कृतवंतः स्मः । उतापि च यत्कृतं पापं न विद्म न जानीमः ता तानि सर्वा सर्वाणि
> शिथिरेव शिथिलानीव शिथिलबंधनानि फलानीव वि ष्य । विमोचय ॥ सृतिरुपसृष्टो
> विमोचने ॥ हे देव आधानंतरं ते तव प्रियासः प्रियाः स्याम । भवेम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

कितवासः (*kitavāsaḥ*) are gamblers; "what have you? all has been won by me", so says one, hence the gambler. "Not in play", that is, in gaming, in the shining; as in play they make a charge and impute sin, so haters of us accuse thus: "you did this sin against me". Here the word for "not" stands in front, as a particle of comparison. **NOTE** Or else the particle घ (*gha*) is a filler. The sin which we have committed truly, without any false imputation; and also the sin committed which we do not know: all those, like loosened, that is, slack-bound fruits, release. And, O god, thereafter may we be dear to you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- देव (*deva*)—O Varuṇa who are of divine nature
- वरुण (*varuṇa*)—O Varuṇa
- दीवि (*dīvi*)—in dice and the like
- कितवासो (*kitavāso*)—like gamblers
- यत् रिरिपुः (*yat riripuḥ*)—whatever deceitful act we have committed (deliberately)
- वा (*vā*)—or
- यत् (*yat*)—whatever sinful act
- सत्यं (*satyaṃ*)—certainly
- न विद्य (*na vidya*)—we have done without knowing
- सर्वा ता (*sarvā tā*)—all those sins also
- शिथिरेव (*śithireva*)—as fruits become loose from the branch and fall down
- विष्य (*viṣya*)—push away from us
- अध (*adha*)—only thereafter
- ते (*te*)—to you
- प्रियासः (*priyāsaḥ*)—beloved
- स्याम (*syāma*)—we shall be

### Printed p. 241

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Varuṇa, who are of divine nature, as gamblers in dice and the like deliberately commit deceitful acts, so if we have committed deceitful acts, or if through our ignorance we have committed any sinful act, put all that sin far from us, as a fruit loosened from the branch of a tree falls down; only thereafter shall we be dear to you. And grant that it may be so.

##### English translation
If, like gamblers, who cheat at play, (we commit offences) knowingly, or (those) of which we know not, divine Varuna, extricate us from them all, as if from loosened (bonds), so that we may be dear, Varuna, to you.

**NOTE**

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इंद्राग्नी यमिति षडृचं चतुर्दशं सूक्तमत्रेरार्षमानुष्टुभमैंद्राग्नं ।
> षष्ठी विराट् पूर्वा । आद्यौ दशकावष्टकास्त्रयः । अनु. ९-११ । इति परिभाषितत्वात् ।
> तथा चानुक्रांतं । इंद्राग्नी षळ्ळैंद्राग्नमानुष्टुभं विराट् पूर्वांतमिति ॥
> विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta इंद्राग्नी यमिति (*iṃdrāgnī yamiti*) has six Ṛks and is the fourteenth sūkta. It is of Atri's seership, in the Anuṣṭubh metre, and its deity is Indra and Agni. The sixth Ṛk is Virāṭ-pūrvā, because of the definition आद्यौ दशकावष्टकास्त्रयः (*ādyau daśakāvaṣṭakāstrayaḥ*) (Anu. 9-11). **NOTE** Accordingly it is described in the Anukramaṇī: इंद्राग्नी षळ्ळैंद्राग्नमानुष्टुभं विराट् पूर्वांतमिति (*iṃdrāgnī ṣaḷḷaiṃdrāgnamānuṣṭubhaṃ virāṭ pūrvāṃtamiti*). Its ritual use is by indication (laiṅgika).

Translation: The sūkta इंद्राग्नी यं (*iṃdrāgnī yaṃ*) is the fourteenth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It has six Ṛks. Atri is the seer of this sūkta; Indra and Agni are the deities. Anuṣṭubh is the metre. Only the sixth Ṛk is of the metre called विराट् पूर्वा (*virāṭ pūrvā*). In the Chandonukramaṇī it is said: आद्य दशकावष्टकास्त्रयः (*ādya daśakāvaṣṭakāstrayaḥ*). That is, the Ṛks of this metre have five pādas in all; the first two pādas have ten syllables each and the last three pādas eight syllables each; thus the characteristic of this metre called विराट् पूर्वा (*virāṭ pūrvā*) is stated in the Chandonukramaṇī. This metre also has the name पंक्युत्तरा (*paṃkyuttarā*) (Chaṃ. Anu. 9-11). **NOTE** In the Anukramaṇī it is said: इंद्राग्नी षळ्ळैंद्राग्नमानुष्टुभं विराट् पूर्वांतं (*iṃdrāgnī ṣaḷḷaiṃdrāgnamānuṣṭubhaṃ virāṭ pūrvāṃtaṃ*). The ritual use is by indication.

### Printed p. 242

Sūkta 86. Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 32. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 6. Seer: Atri. Deity: Indra and Agni. Metre: Ṛks 1-5 Anuṣṭubh; Ṛk 6 Virāṭ-pūrvā.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इंद्रा॑ग्नी॒ यमव॑थ उ॒भा वाजे॑षु॒ मर्त्यं॑ ।  
> दृ॒ळ्हा चि॒त्स प्र भे॑दति द्यु॒म्मा वाणी॑रिव त्रि॒तः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इंद्राग्नी इति । यं । अवथः । उभा । वाजेषु । मर्त्यं ।  
> दृळ्हा । चित् । सः । प्र । भेदति । द्युम्ना । वाणीःइव । त्रितः ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्राग्नी उभोभौ परस्परावियुक्तौ युवां यं मर्त्यमवथो
> रक्षथो वाजेषु संग्रामेषु स मर्त्यो दृळ्ता चित् दृढान्यपि द्युम्ना द्योतमानानि धनानि शत्रुसंबंधीनि भेदति । भिनत्ति । त्रित ऋषिर्वाणीरिव प्रतिवादिवाक्यानीव ।
> अथवा त्रिषु स्थानेषु वर्तमानोऽग्निः शत्रुणां वाक्यानीव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra and Agni, you two who are never parted from each other: the mortal whom you protect in the battles, that mortal breaks even the firm, shining possessions that belong to the enemy. He breaks them as the seer Trita breaks the words of an opponent, that is, like the statements of the opponent. Or else, Agni, who is present in the three places, breaks them like the words of the enemies.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्राग्नी (*iṃdrāgnī*)—O Indra and Agni
- उभा (*ubhā*)—both of you together
- यं (*yaṃ*)—which
- मर्त्यं (*martyaṃ*)—man
- अवथः (*avathaḥ*)—you protect
- सः (*saḥ*)—that man, the sacrificer
- त्रितः (*tritaḥ*)—the seer named Trita
- वाणीरिव (*vāṇīriva*)—as he breaks the words of the opposing side
- वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*)—in battles
- दृळ्हा चित् (*dṛḷhā cit*)—even though very strong
- द्युम्ना (*dyumnā*)—the wealth of the enemies
- प्र भेदति (*pra bhedati*)—breaks and scatters

### Printed p. 243

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Agni, the man (the sacrificer) whom you both together protect breaks and scatters in the battles the wealth of the enemies, even though it is guarded very firmly, as Trita breaks the words of the opposing side.

##### English translation
Indra and Agni, the mortal whom you both protect scatters the the substantial treasures (of his enemies), as Trita (confutes) the words (of his opponents).

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> या पृत॑नासु दु॒ष्टरा॒ या वाजे॑षु श्र॒वाय्या॑ ।  
> या पंच॑ चर्ष॒णीर॒भीं॑द्रा॒ग्नी ता ह॑वामहे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> या । पृतनासु । दुस्तरा । या । वाजेषु । श्रवाय्या ।  
> या । पंच । चर्षणीः । अभि । इंद्राग्नी इति । ता । हवामहे ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> या याविंद्राग्नी पृतनासु : दुष्टरा दुष्टरावनभिभाव्यौ । या यौ च
> वाजेषु संग्रामेष्वन्नेषु वा श्रवाय्या श्रवाय्यौ स्तुत्यौ । या यौ पंच
> चर्षणीः । चर्षणयो मनुष्याः । तानभि । रक्षत इति शेषः । ता त् महानुभावौ
> हवामहे । स्तुमः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra and Agni, who are hard to overcome in the armies, that is, not to be overpowered; who in the battles, or in the matter of food, are praiseworthy, that is, to be praised; who protect the five peoples, the peoples being men (the word "protect" is to be supplied): those two, of great majesty, we invoke, that is, we praise.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्राग्नी (*iṃdrāgnī*)—O Indra and Agni
- या (*yā*)—you who
- पृतनासु (*pṛtanāsu*)—among the enemy armies
- दुष्टरा (*duṣṭarā*)—are unassailable
- या (*yā*)—you who
- वाजेषु (*vājeṣu*)—in battles
- श्रवाय्या (*śravāyyā*)—are praised
- या (*yā*)—you who
- पंच चर्षणीः (*paṃca carṣaṇīḥ*)—the five peoples beginning with men
- अभि (*abhi*)—wholly protect
- ता (*tā*)—such venerable ones, you
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—I praise

### Printed p. 244

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Agni, you who are unassailable among the enemy armies, who are praised in the battles, and who wholly protect the five peoples beginning with men, such venerable ones, you we praise and invoke.

##### English translation
We invoke the two, Indra and Agni, who are irresistible in conflicts, who are renowned in battles, who protect the five (classes of) men.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> तयो॒रिदम॑व॒च्छव॑स्ति॒ग्मा दि॒द्युन्म॒घोनोः॑ ।  
> प्रति॒ द्रुणा॒ गभ॑स्त्यो॒र्गवां॑ वृत्र॒घ्न एष॑ते ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> तयोः । इत् । आमवत् । शवः । तिग्मा । दिद्युत् । मघोनोः ।  
> प्रति । द्रुणा । गभस्तोः । गवां । वृत्रस्ने । आ । ईषते ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अमवदभिभावुकं शवो बलं तयोरित् तयोरेव ! मघोनोर्मघवतोरन्नवतोर्धनवतोर्वा तयोर्दिद्युत् वज्रं तिग्मा तीक्ष्णं वर्तते गभस्योर्हस्तयोः । एवंभूत् देवौ गवामुदकानां पणिभिरपहृतानां ना गवां लाभाय
> द्रुणा द्रुविकारेण गमनशीलेन वैकेन रथेन वृत्रघ्न अवरकमेघहननाय तथाविधवृत्रासुरवधाय वा प्रतीषते । प्रतिगच्छतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The strength, the power, of those two alone is overpowering. Of those two bountiful ones, who possess food or wealth, the lightning, that is, the thunderbolt, is sharp in their hands. Such gods set out, for the gain of the cows, that is, of the waters, carried off by the Paṇis **NOTE**, in a single swift chariot made of wood, **NOTE**, for the killing of Vṛtra, the cloud that obstructs, or of the asura Vṛtra of that kind.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- तयोः इत् (*tayoḥ it*)—of these very Indra and Agni
- शवः (*śavaḥ*)—the strength
- अमवत् (*amavat*)—is that which restrains enemies
- मघोनोः (*maghonoḥ*)—of these two wealthy ones
- दिद्युत् (*didyut*)—the shining thunderbolt
- तिग्मा (*tigmā*)—sharply
- गभस्तोः (*gabhastoḥ*)—in their arms (shines) (such great ones)

### Printed p. 245

- गवां (*gavāṃ*)—for the gain of cows
- वृत्रघ्ने (*vṛtraghne*)—for the destruction of Vṛtra
- द्रुणा (*druṇā*)—seated in a single swiftly running chariot
- प्रति ईषते (*prati īṣate*)—set out

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The strength of the famous Indra and Agni is that which restrains enemies. The shining thunderbolt of these two wealthy ones shines sharply in their arms. Such great ones set out, for the gain of cows and also for the destruction of Vṛtra, seated in a single swiftly running chariot.

##### English translation
Overpowering is the might of these two: the bright (lightning) is shining in the hands of Maghavan, as they go together in one chariot for the (recovery of the) cows, and the destruction of Vritra.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता वा॒मेषे॒ रथा॑नामिंद्रा॒ग्नी ह॑वामहे ।  
> पती॑ तु॒रस्य॒ राध॑सो वि॒द्वांसा॒ गिर्व॑णस्तमा ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । वां । एषे । रथानां । इंद्राग्नी इति । हनामहे ।  
> पती इति । तुरस्य । राधसः । विद्वांसा । गिर्वणःतमा ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे देवौ ता तौ वक्ष्यमाणगुणौ वां युवां हवामहे । स्तुमः । किमर्थ० ।
> रथानामेषे युद्धे रथानां प्रेरणाय । कीदृशौ युवां । इंद्राग्नी इंद्रमग्निं
> च परस्परयुग्मभूतौ तुरस्य जंगमरूपस्य राधसो धनादिलक्षणस्य पती स्वामिनौ
> विद्वांसा सर्वज्ञा गिर्वणस्तमा गीर्भिर्वननीयतम् । ईदृशौ युवां हवामहे ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O gods, those two, of the qualities to be told, we invoke, that is, we praise. For what purpose? For the sending forth of the chariots in the battle. What are you like? Indra and Agni, who are a pair joined with each other; lords of the moving kind of wealth, which has riches and the like as its mark; knowing, that is, all-knowing; to be served most of all with words of praise. Such are you whom we invoke.

### Printed p. 246

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्राग्नी (*iṃdrāgnī*)—O Indra and Agni
- तुरस्य (*turasya*)—of the moving kind
- राधसः (*rādhasaḥ*)—of all wealth
- पती (*patī*)—the lords
- विद्वांसा (*vidvāṃsā*)—the all-knowing
- गिर्वणस्तमा (*girvaṇastamā*)—who are worthy of the choicest praises
- ता वां (*tā vāṃ*)—such you
- रथानां (*rathānāṃ*)—your chariots
- एषे (*eṣe*)—to send forth for battle
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—we pray and praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Agni, who are lords of all the moving wealth, all-knowing, and worthy of the choicest praises, you we pray and praise to send your chariots forth for battle.

##### English translation
We invoke you both, Indra and Agni, for (sending) your chariots to the combat, lords of moveable wealth. all-knowing, most deserving of praise.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ता वृ॒धंता॒वनु॒ द्यून्मर्ता॑य दे॒वान॒दभा॑ ।  
> अर्हं॑ता चित्पु॒रो द॒धे०शे॑व दे॒वान्वर्॑ते ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ता । वृधंतौ । अनु । द्यून् । मर्ताय । देव् । अदभा ।  
> अर्हंता । चित् । पुरः । दधे । अंशा इव । देव् । अर्वते ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ता तावनु द्यून् प्रतिदिनं वृधंत् वर्धमान् । सर्वदा प्रवृद्धावित्यर्थः ।
> वर्धने दृष्टांतः । मर्तेव मनुष्याविव । त् यथा प्रतिदिनं वर्धेते । देवौ
> द्योतमानावदभाहिंस्यावर्हंता चित् प्रकर्षण पूज्यौ । चिदिति पूजायां ।
> तादृशौ महानुभावौ देवावर्वतेऽश्वलाभाय पुरो दधे पुरस्कुर्वे । अंशेव ।
> अंशो नाम द्वादशादित्यमध्यवर्ति देवः । स भगस्याप्युपलक्षकः । अंशाविव ।
> आदित्याविव । दीप्तावित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those two grow day by day; that is, they are always increased. As an illustration of the growth: as two mortals, that is, two men, grow day by day. The two gods are shining and not to be injured, and worthy of worship, that is, to be honoured exceedingly; the particle चिदिति (*ciditi*) is used in the sense of worship. Such great gods I place in front, that is, I honour first, for the gain of a horse. As for the comparison अंशेव (*aṃśeva*): Aṃśa is a god within the twelve Ādityas, and he stands also for Bhaga. Like two Aṃśas, that is, like two Ādityas, which means blazing.

### Printed p. 247

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

(O Indra and Agni)

- मर्तेव (*marteva*)—like men
- अनु द्यून् (*anu dyūn*)—every day
- वृधंता (*vṛdhaṃtā*)—you who prosper
- देवौ (*devau*)—resplendent
- अदभा (*adabhā*)—unharmed
- अंशा इव (*aṃśā iva*)—like the Ādityas
- देवौ (*devau*)—resplendent
- अहंता चित् (*ahaṃtā cit*)—and greatly venerable
- ता (*tā*)—you
- अर्वते (*arvate*)—for the gain of a horse
- पुरः दधे (*puraḥ dadhe*)—we honour and worship

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra and Agni, who prosper every day like men, who are resplendent and unharmed, and who are resplendent and venerable like the Ādityas, you we honour and worship for the gain of horses.

##### English translation
I adore you, irresistible deities, for (the sake of obtaining) horses : you who are increasing day by day like mortals, who are worthy of worship, like two Adityas.

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ए॒वेंद्रा॒ग्निभ्या॒महा॑वि ह॒व्यं शू॒ष्यं॑ घृ॒तं न पू॒तमद्रि॑भिः ।  
> ता सू॒रिषु॒ श्रवो॑ बृ॒हद्र॒यिं गृ॒णत्सु॑ दिधृत॒मिषं॑ गृ॒णत्सु॑  
> दिधृतं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> एव । इंद्राग्निभ्यां । अहावि । हव्यं । शूष्यं । घृतं । न । पूतं । अद्रिभिः ।  
> ता । सूरिषु । श्रवः । बृहत् । रयिं । गृणत्सु । दिधृतं । इषं । गृणत्सु ।  
> दिधृतं ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> एवैवमिदानीं क्रियमाणप्रकारेण । स्तुतिसाहित्येनेत्यर्थः । इंद्राग्निभ्यां
> हव्यं हविरहावि । परित्यक्तमासीत् । कीदृशं तत् । शूष्यं बलकरं । किमिव । अद्रिभिग्रावभिः पूतमभिषुतं धृतं न सोमरसमिव । तद्यथा युवाभ्यां प्रदीयते

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Thus", that is, in the manner in which it is now being done, with praise. By Indra and Agni the oblation was given up, that is, offered. What kind of oblation? Strength-giving. Like what? Like the Soma juice, pressed and purified with the stones, that is, the pressing-stones, as if it were ghṛta **NOTE**. As it is given to you both

### Printed p. 248

> तद्वच्चरुपुरोडाशादिकमपि । ता तौ देवौ सूरिषु मेधाविष्टस्मासु बृहत् प्रभूतं
> श्रवोऽन्नं यशो वा रयिं धनं च दिधृतं । धारयतं । इषमन्नं गृणत्तु स्तुवत्सु
> दिधृतं । पुनरुक्तिरादरार्था ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"So too" the cooked offering (caru), the cake (puroḍāśa) and the rest. Those two gods, Indra and Agni, are asked to bestow on us, the wise ones who praise them, abundant food or fame, and wealth: "may you hold" (दिधृतं (*didhṛtaṃ*)), that is, "may you bear it". Food too, for those who praise: "may you hold". The repetition is for the sake of respect.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अद्रिभिः (*adribhiḥ*)—by the crushing stones
- पूतं (*pūtaṃ*)—pressed out
- धृतं न (*dhṛtaṃ na*)—like Soma juice
- शूष्यं (*śūṣyaṃ*)—strength-giving
- हव्यं (*havyaṃ*)—the oblation
- इंद्राग्निभ्यां (*iṃdrāgnibhyāṃ*)—addressed to Indra and Agni together
- एव (*eva*)—according to the prescribed rite
- अहावि (*ahāvi*)—has been offered
- ता (*tā*)—those same gods
- सूरिषु (*sūriṣu*)—in us, who are wise
- गृणत्सु (*gṛṇatsu*)—and who praise them
- बृहत् (*bṛhat*)—abundant
- श्रवः (*śravaḥ*)—food
- रयिं (*rayiṃ*)—wealth
- दिधृतं (*didhṛtaṃ*)—may they fill up
- गृणत्सु (*gṛṇatsu*)—for those who praise them
- इषं (*iṣaṃ*)—food
- दिधृतं (*didhṛtaṃ*)—may they fill (it) up

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

An oblation, powerful like the Soma juice pressed out by the stones that crush and press the Soma plants, has been offered addressed to Indra and Agni together. May those same gods fill up abundant food and wealth in us, who are wise and who praise them. May they fill up food for those who praise them.

##### English translation
The invigorating oblation has been offered, like the Soma-juice expressed by the sounding stones : bestow food upon the pious; great riches upon those who praise you: bestow food also upon those who praise you.

## Sūkta 87 (Maṇḍala 5)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र वो मह इति नवर्चं पंचदशं सूक्तमेवयामरुदाख्यस्यात्रेयस्य
> मुनेरार्षं मरुद्देवताकमतिजगतीच्छंदस्कं । तथा चानुक्रमणिका । प्र वो
> नवैवयामरुन्मारुतमतिजागतमिति ॥ दशरात्रे षष्ठेऽहनि तृतीयसवनेऽच्छावाकशस्त्र
> इदं सूक्तं । सूत्रितं च । एवयामरुदुक्तो वृषाकपिना । आ. ८-४ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sūkta beginning प्र वो मह (*pra vo maha*) has nine Ṛks and is the fifteenth sūkta. The seer is the sage named Evayāmarut, of the Atri family; the deity is the Maruts; the metre is Atijagatī. The Anukramaṇikā says: प्र वो नवैवयामरुन्मारुतमतिजागतमिति (*pra vo navaivayāmarunmārutamatijāgatamiti*). In the Daśarātra, on the sixth day, at the third pressing, this sūkta is used in the Acchāvāka's śastra. It is also given by the sūtra: एवयामरुदुक्तो वृषाकपिना (*evayāmarudukto vṛṣākapinā*), Ā. 8-4.

### Printed p. 249

##### Anuvāda

This sūkta beginning प्र वो महे (*pra vo mahe*) is the fifteenth sūkta in the sixth anuvāka. It contains nine Ṛks. The seer of this sūkta is Evayāmarut, born in the Atri family; the deities are the Maruts; the metre is Atijagatī. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: प्र वो नवैवयामरुन्मारुतमतिजागतं (*pra vo navaivayāmarunmārutamatijāgataṃ*). The use of this sūkta is in the śastra-mantras recited by the priest called Acchāvāka at the third pressing on the sixth day of the sacrifice called Daśarātra; this is set out by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra, एवयामरुदुक्को वृषाकपिना (*evayāmarudukko vṛṣākapinā*) (Ā. 8-4). **NOTE**

##### Particulars of the sūkta

Sūkta 87. Maṇḍala 5; Anuvāka 6; Aṣṭaka 4; Adhyāya 4; Varga 33, 34. Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 9. Seer: Evayāmarut Ātreya. Deity: the Maruts. Metre: Atijagatī.

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र वो॑ म॒हे म॒तयो॑ यंतु॒ विष्ण॑वे म॒रुत्व॑ते गिरि॒जा  ॒॑॑
> एवयामरुत् ।  
> प्र शर्धा॑य॒ प्रय॑ज्यवे सुखा॒दये॑ त॒वसे॑ भं॒ददि॑ष्टये॒  
> धुनि॑व्रताय॒ शव॑से ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । वः । महे । मतयः । यंतु । विष्णवे । मरुत्वते । गिरिजाः । एवयामरुत् ।  
> प्र । शर्धाय । प्रयज्यवे । सुखादये । तवसे । भंदत् इष्टये । धुनिव्रताय ।  
> शवसे ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्र यंतु प्रगच्छंतु गिरिजा वाचि निष्पन्ना मतयः स्तुतयः । कस्मै वस्तुभ्यं ॥
> वचनव्यत्ययः ॥ विष्णवे न्याप्तायेंद्राय विष्णवे ना मरुत्वते मरुद्भिस्तद्वते ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

"Let them go forth" (प्र यंतु (*pra yaṃtu*)): let the praises (मतयः (*matayaḥ*)), born in speech (गिरिजा (*girijā*)), go forth. To whom? The text reads वस्तुभ्यं (*vastubhyaṃ*), and he notes a change of number. **NOTE**. To Viṣṇu, that is, the all-pervading Indra; to him who is accompanied by the Maruts.

### Printed p. 250

> कस्य स्तुतय इत्युच्यते । एवयामरुत् एतन्नामकस्य ऋषेः ॥ षष्ट्या लुक् ॥
> अथवायमृषिगिरिजाः स्तुतेर्जनयिता भवति । उत्तरार्धे मरुत्तुतिः । किंच प्र
> यंतु स्तुतयः । कस्मै । शर्धाय बलाय मारुताय । इतरत्सर्वं बलविशेषणं ।
> प्रयज्यवे प्रकर्षण यष्टव्याय सुखादये शोभनाभरणाय । खादिराभरणविशेषः । हस्तेषु खादिश्च । ऋग्वे. १-१६८-३ । पत्सु खादयः । ऋग्वे. ५-५४-११ ।
> इति च श्रुतेः । तवसे बलवते भंददिष्टये । स्तुतिरूपेष्टिर्यस्य तद्बंधदिष्ठि । तस्मै
> ध्रुनिव्रताय मेघानां चालनं कर्म यस्य तादृशाय शवसे गमनवते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of whom are the praises? It is said: of the seer named Evayāmarut; the genitive ending is dropped. Or else, this seer is the producer of the speech-born praises; in the second half there is praise of the Maruts. Moreover, let the praises go forth to strength (शर्धाय (*śardhāya*)), that is, to the strength of the Maruts; the other words are all epithets of strength. प्रयज्यवे (*prayajyave*): to the one to be worshipped most excellently. सुखादये (*sukhādaye*): with beautiful ornaments; खादि (*khādi*) is a special ornament. As the Śruti says: "On their hands are khādis" (Ṛgve. 1-168-3) **NOTE**, and "on their feet khādis" (Ṛgve. 5-54-11). तवसे (*tavase*): to the powerful. भंददिष्टये (*bhaṃdadiṣṭaye*): he whose desired offering is praise; to him. ध्रुनिव्रताय (*dhrunivratāya*): to such a one whose work is to set the clouds in motion. शवसे (*śavase*): to the swift-going.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra)
- एवया मरुत् (*evayā marut*)—of the seer Evayāmarut
- गिरिजाः (*girijāḥ*)—born of speech
- मतयः (*matayaḥ*)—praises
- महे (*mahe*)—to the great one
- मरुत्वते (*marutvate*)—who is accompanied by the Maruts
- विष्णवे (*viṣṇave*)—the all-pervading
- वः (*vaḥ*)—to you
- प्र यंतु (*pra yaṃtu*)—let them be offered with fullness
- प्र यज्यवे (*pra yajyave*)—the most worthy of worship
- सुखादये (*sukhādaye*)—who has excellent ornaments
- तवसे (*tavase*)—the powerful
- भंददिष्टये (*bhaṃdadiṣṭaye*)—who has a sacrifice in the form of praise
- धुनिव्रताय (*dhunivratāya*)—who makes the clouds move
- शवसे (*śavase*)—the swift-moving
- शर्धाय (*śardhāya*)—addressed to the host of the Maruts
- प्र (*pra*) (यंतु (*yaṃtu*))—let them go forth and reach

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, may the praises of the seer Evayāmarut, made of speech, be offered with fullness to you, who are great, accompanied by the Maruts, all-pervading; and may these praises go forth and reach the host of the Maruts, which is most worthy of worship, has excellent ornaments, is powerful, has a sacrifice in the form of praise, makes the clouds move, and is swift-moving.

##### English translation
May the voice-born praises of Evayamarut reach you, Vishnu, attended by the Maruths : (may they reach) the strong, the adorable, the brilliantly-adorned, the vigorous, praise-loving, cloud-scattering, quick-moving company of the Maruts.

#### Ṛk 2

### Printed p. 251

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र ये जा॒ता म॑हि॒ना ये च॒ नु स्व॒यं प्र वि॒द्मना॑ ब्रु॒वत॑  ॒॑
> एव॒याम॑रुत् ।  
> क्रत्वा॒ तद्वो॑ मरुतो॒ नाधृषे॒ शवो॑ दा॒ना म॒ह्ना तदे॑षा॒मधृ॑ष्टासो॒ नाद्र॑यः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । ये । जाताः । महिना । ये । च । नु । स्वयं । प्र । विद्मना । ब्रुवते । एवयामरुत् ।  
> क्रत्वा । तत् । वः । मरुतः । न । आधृषे । शवः । दाना । महा । तत् । एषां ।  
> अधृष्टासः । न । अद्रयः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ये महिना महता विष्णुनेंद्रेण वा सह प्र जाताः प्रादुर्भूतास्तान्ब्रुवते।
> ये च नु क्षिप्रं स्वयमेव विद्मना यज्ञगमनविषयज्ञानेन सह प्र जाताः प्रादुर्भूतास्मान्मरुत एवयामरुद्ब्रुवते । स्तौति ॥ प्रेत्ययमाख्यातेन वा संबंधनीयः । ब्रुवत इति व्यत्ययेन बहुवचनं । एकवचने वा छांदसः शः । निघाताभावश्चांदसः ॥ अथ प्रत्यक्षवादः । हे मरुतो वो युष्माकं तत्पसिद्धं शवो बलं
> क्रत्वा चालनादिरूपेण कर्मणा युक्तं नाधृषे । कैश्चिदप्याधर्षणीयं न भवति ।
> अन्येषामसुरादीनां क्रत्वा कर्मणा वानभिभाव्यं भवति ॥ कृत्यार्थे केन्॥ किंचैषां
> वो तत् बलं दानाभिमतदानेन मह्ना महत्त्वेन चोपेतं । यूयं प्रबलाः ।
> अधृष्टासोऽधर्षणीयाः । तत्र दृष्टांतः । अद्रयो न । ते यथाधृष्यास्तद्विद्यूयमपि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Maruts who were born (प्र जाताः (*pra jātāḥ*)) together with the great (महिना (*mahinā*)) Viṣṇu or Indra, that is, appeared: Evayāmarut praises them (ब्रुवते (*bruvate*)). And those who, quickly and of themselves, were born together with knowledge (विद्मना (*vidmanā*)) of the going to the sacrifice, he praises. The word प्र (*pra*) is to be construed with the verb. The plural in ब्रुवत (*bruvata*) is by change of number; or, if singular, the ending is Vedic. The absence of the accent-lowering is Vedic. Now a direct address: O Maruts, that well-known strength (शवः (*śavaḥ*)) of yours, joined with action (क्रत्वा (*kratvā*)) such as setting in motion, cannot be assailed (नाधृषे (*nādhṛṣe*)) by anyone; it is not to be overcome by the action of other beings such as the Asuras. The ending केन् (*ken*) is in the sense of fitness. Moreover, that strength of yours is endowed with gifts (दाना (*dānā*)), the bestowal of what is desired, and with greatness (मह्ना (*mahnā*)). You are mighty and unassailable; the example is the mountains (अद्रयो न (*adrayo na*)): as they are not to be assailed, so are you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ये (*ye*)—those Maruts who
- महिना (*mahinā*)—together with the great Indra
- प्र जाताः (*pra jātāḥ*)—were born
- ये च (*ye ca*)—and those who
- नु (*nu*)—quickly
- स्वयं (*svayaṃ*)—of themselves
- विद्मना (*vidmanā*)—having knowledge of the going to the sacrifice
- प्र (*pra*) (जाताः (*jātāḥ*))—were born; such Maruts
- एवयामरुत् (*evayāmarut*)—the seer Evayāmarut
- ब्रुवते (*bruvate*)—praises
- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your

### Printed p. 252

- तत् (*tat*)—that well-known
- शवः (*śavaḥ*)—strength
- क्रत्वा (*kratvā*)—joined with extraordinary actions
- न आदृषे (*na ādṛṣe*)—remains unassailed
- एषां (*eṣāṃ*)—your
- तत् (*tat*)—that same strength
- दाना (*dānā*)—joined with the bestowal of what is desired
- मह्ना (*mahnā*)—and with greatness
- अद्रयो न (*adrayo na*)—like mountains
- अधृष्टासः (*adhṛṣṭāsaḥ*)—remain without movement (they remain firm)

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Maruts who were born together with the great Indra, who quickly and of themselves were born with the going to the sacrifice: such Maruts the seer Evayāmarut praises. O Maruts, your well-known strength, joined with extraordinary actions, remains unassailed. That same strength of yours, joined with the bestowal of what is desired and with greatness, remains unmoved like mountains.

##### English translation
Evayamarut glorifies those who are manifested with the great (Indra), who appear spontaneously and speedily with the knowledge (that the sacrifice is prepared) : your strength in action, Maruts, is not to be resisted, (though qualified) by (your) infinite liberality : you are immoveable as mountains.

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र ये दि॒वो बृ॑ह॒तः शृ॑ण्वि॒रे गि॒रा सु॒शुक्वा॑नः सु॒भ्व॑ एव॒याम॑रुत् ।  
> न येषा॒मिरी॑ स॒धस्थ॒ ईष्ट॒ आ अ॒ग्नयो॒ न स्ववि॑द्युतः॒ प्रस्यं॒द्रासो॒ धुनी॑नां ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । ये । दिवः । बृहतः । शृण्विरे । गिरा । सुशुक्वानः । सुभ्वः । एवयामरुत् ।  
> न । येषां । इरी । सधस्थे । ईष्टे । आ । अग्नयः । न । स्वविद्युतः । प्र । स्यंद्रासः ।  
> धुनीनां ॥ ३ ॥

### Printed p. 253

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ये मरुतो बृहतो दिवो द्युलोकादागच्छंतः प्र शृण्विरे शृण्वंति
> तान्मरुतो गिरैवयामरुत्सुतवान् । कीदृशास्ते । शुशुक्वानो दीप्ताः सुभ्वः सुष्टु
> भवंतः । येषां मरुतां सधस्थे सहस्थाने स्वकीये निवासे तिष्ठतामिरीरिता प्रेरिता
> नेष्ट आ । नेशते च चालयितुं । आ इति जार्थे । अग्नयो न अग्नय इव स्वविद्युतः
> स्वयमेव विद्योतमाना धुनीनां नदीनां स्यंद्रासो वर्षणेन चालयितारश्च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Maruts who, coming from the vast (बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)) heaven (दिवः (*divaḥ*)), the world of heaven, are heard (शृण्विरे (*śṛṇvire*)), they are praised by Evayāmarut with his speech (गिरा (*girā*)). What are they like? Shining (शुशुक्वानः (*śuśukvānaḥ*)), and well-being (सुभ्वः (*subhvaḥ*)). In the common dwelling (सधस्थे (*sadhasthe*)), their own abode, of those Maruts, no one, no impeller (इरी (*irī*)), is able to disturb them or to set them in motion; आ (*ā*) is in the sense of "and". They are like fires (अग्नयो न (*agnayo na*)), self-radiant (स्वविद्युतः (*svavidyutaḥ*)), shining of themselves; and they are the impellers (स्यंद्रासः (*syaṃdrāsaḥ*)) of the rivers (धुनीनां (*dhunīnāṃ*)) by raining.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- येषां (*yeṣāṃ*)—of which Maruts
- सदस्ये (*sadasye*)—in the house
- इरी (*irī*)—the hero who would make them move
- आ न ईष्टे (*ā na īṣṭe*)—no one is able (and which Maruts)
- अग्नयो न (*agnayo na*)—like fires
- स्वविद्युतः (*svavidyutaḥ*)—self-radiant
- धुनीनां (*dhunīnāṃ*)—of the rivers (for the flow)
- स्यंद्रासः (*syaṃdrāsaḥ*)—become the impellers (and)
- ये (*ye*)—which Maruts
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—from the vast
- दिवः (*divaḥ*)—heaven, coming
- प्र शृण्वरे (*pra śṛṇvare*)—hear our praises, such
- शुशुक्वानः (*śuśukvānaḥ*)—who shine exceedingly
- सुभ्वः (*subhvaḥ*)—and are extraordinary
- एवयामरुत् (*evayāmarut*)—the seer Evayāmarut
- गिरा (*girā*)—praises with his praises

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

In whose house no hero who would make them move is able to do so, and who, like fires, are the impellers of the flow of the self-radiant rivers, and who, coming from the vast heaven, hear our praises: such Maruts, shining exceedingly and extraordinary, the seer Evayāmarut praises with his praises.

##### English translation
Evayamarut glorifies with praise those who, (coming) brilliant and happy from the vast heaven, hear (his invocation); in whose dwelling there is no one able to disturb them, and, who, like self-radiant fires, are the impellers of the rivers.

### Printed p. 254

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स च॑क्रमे मह॒तो नि॑रुक्र॒मः स॑मा॒नस्मा॒त्सद॑स एव॒याम॑रुत् ।  
> य॒दायु॑क्त॒ त्मना॒ स्वादधि॒ ष्टुभि॒र्विष्प॑र्धसो॒ विम॑हसो॒  
> जिगा॑ति॒ शेवृ॑धो॒ नृभिः॑ ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । चक्रमे । महतः । निः । उरुक्रमः । समानस्मात् । सदसः । एवयामरुत् ।  
> यदा । अयुक्त । त्मना । स्वात् । अधि । स्नुभिः । विस्पर्धसः । विमहसः । जिगाति ।  
> शेवृधः । नृभिः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> स मरुद्दणो महतः प्रवृद्धात्समानस्मात्सर्वेषां साधारणात्सदसः स्थानादंतरिक्षादुरुक्रमो विस्तीर्णक्रमणः सन् निश्चक्रमे । निरगच्छत् । एवयामरुदृषिर्यदा
> स्वात्स्वकीयस्थानात् । अधीति पंचम्यर्थानुवादी । त्मनात्मनैव स्नुभिर्गंतृभिन्नृभिः
> स्वनेतृभिरश्वैरयुक्त युक्तोऽभवदागमनप्रार्थनाय । अथ बहुवदुच्यते । तदानीं
> विस्पर्धसो विविधस्पर्धाः । अहं पुरतो गच्छाम्यहं पुरतो गच्छामिति तेषां
> स्पर्धा । अथवा विगतस्पर्धाः । न ह्येषां सार्धं स्पर्धकाः संति । विमहसो विशिष्टबलाः शेवृधः शेवस्य सुखस्य वर्धयितारो चिगाति । निर्गच्छंति ॥ व्यत्ययेनैकवचनं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

That troop of Maruts went out (निश्चक्रमे (*niścakrame*)) from the sky, a seat common to all, vast, being wide-striding (उरुक्रमः (*urukramaḥ*)). When the seer Evayāmarut, from his own place (the word अधि (*adhi*) merely repeats the sense of the ablative), by himself, with his going (स्नुभिः (*snubhiḥ*)) leaders (नृभिः (*nṛbhiḥ*)), that is, his own guiding horses, was yoked (अयुक्त (*ayukta*)) in prayer for their coming, then, he now speaks in the plural, they are विस्पर्धसः (*vispardhasaḥ*), of various rivalry: "I go in front, I go in front", such is their rivalry; or else they are without rivalry, for they have no rivals. They are विमहसः (*vimahasaḥ*), of distinguished strength, and शेवृधः (*śevṛdhaḥ*), increasers of शेव (*śeva*), happiness. जिगाति (*jigāti*) means "they go forth"; the singular is by change of number.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यदा (*yadā*)—when
- एवयामरुत् (*evayāmarut*)—the seer Evayāmarut
- स्वात् आधि (*svāt ādhi*)—being at his own place (waiting for the prayer, then)
- त्मना (*tmanā*)—his own
- स्नुभिः (*snubhiḥ*)—remarkable
- नृभिः (*nṛbhiḥ*)—with horses
- आयुक्त (*āyukta*)—(the troop of Maruts) was yoked (and then)
- सः (*saḥ*)—that troop of Maruts
- महतः (*mahataḥ*)—from the very extensive
- समानस्मात् (*samānasmāt*)—and common to all
- सदसः (*sadasaḥ*)—region of the sky
- उरुक्रमः (*urukramaḥ*)—having a wide way of travelling
- निः चक्रमे (*niḥ cakrame*)—went out
- विस्पर्धसः (*vispardhasaḥ*)—those full of mutual rivalry
- विमहसः (*vimahasaḥ*)—those with exceeding strength
- शेवृधः (*śevṛdhaḥ*)—and increasers of happiness, the Maruts
- जिगाति (*jigāti*)—go forth

### Printed p. 255

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

As soon as the seer Evayāmarut prayed at his own place for the Maruts, the Maruts, yoked with remarkable horses, went out from the sky, which is very extensive and common to all. The Maruts, who vie with one another in going to the sacrifice, who have exceeding strength and who increase happiness, go forth for the sacrifice.

##### English translation
That wide-spreading troop (of Maruts) has issued from a spacious common dwelling-place, (where) Evayamarut (awaits them) when their car has been spontaneously harnessed with its rapid horses; and, emulous, vigorous, and conferring hapiness, they sally forth.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्व॒नो न वोऽम॑वान्रेजय॒द्वृषा॑ त्वे॒षो य॒यिस्त॑वि॒ष  ॒॑॑
> एवयामरुत् ।  
> येना॒ सहं॑त ऋं॒जत॒ स्वरो॑चिषः॒ स्थार॑श्मानो हिर॒ण्ययाः॑  
> स्वायु॒धास॑ इ॒ष्मिणः॑ ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वनः । नः । वः । अमवान् । रेजयत् । वृषा । त्वेषः । ययिः । तविषः । एवयामरुत् ।  
> येन । सहंतः । ऋंजत । स्वरोचिषः । स्थाऽरश्मानः । हिरण्ययाः । सुऽयुधासः ।  
> इष्मिणः ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतो वो युष्माकं स्वनो वेगजनितः शब्दो न रेजयत् न
> कंपयत्स्वेवयामरुतं मां ॥ सुपां सुलुगिति द्वितीयालुक् ॥ स्वनो विशेष्यते ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Maruts, your sound (स्वनः (*svanaḥ*)), produced by speed, is not to shake (रेजयत् (*rejayat*)), that is, not to make tremble, me, Evayāmarut; the accusative ending is dropped by the rule on case endings. The sound is then qualified.

### Printed p. 256

> अमवान् बलवान् वृषा वर्षिता वृष्टेष्टो दीपो ययिगंता तविषः
> प्रवृद्धः । येन स्वनेन संहतः शत्रूनभिभवंतो यूयमृंजत । प्रसाधयथ ॥
> लडर्थे लोट् ॥ कीदृशा यूयं । स्वरोचिषः स्वायत्तरश्मयः स्थारश्मानः स्थिररश्मयो हिरण्यया हिरण्याभरणाः स्वायुधासः स्वायत्तायुधा इष्मिणोऽन्नवंतो
> गमनवंतो वा ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sound is अमवान् (*amavān*), strong; वृषा (*vṛṣā*), a giver of rain; वृष्टेष्टो दीपो (*vṛṣṭeṣṭo dīpo*) **NOTE**; ययिः (*yayiḥ*), going; तविषः (*taviṣaḥ*), grown great. By this sound, joined together and overcoming your enemies, you ऋंजत (*ṛṃjata*), that is, you accomplish; the imperative is used in the sense of the present. What are you like? स्वरोचिषः (*svarociṣaḥ*), having rays under your own control; स्थारश्मानः (*sthāraśmānaḥ*), having steady rays; हिरण्ययाः (*hiraṇyayāḥ*), having golden ornaments; स्वायुधासः (*svāyudhāsaḥ*), having weapons under your own control; इष्मिणः (*iṣmiṇaḥ*), having food, or having going.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Maruts)
- स्वरोचिषः (*svarociṣaḥ*)—having radiance under your own control
- स्थारश्मानः (*sthāraśmānaḥ*)—having steady rays
- हिरण्ययाः (*hiraṇyayāḥ*)—having ornaments made of gold
- स्वायुधासः (*svāyudhāsaḥ*)—having weapons of your own
- इष्मिणः (*iṣmiṇaḥ*)—having food, you who are such
- येन (*yena*)—by which sound of yours
- सहंतः (*sahaṃtaḥ*)—facing the enemies
- यंजत (*yaṃjata*)—you adorn the world; such
- अमवान् (*amavān*)—powerful
- वृषा (*vṛṣā*)—rain-producing
- त्वेषः (*tveṣaḥ*)—radiant
- ययिः (*yayiḥ*)—moving
- तविषः (*taviṣaḥ*)—grown great
- वः स्वनः (*vaḥ svanaḥ*)—your sound
- एवयामरुत् (*evayāmarut*)—me, Evayāmarut
- न रेजयत् (*na rejayat*)—may it not alarm

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, you who have radiance under your own control, steady rays, ornaments made of gold, weapons of your own and food: may that sound of yours, by which you, facing the enemies, adorn the world, powerful, rain-producing, radiant, moving, grown great, not alarm me, Evayāmarut.

##### English translation
Let not the sound (of your approach, Maruts), which is mighty, the announcer of rain, the shedder of light, diffusive, loud, alarm Evayamarut: that sound wherewith,overcoming (your foes), you who are self-irradiating, lasting-rayed, ornamented with golden ornaments, self-weaponed, bestow-ing food, accomplish your functions

#### Ṛk 6

### Printed p. 257

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒पा॒रो वो॑ महि॒मा वृ॑द्धशवसस्त्वे॒षं शवो॑ऽवत्त्वेव॒याम॑रुत् ।  
> स्थाता॑रो॒ हि प्रसि॑त्सौ सं॒दृशि॒ स्थन॒ ते न॑ उरुष्यता नि॒दः शु॑शु॒क्वांसो॒ नाग्नयः॑ ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अपारः । वः । महिमा । वृद्धशवसः । त्वेषं । शवः । अवतु । एवयामरुत् ।  
> स्थातारः । हि । प्रसित् । संदृशि । स्थन । ते । नः । उरुष्यत । निदः । शुशुक्वांसः । न ।  
> अग्नयः ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतो वो युष्माकं महिमापारोऽनवधिकः । हे वृद्धशवसः
> प्रवृद्धबलास्तेषं दीप्तं शवो युष्माकं बलमवतु रक्षत्वेवयामरुतं मां ॥
> द्वितीयैकवचनस्य लुक् ॥ यूयं प्रसित् प्रबलबंधने नियमनवति यज्ञे संदृशि
> संदर्शने निमित्ते स्थातारो हि स्थाननियमेन तिष्ठंतो भवथ खलु । ते नोऽस्मान्निदो निंदकाच्छत्रोः सकाशादुरुष्यत । रक्षत । अग्निय इव शुशुक्वांसो यूयमिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Maruts, your greatness (महिमा (*mahimā*)) is boundless. O you of grown strength, may your bright, blazing strength protect me, Evayāmarut; the accusative ending is dropped. You are indeed those who stand, by fixity of station, in the sacrifice that has strong binding and regulation, on the occasion of sight (संदृशि (*saṃdṛśi*)). So protect us from the reviler, the enemy. You shine like fires.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Maruts)
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- महिमा (*mahimā*)—power
- अपारः (*apāraḥ*)—is endless
- वृद्धश्रवसः (*vṛddhaśravasaḥ*)—O Maruts, who have exceedingly great strength **NOTE**
- त्वेषं (*tveṣaṃ*)—shining
- शवः (*śavaḥ*)—your strength
- एवया मरुत् (*evayā marut*)—me, Evayāmarut
- अवतु (*avatu*)—may it protect
- प्रसित् (*prasit*)—in the sacrifice that has the form of a rule
- संदृशि (*saṃdṛśi*)—for the sake of the sight
- स्थातारो हि स्थान (*sthātāro hi sthāna*)—you must be present regularly
- अग्नयः न (*agnayaḥ na*)—like fires
- शुशुक्वांसः (*śuśukvāṃsaḥ*)—shining
- ते (*te*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- निदः (*nidaḥ*)—from the enemies who revile
- उरुष्यत (*uruṣyata*)—protect

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, your power is endless. O Maruts of exceedingly great strength, may your shining strength protect me, Evayāmarut. In this sacrifice, which has the form of a rule, you must be present regularly for the sight.

### Printed p. 258

Shining like fires, protect us from the enemies who revile.

##### English translation
Possessors of vast strength, may your unbounded greatness, your brilliant vigour, protect Evayamarut; for you are regulators for overseeing (what is fit for) the limits of the sacrifice: preserve us from those who revile us, you, who are like blazing fires.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> ते रु॒द्रासः॒ सुम॑खा अ॒ग्नयो॑ यथा तुविद्यु॒म्ना अ॑वंत्वेव॒याम॑रुत् ।  
> शर्धांस्यद्भुतैनसां ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> ते । रुद्रासः । सुमखाः । अग्नयः । यथा । तुविद्युम्नाः । अवंतु । एवयामरुत् ।  
> दीर्घं । पृथु । पप्रथे । सद्म । पार्थिवं । येषां । अज्मेषु । आ । महः । शर्धांसि ।  
> अद्भुतएनसां ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतो रुद्रासो रुद्रपुत्राः सुमखाः शोभनयज्ञा अग्नयो यथा
> अग्नय इव तुविद्युम्नाः प्रभूतदीप्तयः । अथवा अग्निसदृरास्तुविद्युम्नाः प्रभूतधना अवंतु रक्षंत्वेवयामरुदेतन्नामानमृषिं मां पार्थिवं। पृथिव्यत्रांतरिक्षं।
> तत्संबंधि दीर्घमायतं पृथु विस्तीर्णं सद्म सदनं पप्रथे। मरुद्भिः प्रथितमभूत् ।
> अद्भुतैनसामपापानां येषामजेषु गमनेषु महो महांति शर्धांसि चा
> गच्छंतीति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Maruts, you Rudras, that is, sons of Rudra, सुमखाः (*sumakhāḥ*), having good sacrifices, like fires (अग्नयो यथा (*agnayo yathā*)), तुविद्युम्नाः (*tuvidyumnāḥ*), of abundant brilliance; or else, fire-like and of abundant wealth: may they protect (अवंतु (*avaṃtu*)) me, the seer named Evayāmarut. पार्थिवं (*pārthivaṃ*): here "earth" means the atmosphere; their dwelling (सद्म (*sadma*)), long and wide, was spread (पप्रथे (*paprathe*)) by the Maruts. Of those who are sinless (अद्भुतैनसां (*adbhutainasāṃ*)), in whose goings (अजेषु (*ajeṣu*)) great strengths (महः शर्धांसि (*mahaḥ śardhāṃsi*)) go: such is the supplied remainder.

### Printed p. 259

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अग्नयो यथा (*agnayo yathā*)—like fires
- तुविद्युम्नाः (*tuvidyumnāḥ*)—having abundant radiance
- रुद्रासः (*rudrāsaḥ*)—sons of Rudra
- सुमखाः (*sumakhāḥ*)—having excellent sacrifices
- ते (*te*)—those same Maruts
- एवयामरुत् (*evayāmarut*)—me, Evayāmarut
- अवंतु (*avaṃtu*)—may they protect
- पार्थिवं (*pārthivaṃ*)—belonging to the atmosphere
- दीर्घ० (*dīrgha0*)—very long
- पृथु (*pṛthu*)—and wide
- सद्म (*sadma*)—the dwelling
- पप्रथे (*paprathe*)—was made bright by the Maruts
- अद्भुतैनसां (*adbhutainasāṃ*)—who are free from sin
- येषां (*yeṣāṃ*)—of these same Maruts
- अजेषु (*ajeṣu*)—at the time of going **NOTE**
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—great
- शर्धांसि (*śardhāṃsi*)—strengths full of splendour
- आ (*ā*)—spread

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the Maruts, who have abundant radiance like fires, who are sons of Rudra and have excellent sacrifices, protect me, Evayāmarut. Their dwelling, which belongs to the atmosphere, is very long and wide, and was made bright by the Maruts themselves. At the time of going of these same sinless Maruts, a great strength full of splendour spreads all around.

##### English translation
May those Rudras, the objects of worship, like resplendent fires, protect Evayamarut : they, whose ethereal dwelling, extended and wide, has been made illustrious (by them), and of whom, excmpt from blame, the mighty energies (are manifested) in their courses.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒द्वे॒षो नो॑ मरुतो गा॒तुमेत॑न॒ श्रोता॒ हवं॑ जरि॒तुरे॑व॒याम॑रुत् ।  
> विष्णो॑र्म॒हः स॑मन्यवो युयोतन॒ स्मद्र॒थ्यो॒३॒॑ न दं॒सनाप॒ ॑ ॒
> द्वेषांसि सनुतः ॥ ८ ॥

### Printed p. 260

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अद्वेषः । नः । मरुतः । गातुं । आ । इतन । श्रोत । हवं । जरितुः । एवयामरुत् ।  
> विष्णोः । महः । समन्यवः । युयोतन । स्मत् । रथ्यः । न । दंसना । अप । द्वेषांसि । सनतरिति ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मरुतो अद्वेषोऽद्वेषसो यूयं नोऽस्माकं गातुं गमनस्वभावं
> स्तोत्रं प्रत्येतन । आगच्छत । तदर्थं जरितुः स्तोतुरेवयामरुदेवयामरुतो
> मम हवं श्रोत । शृणुत । विष्णोर्व्यापकस्येंद्रस्य महो महतो हे समन्यवः
> समानयज्ञा ययूं स्मत्! आयं प्रशस्तनाम ! प्रशस्ता रथ्यो न रथिनो योद्धार
> इव । ते यथा शत्रूनपाकुर्वंति तद्वदस्माकं दंसना कर्मणा सनुतः । अंतर्हितनामैतत् । अंतर्हितान् द्वेषांसि द्वेष्टन् अप युयोतन । दूरे वियोजयत।
> पृथक्कुरुत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Maruts, you who are without hatred (अद्वेषः (*adveṣaḥ*)), that is, without enmity: come (एतन (*etana*)) towards our praise (गातुं (*gātuṃ*)), which has the nature of going. For this, hear (श्रोत (*śrota*)) the invocation (हवं (*havaṃ*)) of me, Evayāmarut, the praiser (जरितुः (*jarituḥ*)). O समन्यवः (*samanyavaḥ*), partakers of the same sacrifice, of the great Viṣṇu, the all-pervading Indra (विष्णोः महः (*viṣṇoḥ mahaḥ*)): you are praised (स्मत् (*smat*)) **NOTE**, like praised charioteers, warriors (रथ्यो न (*rathyo na*)). As they drive enemies away, so by your work (दंसना (*daṃsanā*)) drive away (अप युयोतन (*apa yuyotana*)) from us the hostile ones (द्वेषांसि (*dveṣāṃsi*)), the hidden ones (सनुतः (*sanutaḥ*), this is a word for "hidden"), to a distance; separate them from us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मरुतः (*marutaḥ*)—O Maruts
- अद्वेषः (*adveṣaḥ*)—you who are without enemies
- नः (*naḥ*)—our
- गातुं (*gātuṃ*)—towards the praise
- एतन (*etana*)—come
- जरितुः (*jarituḥ*)—the praiser
- एवयामरुत् (*evayāmarut*)—my, Evayāmarut's
- हवं (*havaṃ*)—invocation
- श्रोत (*śrota*)—hear
- विष्णोः (*viṣṇoḥ*)—of the all-pervading Indra
- महः (*mahaḥ*)—to the greatness
- समन्यवः (*samanyavaḥ*)—you who are equal sharers in the sacrifice
- स्मत् (*smat*)—praised
- रथ्यो न (*rathyo na*)—like warriors
- दंसना (*daṃsanā*)—by your works
- सनुतः (*sanutaḥ*)—who remain hidden
- द्वेषांसि (*dveṣāṃsi*)—enemies
- अप युयोतन (*apa yuyotana*)—drive far away

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Maruts, you who are without enemies, come towards our praise. Hear the invocation of me, Evayāmarut, the praiser. You who are sharers in the sacrifice equal to the greatness of the all-pervading Indra, drive far away, like praised warriors, by your works, our enemies who remain hidden.

##### English translation
Maruts, devoid of enmity, come to our proffered praise, hear the invocation of your adorer. Evayamarut : do you, who are associated in the worship of Vishnu, drive away, as warriors (scatter their enemies), our secret foes.

#### Ṛk 9

### Printed p. 261

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> गंता॑ नो य॒ज्ञं य॑ज्ञियाः सु॒शमि॒ श्रोता॒ हव॑मर॒क्ष  ॒॑॑
> एवयामरुत् ।  
> ज्येष्ठा॑सो॒ न पर्व॑तासो॒ व्यो॑मनि यू॒यं तस्य॑ प्रचेतसः॒  
> स्न्यात॑ दु॒र्धर्त॑वो नि॒दः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> गंत । नः । यज्ञं । यज्ञियाः । सुशमि । श्रोत । हवं । अरक्षः । एवयामरुत् ।  
> ज्येष्ठासः । न । पर्वतासः । विओमनि । यूयं । तस्य । प्रचेतसः । स्यात । दुःदर्शनः । विदुः ॥ ९ ॥

> धर्तवः । निदः ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अध्यायोपाकरणोत्सर्गयोर्विनियुक्ता । आ. गृ. ३-५ ॥
> हे यज्जिया मरुतो यूयं नोऽस्माकं यज्ञं गंत । आगच्छत । सुशमि
> शोभनकर्म यथा भवति तथा । सुकर्मत्वायेत्यर्थः । तदर्थं नो हवमस्मदीयमाह्वानं श्रोत । शृणुत ॥ अरक्षो रक्षोवर्जिता यूयं ॥ लिंगवचनयोर्व्यत्ययः ॥ ज्येषासो न ज्येषा इव पर्वतासो विंध्यादय इवातिप्रवृद्धा
> व्योमन्यंतरिक्षे वर्तमानाः प्रचेतसो यूयं तस्य निदो निंदकस्य दुर्धर्तवो
> दुर्धराःस्यात । भवत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

This Ṛk is applied in the ceremonies of the beginning of study and of its closing (अध्यायोपाकरणोत्सर्गयोर्विनियुक्ता (*adhyāyopākaraṇotsargayorviniyuktā*)), Ā. Gṛ. 3-5 **NOTE**. O worshipful Maruts, come (गंत (*gaṃta*)) to our sacrifice, in such a way that the work is well done (सुशमि (*suśami*)), that is, for the sake of good work. For this hear our invocation.
